# Tirucchanda Viruttam

## 1. Punilaya aindumay

*pūnilāya aindumāyp punaṟkaṇ ninṟa nāngumāyttīnilāya mūnṟumāyc ciṟanda kāl iraṇḍumāymīnilāyadu onṟumāgi vēṟu vēṟu tanmaiyāynī nilāya vaṇṇa(m) ninnai yār ninaikka vallarē?*

**Пословный перевод:**

pū nilāya aindumāy – the five qualities such as śabda (sound) which are present in earth; punal kaṇ ninṟa nāngumāy – the four qualities of śabda, sparśa (touch), rūpa (form) and rasa (taste) which are present in water; tī nilāya mūnṟumāy – the three qualities of śabda, sparśa and rūpa which are present in fire; siṟanda kāḷ iraṇḍumāy – the two qualities of śabda and sparśa which are present in the mighty wind; mī nilāyadu onṟumāgi – the single quality of śabda which is present in ethereal layer (sky); vēṟu vēṟu tanmaiyāy – being the indvelling soul of dēva (celestial) etc entities which are distinct from one another; nī nilāya vaṇṇam – the manner in which you are present in the causative and effectual entities; ninnai – you (who are the causative factor for the world); Ar ninaikka vallarē – who is capable of thinking?

**Перевод:**

**Addressing emperumān, āḻvār says that he is the indvelling soul of the five elements of ethereal layer, wind, fire, water and earth, with their qualities of sound, touch, form, taste and smell respectively. He says that emperumān is the causative factor for the world and is the indvelling soul for all the entities such as celestial, human etc. He says that this meaning is beyond the comprehension of those who do not understand vēdams and those who misinterpret vēdams.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-55 “bhūtēbhyō’ṇḍam mahābuddē mahat tadudakēśayam l prākrutam brahmarūpasya viṣṇō:stānamanuttamam ll” (Oh the greatly wise one! This universe, which is huge and which floats in the enveloping water, is made of the panca bhūtams (five elements). For viṣṇu, who is together with a huge divine, auspicious form, this location is a lowly one, made of prākrutam (primordial matter) (distinct from the paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) which is aprākrutam (not made of primordial matter; extraordinary)), you (addressing emperumān) are the materialistic cause for the five elements such as bhūmi (earth) etc which are composed of the five qualities of śabda (sound) etc. You remain as the antaryāmi (indvelling soul) for these elements. This meaning is beyond the capabilities of bāhyas, who do not know the meanings of vēdams (sacred texts) (and who do not accept vēdams) and the kudruśtis (who give wrong interpretations to vēdams)*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pūnilāya aindumāy** – being the five qualities of sound, touch, form, taste and smell which are present in earth. While earth has only one quality, of smell, as brought out by nyāyamatam which says *gandavatī prutvi* (the one which has the quality of smell is earth) and śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-44 which says *sangātō jāyatē tasmāt tasyā gandō guṇōmata*: (earth, which is composed of all the qualities, is made from that tanmātra (subtle element) of smell; for earth, smell is considered to be a unique quality), how is it that āḻvār says that earth has all the five qualities of sound, touch etc? śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-50 says *ākāśa vāyu tējāmsi salilam prutvī tatā* l *śabdādibhir guṇair brahman samyutānyuttarōttarai* ll (Oh brāhmaṇa! The five elements of ethereal layer, wind, fire, water and earth have the single quality of sound, the two qualities of sound and touch, the three qualities of sound, touch and form, the four qualities of sound, touch, form and taste and the five qualities of sound, touch, form, taste and smell, respectively), Thus, since earth, which is the end effect of ethereal layer, wind, fire and water, has the qualities of the respective element, it is said that earth has the qualities of all five elements.

**punaṟkaṇ ninṟa nāngumāy** – having the four qualities of sound, touch, form and taste which are present in water.  
śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-42 says *sambhavanti tatōmbhāmsi rasādārāṇi tāni tu* (water is formed from the *tanmātrā* (subtle element) of taste; it is the repository for taste). While the quality of water is only taste, just as it has been said in the previous discussion, since it is formed from ethereal layer, wind and fire, water has the qualities of all four elements, viz. sound, touch, form and taste.

**tī nilāya mūnṟumāy** – śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-40 says *jyōtir utpadyatē vāyōs tad rūpaguṇam ucyatē* ((Being together with rūpa (form) *tanmātrā)*, fire comes out of wind. It has form as its unique characteristic). Just as it has been mentioned before, along with its characteristic of form, fire, being formed from ethereal layer and wind, has the qualities of sound and touch too and thus has all the three qualities of sound, touch and form.

**siṟanda kāḷ iraṇḍumāy** – Due to its mobility, wind is the reason for creatures to breathe. Hence it has the might of being the sustaining factor for all. It has two qualities, of sound and touch. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-39 says *balavānubhavad vāyus tasya sparśō guṇō mata*: (Mighty wind results from the *tanmātrā* of touch; its distinguished characteristic is said to be ‘touch’). Along with wind’s characteristic of touch, it also has the characteristic ‘sound’ coming from ethereal layer, from which it is formed. Thus, wind has the two qualities of sound and touch.

**mīdilāya onṟumāgi** – being known as *mīdu*, ethereal layer (or sky) has the quality of sound. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-37 says *sasarja śabda tanmātrādākāśam śabda lakśaṇam* (from the tanmātrā of sound, ethereal layer was created, having the quality of sound as its unique characteristic). Thus, ethereal layer has the single characteristic of sound. *mīdu* – that which remains. The last letter (*tu*) is hidden and the word becomes *mī*. Ethereal layer has the pride of being the causative factor for the other four elements and it also has the greatness of giving space for all elements, other than itself, to stay. This ethereal layer, thus, is denoted as *mīdu* – that which remains \[in excess\]. Earth and the other four elements are the reason for the existence of this universe. These elements (ethereal layer, wind fire, water and earth) are under the control of emperumān. Along with the elements, their characteristics are also under the control of emperumān (sound, touch etc). nammāḻvār, in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-9-1 has said “*nīrāy nilanāyt tīyāyk kālāy neduvānāy…*”(emperumān manifests himself as water, earth, fire, wind and the expansive ethereal layer), indicating a *śarīra-śarīri* (body and soul) connection, with emperumān as soul, dwelling inside these elements. Since qualities (śabdam, rūpam etc) cannot be pervaded, we cannot give this connection between qualities and emperumān. However, since the qualities’ *sattā stiti pravrutti* (existence, position and activities) are under the control of emperumān, they will have a connection with emperumān through substrate-attribute manner (emperumān is the substrate and these qualities are his attributes).

While *mahān* (first effectual entity or derivative from *mūla prakruti* (primordial matter)) and *ahankāram* (ego, the derivative from *mahān*) are the causative factors for the five elements, why is it that the five elements alone are connected directly with emperumān \[and not these two entities\]? taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 1 says “*tasmād vā ētasmād ātmana ākāśas sambhūta ākāśād vāyu*: …….. *adbhya*:*prutivī*” (from such a paramātmā, the five great elements such as ethereal layer etc came about). Just as *vēdam* had set aside *mahān* and *ahankāram* which cannot be perceived by the senses and projected the five elements as causative factors for the universe, since they can be perceived by the senses, here too, only the five great elements have been identified with emperumān directly. As mentioned in the first ślōkam (from śrī viṣṇu purāṇam) under *avatārikai*, haven’t sages too said that it is only the five elements, which have been mingled together, which are the reason for universe? While *acit tatvams* (entities without the ability to think) \[such as ethereal layer, wind, fire etc\] have been mentioned here, since brahmam (supreme entity, emperumān) who is together with the subtle forms of cētana (those with knowledge) and acētana (those without knowledge) entities, is the causative factor for the universe which is with the gross forms of cētana and acētana entities, it can be said that the collection of badda jīvātmās (cētana entities who are bound together with samsāram), which is bound with the acit tatvams, is also under the control of emperumān. Thus, it has been affirmed with these statements that emperumān is the material causative factor for universe, which is primordial in nature. Further \[in the pāsuram\], it says that he is the causative factor for all the entities within these elements.

**vēṟu vēṟu tanmaiyāy** – emperumān is the indvelling soul for the four different types of living creatures such as *dēva* (celestial), *manuśya* (human), *tiryak* (animal) and *stāvara* (immovable) entities, which are present inside the universe. While the *svarūpam* (basic nature) of ātmās is one of *jñāna* (knowledge) and *ānanda* (happiness), the meaning of *vēṟu vēṟu tanmai* (different qualities) is that due to the effect of their *karmas* (past deeds), they take different physical forms such as *dēva, manuśya* etc.

**nī nilāya vaṇṇam** – you (refers to emperumān) are together with cētana and acētana śarīrams (physical forms), in both the causative and effectual \[subtle and gross\] states, as their indvelling soul. In other words, despite pervading them from within and outside, the *svarūpa vikāram* (transformation of basic form) of acētana entities and the *svabhāva vikāram* (transformation of emotions such as ignorance, sorrow etc) of cētana entities do not affect you.

**ninnai** – you, who are together with cētana and acētana entities, and are the material causative factor for the worlds

**vaṇṇam ninnai** – the manner in which you are remaining in the world and the way that you are the materialistic causative factor for the world

**Ar ninakka vallarē** – who will know you, truly? Could those who follow the *vaiśēśika* philosophy that the joined atoms are the causative factor for the universe, know you? Could those who follow the *sānkhya* philosophy that the primordial matter is the materialistic cause of the universe and that the union of cētana and acētana entities sustains the worlds, know you? Could the śaivaites who follow the philosophy that Sivan is the efficient cause and nārāyaṇan is the materialistic cause, which goes against the grain of vēdams since efficient cause and materialistic cause cannot be two different entities, know you? Could those who follow the *bhēdābhēda* philosophy which states that cit, acit and īśvaran are transformations of brahmam, know you? Could those who follow *māyāvādi* philosophy which states that everything other than brahmam, which is without any distinguishing feature, is illusion, know you? āḻvār says that other than those who know the truthful enunciations of vēdāntams (upaniśats), no one else, such as those who do not believe vēdams and those who give wrongful interpretation to vēdams, is capable of knowing that emperumān is the causative factor for the world.

We will next take up the 2nd pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 2. Arum arum

*āṟum āṟum āṟumāy ōr aindum aindum aindumāyēṟu sīr iraṇḍu mūnṟum ēḻum āṟum ettumāyvēṟu vēṟu jñānamāgi meyyinōdu poyyumāyūṟodōsai āya aindum āya āya māyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āṟum – six activities, starting with learning to recite vēdam; āṟum – six seasons appropriate for observing various karmas (deeds); āṟumāy – six yāgams (rituals) observed, starting with āgnēyam; ōr aindum – five mahā yajñams (offerings) such as dēva yajñam (offering to celestial entities), pitru yajñam (offering to forefathers), bhūta yajñam (offering to other creatures), manuśya yajñam (offering to guests) and brahma yajñam (offering to emperumān); aindum – offering to the five vital airs \[before eating\]; aindumāy – offering to five fires such as gārhapatya, āhavanīya, dakśiṇāgni, sabhyam and āvasadyam; ēṟu – distinguished; sīr – having wealth; iraṇḍum – jñānam (knowledge) and vairājñam (detachment); mūnṟum – the three ultimate benefits of aiśvaryam (wealth), kaivalyam (ātmā enjoying itself) and bhagavat prāpti (attaining emperumān) resulting from carrying out upāsanam (devotion to emperumān) (alternatively, the three stages of parabhakti, parajñānam and paramabhakti, which result from jñānam and vairājñam); ēḻum – seven traits starting with vivēkam (prudence) which are aids to devotion; āṟum – six qualities of jñānam (knowledge), śakti (strength) etc which nurture devotion; ettumāy – eight qualities starting with apahatapāpmā, seen with muktātmās; vēṟu vēṟu jñānamāgi – conducting other philosophies such as bāhya (not believing in vēdams), kudruśti (misinterpreting vēdams) etc; meyyinōdu – being truthful (to those who are desirous of mōkśam); poyyumāy – (granting lowly benefits to those who seek such benefits, in order to move away from them) being untruthful; ūṟodu ōsaiyāya aindum āya – (for those who have paramabhakti) being the five subtle elements of śabdam etc; āya māyanē – Oh the amazing entity who incarnated as a covherd

**Перевод:**

**emperumān remains as the six activities starting with learning to recite vēdam; he is the six seasons which are appropriate for carrying out deeds; he is the six rituals, starting with āgnēyam, observed by people. He is the five mahāyajñams as well as offerings to the five vital airs and five fires. He is the distinguished and wealthy jñānam and vairājñam as well as the three benefits of aiśvaryam, kaivalyam and mōkśam (Alternatively, the three stages of parabhakti, parajñāna and paramabhakti). He is the seven traits starting with vivēkam, the eight qualities which muktātmās have, starting with apahatapāpmā. It is he who conducts all the other philosophies too, such as bāhya, kudruśti etc. He is truthful to those who desire mōkśam and untruthful to those who desire lowly benefits. He is the five subtle elements starting with śabdam for those who have paramabhakti. He is the amazing entity who incarnated as a covherd \[kriṣṇāvatāram\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Just as it is mentioned in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 3-10 “sahayajñai: prajā: sruśtvā purōvāca prajāpati: l anēna prasaviśyadvamēśavō’stviśta kāmaduk ll” (bhagavān, the lōkanāta (lord of the universe), after creating the cētanas (entities with knowledge), along with yajñams (rituals) in the early times, told them the following: – with this, you expand your clan. This will fulfil all your desires), jīvātmās, were created \[given body\] by you \[emperumān\], the causative factor for the universe. Starting with karmayōgam (the path of attaining emperumān through their deeds) and ending with paramabhakti (the stage where the jīvātmā cannot sustain self without attaining emperumān), the jīvātmā has to attain various stages, pray to emperumān and meditate on emperumān’s divine, auspicious form. In order to be the desired entity for such jīvātmās, emperumān incarnated as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa). āḻvār wonders as to who could think of such simplicity on the part of emperumān (in carrying out all these for the sake of jīvātmās),*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**āṟum āṟum āṟumāy** – the six activities of brāhmaṇas are: *adyayanam* (reciting vēdam), *adyāpanam* (teaching vēhdam), *yajanam* (carrying out yāgams (rituals)), *yājanam* (doing yāgams for others), *dānam* (offering alms to others) and *pratigraham* (accepting alms from others). Knowing the six seasons when the karmas (deeds) arising out of the first mentioned *adyayanam* \[above\] are to be done, such as *vasantam* (spring)*, grīśmam* (summer)*, varśam* (monsoon)*, śarat* (autumn)*, hēmantam* (winter) and *śiśiram* (cold)*.* Carrying out the six yāgams of *āgnēyam,* *agnīśōmīyam*, *upāmsuyājam*, *aindram dadi*, *aindram paya*: and *aindrāgnam*. These six yāgams are representative of all the karmas. The reason for mentioning these activities in the same breath as emperumān \[or equating these six with him\] is due to the fact that he implores his devotees to carry out these deeds, just as he has said in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 10-10 “*tēśām satatayuktānām bhajatām prīti pūrvakam* l *dadāmi buddi yōgam tam yēna māmupayānti tē* \|\|” (I affectionately shower intelligence to those who desire to be with me all the time and who worship me such that they can use it as a path to attain me). The reason for mentioning emperumān in the same breath as the six seasons, for carrying out karmas, is that it is he who conducts them. The reason for mentioning the deeds to be followed by cētanas, in the same breath as emperumān is due to the fact that it is only he who is vorśipped through these karmas.

**ōr aindum aindum aindumāy** – emperumān is vorśipped through the *pancamahāyajñams* (five great rituals), which create the qualification for a person to observe the karmas mentioned earlier, as well as the five offerings made in the rituals. Also, he is the *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) for the five *agnis* (ritualistic fires). *dēva yajñam, pitru yajñam, bhūta yajñam, manuśya yajñam* and *brahma yajñam* are the five yajñams (rituals) denoted by the first term *aindu*. At the time of taking food, *āhutis* (offerings) are made to the five vital airs through the chanting *prāṇāya svāhā, apānāya svāhā, vyānāya svāhā, udānāya svāhā* and *samānāya svāhā*. These five āhutis (offerings) are denoted by the second term *aindu*. The five agnis (ritualistic fires) namely *gārhapatyam,* *āhavanīyam*, *dakśiṇāgni*, *sabhyam* and *āvasadyam* are denoted by the final *aindu* in the verse. Since he is vorśipped through the five mahāyajñams and the five *āhutis* and since he is the *antaryāmi* of the five agnis, he is mentioned in the same breath as these.

**ēṟu sīr iraṇḍum** – taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli says “*darmēṇa pāpamapanudati*” (by observing activities relating to karmas, (the jīvātmā) gets rid of the sins). In line with this, by carrying out the activities ordained in the śāstras seen previously, sins, which were a hurdle in attaining mōkśam, get removed. Then, knowledge about emperumān in the form of devotion and detachment towards other matters, happen. These two \[knowledge about emperumān and detachment towards other matters\] are termed as *ēṟu sīr iraṇḍum*. pēyāḻvār had referred to these two in his mūnṟām tiruvandaādi 14<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*māṟpāl manam suḻippa mangaiyar tōḷ kaivittu*” (engaging the mind with emperumān and leaving the desire in worldly pursuits). Since emperumān creates these two, he is mentioned in the same breath as these two.

**mūnṟum** – he is also seen as the benefits derived from devotion mentioned above, namely *aiśvaryam* (wealth), *kaivalyam* (ātmā engaging with itself) and *bhagavadprāpti* (attaining emperumān). Alternatively, the three characteristics of *parabhakti* (seeing emperumān through the mind)*, parajñāna* (engaging with emperumān) and *paramabhakti* (inability to sustain oneself in separation from emperumān), which are the results of earlier mentioned devotion and detachment, could be considered as having been referred to by the term *mūnṟum*.

**ēḻum** – seven instruments (means) starting with *vivēkam,* which are responsible for removing the blemishes in the mind due to which the mental make-up for engaging in *karma yōgam* to *parabhakti* as stated earlier is created. These seven means are mentioned in the vākyakāra vākyam which was written at the beginning of jijñāsa adikaraṇam of śrī bhāśyam (a treatise which explains in detail, vēdavyāsar’s major work, brahma sūtram) “*tallabdir vivēka vimōkābhyāsa kriya kalyāṇānavasādānuddarśēbhya*:” (devotion is taken to its completion through: (1) vivēkam (ability to know the distinction) which comes from purity of body, which in turn results from purity of food taken in, (2) vimōkam (liberation from worldly objects), (3) abhyāsam (practicing meditation), (4) kriya (deeds, which are followed to the extent possible, of *pancamahāyajñam* (as seen under the first *aindu*, above), (5) kalyāṇa guṇas (auspicious qualities such as truth, compassion, donation, not hurting others etc), (6) being without mental sorrows and (7) being without excessive happiness). Thus, it has been said that devotion results from observing the seven ordained activities of vivēkam etc along with the karmas which are ordained in śāstras as *kriyā*. Since these are not possible without emperumān’s mercy, these are mentioned in the same breath as emperumān.

**āṟum** – the six qualities which nurture devotion and which by themselves are apt to be attained, namely *jñānam* (knowledge), *balam* (strength), *aiśvaryam* (wealth), *vīryam* (valour), *śakti* (power) and *tējas* (splendour).

**ettumāy** – cāndōjña upaniśat 8-7-1 says “*ya ātmā apahatapāpmā vijarō vimrutyur viśōkō vijigatsō’pipāsas* *satyakāmas satyasankalpas sōnvēśtavya*:” (That ātmā who is without any sins, without old age, without death, without sadness, without hunger, without thirst, without wasted desire and with ability to complete any task taken, is apt to be sought). These are the eight qualities which emperumān, who showers his mercy on a jīvātmā who is about to be liberated \[from samsāram and who is about to reach paramapadam\], grants to that jīvātmā so that he becomes similar to emperumān in these eight qualities. cāndōjña upaniśat 8-12-2 says “*ēśa samprasādō’smāccarīrāt samuttāya param* *jyōtir upasampadya svēna rūpēnābhiniśpadyatē*” (this jīvan, leaving from this (physical) form, attains the supremely splendorous śrīman nārāyaṇan and attains his basic natural characteristics). Since these qualities are mercifully granted by emperumān, these are mentioned in the same breath as emperumān.

**vēṟu vēṟu jñānamāgi** – emperumān had taken various incarnations such as budda et al, in which he had shown, to those who have not had a mind which is free from defects and who have not had the fortune of being blessed by emperumān even when they were in their mothers’ wombs, *smritis* (spiritual texts) which were against the vēdams and which were wrongfully interpreting vēdams.

**meyyinōdu poyyumāy** – by showing himself to mumukśus (those who are desirous of attaining mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) who were mentioned in the first part of this pāsuram, emperumān acts in a truthful manner. At the same time, to those who desire lowly benefits, he grants them those benefits and stands aloof. Thus, he is untruthful to them \[in comparison with the mumukśus\]. The words *mey* (truth) and *poy* (lie) refer to the person who speaks them (emperumān).

**ūṟodōsaiyāya aindumāy** – The term *ūru* refers to the sense of touch and the term *ōsai* refers to the sense of sound. Mentioning these two words would extend to the other three senses of form, taste and smell too. Thus, emperumān remains as the five senses for those who have *paramabhakti*, manifesting in all the sweet materials, for them. For *jñānis* (wise people) who are *mahātmās* (great souls) he is all these materials. Has it not been said in bhagavat gītā 7-19 “*vāsudēvas sarvamiti samahātmā*” (one who says that vāsudēvan (emperumān) is the sustaining, nourishing and enjoyable entity is a mahātmā), in tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-1 “*uṇṇum sōṟu parugu nīr tinnum veṟṟilaiyum ellām kaṇṇanē*” (It is only kriṣṇa who is the sustaining food, nourishing water and enjoyable betel leaves) and in tiruvāimoḻi 5-1-8 “*sēlēy kaṇṇiyarum perum selvamum nanmakkaḷum mēlāt tāy tandaiyum iniyāvārē*” (hereafter, only emperumān is the women with fish-like eyes, great opulence, children with auspicious qualities and eminent parents for me)?

**āya māyanē** – one who has the amazing quality of incarnating in the clan of covherd-people and becoming one amongst them! In other words, emperumān does not merely confer benefit on his followers, standing in an exalted position, he also incarnates with his most divine, auspicious physical form amidst samsāris so that those who worship him can meditate on his auspicious, attainable form. For those who take refuge under him (looking up to him as the means), he redeems them from worldly pursuits and becomes dear to them. For those who engage with him with parabhakti, just as it is mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 8-5-2 “*kāṇa vārāy enṟenṟu kaṇṇum vāyum tuvarndu*” (I have been calling you, to come and manifest your divine form to me, for a very long time, with my eyes and mouth having dried up totally), he remains as the sweet materials which they wish for. Has he not said at the beginning of the fourth chapter of bhagavat gītā that his incarnations are only for protecting the eminent people, and to establish righteousness? In the vyākhyānam, periyavāccān piḷḷai has said that we should bring forward the lines *yār ninaikka vallār* (who is capable of thinking?) to this pāsuram and get the appropriate meaning (no one can think of his amazing activities).

Next, we will take up the 3rd pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 3. Aindum aindum

*aindum – te five elements, starting vit etereal layer (sky)aindum – te five jñānēndriyams (senses of knovledge)aindumāgi – becoming te five karmēndriyams (senses of activity)aindum – te five tanmātrās (subtle elements)mūnṟum – prakruti (primordial matter), mahat (causative factor, called as great) and ahankāram (causative factor of mahat, called as ego)onṟum āgi – becoming manas (mind)allavaṟṟuḷ āyumāy – becoming te antaryāmi (in-dvelling soul) for te cētanas (entities vhic are vit knovledge), vho are different from te earlier mentioned acētanas (entities vhic are vitout knovledge)ninṟa – one vho remained during delugeādi – being te causative factordēvanē – ōh one vho acts casually!aindum – for te aprākruta pancaśaktis (five elements vhic are seen in nitya vibhūti)aindum – for te five jñānēndriyamsaindumāgi – being te controller for te five karmēndriyams tooandarattu – in paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam)aṇaindu – fitting vellninṟu – remainingaindum – te five enjoyments, starting vit śabdam (sound) etcaindum āya – te five entities of bhōgastānam (place vhere someting is enjoyed), bhōgōpakaraṇam (te instrument utilised for enjoyment), tose among nityasūris vho engage vit emperumān’s qualities and tose vho engage in serving him in śrī vaikuṇṭam, and muktātmāsninnai – youār ninaikka vallarē – vho is capable enough to knov you?*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh the great one who remained during the time of deluge! You are the five great elements, starting with the ethereal layer. You are the five organs of knowledge and the five organs of activities. You are the five subtle elements (starting with sound), the three entities of primordial matter, mahat and ahankāram. You are the mind too \[thus describing the 24 acētana entities\]. You are the in-dwelling soul of the cētanas, who were not included in the earlier list of 24 entities. You are the controller of the five aprākruta śaktis in paramapadam, the five organs of knowledge and activities, there. You fit well in paramapadam, remaining as the five enjoyments, starting with sound, the five entities of bhōgastānam, bhōgōpakaraṇam, the two classifications of nityasūris and finally, the muktātmās. Who is capable of knowing you?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the first pāsuram, āḻvār said that emperumān is the causative factor for the worlds. In the second pāsuram, he mentioned the means by which one can attain him. He says, in this pāsuram, that emperumān manifested to him the way he was together with the leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) for which he was the cause, as mentioned in the first pāsuram and the way he was together with nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm) which is the benefit for practicing the means, as mentioned in the second pāsuram. He says that he was able to see these because of the causeless mercy shown by emperumān towards him and that there is none in the world who could see these on one’s own efforts. aindum aindum aindumāgi allavaṟṟuḷāyumāyaindum mūnṟum onṟumāgi ninṟa ādi dēvanēaindum aindum aindumāgi andarattu aṇaindu ninṟuaindum aindum āya ninnai yār ninaikka vallarē Word-by-Word Menaings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**aindum aindum aindumāgi** – being the five elements of ethereal layer, wind, fire, water and earth; the five jñānēndriyams (senses of knowledge) of skin, mouth, eyes, ears and nose; and, the five karmēndriyams of speech, hands, legs and the two organs of excreta.

**aindum mūnṟum** – the five subtle elements namely sound, touch, form, taste and smell, which are the causes for the five elements, as well as the three entities which are responsible for these five subtle elements, namely ahankāram (ego), mahān (great) which is the cause for ahankāram and the very fine prakruti (primordial matter) which is the cause for mahān.

**onṟumāgi** – Just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 6-7-28 “*mana Eva manuśyāṇām kāraṇam banda mōkśayō*: l *bandāya viśayā sangi muktyai nirviśayam mana*: ll” (It is the mind which is the cause for people’s bonding to, as well as liberation from, samsāram; the mind which engages with śabdam and other worldly matters becomes the reason for bondage with samsāram while the mind which is detached from worldly pursuits is the reason for liberation) mind is the entity which is capable of binding the jīvan (cētana or one with knowledge) to samsāram or liberating the jīvan from it. This entity of mind, along with the earlier mentioned 23 entities, together form the group of 24 entities which are acētana, and within which emperumān remains as the antaryāmi (indvelling soul), controlling them.

**allavaṟṟuḷāyumāy** – he also remains the antaryāmi of samsāri cētanas, which are distinct from the 24 acētana entities and which are bound with prakruti.

**allavaṟṟuḷ** – the opinion is that cētanas are very much distinct from the acētana entities mentioned earlier.

**ninṟa ādi dēvanē** – Oh one who is the causative entity of the universe, who remained together with the stūla cētanācētana entities (the cētana and acētana entities which are in gross manifestation, with names and physical forms) just as you had remained together with the sūkśma cētanācētana entities (the cētana and acētana entities which are in subtle manifestation, without names and physical forms)!

Thus, until now āḻvār has described about how emperumān, remaining as antaryāmi of leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm, comprising the 24 acētana entities and one cētana entity), manifested himself to him. In the next half of the pāsuram, āḻvār describes how emperumān manifested himself to him, being together with nitya vibhūti (spiritual realm), along with divine, auspicious form.

**aindum aindum aindumāgi** –  pāncarātram ślōkam “*paramēśti pumān viśvō nivruttis sarva Eva hi*: \| *paramēśti smruta*:*śabdē sparśē tu puruśa*: *smruta*: ll *viśvātmā tējasi prōktō nivruttyātmā rasē smruta*: \| *sarvātmā katitōgandē viśayē puruśs smruta*: ll” (the five upaniśats are: paramēśti, pumān, viśvan, nivrutti, sarvan. Of these, paramēśti has the subtle element of sound, pumān has touch, viśvan has form, nivrutti has taste, sarvan has smell and puruśan has matter). Unlike prakruti (primordial matter) which has a mixture of satva (purely good or noble qualities), rajas (qualities of passion and anger) and tamas (qualities of ignorance, laziness etc), the aprākruta realm of nityavibhūti is full of satva guṇa alone. emperumān has divine, auspicious form which is composed of the five śaktis \[five upaniśats\], containing five jñānēndriyams, five karmēndriyams, controlling them fully.

Next, the pāsuram says that he has nitya vibhūti.

**andarattu aṇanindu ninṟu aindum aindum** – the five entities which are to be enjoyed, namely śabda, sparśa, rūpa, rasa and ganda (sound, touch, form taste and smell), (1) the place for enjoyment where these five entities are enjoyed, (2) the implements with which these are enjoyed, such as umbrella, covry (fly-flapper), (3 , 4) nityasūris who are completely engaged in carrying out kainkaryams to emperumān and who are fully engaged in enjoying his auspicious qualities, just as it has been mentioned by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-9 *vaigundattu amararum munivaram* and (5) muktātmās. These five form the next set of five entities

**andarattu aṇanindu ninṟu** – fitting well in paramapadam which is praised as *paramavyōmam* and *paramākāśam* (supreme ethereal layer) in vēdas. Thus, it has been mentioned that he is together with nitya vibhūti.

**āya ninnai** – you, who are together with the two vibhūtis (leelā and nitya vibhūtis)

**yāvar kāṇa vallarē** – who can see you, with their efforts? Could celestial entities see you just because they have superior knowledge to people on earth? Could entities such as brahmā et al, who have superior knowledge to such celestial entities with both karmabhāvana (engaging with regular tasks) and brahmabhāvana (engaging with emperumān) see you? ōr could sanaka, sananta, sanātana and sanatkumāra \[sons of brahmā\] who are totally engaged only with brahma bhāvana, see you?

We will next consider the 4th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 4. Munru muppadarinodu

*mūnṟu muppadāṟinōdu ōr aindum aindum aindumāymūnṟu mūrtiyāgi mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟumāytōnṟu sōdi mūnṟumāyt tuḷakkamil viḷakkamāyēnṟen āviyuḷ pugundadu enkolō em īsanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mūnṟu muppadu – thirty-three compound letters (vowel consonant); āṟinōdu ōr aindum aindum – sixteen vowels; aindumāy – five letters starting with the letter La; mūnṟu mūrtiyāgi – being mentioned by the three vēdams of rig, yajur and sāmam; mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟumāy – being the meaning of dvādaśākśaram, which is composed of four times three, viz. twelve letters; mūnṟu – in praṇavam which has three letters; tōnṟu sōdiyāy – shining resplendently; mūnṟu – in the three vēdams; tōnṟu – shining; sōdiyāy – being spoken of by the resplendent letter of praṇavam; tuḷakkam – perturbation; il – without; viḷakkamāy – being the meaning of akāram (the syllable, a) which shines; em – to us; īsanē – Oh one who is the absolute controller, my lord!; ēnṟu – considering (as your deed); en āviyuḷ – my mind; pugundadu – entering, and manifesting yourself in a radiant manner; en kolō – What sort of simplicity is this!

**Перевод:**

**emperumān manifests himself as the fifty-four alphabets \[in the dēvanāgari script\], composed of thirty-three letters of vowel-consonants, sixteen letters of vowels, and the five special letters starting with La. You are mentioned by the three vēdams of rig, yajur and sāmam, and you are the meaning of dvādaśākśaram, which has twelve letters. You are shining resplendently in praṇavam, which has three letters (Alternatively, you are shining resplendently in the letter of praṇavam which is spoken of by the three vēdams). You are the meaning of the syllable ‘a’ which shines without any perturbation. Oh my lord, who is the absolute controller to us! You entered my mind, considering it as your deed, and manifested yourself in a radiant manner. What sort of simplicity is this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In line with the etymology “vēdayitīti vēda:” vēdams explain all the meanings which are to be known. emperumān had manifested both the vēdams and the mantra rahasyams (esoteric meanings of mantrams or hymns). āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Instead of allowing me to carry out devotion, by knowing about you in line with those vēdams and mantra rahasyams, you had come into my mind on your own. What a great quality is this deed!”*

**vyākhyānam** **(Commentary)**

**mūnṟū muppadāṟinōdu ōr aindum aindum aindumāy** – being the controller of the fifty four letters which are the starting point for vēdams. *mūnṟu muppadu* – thirty-three compound vowel-consonant letters of ka, kha, ga, gha, nga, ca, ca, ja, jha, gna, ta1, ta2, da3, da4, na, ta1,ta2, da3, da4, Na, pa1, pa2, ba3, bha4, ma, ya, ra, la va, śa, śa, sa, ha (also called as 33 *hallu* letters in samaskrutam)

**āṟinōdu ōr aindum aindum** – the sixteen vowel letters of a, A, i, I, u, U, ru, rū, lu, lū, E, ai, O, au, am, aha, which are termed in samskrutam as *accu* letters.

**aindumāy** – five letters which are not included in the above-mentioned classifications, namely La, kśa, jgna, :ka and :pa. Thus, these three classifications, together, sum up to 54 letters.

**mūnṟu mūrtiyāgi** – being manifested in rig vēdam, yajur vēdam and sāma vēdam. Based on their importance, vēdams are classified as *trayī* (three), leaving aside atarvaṇa vēdam. The fifty-four letters which were mentioned at the beginning, would make sense only if they combine to form building blocks and become vēdam. Hence, āḻvār is referring to this. Alternatively, we could consider that these refer to the three classifications of the letters as mentioned earlier, as *hallu, accu* and five letters beginning with *La*. Then, we could construe this to mean the three classifications of letters and the vēdams.

**mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟu mūnṟumāy** – being spoken of by *dvādaśa akśarams* (twelve syllables) as seen in *Om namō bhagavatē vāsudēvāya*, which is the meaning of the vēdams.

**tōnṟu sōdi mūnṟumāy** – the words are to be rearranged as *mūnṟu tōnṟum sōdiyāy* – praṇavam is a combination of the syllables *a, u* and *m*. Thus, emperumān is spoken of by the splendorous praṇavam (ōnkāram). vēdam says *ōnkārō bhagavān viṣṇu*: (bhagavān viṣṇu is revealed by ōnkāram).

**tuḷakkamil viḷakkamāy** – emperumān is the cause for ōmkāram which is the cause for all the vēdams and he does not have a causative factor for himself. taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 10 says “*yat vēdādau svara*: *prōkta*: *vēdāntē ca pratiśtita*: l *tasya prakruti leenasya ya*: *para*: *sa mahēsvara*: ll” (sarvēśvaran (lord of all) is the meaning of akāram (the syllable *‘a’* which is the causative factor for *ōnkāram* which is recited at the beginning and end of vēdam). This is the authentic proof for this surmise. tiruvaḷḷuvar, who composed tirukkṟaḷ, also said *agara mudala eḻuttellām ādibhagavan mudaṟṟē ulagu* (the syllable *a* is the causative factor for all the syllables just as bhagavān is the causative factor for all the worlds). emperumān himself said in bhagavat gītā *akśārāṇām akārō’smi* (among all the syllables I remain as the syllable ‘*a’*). Since he is the causative factor for all the rest and does not have a causative factor for himself, the syllable ‘*a’* is said to be without any perturbation.

**ēnṟu ennāviyuḷ pugundadu enkolō** – Though you had created vēdas, mantra rahasyams etc for me to know about you and to worship you, you entered my mind, taking it as your responsibility, and revealed yourself to me as you are, so that I did not go the way of knowing about you through vēdams. What a great quality is this!

**em īsanē** – the way which emperumān revealed to āḻvār is that emperumān is the svāmy (one who is the lord or owner) and āḻvār is the śēśan (servitor or possession). Alternatively, we can construe this to mean that emperumān considered āḻvār’s benefits and losses as his own (emperumān’s) and revealed that relationship to āḻvār.

We will next take up the 5th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 5. Ninru iyangum

*ninṟu iyangum onṟalā urukkaḷ dōṟum āviyāyonṟiyuḷ kalandu ninṟa ninna tanmai innadenṟuenṟum yārkkum eṇṇiṟanda ādiyāy nin undi vāyanṟu nānmugan payanda ādi dēvan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninṟu – being botanical life; iyangum – being animal life; onṟalā – not of use to anyone or anything; onṟalā – not being constant in any way; urukkaḷ dōṟum – in all the physical forms; āviyāy – being the ātmā; onṟi – fitting well; uḷ kalandu ninṟa – mingling with them in such a way that it would appear that they \[you and the ātmā\] are identical; ninna – your; tanmai – basic nature; innadenṟu – \[it is\] like this; enṟum – in all the three phases of time \[past, present and future\]; yārkkum – for any jñāni (wise person); eṇṇiṟanda – beyond one’s ability to think of; ādiyāy – one who is prominent in both the seen and heard objects; anṟu – during the time of deluge; nin undi vāy – in your divine navel; nānmugan – caturmukhan (brahmā, who has four faces); payanda – one who sired; ādi – only causative factor for the universe; dēvan allaiyē – Are you not the one who acts in a casual manner!

**Перевод:**

**You \[refers to emperumān) are the indvelling soul in all the entities, whether they are stationary \[plant origin\] or moving \[animal origin (here, the term moving includes human entities too)\], even if they are not of any use to anyone (alternatively, even if they keep changing their form).  You fit well with them, mingling with them in such a way that it would appear that they are identical. You are beyond the thinking ability of even the wisest of people, whether they are in the past or present or future, who could say that this is your nature. You remain the prominent entity among both the entities which are seen and the entities which are heard. During the time of deluge, you sired the four-faced brahmā, from your divine navel. Are you not the only causative factor for the universe and aren’t you the one who acts in a casual manner!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the third pāsuram āḻvār had asked as to who was capable of seeing emperumān. emperumān in his divine mind thought “In the previous pāsuram, you had said that vēdams and mantra rahasyams have said clearly about the nature of emperumān. When such is the case, how did you raise the doubt in the third pāsuram?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram, as a response to emperumān’s query “Since you are the indvelling soul for all and since you are the only causative factor for all the worlds, jīvātmās can only know your nature of being the sustaining entity in the gross form; however, since they cannot ascertain that ‘emperumān is of this nature’ just as you had manifested yourself to me on your own, my query as to who can see you is apt”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ninṟu iyangum onṟalā urukkaḷ dōṟum** – among all the four types of physical forms \[celestial, human, botanical and animal\], whether they are immobile, like the plant forms or mobile, like the animal forms and which are not of any use for uplifting human entities. The term *onṟalā* refers to the inability of the form to be of any use for uplifting human entities. Alternatively, it could also be construed to refer to the changing nature of the physical forms.

**āviyāy onṟi uḷ kalandu ninṟa** – being the indvelling soul, who has pervaded all those physical forms, and fitting well inside them, not looking at the lowliness of such forms. ūtilising his *aghatita ghatanā* *sāmartyam* (ability to bind together entities which cannot be bound at all), emperumān enters these forms with his *vibhu* (all-pervading) form \[even if it is a worm, he has the ability to pervade it with his all-pervading form\]. Mingling with them fully, he makes it appear as if there is only one entity and not two.

**ninna tanmai** – your basic nature. In other words, even though he pervades and mingles with them fully, their deficiencies do not affect him in any way. Also, though he is *vibhu* (without any boundary), he enters those forms which are limited, with his full form. In *brahmasūtram* (a treatise compiled by vēdavyāsar), in the chapter *krutsna prasaktyadikaraṇam*, did vyāsar not conclude “Though it is beyond our limited knowledge that empeurmān has pervaded every entity, since *śruti* (vēdam) says so, we have to accept that”?

**innadu enṟu** – to say that “emperumān is like this”. Showing an allegory and saying that emperumān is like this.

**enṟum yārkkum** – in all the three times of past, present and future, even for the most knowledgeable such as brahmā and rudran.

**eṇṇiṟanda** – being beyond the realm of thoughts too. Did not vēdams too proclaim loudly as in rig vēdam viṣṇu sūktam “*na tē viṣṇō jāyamānō na jāta*: *dēva mahima*: *param antamāpa*” (Oh viṣṇu, the lord! No one who was born earlier or who is born now {or who will be born in future) will know the upper limit of your greatness) and as in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 9-1 “*yatō vācō nivarttantē aprāpya manasā saha*” (from which brahmam both the mind and the faculty of speech returned without succeeding in estimating (it))!

**ādiyāy** – Oh one who is the most prominent among all entities, whether seen or heard! You are the object behind any object which is seen and you are the meaning of any word which is uttered. nānmugan tiruvandādi 54 says “*niṟkinṟadellām nedumāl*” (all the objects which are present are (part of) tirumāl (emperumān)); emperumān is the substrate of all cētanācētana entities (entities with and without knowledge) and is the most prominent entity and all cētanācētana entities are his attributes and are the secondary entities. Similarly, when a word is uttered, just as it has been mentioned in jitantē ślōkam 1-7 “*kāryāṇām kāraṇam pūrvam vacasām vācyamuttamam* l *yōgānām paramām siddim paramam tē param vidu*: ll” (People know your most desirable and attainable nature as being the causative factor for all the effectual entities; you are the eminent meanings of words; you are the ultimate refuge beyond all (karma, jñāna and bhakti) yōgams), you are the most prominent by being the meaning of the word uttered, as seen from all the authentic texts.

**nin undi vāy anṟu nānmugaṟpayanda ādidēvan allaiyē** – could anyone know that you are the only causative factor for all the worlds? Just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 4-10-1 “*onṟūm dēvum ulagum uyirum maṟṟum yādum illā anṟu*” (at that time when there was no other world, life, celestial or any other entity) when all the entities starting with dēva (celestial entity etc) in leelā vibhūti had perished, did you not create brahmā in your divine navel and become the only causative factor for the universe?  When one recalls that brahmā, unlike the other cētanas, had not spent time in the womb of a mother, one cannot say that that that divine lotus (emperumān’s navel) is *prākrutam* (belonging to samsāram).  When one recalls that emperumān was the cause for the birth of brahmā who is a *badda jīvan* (an entity bound to samsāram), one cannot conclude that that divine lotus is *aprākrutam* (not belonging to samsāram). Thus, when one recalls the amazing activities of emperumān such as: despite pervading all the entities, their deficiencies do not affect emperumān; despite being a *vibhu*, he pervades all the entities with his fulsome (vibhu) form; creating brahmā who is a prākruta entity from his divine navel which is part of his aprākruta divine form, could those who are under the control of karma, such as brahmā, rudran et al, and who have limited knowledge, know that ‘emperumān is of this nature’?

Next, we will take up the 6th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 6. Nagam endu meru

*nāgam ēndu mēru veṟpai nāgam ēndu maṇṇinaināgam ēndu māgam āga māgam ēndu vārpunalmāgam ēndu mangul tīyōr vāyu aindum amaindu kāttuēgam ēndi ninṟa nīrmai ninkaṇē iyanṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nāgam ēndum āga – Oh one who has the divine form which is supported by tiruvanantāḻvān! (ādiśēśan); nāgam – svargam (heaven); ēndu – support; mēru – known as mēru; veṟpai – the mountain; nāgam – with the eight elephants in each direction; nāgam – through tiruvanantāḻvān; ēndu – supported by; maṇṇinai – earth too; māga – paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam); māgam – in the ethereal layer; vār – flowing; punal – river gangā, all these; māgam – by the ethereal layer; mangul – cloud too; ōr tī – vaiśvānarāgni (a type of fire which helps in digesting food); vāyu aindum – the five prāṇans (vital airs) which help in nourishing; amaindu – fitting well in the form of protecting; kāttu – protecting them; ēgam – only by one entity; ninṟa – being \[this way\]; nīrmai – quality; iyanṟadē – is present

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who has the divine form lying on tiruvanantāḻvān! Heaven is supported by the celestial mountain mēru. Earth is supported by eight elephants (Alternatively, earth is supported by tiruvanantāḻvān in pātāḷa lōkam, the nether world). The river gangā which is flowing in the ethereal layer is supported by paramapadam. Cloud is supported by the ethereal layer. All these supporting entities, as well as the vaiśvānarāgni, along with the five vital airs are protected by only one entity. This quality \[of supporting everything\] is present only with you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that since emperumān is the antaryāmi (in-dwelling soul) for all, he is the ādāram (support; prop) for all. emperumān asks āḻvār “It is seen in the world that one substance supports another; when such is the case, how do you say that I alone am the prop for everything?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “vēdāntams (end portions of vēdas, also called as upaniśats) eminently say that you are the prop for all the materials and that this is firmly established only in you; none can see that you are supporting everything as the base, on one’s own efforts”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nāgam ēndu mēru veṟpai** – the mountain which is known as mēru, which supports heaven. As seen in the vēdavākyam (context in vēdams), ‘*svargō vai lōkō nāka*:’, the word *nāga* refers to heaven. śāstras say that mēru mountain, with the help of the beam of rays on its peak, supports heaven.

**nāgam ēndu maṇṇinai** – The word *nāga*: would refer to the serpent ādiśēśan as well as to elephant. purāṇas say that earth is borne by ādiśēśan, or alternatively, by eight elephants, in each direction. Thus, it could be construed that earth is supported either by the eight elephants or by ādiśēśan, in pātāḷam (nether world).

**nāgam ēdu māgam** – paramapadam which is also known as paramākāśam, which is full of happiness without any trace of sorrow.

**māgam** – svargam which could be considered as parmākāśam, since it is full of happiness without the sorrows seen in this world. Alternatively, since it is larger than the other four elements \[of wind, fire, water and earth\], this could be considered as *mahāgam* (large ethereal layer)

**māgam ēndu vār punal** – the ākāśa gangai (river gangā) which flows in that ethereal layer, supported by it. When *māgam*, which is the tamiḻ form of *mahākam* is occurring thrice here, the first instance refers to paramapadam, the second refers to heaven and the third, to the ethereal layer in earth. Alternatively, the first refers to paramapadam, and the latter two, to ethereal layer. For the first instance of *māgam*, what is the meaning of *nāgam ēndu* qualifying it? Its meaning is that it refers to total happiness without any trace of sorrow. How is this arrived at? The word *kham* in samaskrutam refers to mirth. *akham* would refer to the opposite of happiness, which is sorrow; when a *na* is added to *akham* (to get *nākham* in samaskrutam and *nāgam* in tamiḻ) this refers to happiness without any trace of sorrow. Since the word *ēndu* refers to ‘overflowing’, this phrase *nāgam ēndu māgam* refers to paramapadam which is referred to in upaniśat as *paramavyōman* (taittirīyam), full of happiness. Alternatively \[just as we have seen at the beginning of word-by-word meaning\], “*nāgamēndumāga*” would be an expression to call or address someone, conveying the meaning “Oh one who is borne by tiruvanantāḻvān!” The earlier *māgam* refers to paramapadam and the next *māgam* refers to heaven.

**māgamāgamēndu vār punal** – paramapadam and the flowing river of gangā which is borne by ethereal layer. Just like svargam, earth, ethereal layer etc were mentioned as being borne by one another with the grace of emperumān, paramapadam is borne by emperumān himself directly, without any intermediary entity.

**māgamēndu mangul** – cloud which is borne by ethereal layer.

**tīyōr vāyuvaindamaindu kāttu** – the word *ōr* (unique) is to be added to both the earlier and later terms \[*0r tī* and *ōr vāyu aindum aindum amaindu kāttu*\]. emperumān fits well in the pathos of protection and protects the unique vaiśvānarāgni along with the five vital airs of *prāṇa, apāna, vyāna, udāna* and *samāna*. bhagavat gītā 15-14 says  
“*aham vaiśvānarō bhūtvā prāṇinām dēhamāśrita*: l *prāṇāpāna samāyukta*: *pacāmyannam caturvidam* ll”  
(I am present inside the physical forms of all jīvātmās as vaiśvānara agni (also called as jātarāgni, for digesting food), being together with their vital airs such as prāṇa, apāna etc, and digest the four types of food which they eat). Thus, it is emperumān himself who protects jātarāgni and the five vital airs by being their antaryāmi. The text is also written as *ōr vāyuvaindumaindu kāṟṟu* \[*kāṟṟu* or wind instead of *kāttu* or protection\] in which case the meaning would be – along with the five vital airs \[as seen just now\], there are also five kinds of wind as mentioned in “*nākaśca kūrma*: *krukara*: *dēvadatta*: *dananjaya*:” (winds are classified as nākan, kūrman, krukaran, dēvadattan and dananjayan), thus making ten different types in all. Just as it is seen in the authentic text “*mēghōdayas sāgarasannivruttir vidyut vibhanga*: *spuritāni vāyō*: l *indōr vibhākhō gatamuṣṇaraśamēr viṣṇōr vicitrā*: *prabhavanti māyā* ll” (cloud forms and disappears; wave from the ocean reaches the shore and returns; lightning manifests briefly and disappears; wind breezes through; moon waxes and wanes; sun moves. All these are due to the amazing activities of viṣṇu), all the entities which move about in the ethereal layer, which is not a prop for anything, do so with the *sankalpam* (thought) of emperumān. Has it not been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 9-6 “*yatākāśastitō nityam vāyus sarvatragōmahān* l *tatā sarvāṇibhūtāni matstānītayupadāraya* ll”  
(Know that just as the expansive wind spreads in the ethereal layer (which is not a prop for anything), in the same way all the entities stand firmly in me)!

**ēgamēndi ninṟa nīrmai** – In the following references, vēdam says, with pride, that emperumān alone supports entire world: (1) subāla upaniśat “*dātā vidātā nārāyaṇa*:” (it is only śrīman nārāyaṇan who creates and supports every entity); (2) tattirīya āraṇyakam “*sa dātāra prutivīm dyām utēmām*” (it is only that supreme being who supports all these lower and higher worlds); (3) “*ēka Eva sa idam bibharti viśvam*” (he alone supports all the world); (4) taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli “*viśvam bibharti bhuvanasya nābhi*:” (for the world, which is like a wheel, he alone bears (as the pot) through his divine navel)

**nin kaṇē iyanṟadē** – it is present only with your highness. The word *iyaṟṟal* (noun form of the verb *iyanṟadē*) would mean both conducting and nurturing. Here it is considered as conducting. In other words, this action of supporting every entity is firmly fitting with emperumān. The verse *yāvar kāna vallarē* seen in the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram is to be brought here to get the meaning that just as I had seen your greatness of supporting all these entities, as revealed by you, could anyone else see these?

We will next take up the 7th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 7. Onrirandu murtiyay

*onṟiraṇḍu mūrtiyāy uṟakkamōdu uṇarcciyāyonṟiraṇḍu kālamāgi vēlai jñālamāyināyonṟiraṇḍu tīyumāgi āyanāya māyanēonṟiraṇḍu kaṇṇinānum unnai ētta vallanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

onṟu – being the most important form of viṣṇu; iraṇḍu mūrtiyāy – being the \[indvelling soul for the\] unimportant brahmā and rudra forms; uṟakkamōdu – being the controller for sleep which is without awareness and; uṇarcciyāy – for being awake, which is due to having knowledge; onṟiraṇḍu kālamāgi – being the controller for the three parts of time \[past, present and future\], which is full of satva; vēlai – surrounded by ocean; jñālamāyināy – being the controller for earth; onṟiraṇḍu tīyumāgi – being the controller for three types of fires, namely āhavanīya agni, gārhapatya agni and dakśiṇāgni; āyanāya – one who incarnated in the clan of covherd people; māyanē – Oh one who has amazing activities!; onṟiraṇḍu kaṇṇinānum – even mukkaṇṇan (one with three eyes, rudra) who grants jñānam (knowledge); unnai – you; ētta – to praise; vallanē – does he possess the ability?

**Перевод:**

**You are the most important form of viṣṇu as well as the unimportant forms of brahmā and rudra. You are the controller for both being asleep and being awake, which have the qualities of not having and having knowledge, respectively. You are the controller of the three parts of time, which are full of purely noble, passionate and ignorant qualities. You are the controller of earth, which is surrounded by ocean. You are the controller of the three types of fires. Oh one who incarnated in the clan of cattle grazers and who has amazing activities! Even rudran, who grants knowledge to others, does not possess the ability to praise you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān tells āḻvār “Once I have become accessible to people’s power of knowledge, if they are unable to measure my abilities, is it not because of deficiency in their knowledge? When those with full depth in knowledge know well about me, how apt is your statement ‘who can see?’” āḻvār responds saying “Even rudra, who is eminent due to his knowledge and power, is not capable of comprehending your highness’ simplicity of being born as one among the covherd people and protecting them. He is not capable of praising you”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**onṟiraṇḍu mūrtiyāy** – being the important divine form of viṣṇu and the unimportant forms of brahmā and rudra. emperumān entered the physical forms of brahmā and rudra as the indvelling soul and carried out the functions of creation and destruction respectively as a result of which the forms of brahmā and rudra are said to be unimportant. Since he took a form similar to the forms of brahmā and rudra and granted whatever was prayed for by his followers, without making any distinction between lowly benefits and mōkśam, the form of viṣṇu is considered as important.

**uṟakkamōdu uṇarcciyāy** – conducting the knowledge about these three forms. Just as it has been mentioned in māṇḍūkya kārikai “*anādi māyayā suptō yadā jīva*: *prabudyatē*” (when jīvātmā, who is sleeping due to the timeless illusion of samsāram, awakens), since those who do not have knowledge resemble those who sleep, the act of sleeping is considered as being ignorant, in this context. Ignorance is saying that all the three (brahmā, viṣṇu and rudra) are controlled by the same paramātmā, which is called trimūrti aikyavādam (saying that all three merge into one) or that though they are in three different forms, all three are the same, which is called as trimūrti sāmyavādam. Knowledge is knowing that viṣṇu, who is in the middle, between brahmā and rudra, is the lord of all, protecting brahmā and rudra and that the other two are protected by viṣṇu. It is emperumān alone who controls both knowledge and ignorance.

**onṟiraṇḍu kālamāgi** – he is the controller of the time when satva (noble) quality, which is the reason for knowledge and when rajas (passion) and tamas (ignorance) which are the reasons for ignorance are in the forefront. Just as it has been said in the saying “*trētāyām jñānam ucyatē*” (it is said that during the time of trētā yugam, true knowledge is present), it is emperumān who creates true knowledge about himself, during trētā yugam. Just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-1-50 “*kalau jagatpatim viṣṇum sarvasraśtāramīśvaram* l *nārcayiśyanti maitrēya*! *pāśaṇḍōpahatā janā*: ll” (Oh maitrēya! During the time of kali yugam, people who have been spoiled by disbelievers, will not worship viṣṇu, who created, controlled and who is the svāmy (lord) of all entities), it is emperumān who creates ignorance about him during the time of kaliyugam.

**vēlai jñālamāyināy** – it is emperumān alone who conducts the earth, which is surrounded by ocean. The opinion here is that emperumān is the controller of bhārata varśam (the country of bhāratam), which is also called as karma bhūmi (the place where one carries out all the ordained deeds), where those who have been showered with knowledge, as mentioned earlier, live. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-3-7 says *“ayam tu navamas tēśām dVipa*: *sāgarasamvruta*:” (this ninth dvīpam (surrounded by oceans) of bhārata varśam is also surrounded by ocean). In jambūdvīpam which has nine divisions, bharata varśam which is apt for carrying out one’s ordained deeds, is also surrounded by ocean.

**onṟiraṇḍu tīyumāgi** – emperumān is the controller of the primary *agni* (ritualistic fire) of āvahanīyam and the secondary fires of gārhapatyam and dakśiṇāgni, which are necessary for carrying out *yajñam* (sacrificial rituals) and other deeds which are ordained to be carried out in the karma bhūmi, as mentioned earlier. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-3-25 says “*karmāṇya sankalpita tatphalāni sanyasya viṣṇau paramātmarūpē* l *avāpya tām karma mahīmanantē tasmin layam yē tvamalā*: *prayānti* ll” (A few pious souls reach this earth and carry out *yajñam* and other deeds without praying for any benefit. They proffer these to viṣṇu, who is the paramātma svarūpi (one who is in the form of the greatest soul) and take refuge under him, who is endless). The opinion here is that it is emperumān who conducts *yajñam* and other deeds which are to be carried out by those who desire mōkśam and is the controller of the three ritualistic fires which are the ancillaries for these *yajñams*.

**āyanāya māyanē** – Oh kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa) who incarnated as a covherd with the divine and auspicious form which is beyond our five senses, and who is the epitome of amazing activities! Through this it is reiterated that it is emperumān alone who is vorśipped through the earlier mentioned *yajñams* and who incarnated as a covherd. He has affirmed this in bhagavat gītā 9-24 ślōkam “*aham hi sarvayajñānām bhōktā ca prabhurēvaca* l *na tu māmabhijānanti tattvē nātaś cyavanti tē* ll” (I am the enjoyer of all the yajñams; I shower all the benefits of such yajñams; those who are firmly engaged with other deities, do not know me truly; hence, they slip away from me). Did not authentic texts convey this as in dānadarmam – vēdyudāharaṇam “*sarvē vēdās sarva vēdayā*: *saśāstrā*: *sarvē yajñās sarva ijyāśca  kruṣṇa*: l *vidu*: *kruṣṇam brāhmaṇās tattvatō yē tēśām rājan sarvayajñā*: *samāptā*: ll” (Oh king! It is only kriṣṇa who is all the vēdas, śāstras, all the entities (which are known through those śāstras), all the yajñas, and all the celestial entities who are vorśipped through those yajñas For those brāhmaṇas who truly know kriṣṇa, all their yajñas come to completion) and in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 4-8 “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām vināśāya ca duśkrutām* l *dharma samstāpanārtāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē* ll” (I incarnate in every yugam in order to protect the good people, to annihilate the bad ones and to establish righteous path)?

**onṟiraṇḍu kaṇṇinānum** – rudra who has an important eye on his forehead, with which he has the power to burn his enemies, and two normal eyes like everyone else, which are used only for seeing others. mātsya purāṇam 67-41 says  
“*ārōjñam bhāskarādiccēt danamiccēt hutāsanāt* l *īśvarāt jñānam anviccēt mōkśamiccējjanārdanāt* ll” (Let him (a cētana) desire health from sūryan (sun); wealth from agni (deity for fire); knowledge from Sivan; mōkśam from śriman nārāyaṇa). Just because he has excessive knowledge and excessive power since he has the third eye on his forehead, is rudra capable of truly knowing you, who are conducting both the vibhūtis (leelā vibhūti, or samsāram, and nitya vibhūti, or śrī vaikuṇṭam) and worshiping you?

**unnai ētta vallanē** – through śaivāgamam (rules and regulations mentioned for vorśipping rudran) he is capable of vorśipping himself. Is he capable of vorśipping you? We could also consider this as saying, just as it has been said in nānmugan tiruvandādi 7 “*Ala niḻaṟkīḻ*” (under the shadow of a banyan tree), that he is capable of instructing others that emperumān alone is the supreme entity, whenever he has excess of *satva* (noble) qualities in him. But, is he capable of knowing you truly and vorśipping you?

We will next take up the 8th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 8. Adiyana vanavarkku

*ādiyāna vānavarkkum aṇḍamāya appuṟattuādiyāna vānavarkkum ādiyāna ādi nīādiyāna vānavāṇar anda kālam nī uraittiādiyāna kāla ninnai yāvar kāṇa vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ādiyāna – those who are involved in creating the world etc; vānavarkkum – for deities such as brahmā et al; aṇḍamāya – leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) which is the meaning for the word aṇḍam (oval shaped universe); appuṟattu – those who have mercifully taken residence in paramapadam which is beyond \[leelā vibhūti\]; ādiyāna – those who are responsible for the creation of worlds; vānavarkkum – for nityasūris; ādiyāna – one who is the controller; ādi – the prime entity; nī – you \[are the one\]; ādiyāna – being the creator of worlds; vāṇar – brahmā et al, who are the head; anda kāḷam – \[their\] end time; nī uraitti – you mercifully determined; ādiyāna kālam – controller of the period of deluge; ninnai – you; yāvar kāṇa vallarē – who is capable of seeing?

**Перевод:**

**You are the controller and prime entity for deities such as brahmā et al who are involved in the creation of worlds in leelā vibhūti as well as for nityasūris, who are responsible for creation of the worlds and who have mercifully taken residence in paramapadam, which is beyond the leelā vibhūti. You determine the end time for brahmā et al who are the heads and creators of upper worlds. You are controller of the period of deluge. Who is capable of seeing you?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that Sivan, who has three eyes, is not capable of knowing you and vorśipping you. In this pāsuram he says “Is it only Sivan who is not capable of knowing or vorśipping you? All the entities in both leelā vibhūti (samsāram) and nitya vibhūti (śrī vaikuṇṭam) are under your control for their existence, sustenance as well as their activities. Time, which conducts prākruta lōkam (world which was created from primordial matter) both during its causal and effectual states \[viz. when the world is fully destroyed by deluge and when the world is fully recreated\] also is under your control. No one, either in samsāram or in paramapadam is capable of knowing about such an entity as you”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ādiyāna vānavarkkum** – for celestial entities who are involved in creation, protection and destruction of worlds. brahmā, the ten prajāpatis (procreators), seven riśis (sages) and the twelve ādityas (suns) are engaged in the creation of worlds; indra (head of celestial entities) and fourteen manus are engaged in the protection of the created worlds; rudra, agni and yaman are engaged in destruction of the worlds. These have been described well in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-22. In order to denote their activities of creation, protection and destruction, these celestial entities are called as *ādi* (starting with).

**aṇḍamāna appuṟattu ādiyāna vānavarkkum** – for nityasūris who are residents of paramapadam which is located beyond the prākruta lōkam (world created from primordial entity) and who are the causative factor for creation of worlds. It is pertinent to note here that in the śrī viṣṇu purāṇam (1-22) cited earlier, in the latter half of astrabhūśaṇādyāyam, it has been said that śrī kaustubam \[a pendant worn by emperumān\] is representative of jīvātmās and that śrīvatsam \[a divine mole on the chest of emperumān\] is representative of prakruti and starting with these, causative feature was mentioned, in a way, for nityasūris. Alternatively, just as it has been mentioned in the ślōkam in puruśa sūktam “*tēha nākam mahimānas sacantē yatra pūrvē sādyās santi dēvā*:” (in which paramapadam the sādyās, who have been present since time immemorial, are residing, they make that paramapadam great) and in yajur-accidram “*yatrarśaya*: *pratamajā yē purāṇā*:” (in which paramapadam nityasūris, who have been present since time immemorial, who are old and new and who are capable of seeing everything, reside), we can consider that nityasūris, who have been mentioned as existing since time immemorial, are termed as *ādiyāna vānavar*.

**ādiyāna ādi nī** – you are the prime and causative entity responsible for the existence etc of entities who are important and who are residing in both the vibhūtis.

The second half of the pāsuram states that the entity of time, which conducts the leelā vibhūti, is under your control.

**ādiyāna vānavāṇar anda kāḷam nī uraitti** – you are the one who determines the end time for the positions of celestial entities such as brahmā et al who are engaged in the activities of creation etc of the worlds and who are the heads of upper worlds such as *satyalōkam* (brahmā’s world) etc. bhagavat gītā 8-17 says “*sahasra yuga paryantamahar yad brahmaṇō vidu*: *l* *rātrim yugasahasrāntām tē’hōrātra vidō janā*: ll” (the wise people, who know about day and night, know that brahmā’s daytime has a spread of 1000 catur yugams \[one catur yugam is 4.32 million earth years\] and that brahmā’s night time has a spread of 1000 catur yugams). Since emperumān has said this in bhagavat gītā, āḻvār says that emperumān has determined their end-time.

**ādiyāna kālam ninnai** – you are like the embers of a charcoal. In other words, when a charcoal is in the effectual state (burning fully), it is like *jvālāgni* (effulgent fire) and when it is in the causal state (not burning) it is like coal whose fire has been put out. In a similar way, emperumān has controlled both cētana and acētana entities within himself. cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 says “*sadēva sōmya idamagra āsīt ēkamēva advitīyam*” (Oh child! brahmam, which is also called as sat \[that which exists\] (which is now seen with names and forms for all entities) was only one (since it was without names and forms for entities) during the time of deluge (and since it was without an identical entity) it was without a second entity). Just as it has been seen in this, you are the one who conducts the period of deluge which is the starting time of everything.

**yāvar kāṇa vallar** – who can estimate you and know about you when the existence of every entity in both the vibhūtis is under your control?

Next, we will take up the 9th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 9. Tadulavu konrai

*tādulāvu konṟai mālai tunnu senjadaiccivannīdiyāl vaṇangu pāda ninmalā nilāya sīrvēdavāṇar gīdavēḷvi nīdiyāna kēḷviyārnīdiyāl vaṇanguginṟa nīrmai ninkaṇ ninṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tādu – stamens (pollens of flowers); ulāvu – being in abundance; konṟai mālai – konṟai flower (a kind of Indian laburnum; Indian trumpet flower); tunnu – dense; sem – reddish; sadai – matted hair (one who has all these); sivan – rudran; nīdiyāl – due to having been ordained in vēdams (sacred texts); vaṇangu – fit to be vorśipped; pāda – Oh one who has divine feet!; ninmalā – Oh one who is without any fault!; nilāya – well grown; sīr – auspicious qualities such as humility; vēdavāṇar – those who are best in following vēdams and who are controllers of vēdams; gīda – having sāmagānam (sung to a tune); vēḷvi – rituals; nīdiyāna – not slipping from the procedure laid out in vēdams; kēḷviyār – those who hear properly \[from their teachers\]; vaṇanguginṟa – vorśipping; nīrmai – quality; ninkaṇ ninṟadē – is present only with you

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is without any fault! Oh one who has divine feet which are fit to be vorśipped! rudran, who is wearing a garland of konṟai flowers which have stamens in abundance, and who has dense, reddish matted hair, as well as those who are best in following vēdams and who are controllers of vēdams, singing sāmagānam during rituals without slipping from the procedure laid out in vēdams, who have heard the vēdams properly, worship you. The quality of being vorśipped like this is present only with you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Sivan, who is the leader of those who desire other benefits \[instead of emperumān himself\] since he has inestimable greatness as seen in the previous pāsurams, and sātvika jñānis (wise people who have noble qualities) who desire only you, take refuge only under you. Hence, you are the entity to be attained by everyone”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tādulāvu konṟai mālai tunnu senjadaic civan** – rudran who has konṟai garland, the flowers of which are rich in stamens and who has reddish matted hair. Since he is wearing konṟai garland, it is clear that Sivan is very much interested in enjoying the pleasures in this world. Since his matted hair is dense, it is clear that he has been carrying out penance for a long time and since it is reddish in colour, it is clear that he has been taking bath frequently. These make it clear that he has been carrying out penance in order to attain the enjoyments which he desired. Thus, to him, enjoying the pleasures of this world is the desired goal and that he had attained that only by carrying out penance towards emperumān

**nīdiyāl  vaṇangu pāda** – vēdam says that those who desire wealth as the end benefit \[goal\] should worship your highness \[addressing emperumān\] as one who is with opulence. Thus, one who has divine feet which are vorśipped by that Sivan!

**ninmalā** – Sivan desires other benefits in this world and when tamō guṇam (quality of ignorance) stands out in him, he believes that he is īśvaran (one who controls everyone and everything). Due to this ego, he clashes with emperumān too, on occasions. Without considering these faults in Sivan, looking only at this quality of Sivan that he has approached only him in order to get what he desires, emperumān grants him that just as he grants the wishes of those who approach him without having desires in worldly pursuits. emperumān is such a faultless entity. What is the wealth granted by emperumān to Sivan? mahābhāratam rājadarmam “*mahādēvas sarvamēdē mahātmā hūtvātmānam dēvadēvō babhūva* l *sarvān lōkān vyāpya viśtabhya kīrtyā virājatē dyutimān kruttivāsā*: ll” (Sivan, the great soul, offered himself in the sarvamēda yāgam (a type of sacrificial ritual) and became *dēva dēva* (head of celestial entities). Granting such desires to Sivan who attained him with such wishes is representative of granting such desires to others too such as brahmā et al who approach him, for other benefits. Next, āḻvār speaks about *sātvikajñānis* (wise people who are with noble quality) who take refuge under emperumān, desiring only him.

**nilāya sīr** – those who have auspicious qualities which keep growing. In other words, those who have ātma guṇas (qualities of the ātmā) such as humility, sincerity etc as espoused in the thirteenth chapter of bhagavat gītā as being important for practicing jñāna yōgam (path of knowledge) and qualities such as śamam (controlling one’s mind) and damam (controlling one’s senses) which are required for practicing devotion. When this word is meant as an adjective for *vēdavāṇar* (those who are experts in vēdams), as in *sīr vēdavāṇar* this is the meaning to be considered. If, on the other hand, this word is taken as an adjective for vēdam itself as in *nilāya sīr vēdam*, then the meaning to be considered will be – qualities of vēdam such as its being eternal, its *apauruśēyam* (not written by anyone), its being without any deficiency, the greatness of vēdam due to these qualities; as a result of this, it could be said that it will refer to auspicious qualities of vēdam without any shortcoming.

**vēdavāṇar** – those who have seen the inner meanings of vēdams such that one could say that they conduct the vēdams.

**gīdavēḷvi** – those who are involved in yāgams (rituals) which follow the meanings of such vēdams, and which have sāmagānam (singing sāma vēhdam) as the predominant feature. In other words, not conducting that yāgam towards any specific benefit, along with the true meaning of ātmā such as ‘the benefit of this yāgam is not for me’, ‘This is not my karma’ and ‘I am not the one who is carrying out this karma’ (the three sacrifices which are to be made while observing a yāgam). It should be done with the knowledge that this is done as a worship of emperumān. Those who carry out yāgams in this manner.

**nīdiyāna kēḷviyār** – those who have the knowledge through hearing, from their teachers, of the inner meanings of vēdam and through that have the knowledge for carrying out karma yōgam, jñāna yōgam and bhakti yōgam (the paths of deeds, knowledge and devotion, respectively). Who are such persons? muṇḍakōpaniśad 1-2-12 says “*parīkśya lōkān karmacitān brāhmaṇō nirvēdamāyāt*” (after analysing the benefits attained through karma repeatedly, brāhmaṇas will become dejected), Becoming very sorrowful, they will attain an ācārya (teacher) who is a brahmajñāni (one who has knowledge of the supreme being, brahmam), and as said in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśad 6-5-6 “*ātmā vā arē draśtavya*: *śrōtavya: mantavya*: *nididyāsitavya*:” (ātmā (here the word ātmā refers to paramātmā, the supreme being) is apt to be heard, to be thought of and to be meditated upon; it is apt to be seen), they are those who keep hearing and thinking about emperumān.

**nīdiyāl vaṇanguginṟa nīrmai** – it is the quality of being the target of being meditated upon continuously, as mentioned in the bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśad ślōkam seen above.

**nin kaṇ ninṟadē** – this is to be rearranged as *nin kaṇē ninṟadu* – it is only with you. Through the long syllable used (*kaṇē*) it is emphasized that there is none else to be attained by those who desire mōkśam \[liberation from samsāram and reaching paramapadam\].

We will take up next, the 10th pāsuram of this prabandam.
