## 10. Tannule tiraittu

*tannuḷē tiraitteḻundu taranga veṇ tadam kadaltannuḷē tiraitteḻundu adanguginṟa tanmai pōlninnuḷē piṟandiṟandu niṟpavum tiribavumninnuḷē adanguginṟa nīrmai ninkaṇ ninṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

eḻum – rising; taranga – having waves; veṇ – whitish; tadam – expansive; tannuḷē – within itself; tiraittu – agitating; eḻundu – throwing out waves; adanguginṟa – subsiding; tanmai pōl – like its nature; piṟandu – being born; niṟpavaum – stationary entities such as plant life etc; tiribavum – mobile entities (the worlds which have all these); ninnuḷē – within you; adanguginṟa – subsiding; nīrmai – quality; ninkaṇē – only with you; ninṟadu – is present

**Перевод:**

**Waves which rise up from the expansive, whitish ocean, due to agitation, fall down and subside within that ocean. In the same way, all the immobile and mobile creations which appear and die within you, subside within you. This quality is present only with you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that it is emperumān who is apt to be attained by everyone and as a result of this, it is only he who is to be vorśipped in order to reach mōkśam (liberation from samsāram). Apart from this, as said in the pramāṇam (authentic text) atarvasikai “kāraṇantu dyēya:” (only that entity, which is the causal factor, is to be meditated upon), whichever entity is the material causative factor for this world is apt to be attained. To prove that only that entity who is the material causal factor for the world is to be attained, āḻvār cites an analogy, in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tannuḷē tiraitteḻum taranga veṇ tadam kadal ……..** – In the first part of the pāsuram, āḻvār cites the analogy of the ocean which has the quality of being the causative factor \[for waves\]. In the second part of the pāsuram, he cites the occurrence of the same quality in emperumān. He refers to ocean by the term *tannuḷē* and refers to emperumān by the term *ninnuḷē*. Just as the activities of the waves rising up in the ocean and subsiding back in the ocean are under the control of the ocean, the activities of all the *cētanācētanas* (entities with knowledge and without knowledge), which are part of emperumān’s physical form, appearing and disappearing within emperumān, are under his control.

**tiraitteḻum tarangam** – ocean, which is without waves initially, starts getting agitated once wind starts blowing over it. The waves rise up and spread everywhere. In a similar manner, due to the sankalpam \[vow or thought\] of emperumān, during the time of creation, there is imbalance among the three qualities of *satva* (noble), *rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance) in *prakruti* (primordial matter) which is a small part of emperumān’s divine form.

**vēn tadam kadal** – expansive ocean has the waves which are moving and white colour (in the waves) which is stationary. This is an allegory for emperumān’s physical form where *tiribavum* refers to the mobile creations \[entities such as celestial, human and animals/birds\] while *niṟpavum* refers to the stationary creations \[such as plants, trees, mountains etc\].

**tannuḷē tiraitteḻundu adanguginṟa tanmai pōl** – similar to the quality of waves, which, being immobile when there is no wind, start agitating with each other and rise up in the ocean when wind passes over the ocean, fall back and subside within the ocean, when wind stops passing over it. Waves agitating with one another and rising in the ocean when wind blows is an example of the phenomenon of creation starting to happen due to the sankalpam of emperumān. Similarly, the subsiding of waves back in the ocean once wind stops is an example of creation coming to an end once emperumān’s sankalpam changes and the process of destruction sets in. This does not mean that it is referring to *sānkya matam* (the philosophy of there being only two entities, *prakruti*, the primordial matter and *puruśa*, the ātmā). According to *sānkya matam*, prakruti undergoes changes and these changes get reflected in ātmā. Since changes take place only in the ocean, it could be construed that this is referring to *sānkya matam*, but this is negated. In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that emperumān is the one to be vorśipped. This pāsuram says that since emperumān is the material causative factor, he is to be vorśipped. In other words, brahmam (emperumān), which is together with cētanācētana entities in their subtle forms (without names and physical forms), is the material causative factor for the worlds. brahmam, which is together with cētanācētana entities in their gross forms (with differences in their names and physical forms) is the effectual form. This has been said in a distinguished manner in vēdams. brahmasūtrakārar (vēdavyāsar who wrote a treatise called as brahmasūtram which explains, among other things, that emperumān is the causal factor for the worlds), explained this in brahma sūtram 2-1-35. “*na karmāvibhāgāditi cēd na, anāditvād upapadyatē cāpupalabhyatē ca*” (If it is said that since there is nothing other than brahmam, before creation jīvātmās and their karmas are non-existent, then it is incorrect. (Since jīvātmās and their karmas are) timeless, even if they did not have a beginning, (since they are together with brahmam and cannot be separated from brahmam), the vēdas’ context of inseparability is applicable. Their being without a beginning is seen in both *śruti* (vēdams) and *smritis* (texts which came about to explain the vēdams)). When analysing cāndōjña upaniśad, “*sadēva agra āsīt”* (only *sat* (brahmam) was present at the beginning), it will be seen that only brahmam, referred to as *sat* here, was present during the time of deluge. This could be construed to say that jīvātmās were not there and hence, karmas were also not there. However, śvētāsvatara upaniśad 1-9 says “*jñājñau dvāvajāvīśanīśau*” ((paramātmā and jīvātmā) were existing as omniscient and without knowledge, respectively. They existed as *īśvaran* (one who controls) and *anīśvaran* (one who is without any control)).  Also, śvētāsvatara upaniśad 6-13 says “*nityō nityānām cētanas, ēkō bahūnām yō vidādī kāmān*” (For countless jīvātmās who are eternal, that emperumān who is eternal and who grants all their wishes). From these pramāṇams (authentic texts), it is clear that jīvātmās and their flow of karmas are eternal and hence the earlier mentioned hypothesis that they were not present during deluge is incorrect.  Thus, the emphasis in *sadēva* (*only* sat (brahmam or emperumān) existed earlier) is acceptable since cētana and acētana entities existed \[together with brahmam\] without name and form in their subtle form.

It is not āḻvār alone who had cited a single material (ocean) as an example to prove that brahmam, existing together with cētana and acētana entities, is the materialistic causative factor for creation. In cāndōjña upanishad 6-1-4, it is said *“yatā sōmyaikēna mrutpiṇḍēna sarvam mruṇmayam vijñātam syāt*” (Oh child! If one knows mud, materials such as pot, lid, plate etc (made from that mud) will all be known. \[In the same way, if brahmam is known, everything (made by and through it) will be known). Thus, upaniśat too cited a single material (mud, in this case) as an example to prove this concept. parāśara maharishi too, in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 3-7-16 cited gold (as a single material) as an example for the same purpose. “*kataka makuta karṇikādi bhēdai*: *kanakam abhēdam apīśyatē yataikam* l  
*sura paśu manujādi kalpanābhir harir akhilābhir udīryatē tataika*: ll” ((In the effectual state) just as gold is considered as a single material even though armlet, crown, earrings etc made from it are considered as different materials, in the same way (in the effectual state), though celestial entities, humans, *tiryak* (animals and birds) are different, hari (emperumān) is considered as one, who has all these entities together with him).

Now, āḻvār shows the similarities between the object of comparison \[ocean\] and the subject of comparison \[emperumān\].

**ninnuḷē piṟandiṟandu niṟpavum tiribavum** – all the stationary and moving entities which are located within your basic form, which are born from a subtle form and which die in the gross form.

**ninnuḷē adanguginṟa nīrmai** – during the start of deluge, when all the entities which originated from *prakruti* go back to it. Just as it has been said in subālōpaniśad 2 “*tama*: *parē dēva ēkībhavati*” (primordial matter, called as *tamas*, subsides in paramātmā), the greatness of subsiding within you, the paramātmā. Just as waves which originate from ocean subside in the ocean, all the worlds \[the term world refers to all the entities which take birth in the world\] which generate from you during the time of creation, subside within you during the time of deluge.

**ninkaṇ ninṟadē** – should be rearranged to read as *ninkaṇē ninṟadu.* This is present only in you. āḻvār emphasises through the long syllable (*ninkaṇē ninṟadu*) that this feature of materialistic causative factor is not present in anyone else. āḻvār proves, through an example in the tenth pāsuram, what he had said in the first pāsuram *pūnilāya* that emperumān has the quality of being the materialistic causative factor for the worlds.

Next, we will take up the 11th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 11. Sollinal todarcci ni

*sollināl todarcci nī solappadum poruḷum nīsollināl solappadādu tōnṟuginṟa sōdi nīsollināl padaikka nī padaikka vandu tōnṟinārsollināl surunga nin guṇangaḷ solla vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sollināl – through vēdam; todarcci – developing an involvement (in the enduring benefit); nī – you; solappadum – being spoken of by śruti (vēdam) and smruti (texts which came to explain these vēdams) as being appropriate to be attained; poruḷum – as ātmā (indvelling soul) for celestial deities; nī – you; sollināl – by vēdam; solappadādu – inestimable; tōnṟuginṟa – that which appears to be; sōdi – supremely effulgent entity; nī – you; sollināl – through the vēdam given by you; padaikka – in order to create the world; nī – you; padaikka – just as you created; vandu tōnṟinār – (in your divine navel) brahmā et al who appeared; nin – your; guṇangaḷ – auspicious qualities; solla vallarē – are they capable of speaking about?

**Перевод:**

**Through vēdam, you are the entity in whom an involvement is developed, as the lasting benefit. You are the entity who is spoken of by śrutis and smritis as being appropriate to be attained, being the indvelling soul for celestial entities. You are the one who is spoken of by vēdams as being an inestimable, supremely effulgent entity. Entities such as brahmā et al appeared from your divine navel just as you started creation, as spoken of by vēdams. These entities are not capable of speaking even a little bit about your auspicious qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) A query was raised regarding existence of causative factor for other deities such as rudra, brahmā et al. śvētāśvatara upaniśat 4 says “yadā tamas tanna divā na rātrir na sanna cāsacciva Eva kēvala” (During that time when stūla cidacit which are existing and non-existing were not present, when distinction between day and night was not there and when only primordial matter, called as tamas and Sivan were existing) and taittirīya samhitai 4-2-8 says “hiraṇya garbhas samavartatāgrē” (only brahmā was present at the beginning). These were cited as proof for this query. āḻvār says in this pāsuram that these deities were created by you; they do not have the capability to praise even a part of your greatness. When such is the case, where is the possibility for these deities to be attained \[by people\] as a refuge since they are the causative factors? The texts taken here as proof, as well as well as other texts where causative factor had been assigned to other deities such as indra et al, are to be considered as speaking directly about you only or speaking about you as the indvelling soul for them or to praise about those deities. Thus, there is no deficiency to your being the causative factor.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sollināl todarcci nī** – For those who desire the four ultimate benefits of beneficence, opulence, happiness and liberation, as espoused by vēdams, you are the one who creates a relationship. The word *todar* refers to relationship. Apart from samsāris (those who dwell in this materialistic realm) who think that it is only the pleasures enjoyed through the five senses of sound, touch, form, taste and smell which are the desired goal, even for those wise people who seek the causative factor as espoused by vēdams and who attempt to attain it, you are the one who creates an interest in them. Since brahmā et al are not the natural procreators of jīvātmās, they are not capable of developing this interest in them.

**solappadum poruḷum nī** – you are the indvelling soul of various deities who are spoken of by śruti (vēdams) and smurutis, itihāsams and purāṇams (which came about to explain the śrutis) and who are desired to be attained by people for lowly benefits. You grant them their lowly desires through these deities. Examples of such texts are: manu smruti 12-123 “*ēnamēkē vadantyagnim manuma(marutō’)nyē prajāpatim* l *indramēkē parē prāṇam aparē brahma śāśvatam* ll” (Some of the branches in vēdams say that agni (deity for fire) is this supreme entity; some others say that it is manu (maruttu); some others says that it is prajāpati (procreators); some others say that it is indra (head of celestial enbtities); some others say that it is prāṇan (vital air); others say that it is the eternal brahmam). taittirīya āraṇyakam – 3 “*catur hōtārō yatra sampadam gaccanti dēvai*:” (Those who, through celestial entities, by carrying out yāgams (sacrificial fires), attain the desired benefits from paramātmā (supreme entity) who is the indvelling soul for such celestial entities). dakśa smruti “*yē yajanti pitrūn dēvān brāhmaṇān sahutāśanān* l *sarvabhūtāntarātmānam viṣṇumēva yajanti tē* ll” (Those who worship forefathers, celestial entities, brāhmaṇas who nurture agni, worship viṣṇu who is the indvelling soul for all jīvātmās)

**sollināl solappadādu tōnṟuginṟa sōdi nī** – you are the one who is spoken of by vēdams as being inestimable splendour. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 9-1 says “*yatō vācō nivartantē aprāpya manasā saha* l  
*ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān* l *na bibhēti kutaścanēti* l” (Word and mind which set out to estimate brahmam, returned without success. One who knows the extent of happiness of that brahmam, will not have fear towards anyone). kēna upaniśat 2-3 “*yasyāmatam tasya matam matam yasya na vēda sa*: l *avijñātam vijānatām vijñātam avijānatām* ll” (To one for whom brahmam is inestimable, it becomes known. To one for whom it is estimable, he has not known it. brahmam is known to the unknown and unknown to the known). taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*nārāyaṇa param brahmatatvam nārāyaṇa*: *para*: l *nārāyana parōjyōtirātmā nārāyaṇa*: *para*: ll” (nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme brahmam; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme entity; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme effulgence; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme soul).

From the above-mentioned pramāṇams (authentic texts) it has been clearly stated that your basic nature, your divine form, your divine auspicious qualities and your divine wealth have been mentioned as inestimable.

**sollināl padaikka** – Just as seen in śvētāśvatara upaniśat 6-18 “*yō brahmāṇam vidadāti pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai* l *tam ha dēvam ātmabuddi prasādam mumukśurvai śaraṇam aham prapadyē* ll” (I, desiring to attain mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), surrender to that supreme entity, who initially created brahmā and who instructed him all the vēdams), in order to create the word through the vēdam instructed by you, and just as it has been said in “*sa bhūriti vyāharat*” (that brahmā said bhū:), it has been said that brahmā carried out the task of creation by reciting *vēdaśabdams* (verses from vēdams).

**nī padaikka vandu tōnṟinār** – Just as you created, brahmā and other celestial entities appeared from your divine navel, as we had seen in the śvētāśvatara upaniśat ślōkam mentioned above.

**sollināl surunga nin guṇangaḷ solla vallarē** – It is not only that they cannot speak expansively about your auspicious qualities; they are not capable of even speaking briefly about one of your qualities. They cannot estimate the boundary of even one among your various qualities which show your superior nature, which establish your being the causative factor for the worlds and which shower mercy on your devotees etc.

We will next take up the 11th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 12. Ulagu tannai

*ulagutannai nī padaitti uḷḷodukki vaitti mīṇḍuulagutannulē piṟatti ōridattai allaiyālulagu ninnodu onṟi niṟka vēṟu niṟṟi ādalālulagu ninnai uḷḷa sūḻal yāvar uḷḷa vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ulagu tannai – world; nī – you; padaitti – created; uḷ – inside the stomach; odukki vaitti – you hid it; mīṇḍu – once again; ulagu tannuḷē – in the world; piṟatti – you incarnated; ōridaittai allaiyāl – you are not having a specific place \[as yours\]; ulagu – the universe; ninnodu – with you, the śariri (indvelling ātmā); onṟi – hiding; vēṟu niṟṟi – (with a distinguished divine form) you are remaining as a separate entity; ādalāl – thus, since it is impossible to estimate; sūḻal uḷḷa – having amazing qualities; ninnai – you; yāvar – who; uḷḷa vallarē – is capable of thinking?

**Перевод:**

**You had created the world \[during the time of creation\] and hid it within your form \[at the time of deluge\]. Subsequently \[after creating the world again\], you incarnated in the world. You do not have a specific place as yours. Even as the universe is hiding within you, with you being the indvelling soul, you are remaining as a separate entity. Thus, who in the world is capable of thinking about you, when you have such amazing qualities?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār asks of emperumān “Being the causative factor for the world, you create, protect and destroy, protecting the world. Apart from this, you incarnate in this world with your divine form and protect this world. Who is capable of thinking about these qualities?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ulagu tannai nī padaitti** – with the primordial matter, which is an acētana entity (that which does not have knowledge), as the implement, you created the world, initially on your own until the universe is created. Then, through brahmā, who was created by you and in whom you dwell as the soul, you created rest of the celestial deities and others.

**uḷḷodukki vaitti** – during the final deluge when all the worlds get destroyed as well as during *prākruta* deluge which takes place when brahmā’s day time is over, you keep all the worlds inside your stomach and protect them. Since you create all the cētanācētanas (entities with and without knowledge) during creation and protect them by keeping them within your stomach, during deluge, upaniśats proclaim that you are the materialistic causative factor for the worlds, just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-3 “*tadaikśata bahusyām prājāyēyēti* l *tattējō’srujata*” (that para brahmam (supreme entity) took a *sankalpam* (vow) – let me become manifold; let me be born; it created effulgence)

**mīṇḍu ulagu tannuḷ piṟatti** – hiding your eminent quality of being the single causative factor for the world, you incarnate in this world by becoming the son of one of your creations in this samsāram (materialistic realm). *mīṇḍu* – reverting from the quality of eminence. The implied meaning is that emperumān hides his quality of eminence and takes birth in the world created by him to show his quality of simplicity.  How strange is it that you choose one of the samsāri jīvātmās as your father and take a birth here! When you protect all the worlds during the time of deluge by keeping them in your stomach, you choose the stomach of one of the jīvātmās protected by you and incarnate as her son; how amazing is this! Has it not been mercifully mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-2-21 “*prasīda dēvi sarvasya jagata*: *Sam śubhē kuru* l *prītyā tvam dārayēśānam drutam yēnākhilam jagat* \|\|” (\[celestial entities pray to dēvaki pirātti\] Oh the auspicious one! You should show mercy on us. You should shower all auspicious qualities on the world. You should bear with joy that emperumān, through whom all the worlds are protected, in your stomach)!

**ōridattai allaiyāl** – Since it is impossible to determine that you are like this, it is not possible to know to which type you belong. In other worlds, since there is no shortcoming in your being the causative factor, you could not be classified as being an effectual entity. When you incarnate, since there is no shortcoming when you are together with celestial, human entities etc, you could not be classified as a causative factor. āḻvār says that this is amazing indeed \[he is complete in all aspects, whether he creates the worlds or whether he is born in that world\].

**ulagu ninnodu onṟi niṟka vēṟu niṟṟi** – This world is part of your divine, physical form and hence is together with you all the time, without separating from you. Despite that, with an *aprākruta* (not composed of primordial matter) divine form, you remain distinctly different from the created world which is together with you. The opinion here is that when the jīvātmās, during the time when they do not know you, ignore you, you sustain them through your *svarūpam* (basic nature) by being their indvelling soul, protecting them. When they learn about you and turn towards you favourably, you manifest your divine, auspicious form and protect them such that they could meditate on you. In either way, you are distinctly different from them.

**ādalāl** – thus, since you are inestimable in any manner

**ulagu ninnai uḷḷa sūḻal yāvar uḷḷa vallarē** – the words are to be rearranged as “*sūḻal uḷḷa ninnai ulagil yāvar uḷḷa vallarē*” to get the correct meaning. Who in this world is capable of knowing you, when you are having such amazing qualities? *sūḻal* – ingenuity – having amazing qualities which cannot be estimated.

We will next consider the 13th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 13. Innaiyenru

*innaiyenṟu sollalāvadu illai yādumittidaippinnai kēḷvan enbar un piṇakku uṇarnda peṟṟiyōrpinnaiyāya kōlamōdu pērum ūrum ādiyumninnai yār ninaikka vallar nīrmaiyāl ninaikkilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

innai enṟu – (you are) like this; sollalāvadu – to be spoken of; yādum illai – there is nothing; un piṇakku uṇarnda peṟṟiyōr – the learned people who have known the debate between those who know and who do not know about matters relating to your incarnation, names and activities; ittu – delicate; idai – having waist; pinnai – for nappinnai, who is neeḷā dēvi’s incarnation; kēḷvan enbar – will say that you are dear \[to her\]; pinnai – even as you remain as an equal with others, during your incarnation; āya – being distinct from them; kōlamōdu – with your divine form; pērum – your divine names; ūrum – the distinct features of the places where you had incarnated; ādiyum – the cause for your such divine form; ninnai – you; nīrmaiyāl – with your quality of simplicity; ninaikkil (alladu) – apart from knowing; yār – who; ninaikka vallavar – is capable of knowing on his/her own?

**Перевод:**

**No one can say that you are like this. Learned people, who have known the debates between your followers and others who are not, regarding your incarnations, names and activities, will say that you are the consort of nappinnaip pirātti. Even as you incarnate and appear to be equal to the others, you are distinct from the rest in terms of your divine form, divine names, divine places where you incarnate and the cause for such divine forms. Who is capable of thinking about you, other than when you show yourself out of your quality of simplicity?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that there is none who had known the secrets about emperumān’s incarnations. emperumān asked āḻvār “In that case, is there no one who knows this secret?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “After you incarnated in the covherd clan and manifested yourself, some people have seen your greatness. However, no one has seen this with his / her capability”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**innai enṟu sollalāvadu yādum illai** – during the time of your incarnation, considering that your qualities such as knowledge, strength etc which will be always together with you, also considering that qualities which are specific to your incarnation such as *saulabhyam* (easy to approach), *sauśeelyam* (simplicity), *vātsalyam* (treating shortcomings in others as good, like motherly forbearance) etc, if an attempt were made to estimate you, there is nothing by which can it be said that “You are like this”.

**ittidaip pinnai kēḷvan enbar** – people will say that you are dear to nappinnaip pirātti, who is an incarnation of neeḷā dēvi, who has a slender waist. The word *ittu* is a transformation of the word *ittaḷam* which means slender. puruśa sūktam uttarānuvākam says “*ajāyamānō bahudā vijāyatē tasya dīrā*: *parijānanti yōnim*” (though (paramapuruśan, the supreme entity) is without a birth, he incarnates in many ways. Only the learned people will know his incarnations). Thus, even the learned ones, who know the secrets of your incarnation, will say “You, who are together with nappinnaip pirātti, are apt to be attained”, making a distinction between you and the rest.  We could also construe the meaning after rearranging the verse as “*yādumittu idaippinnai kēḷvan enbar*” – the learned people will leave aside all the praises which vēdams say and will say that you, who incarnated as one among the covherd people and who married nappinnaip pirātti, are the apt divine form to take refuge under. The praises of vēdam are as in katōpaniśat 2-4-12, 13, 17 “*anguśta mātra*: *puruśa*:” (emperumān remains as the indvelling soul with a divine auspicious form which is about the length of one’s thumb); as in muṇḍaka upaniśat 2-1-4 “*agnir mūrtā cakśuśi candrasūryau diśa*: *śrōtrē vāgvivrutāśca vēdā* l *vāyu*: *prāṇō hrudayam viśvamasya padbhyām prutvī hyēśa sarvabhūtāntarātmā* ll”  
(for him (brahmam, emperumān), dēvalōkam, called as agni, is the head; candra (moon) and sūrya (sun) are the eyes; directions are the ears; vēdas are the words; vāyu is the prāṇan (vital air); all the worlds are the heart; earth is the feet; he is śrīman nārāyaṇan who is the indvelling soul of all). Negating all these which state that he has all the three worlds as his divine form which is fit to be attained, they will only consider the saying of śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-145 “*dēvatvē dēvadēhēyam manuśyatvē mānuśī* l *viṣṇōr dēhānurūpām vai karōtyēśātmanastanum* ll” (When (bhagavān) incarnates as a celestial entity, this pirātti takes the form of a celestial entity; when he incarnates as a human, she too takes a human form; she takes a form which is apt for the divine form of viṣṇu). In this meaning, the term *idai* would refer to the clan of herd-people and should be considered as an adjective for either pinnai kēḷvan (consort of nappinnaip pirātti) or for pinnai (nappinnaip pirātti).

**un piṇakkuṇarnda peṟṟiyōr** – the learned people who know the constant argument between those who are favourable to you and unfavourable to you. Your devotees would know, just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-4 “*pērum ōr āyiram piṟapala udaiya emperumān*” (emperumān has many divine forms and thousands of names for those forms) that you have countless divine forms and divine names. Those who are not your devotees would say, just as it has been said in the same tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 1-3-4 “*pērum ōr uruvamum uḷadillai*” (for those who are unfavourable to emperumān, it will be difficult to understand that even at a gross level, that emperumān has a divine form and a divine name) that he does not have a form or name. The learned people, who analyse this argument, will realise that “Based on the karmas, it is only you who grant knowledge or deny knowledge to people and it is only you who conducts this debate all the time”

**pinnaiyāya kōlamōdu pērum ūrum ādiyum ninnai** – during your incarnations which come after your *para* (your form as *para vāsudēvan* in śrī vaikuṇṭam) and *vyūha* (your form as *vibhava vāsudēvan* in kśīrābdi or milky ocean) forms, you come in the form of celestial entity or human or animal or plant entity. In all these, you cohabit with the respective species, as an equal. However, even as you cohabit as an equal with them, you have distinct, divine, auspicious form, divine names which reflect on the divine qualities and activities of those auspicious forms, the divine abodes such as ayōddi, maturā etc which you choose to incarnate, the vyūha form which is the cause of such an incarnation. Here, the word *pinnai* (after or subsequently) refers to the time when you incarnate in this world. Alternatively, *pinnai* could also refer to the period which comes at the end of such an incarnation. In this case, it would refer to your divine forms such as *anirudda, śankarśaṇa* et al, who are in milky ocean, the corresponding divine names which refer to the auspicious qualities and activities of those divine forms, the specific abode for those forms such as *āmōdam*, *pramōdam, mōdam* and *sammōdam*, the divine form of *para vāsudēvan* in paramapadam, who is the cause for these vyūha forms.

**nīrmaiyāl ninaikkil alladu yār ninaikka vallarē** – if you choose to reveal through your divine qualities of friendliness, simplicity etc, one may know about you. Who can comprehend you on his / her own effort? The term *alladu* has to be added to the verse in order to get the appropriate meaning. When did emperumān reveal about himself? bhagavat gītā 4-5 says “*bahūnimē vyatītāni janmāni tava cārjuna*” (Oh arjuna! For both you and me, several *janmas* (births) have taken place). Starting with this ślōkam, and until the 9<sup>th</sup> ślōkam in the same chapter of bhagavat gītā (viz. until 4-9) which says “*janma karma ca mē divyam ēvam yō vētti tatvata*: l *tyaktvā dēham punar janma naiti māmēti sō’rjuna* ll” (Oh arjuna! One who truly knows like this, my *aprākruta* (not relating to primordial matter) birth and activities, does not have another birth after relinquishing his body in that birth. He attains me), you have revealed the secrets of your incarnation to arjuna. Through that way, a person may know about you but not in any other way.

We will next consider the 14th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 14. Tuymai yogamayinay

*tūymai yōgamāyināy tuḻāyalangal mālaiyāy!āmaiyāgi āḻkadal tuyinṟa ādi dēva! ninnāmadēyam innadenna vallam alla āgilumsāmavēda gīdanāya cakrapāṇi allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tūymai – purity (of samsāris); yōgamāyināy – Oh one who creates!; tuyāḻalangal – made with the petals of divine tuḷasi; mālaiyāy – Oh one who has the garland!; āmaiyāgi – in the form of tortoise; āḻ – deep; kadal – in the ocean; tuyinṟa – mercifully resting; ādi – being the causative factor; dēva – Oh, the lord!; nin – your highness’; nāmadēyam – the auspicious qualities and activities revealed by your divine names; innadenna – to say that they are like this; vallam – we do not have the capability; allavāgilum – despite that; sāmavēda – in cāndōjña upaniśat; gīdanāya – mercifully spoken of; cakrapāṇi allaiyē – are you not the one who is with the divine disc, residing in the solar galaxy!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who brings about purity in samsāris! Oh one who wears the garland made with petals of divine tuḷasi! Oh the lord and the causative factor, who is mercifully resting as a tortoise in the deep ocean! We do not have the capability to say that you are like this, after knowing the auspicious qualities and activities, as revealed by your divine names. However, are you not the one who is mercifully spoken of in cāndōjña upaniśat and residing in the solar galaxy, with the divine disc!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār “How did you know the secrets about my incarnations?” āḻvār says “While I cannot estimate your qualities of simplicity etc with which you had incarnated as a tortoise in order to help celestial entities who always desire other benefits only, I learnt that the divine beauty of that incarnation can be known only through vēdas”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tūymai yōgamāyināy** – due to connection, since time immemorial, with the physical form which is acit (without knowledge), the samsāri jīvan (ātmā, the soul) has become impure. Oh one who is together with purity, who, through your mercy, removes the physical form and liberates the jīvan to be with nityasūris and to enjoy you! This implies that emperumān is the means for the samsāri to cross the ocean of samsāram and attain him. Not only does emperumān remain pure, but he also brings in purity in others (samsāris) since he is the opposite of everything which is lowly. Next, āḻvār says that emperumān remains as the goal to be attained.

**tuḻāy alangal mālaiyāy** – Oh one who is decorated with the garland of divine tuḷasi, as a symbol of supreme being! tuḷasi garland has been mentioned as the symbol for the supreme entity in śāstrams (sacred texts); it also distinguishes him from those who wear bones, ash etc and hence he is the one to be attained.

**āmaiyāgi** – you had taken the form of a tortoise in order to churn the ocean to get nectar, for the sake of celestial entities, who desire only worldly pursuits and not you.

**āḻ kadal tuyinṟa** – Oh one who mercifully took rest in the ocean such that the mantara mountain would not get immersed in the unfathomable ocean \[during the time of churning of ocean\]! Hasn’t parāśara maharishi too mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-99 “*kśīrōdamadyē bhagavān kūrmarūpī svayam hari*: l *mantarādrēr adiśtānam bhramatō’bhūn mahāmunē* ll” (bhagavān hari (śrīman nārāyaṇan), in the form of divine tortoise, was the support for mantara mountain which was rotating in the middle of milky ocean)!

**ādi dēva** – Oh one who revealed that you alone are the causative factor for the world through this divine activity! The implied meaning is that unless he were the causative factor, emperumān would not have had to transform himself as a tortoise, disregarding his greatness as well as disregarding the lowliness of the celestial entities who desired only worldly pursuits.

**nin nāmadēyam innadenna vallam alla āgilum** – even though I do not have the capability to estimate your highness’ auspicious qualities and activities and say that “They are like this”, after seeing the way that your highness had transformed into a \[lowly\] tortoise. Since it is not apt to say that, for emperumān, who has thousands of names, it is not possible to say that this is his name, we consider that here, the term *nāmadēyam* would refer to his auspicious qualities and divine activities.

**sāma vēda gīdanāya cakrapāṇi allaiyē** – I learnt that your divine form of tortoise, which you took on, disregarding your greatness, is the golden hued *aprākruta* (not belonging to this materialistic realm) divine form, from cāndōjña upaniśat, which forms part of sāma vēdam. cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6 and 1-7 say “*ya ēśō’ntarādityē hiraṇmaya*: *puruśō druśyatē hiraṇyaśmaśrur* *hiraṇya kēśa āpraṇakhāt sarva Eva suvarṇa*: l *tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvamakśiṇī* ll (This supreme entity who is seen amidst sūrya (solar galaxy) with a beautiful, golden hued form, has a beautiful, golden moustace and golden hair; he has two divine eyes which are like lotus, blossomed by sun). nārasimha purāṇam says “*dyēyas sadā savitru maṇḍala madyavarttī nārāyaṇas sarasijāsana sanniviśta*: l *kēyūravān makara kuṇḍalavān kiritī hārī hiraṇyamayavapur druta śankha chakra*: ll  
(nārāyaṇa, who is in the middle of solar galaxy, who is sitting on a lotus flower, who is wearing shoulder amulets, fish-like ear rings, crown and chain, who has a golden divine form, who has divine conch and disc, is apt to be meditated upon always), to bring out this understanding.

We will next take up the 15th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 15. Angam aru

*angamāṟu vēdanāngumāgi ninṟu avaṟṟuḷētanguginṟa tanmaiyāy tadam kadal paṇattalaiccengaṇ nāgaṇaikkidanda selvamalgu sīrināysangavaṇṇam anna mēni sārngapāṇi allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

angam āṟum – for the six auxiliaries \[of vēdam\] such as śīkśā etc; vēdam nāngum – and for the four vēdams which are the main; āgi ninṟu – being the controller; avaṟṟuḷē – among the four vēdams; tanmaiyāy – Oh one who has the qualities; kadal – in the milky ocean; paṇa – on the hoods; talai – on top; sem – reddish; kaṇ – having divine eyes; nāgaṇai – on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); kidanda – as one who is reclining; malgu – growing abundantly; selvam – having opulence; sīrināy – having divine auspicious qualities; śanga – sankham (conch); vaṇṇam anna – similar to its colour; mēni – having a divine form; sārngapāṇi – as śrī rāma, who holds the divine bow, śārngam; allaiyē – did you not incarnate?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is the controller of the four vēdams and their six auxiliaries and who remains as the meanings of those vēdams! Oh one who has abundantly growing opulence and auspicious qualities, and who is reclining in the milky ocean, on the mattress of ādiśēśan who has reddish, divine eyes on his hoods! You have a complexion like the white coloured divine conch (during kruta yugam). Did you not incarnate as śrī rāma (in trētā yugam) holding the divine bow, śrī śarngam?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “You are known only through vēdams. In order to shower your mercy on your followers, you, who are reclining in kśīrābdi (milky ocean), which is equivalent to your form in paramapadam, come down to this earth with a form desired by your followers. Is it possible for anyone to estimate your simplicity?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**angamāṟu vēda nāngum āgi ninṟu** – śikśā, vyākaraṇam, candas, niruktam, jyōtiśam and kalpam are the six auxiliaries for the four vēdams rig, yajur, sāma and atarvaṇa. emperumān remains as the one who revealed these to the world.

**avaṟṟuḷē tanguginṟa tanmaiyāy** – Oh one who fits well with the great meanings of words given in the four vēdams along with their six auxiliaries, making them to belong only to you! The verse *āgi ninṟāvaṟṟuḷē* could be considered as a single word, in which case the meaning would change to – remaining within the vēdams which are authentic texts which do not have the deficiencies seen in other texts. What are the qualities of greatness enunciated in vēdams?

- Auspicious qualities enunciated by upaniśats

taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 1-2 “*satyam jñāṇam anantam brahma* l *yō vēda nihitam guhāyām* l *sō’śnutē sarvān kāmān saha* l *brahmaṇā vipaścitēti* ll” (brahmam (supreme entity) (being without transformation) is full of truthfulness and knowledge, and (being unbounded) is without an end. One who knows that such brahmam is inside the cave of one’s heart, enjoys all the auspicious qualities with that omniscient brahmam)

muṇḍakōpaniśat 1-1-10 “*yas sarvajñas sarvavit yasya jñānamayam tapa*:” (that paramātmā (supreme entity) who knows the svarūpam (basic nature) and svabhāvam (basic characteristics) of all entities and whose sankalpam (vow; thought) is full of knowledge …..)

- Qualities of being the lord of both leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) and nitya vibhūti (spriritualistic realm)

taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*yacca kincit jagatyasmin druśyatē śrūyatēpivā* l *antar bahiśca tat sarvam vyāpya nārāyaṇa*: *stita*: ll” (nārāyaṇan pervades both on the inside and outside all the entities which are seen and heard in this world)

- Qualities of togetherness with the divine form

bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat “*tasya haitasya puruśasya rūpam yatā māhārajanam vāsa*:” (that divine form of supreme entity is like a golden dress)

- Qualities of incarnations

cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6 & 7 “*ya ēśō’ntarādityē hiraṇmaya*: *puruśō druśyatē hiraṇyaśmaśrur*  
*hiraṇya kēśa āpraṇakhāt sarva Eva suvarṇa*:l *tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvamakśiṇī* ll” (This supreme entity who is seen amidst sūrya (solar galaxy) with a beautiful, golden hued form, has a beautiful, golden moustace and golden hair; he has two divine eyes which are like lotus, blossomed by sun)

- Qualities of togetherness with nitya vibhūti

rig vēdam – viṣṇu sūktam “*tad viṣṇō*: *paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya*: l *divīva cakśurātatam* ll *tad viprāsō vipanyavō jāgruvāmsas samindatē viṣṇōr yat paramam padam* ll” (nityasūris constantly keep looking at that superior abode of viṣṇu. That supreme abode is effulgent like sūriyan (sun) who reveals everything, who is in the sky, who is like the eyes of the world. In that paramapadam, greatly wise people, people who keep constantly vorśipping, ever alert nityasūris shine brightly)

**tadam kadal paṇattalaic cengaṇāgaṇaikkidanda selva malgu sīrināy** – oh one who revealed these qualities by resting in *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean) instead of being merely a hearsay by being known through only in the vēdams! The meaning here is that emperumān is revealing his unlimited opulence and auspicious qualities by lying on the mattress of ādiśēśan who has reddish eyes which are on top of his hoods. These hoods have expanded due to the touch of emperumān and his eyes are reddish due to that intoxication. emperumān is revealing those unlimited opulence and auspicious qualities to his followers, lying in the milky ocean.

**sangavaṇṇam anna mēni sārngapāṇi allaiyē**– reclining in *tiruppāṟkadal* cannot be enjoyed by ordinary people, since it is almost like his supreme form in paramapadam. To ensure that even those people can enjoy him, emperumān incarnated during kruta yugam (first among the four epochs of kruta, trētā, dvāpara and kali) with whitish complexion, as desired by people in that yugam. Subsequently, during trētā yugam, as desired by the people during that time, in order to annihilate cruel people such as rāvaṇa et al, did he not incarnate in the kśatriya clan (clan of warriors) as śrī rāma, holding the divine bow, śrī śārngam? He transformed his natural dark, complexion to a whitish complexion during kruta yugam for the sake of his followers. During trētā yugam, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-11 “*abravīt tridaśaśrēśtān rāmō darmabrutām vara:* l *ātmānam mānuśam manyē rāmam daśaratājmajam* ll” (śrī rāma, who is more eminent than *darmavān* (those who follow righteous ways) told the great people among celestial entities “I consider myself as a human being and as rāma, the son of daśarata”). Who would be able to estimate this simplicity of yours when you have shed your *paratvam* (being a supreme entity) and taken up the quality of being a human being?

We will next consider the 16th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 16. Talaikkanam tugal

*talaikkaṇat tugaḷ kuḻambu sādi sōdi tōṟṟamāynilaikkaṇangaḷ kāṇavandu niṟṟiyēlum nīdurumkalaikkaṇangaḷ soṟporuḷ karuttināl ninaikkoṇāmalaikkaṇangaḷ pōl uṇarttum mātci ninṟan mātciyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

talaikkaṇam – the superior celestial species; tugaḷ – the inferior plant species; kuḻambu sādi – the mixed species of humans and animals; sōdi – with aprākruta (not made of anything from primordial matter) effulgent, divine form; tōṟṟamāy – incarnating; nilaikkaṇangaḷ – even botanical entities; kāṇa vandu – came in order to be enjoyed (by them); niṟṟiyēlum – even if you remained; nīdu – eternal; irum – being huge; kalai – belonging to vēdams and vēdāntams; kaṇangaḷ – collection of; sol poruḷ – meanings of words; karuttināl – through inner meanings; malaikkaṇangaḷ pōl – like a cluster of mountains; uṇarttum mātci – (through narratives) greatness which is revealed; ninṟan mātciyē – is only your greatness

**Перевод:**

**You incarnate with your aprākruta, divine, effulgent form in the superior species of celestials, in inferior species of plants as well as in the mixed species of humans and animals. Even though you came in order to be enjoyed by even plant entities \[which do not have the senses associated with such enjoyment\], the huge collection of vēdams and vēdāntams, through their normal meanings and inner meanings, will not know you.  Through all these, the greatness, which is revealed like a huge cluster of mountains, is your greatness alone.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about how emperumān incarnated as a celestial and human entity and protected the world. In this pāsuram he says that even though emperumān incarnated in all the four classes of entities (celestial, human, animal and plant), such that everyone could enjoy him, his greatness of incarnation was such that even all the pramāṇams (authentic texts) could not estimate him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**talaikkaṇat tugaḷ kuḻambu sādi** – Among the four types of species (forms of existence), celestial species is considered to be superior. The term *tugaḷ* refers to fault or dust; here, this refers to something inferior. Thus, the lowly plant species is referred to as *tugaḷ sādi*. Human and animal species, which are in between these two extremes, are referred to as *kuḻambu sādi*. Thus, there are four types of forms, namely celestial, human, animal and plant.

**kuḻambu sādi** – those, who have virtuous karma in them, are born as celestial entities. Hence, this species is classified as superior species. Those with sinful karma in them are born as plant species. Hence, this species is referred to inferior species. Those with both virtuous and sinful karma in them are born as humans and animals \[animal species would include birds too\]. Thus, they are classified as *kuḻambu* sādi (mixed species). Animals have similar habits as humans in terms of eating, sleeping etc and are clubbed together with human species.

**sōdi tōṟṟamāy** – incarnating with a splendorous form. Just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 8-12-2 “*ēśa samprasādō’smāccarīrāt samuttāya param* *jyōtir upasampadya svēna rūpēnābhiniśpadyatē*” (the jīvan, leaving from this (physical) form, attains the supremely splendorous śrīman nārāyaṇan and attains his basic natural characteristics), emperumān incarnates in the just mentioned four species with his *aprākruta divyamangaḷa vigraha svarūpam* (divine, auspicious, splendorous form which is not made of primordial matter). In the celestial species, he incarnated as vāmana et al; in the human species, he incarnated as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al; in the animal species, he incarnated as matsya (fish), kūrma (tortoise), varāha (wild boar) et al; among plant species, he incarnated as *kubjāmram* (short mango tree).

**nilaikkaṇangaḷ kāṇa vandu niṟṟiyēlum** – Even though you came to be enjoyed by celestial and human entities which have thinking power and hence could enjoy your presence, you were enjoyed by plant species too which do not have thinking power and are stationary. Have plant species enjoyed emperumān? During his incarnations as śrī rāma and kriṣṇa, it has been said in purāṇams that the wind which touched them (śrī rāma and kriṣṇa) also touched plants in ayōdyā and brindāvan respectively. As soon as that breeze touched plants, they started flowering and bearing fruits, in unseasonal times, as mentioned by great sages such as vālmīki and parāśarar.

In the latter part of the pāsuram, āḻvār says that “Even though you lowered your greatness and incarnated in various species, *pramāṇams* such as vēdam and vēdāntam could not estimate the greatness of your incarnations”.

**nīdirum kalaikkaṇangaḷ** – expansive vēdams and vēdāntams which are eternal. The term *kalai* refers to vēdams and their auxiliaries. vēdams are eternal through their svarūpam (basic nature), since they have eternal *ānupūrvi* (sequence of words in their texts). Their auxiliaries are eternal through their *pravāham* (flow of text, as created during each period of brahmā). Thus, these are both eternal and expansive. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 3-6-28 says “*angāni caturō vēdā*: *mīmāmsā nyāya vistara*: l *purāṇam darmaśāstram ca vidyā hyētās caturdaśa*: ll” (the six auxiliaries, four vēdams, along with those which came to explain these, viz. mīmāmsai, tarkka śāstram, purāṇam and dharma śāstram (smruti) form fourteen vidyāstānams (knowledge bases)). These vidyāstānams are referred to here as *kalaikkaṇangaḷ.*

**soṟporuḷ karuttināl ninaikkoṇā** – neither through *abhidānavrutti* (a word referring to its meaning) nor through *tātparyavrutti* (the underlying opinion of a word or group of words) could the vēdams and their auxiliaries even think of the greatness of even one of your incarnations.

**malaikkaṇangaḷpōl uṇarttum mātci ninṟan mātciyē** – the activities and narratives of your incarnations are like a group of mountains; they cannot be split or cracked. They cannot be estimated either. The beauty or greatness of your auspicious qualities is yours alone.

We will take up the 17th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 17. Ega murti

*ēga mūrti mūnṟu mūrti nāngu mūrti nanmai sērbōgamūrti puṇṇiyattin mūrti eṇṇil mūrtiyāynāgamūrti sayanamāy nalangadal kidandu mēlāgamūrti āyavaṇṇam enkol ādi dēvanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ādi dēvanē – Oh the causative factor!; ēga mūrti – having the form of para vāsudēvan; mūnṟu mūrti – having the three forms of śankarśaṇan et al; nālu mūrti – having the four forms of pradānam (acētana or primordial entity, without knowledge), puruśa (cētana or entities with knowledge), avyakta (derivative of pradānam) and kālam (time); nanmai sēr – together with the benefit of having abundance of mercy; bōgamūrti – as an epitome of enjoyability; puṇṇiyattin mūrti – as the strength of the virtues of fortunate ones; eṇṇil – without a limit; mūrtiyāy – having forms of vibhavāvatāram (incarnations); nalam – having the benefit (of śrīman nārāyaṇan having happily taken residence in); kadal – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); nāga – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan)’s; mūrti – divine form; sayanamāy – having as mattress; kidandu – reclining; mēl – further more; āgamūrtiyāya vaṇṇam – the quality of having incarnated as arcāvatāram (divine forms in various abodes) as desired by followers; enkol – how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**You have the form of para vāsudēvan, the supreme entity in paramapadam. You have the three forms of śankarśaṇan, pradyumnan and aniruddan, in vyūham (tiruppāṟkadal, milky ocean). You have the four forms of pradānam, puruśam, avyaktam and kālam. You have countless forms of vibhavāvatārams (incarnations as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al), together with abundance of mercy, as an epitome of enjoyability, and due to the strength of the virtues of your followers. You recline on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān’s form, in tiruppāṟkadal, which has the benefit of your having happily taken residence in. On top of all these, you have the quality of having incarnated as arcāvatārams, as desired by your followers. What an amazing quality is this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(introduction) emperumān controls nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm) by remaining in paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) as para vāsudēvan. He also conducts the leelā vibhūti (samsāram or materialistic realm) by taking incarnations among the four species of celestial, human, animal and plant. śāstrams are unable to estimate his greatness in these incarnations. On top of this, he also incarnates in arcāvatāra form (idol form in various divine abodes) as the boundary of simplicity, and as desired by his followers. āḻvār is wondering as to how he could speak about this quality, in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ēga mūrti** – Just as it has been said in the phrase *vāsudēvōsi pūrṇa*: (you remain complete with six qualities as para vāsudēvan), you have the unparalleled form of para vāsudēvan, complete with the six qualities of *jñāna* (knowledge), *bala* (power), *aiśvarya* (wealth), *vīrya* (valour), *śakti* (strength) and *tējas* (radiance) having mercifully taken residence in paramapadam which is like a calm ocean, without waves, enjoyed by nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam) and muktātmās (those who have been liberated from samsāram and have attained śrī vaikuṇṭam).

**mūnṟu mūrti** – having three vyūha forms as śankarśaṇan, pradyumnan and aniruddan, taking two qualities each of the above-mentioned six qualities, and looking after the functions of destruction, creation and protection, respectively.

**nālu mūrti** – since para vāsudēvan exists only for others, adding this divine form to the just mentioned three forms, it says that emperumān exists in four forms. Alternatively, it could be construed as having four forms of *tamas* or *mūlaprakruti* (primordial matter) which assists during creation etc, *puruśas* which indicate the entire gamut of jīvas, *avyaktam* which is created from *tamas* and *kālam* (time).

**nanmai sēr bōgamūrti** – vibhavāvatārams (incarnations such as matsya, kūrma, varāha et al), which have the benefit of abundance of mercy, and being enjoyable for samsāris. Abundance of mercy refers to emperumān’s quality of being visible to samsāris too, though he takes incarnations with his *aprākruta* (not made from *prakruti*) divine form, even as he comes in the species of celestial, human, animal and plant. Alternatively, this could be construed to mean that emperumān has a form which is enjoyed by cētanas after they reach paramapadam, after being liberated from samsāram, as well as one which is enjoyed by cētanas in samsāram itself, during his time of incarnation.

**puṇṇiyattin mūrti** – even this enjoyability in both samsāram and paramapadam will occur when the virtues of the concerned people get matured. Just as it has been mentioned in the bhagavat gītā ślōkam 4-8 “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām*“, his divine form is such that he is the end benefit for those who have paramabhakti (inability to sustain oneself in separation from emperumān), he is the refuge for those who surrender to him and he is the auspicious form for those who are devoted to him. Those who do not belong to any of these types of people are without any virtues, losing on the opportunity of his incarnation, just as emperumān himself had said in bhagavat gītā 9-11 “*avajānanti mām mūd*A *mānuśīm tanumāśritam* l *param bhāvamajānantō mama bhūta mahēśvaram* ll” (The foolish people insult me, not realising my greatness of being in human form, though I am supreme and above all the creatures which exist)

**eṇṇil mūrtiyāy** – having countless divine forms in incarnations. Hasn’t *śruti* (vēdam) said that his incarnations are countless, as seen in puruśa sūktam uttarānuvākam ślōkam “*ajāyamānō bahudā vijāyatē tasya dīrā*: *parijānanti yōnim*” (though (paramapuruśan, the supreme entity) is without a birth, he incarnates in many ways. Only the learned people will know his incarnations)! Hasn’t emperumān himself spoken about his many births in bhagavat gītā 4-5 “*bahūnimē vyatītāni janmāni tava cārjuna*” (Oh arjuna! For both you and me, several *janmams* (births) have taken place)! Hasn’t nammāḻvār too mercifully mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 3-10-1 “*sanmam palapala*” (you have had many births)!

Next, the pāsuram speaks about his great quality of returning to tiruppāṟkadal with the hope that at least in future he should protect them when samsāris attain him, after samsāris did not attain him despite his many incarnations, though he should have actually given up on them and returned to paramapadam.

**nāgamūrti sayanamāy nalam kadal kidandu** – the words should be rearranged as *nalam kadalilē nāgamūrti sayanamāykkidandu* – reclining on the divine form of tiruvanandāḻvān in the beneficial tiruppāṟkadal. Unlike the divine abodes such as ayōddi, maturā etc which disappear after existing for a while, since emperumān joyously reclined on it until the final deluge, tiruppāṟkadal is termed as beneficial.

**mēl āgamūrti āyavaṇṇam enkol** – how to describe the quality of acceding to the request of your followers and pervading the idols of their choice, whether in gold or silver or stone material, with your *aprākruta* divine form! Just as akrūra says, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-4 “*pāpam harati yat pumsām smrutam sankapanāmayam* l  
*tat puṇadrīka nayanam viṣṇōr drakśyāmyaham mukham* ll” (I am going to see that divine face of emperumān which has lotus-like eyes, and which removes the sins of people the moment they think of him in the form of their choice) and just as emperumān himself says in bhagavat gītā 4-11 “*yē yatā mām prabadyantē tāms tataiva bhajāmyaham*” (I attain my devotees in whichever form in which they desire to attain me) don’t you consider the *prākruta* (made of primordial matter) form of materials in which your followers desire to see you as being appropriate for you to desire to enter with your *aprākruta* divine form!

**ādi dēvanē** – While you are the causative factor for the entire world, you transform yourself in line with the desires of your followers! It could also be construed that you are transforming yourself since you are the causative factor for your followers and they are your possession.

Next, we will consider the 18th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 18. Vidatta vay

*vidatta vāyorāyiram irāyiram kaṇ vendaḻalviduttu vīḻvilāda bōgam mikka sōdi tokka sīrtoduttu mēl vidānamāya pauvanīr arāvaṇaipadutta pāyal paḷḷi koḷvadu enkolō vēlai vaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēlai vaṇṇanē – Oh one who has the complexion of blue coloured ocean!; vidatta – having poison; vāy orāyiram – having thousand mouths; irāyiram kaṇ – with two thousand eyes; vem – cruel; taḻal – poisonous fire; viduttu – letting out; vīḻvu – separation from emperumān; bōgam – having enjoyability (having divine form); mikka­ – in abundance; sōdi – being with radiance; mēl vidānamāya – in the hoods which are like a canopy; tokka – being dense; sīr – beauty; toduttu – fastened well; pauvanīr – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); padutta – spread out; arāvaṇai – tiruvanantāḻvān; pāyal – on his mattress; paḷḷikoḷvadu – this posture of reclining; enkol – for what?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is having the complexion of blue coloured ocean! You are reclining on the divine form of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who has a thousand heads which emit poison and two thousand eyes which emit poisonous fire. He does not have separation from you and has dense beauty in abundance which is fastened well in his hoods which are like a canopy. For what purpose are you reclining on him?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said nāgamūrti sayanam and earlier, in pāsuram 15, he had said tadam kadal paṇattalaic cengaṇāgaṇaikkidanda, while describing the reclining posture of emperumān in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean), as a prelude to his incarnations. Immediately, āḻvār’s mind went towards emperumān. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 1-7 “sa hi dēvair udīrṇasya rāvaṇasya vadārttibhi: l artitō mānuśē lōkē jajgnē viṣṇu: sanātana: \|\|” (viṣṇu, who is ancient and who was prayed to by celestial entities who desired annihilation of the demon rāvaṇa, was born in the world of humans), even though there is no necessity for celestial entities to pray to you just as they do for you to incarnate and remove their hurdles, who is going to know your greatness of resting in enemy land with your qualities of beauty etc and come to you for getting their deeds to be done?*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vidatta vāy orāyiram** – having a thousand mouths which emit poison

**irāyiram kaṇ vendaḻal viduttu** – emitting cruel fire through two thousand eyes. ādiśēśan is always enjoying the presence of emperumān who is reclining on him. Why then did he get this cruelty arising out of anger? This is because of his doubt “emperumān is reclining in this samsāram which is inhabited by demons and monsters. What difficulties will come his way here?” ādiśēśan enjoys emperumān due to his insatiable affection for emperumān. In the same way, the hatred and anger towards inimical entities came out of this affection for emperumān. By recalling this, āḻvār feels satisfied that “emperumān is resting well on tiruvanandāḻvān, well protected by him such that wicked people cannot approach emperumān”

**vīḻvilāda bōgam** – having a very sweet divine form which comes about because of emperumān’s embrace and due to which he cannot separate from emperumān. āḷavandār, one of our important preceptors, in his stōtra ratnam 39 says “*tayā sahāsīnam ananta bhōgini*” (one who has mercifully sat along with periya pirātti on the divine form of tiruvanandāḻvān). His divine form is referred to as *bhōgam* in this ślōkam too. Thus, just as he protects emperumān through his ātma guṇas (auspicious qualities of ātmā) of needless doubt and anger arising out of that doubt, anantāḻvān is dear to emperumān through his divine form too.

**mikka sōdi** – due to carrying out kainkaryam (services) to you and enjoying, he gets an unparalleled radiance.

**tokka sīr toduttu mēl vidānamāya** – the words should be rearranged as *mēl vidānamāya tokka sīr toduttu* to get the meaning of “In the canopy of hoods, gathering the dense beauty”. *tokku* – dense; *sīr* – beauty. In the earlier part, the enjoyability which resulted for emperumān due to ādiśēśan’s contact was mentioned. Here, it describes the way emperumān is reclining on ādiśēśan, enjoying the beauty of his hoods which are with different complexions, along with the comfortable joy of experiencing his contact as mentioned in the earlier part.

**pauva nīr arāvaṇaip padutta pāyal** – apart from reclining on the ocean appropriate to his soft, divine form, emperumān is reclining on the mattress of tiruvanandāḻvān so that even the irritation arising out of the ocean will not cause him any discomfort.

**paḷḷi koḷvadu enkol** – for what purpose are you reclining? Did you do this because brahmā et al prayed to you saying “You should recline here so that you could protect us during our times of danger”? The implied meaning here is that emperumān is reclining here even when none prayed to him to do so, in order to help them when danger encircles them.

**vēlai vaṇṇanē** – did you recline with the complexion of bluish sea, on the white coloured (milky) ocean so that some people will know about it and attain you to enjoy it? The implied meaning is that celestial entities approach him only to beseech him to destroy their enemies and not to enjoy his beauty.

The purpose of mentioning his reclining on tiruvanandāḻvān was not to remove the doubt of “Is there no one to carry out service for him?” but to make it appear that he is always alert to hear the voice of samsāris such as brahmā et al who come to him when they are hindered by demons.

We will next take up the 19th pāsuram of this prabandam

## 19. Pulladagi vedam

*puḷḷadāgi vēdam nāngum ōdināy adanṟiyumpuḷḷin vāy piḷandu putkodip piditta pinnarumpuḷḷai ūrdi ādalāl adu enkol min koḷ nēmiyāypuḷḷin meyppagaik kadal kidattal kādalittadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

min koḷ nēmiyāy – Oh one who is holding the resplendent divine disc!; puḷḷin – bird (bakāsuran)’s; vāy – mouth; piḷandu – dissecting it; adanṟiyum – apart from that; puḷ – periya tiruvadi (garudan); kodi – as flag; piditta pin – apart from keeping it; arum – distinguished; puḷḷai – that periya tiruvadi; ūrdi – you are conducting as your vehicle; ādalāl – being a natural protector, like this; puḷḷadāgi – incarnating as a swan; vēdam nāngum – the four vēdas; ōdināy – (you had) instructed; kadal adu – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); puḷḷin – for periya tiruvadi; mey pagai – on top of tiruvanandāḻvān (ādiśēśan) who is a natural enemy; kadalittu – desirously

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is holding the divine, resplendent disc! You had incarnated as a swan and instructed the four vēdams \[to brahmā\]. Apart from that, you had dissected a demon \[bakāsuran\] who had come in the form of a bird. You have garudan, as your flag; apart from that, you are also conducting that distinguished garudan as your vehicle. Being a natural protector, as shown by all these examples, how is it that you are having garudan and ādiśēśan, who are seen by the common people of the world as natural enemies, to be your servitors, together, even as you are desirously reclining in tiruppāṟkadal?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “What simplicity is this when you come and recline in tiruppāṟkadal even when none has come and prayed to you for help!”. In this pāsuram, after seeing emperumān carrying out many incarnations in this world and protecting people, and in line with śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 1-22-21 “na hi pālana sāmartyamrutē sarvēśvaram harim l stitau stitam mahāprājña bhavatyanyasya kaśyacit ll” (Oh one with lot of wisdom! Apart from hari (emperumān), who is firmly rooted in protecting people, is it not a fact that there is none who has the ability to protect?) since there is none who is capable of protecting, since this world is exclusively under your control, there is no need for anyone to beseech you for protection. To bring this out clearly, āḻvār speaks about various activities of emperumān in protection and says “What is the surprise in seeing anantan (ādiśēśan) and garudan, who are natural enemies when seen from the angle of the common people in the world, serving you together, when you are reclining?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**puḷḷadāgi vēdam nāngum ōdināy** – if you \[emperumān\] had instructed vēdams to brahmā with your divine, auspicious form, people would have thought that you only had composed vēdam \[vēdams’ distinction is that they are *apauruśēyam* (not written by anyone)\]. Hence, you transformed your divine form to that of a swan and mercifully revealed the true meanings of vēdams and the means to achieve you, so that the darkness of ignorance would disappear from this world. Hasn’t śrī parāśara bhattar (the divine son of kūrattāḻvān, rāmānujar’s important disciple) mercifully said in śrī rangarāja stavam uttara śatakam 2-1 that vēdams remove the darkness of ignorance? “*hartum tamas sadasatī ca vivēktumīśō mānam pradīpamiva kāruṇikō dadāti* l *tēnāvalōkya krutna*: *paribhunjatē tam tatraiva kē’pi capalā*: *śalabhībhavanti* ll ” (emperumān, who is an ocean of mercy, gives vēdam which is like a light to remove the darkness (of ignorance) and to analyse the benefits and disadvantages. Those, who are favourable to emperumān, attain emperumān with the help of that lamp; those with fickle minds (who desire worldly pursuits) fall into the lamp like moths and perish)

**adanṟiyum** – apart from removing the darkness of ignorance which is like a constant enemy to jīvātmās

**puḷḷin vāy piḷandu** – dissecting the mouth of a demon bakāsuran who came in the form of a crane, after you had incarnated as kriṣṇa. This implies that just as he had instructed vēdams, the authentic text, and removed the inner enemy of ignorance, emperumān annihilated bakāsuran and revealed himself, the pramēyam (that which is revealed by the vēdams as the supreme entity) to sustain his followers.

**putkodip piditta pinnarum puḷḷai uṟdi** – emperumān held aloft garudan as his flag, to reveal that he is the protector, so that those who are suffering in this world would come to him and get rid of their sorrows. Apart from that, you conduct garudan as your vehicle too in order to reach the place, where your followers are present, to help them.

**min koḷ nēmiyāy** – Oh one who has the divine disc which is resplendent with a reddish complexion, contrasting with your bluish complexion! This emphasises that protecting others is a distinguishing feature seen with him. This reaffirms nammāḻvār’s words in periya tiruvandādi 87 *kai kaḻalā nēmiyān* (one from whose hands the divine disc will never separate).

**ādalāl** – as a result of being a natural protector like this. Here, periya vāccān piḷḷai, the vyākyātā (commentator) for this prabandam, says that the verse should realigned as “*min koḷ nēmiyāy*! *puḷḷadāgi vēdam nāngum ōdināy; adanṟiyum puḷḷin vāy piḷandu putkodip piditta pinnarum puḷḷai ūrdi; puḷḷin meyppagaik kadal kidattal kādalittadu adu en kol*” to get the apt meaning.

**puḷḷin meyppagaik kadal kidattal kādalittadu adu en kol** – when looked from the viewpoint of a worldly person, there is a natural enmity between ādiśēśan and garudan. However, you are desirously lying atop ādiśēśan in tiruppāṟkadal and both ādiśēśan and garudan are unitedly carrying out servitude to you. For what purpose is this? The implied meaning is: Is it to reveal your ability that you could get served by inimical entities in this samsāram? īsn’t it this capability which makes a samsāri cētanan (a jīvātmā in samsāram) for whom ahankāram (body is prominent) and mamakāram (everything is mine) are desirous qualities, to be at the same level with nityasūris, for whom carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān is a natural phenomenon!

Next, we will take up the 20th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 20. Kusam onrum inri

*kūsam onṟum inṟi māsuṇam paduttu vēlai nīrpēsa ninṟa dēvar vandu pāda mun kidandadumpāsa ninṟu nīril vāḻum āmaiyāna kēsavāēsa anṟu nī kidandavāṟu kūṟu tēṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāsam – snares; ninṟa – remaining; vāḻum – living happily; āmaiyāna – one who incarnated in the tortoise species; kēsavā – Oh one who sired brahmā, rudra et al; kūsam – the thought of “What will happen from someone?”; onṟuminṟi – without anything; māsuṇam paduttu – spreading \[the mattress of\] tiruvanandāḻvān (ādiśēśan); vēlai nīr – in the ocean; dēvar – celestial entities such as brahmā et al; vandu – coming from their respective abode; pāda – to praise; mun – from the time of creation; kidandadum – the quality of reclining; ēsa – to be abused by those who do not know your benevolence; anṟu – that time (when the celestial entities were expectantly looking for you); nī kidandavāṟu – the way you were reclining with the mantara mountain gyrating on your back; tēṟa – to make it known; kūṟu – you should mercifully speak

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who sired brahmā, rudra et al and who incarnated in the species of tortoise, living happily in the ocean filled with weapons such as snares etc! You spread out the mattress of ādiśēśan without any thought of what danger will come from someone, with celestial entities coming from their respective abode, to speak from the opposite side, praising you and to be abused by those who do not know your benevolent qualities. You should mercifully speak about the way in which you were reclining with the mantara mountain gyrating on your back, at that time, so that I will know.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “The eminence of your lying in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) such that celestial entities such as brahmā et al, who desire worldly pursuits, praise you, became the upper limit of simplicity. During the time of amrutamatanam (churning of ocean for nectar), the simplicity of becoming a tortoise to bear the mantara mountain on your back, became the boundary for eminence. You should mercifully shower your grace on me and explain these qualities of simplicity as well as this eminence, separately”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kūsam onṟūm inṟi** – this could be construed in several ways: (1) this place is full of demons and monsters who are experts in warfare, hiding themselves. Without the fear of what sort of trouble will emanate from where or from whom; (2) emperumān’s divine form is such soft that it cannot bear even the fixed glance of pirāttis (śrī, bhū and neeḷā). Without the fear of his divine form being the subject of the looks from the poisonous eyes of evil people; (3) By combining this term with the last part of the verse, viz. *kūsam onṟum inṟi kūṟu tēṟavē*, the meaning will be – \[emperumān would think that\] there is none appropriate enough to speak about our greatness. Without feeling ashamed of the absence of anyone to speak about our greatness. āren’t there people who attempt to leave emperumān saying that he is too eminent an entity for them and people who insult him seeing his simplicity and attempt to leave him?

**māsuṇam paduttu** – spreading out the mattress of tiruvanandāḻvān so that the ocean water will not irritate his gentle form.

**vēlai nīr pēsa** – Since ocean is the abode where emperumān comes to recline, it felt very happy and showed by agitating itself resoundingly.

**ninṟa dēvar** – celestial entities such as brahmā et al, who protect the people who are under them. Alternatively, the verse could be rearranged to get *vēlai nīr māsuṇam paduttu – pēsa ninṟa dēvar*: the meaning will be – celestial entities such as brahmā, Sivan et al who are spoken of by some people as being supreme entity and who are opposite to emperumān. nammāḻvār too, in his tiruvāimoḻi 4.10.4 said “*pēsa ninṟa sivan*” (Sivan is spoken of by some people as the supreme lord, and is standing by such persons’ words)

**vandu pāda** – these celestial entities came from their respective abode in the upper worlds to praise emperumān, in order to get their desires fulfilled.

**mun kidandadum** – this activity of reclining since the time of creation. When brahmā et al praise you, your greatness is seen. At the same time, your simplicity is also seen when along with the agitating noise from the ocean and the praise of brahmā et al for getting their desires of worldly benefits granted, the praising of those from śvētadvīpam (another habitable land mass within the galaxy) who desire only emperumān and nothing else.

**pāsa ninṟa nīril vāḻum āmaiyāna kēsavā** – Oh kēsavā who considered mantara mountain gyrating on your back as your benefit, when you took the form of tortoise in the ocean where varuṇan’s (deity for rain) weapons such as *pāsam* (snares) are present. Alternatively, for the term *pāsam ninṟa nīr*, the meaning could be construed as: tiruppāṟkadal which is dearer to emperumān than paramapadam. The term *kēsavā* refers to the fact that he is the creator of brahmā, Sivan et al since he protected them when they beseeced him to save them so that mantara mountain will not slip into the ocean at the time of churning.

**anṟu nī kidandavāṟu** – when the celestial entities looked up to you to protect them, you fulfilled their desire and stood at a greater level than any of them. Such an entity \[as you\] took the form of a tortoise to protect them, disregarding the abuse thrown at your followers by those who do not know your eminence when they said “Your lord became an animal like tortoise”. This quality of simplicity as revealed by you.

**kūṟu tēṟavē** – you should mercifully reveal to me, yourself, so that I will know. In your quality of simplicity when you took the form of tortoise, your eminence, far above all the others mentioned earlier, was revealed. At the same time, when you reclined in tiruppāṟkadal with brahmā et al beseeching you, your quality of simplicity was revealed. Of these two predominant qualities, you should reveal which comes to the fore, where. This should be done only by you so that I will understand, says āḻvār.

Next, we will take up the 21st pāsuram of this prabandam.
