## 21. Arangane taranga nir

*aranganē taranga nīr kalanga anṟu kunṟu sūḻmarangaḷ tēya mānilam kulunga māsuṇam sulāynerunga nī kadaindapōdu ninṟa sūrar en seydārkurangai āluganda endai kūṟu tēṟa vēṟidē*

**Пословный перевод:**

anṟu – during that time (when the ocean was churned); taranga – having waves; nīr – ocean; kunṟu – mantara mountain; sūḻ – surrounding; marangaḷ – trees; tēya – to wear out; mā – huge; nilam – earth; kulunga – to move; nerunga – closely; māsuṇam – snake vāsuki; sulāy – circling it; nī kadaindapōdu – when you carried out amruta matanam (churning for nectar); ninṟa sūrar – the dēvas and demons, who stood (helplessly); en seydār – what activity did they carry out?; āḷuganda – one who supported; endai – lord; aranganē – Oh the lord of śrī rangam!; idu – this; vēṟu – in a distinguished manner; tēṟa – \[for me\] to know

**Перевод:**

**During the time of amruta matanam, when the ocean was agitated because of the churning movement of mantara mountain, the trees surrounding the mountain wore out. The huge land mass of earth moved due to this. What activity did the celestial entities and the demonic entities indulge in, when you were churning the ocean? Oh the lord of śrī rangam, who supported the monkeys! You should mercifully reveal this to me, for me to know, in a distinguished manner.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) During the time of samudra matanam (churning of ocean), you had incarnated as tortoise \[to support the mantara mountain\]. Apart from this quality of simplicity, you had carried out several activities including churning of the ocean from both sides \[of snake vāsuki, used as a churning rope around mantara mountain), and had said that the celestial entities churned the ocean, giving them the credit for this. When looking at this, it appeared to be similar to your activity of annihilation of demon rāvaṇa, but crediting the success to the army of monkeys such that everyone celebrated this. āḻvār tells emperumān “You must reveal your partiality towards your followers so that I could understand it”. In other words, āḻvār is amazed that while the entire world is emperumān’s possession, he is showing partiality towards his followers alone.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

The word aranganē has to be added to the word *kūṟu* in the last line to get the correct meaning since it is an exclamatory word, which combines with the adjectives mentioned in the pāsuram.

**taranga nīr kalanga** – Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “*kānitas sagarēṇāyam apramēyō mahōdadi*: l *kartum arhati rāmasya jñātvā kāryam mahāmati*: ll” (this king of oceans, who is unfathomable, was severed by (śrī rāma’s ancestor) sagaran. This wise person \[referring to the king of oceans\] will know the importance of śrī rāma’s deed and would carry out accordingly), since it is a deep ocean, it is capable of pushing back those who enter it for a bath, through its powerful waves. Even such an ocean was agitated as if it were just the water under the hoof of an animal.

**anṟu** – On that day when all the celestial entities lost their wealth due to sage durvāsar’s curse and were looking up to you \[referring to emperumān\] to redeem their wealth.

**kunṟu sūḻ marangaḷ tēya** – the trees surrounding the mantara mountain started wearing out after being subjected to churning action by vāsuki (the snake).

**mānilam kulunga** – due to excessive agitation by the ocean, the land mass, *puśkarattīvu*, which was close to tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) was shaken up.

**māsuṇam sulāy nerunga** – should be rearranged as *nerunga māsuṇam sulāy* to get the meaning – encircling the mountain closely \[for churning it\], with the snake, vāsuki. The implied meaning is that since emperumān gave strength to vāsuki through his radiance, he encircled the mountain closely with vāsuki. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 1-9-91 also says that emperumān gave strength to vāsuki as in “*tējasā nāgarājānam tatāpyāyitavān hari*: l *anyēna tējasā dēvān upabrumhitavān prabhu*: ll” (hari (emperumān) nurtured the snake vāsuki with a part of his radiance. With another part of his radiance, he gave strength to the celestial entities).

**nī kadaindapōdu** – celestial entities started the act of churning, just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-84  
“*mantānam mandaram krutvā nētram krutvā ca vāsukim* l *tatō matitumārabdā maitrēya tarasāmrutam* ll” (Oh maitrēya! Subsequently, the mantara mountain was placed as the churning staff, vāsuki was used as the churning rope and celestial entities started churning milky ocean with their strength). However, the one who truly churned that ocean was only your highness, just as it has been mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 5-7-4 “*āyiram tōḷāl alai kadal kadaindān*” (emperumān churned the ocean, with agitating waves, with a thousand shoulders). When your highness churned the ocean…

**ninṟa sūrar enseydār** – the celestial and demonic entities, who had both strength and valour, were looking on helplessly \[when you were churning the ocean\]; what did they do?

**ninṟa sūrar** – celestial entities are referred to as *surars*. We can consider that the term *sūrar* as a variation of the term *surar* and say that this term refers to celestial entities. To be celebrated as “celestial entities churned nectar”, what did they do? Did they take the form of a tortoise and support the mantara mountain? śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-90 says “*uparyākrāntavān śailam bruhatrūpēṇa kēśava*: l *tatāparēṇa maitrēya yanna druśtam surāsurai*: ll”  
(Oh maitrēya! Unknown to the celestial and demonic entities, emperumān took a huge form and pressed on the mountain from the top). Did the celestial entities press on the mountain from the top so that it did not move? Did they provide strength to vāsuki through their radiance? Apart from reaping the benefit of churning nectar, is there anything which they did to be termed that they had churned the ocean? śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-141 says “*bhrugō kyātyām samutpannā śrī*: *pūrvamudadē puna*: l *dēvadānavayatnēna prasūtā’mrutamantanē* ll” (śrī dēvi initially appeared as the daughter of sage bhrugu and his wife, khyāti. Again, during the time of *amruta matanam*, she appeared from the ocean through the efforts of celestial and demonic entities). As mentioned in this, while demonic entities were together with the celestial entities during the churning process, why were they given sorrows and celestial entities were given happiness \[through the distribution of nectar\]? Also, while emperumān had churned the ocean himself, he gave the credit to celestial entities. āren’t these acts of partiality!  What is the reason for emperumān showing partiality towards celestial entities who, instead of desiring him, desired only other benefits? śrī viṣṇu urāṇam 1-9-65 says “*praṇāma pravaṇā nāta daitya sainya parājitā*: l *śaraṇam tvāmanuprāptā*: *samastā dēvatēgaṇā*: ll” (Oh lord! We, the assembly of all the celestial entities, who were won over by the demonic entities, were engaged only with bowing down to you and have taken refuge under you). As mentioned in this ślōkam, is it not because they had taken refuge under you?

**kurangaiyāḷ uganda endai** – an example is cited for the partiality described thus far. The implied meaning is that while emperumān had annihilated rāvaṇa, he had said that the army of monkeys killed him, crediting them with the victory.

**endai** – even though emperumān has the greatness of having nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam) as his servitors, instead of talking despicably about monkeys as “animals”, he had taken them too as his servitors. āḻvār is amazed with this quality of simplicity and calls him as his lord.

**aranganē kūṟu tēṟa vēṟidē** – emperumān has mercifully taken residence in śrī rangam, in this samsāram, where people do not even know that there is a *sarvēśvaran* (lord of all), in order to exhibit his quality of *āśrita pakśapātam* (partiality towards followers), expecting followers to come to him and to accept him. āḻvār tells emperumān, who is reclining like this in śrī rangam, to reveal to him so that āḻvār would understand this in a distinguished way. The implied meaning is that the quality of partiality seen in this pāsuram and in the previous pāsuram, with the vyūha mūrti of *kśīrābhdinātan* (lord of milky ocean) as well as the vibhava mūrti (incarnation) of kūrmam, śrī rāman et al, could be seen directly in periya perumāḷ who is today in śrī rangam.

Next, we will take up the 22nd pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 22. Pandum inrum

*paṇḍum inṟum mēlum āyor bālanāgi jñālamēḻuṇḍumaṇḍi ālilait tuyinṟa ādi dēvanēvaṇḍu kiṇḍu taṇ tuḻāy alangalāy! kalanda sīrppuṇḍarīga pāvai sēru mārba! būmi nādanē!*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇḍum – the time before creation; inṟum – the time of creation; mēlum – time of deluge; āy – being the controller; or – unique; bālanāgi – as a child; maṇḍi – desirously; uṇḍu – ate; ālilai – tender banyan leaf; tuyinṟa – one who reclined; ādi dēvanē – Oh one who is the causative factor!; vaṇḍu – beetles; kiṇḍu – probing; taṇ – invigorating; tuḻāy alangalāy – Oh one who dons the divine tuḷasi garland!; kalanda – being permanently together; sīr – having auspicious qualities; pāvai – pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) who has the quality of natural feminism; sērum – permanently residing; mārba – Oh one who has the divine chest!; būmi nādanē – Oh one who is the consort of śrī bhūmip pirātti!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is the controller of time, prior to creation, during creation and during deluge! Oh one who desirously ate the worlds during deluge and lay on a tender banyan leaf! Oh one who is the causative factor for the worlds! Oh one who dons the divine, invigorating tuḷasi garland in which beetles probe for honey! Oh one who has the divine chest in which pirātti, who is permanently together with auspicious qualities, who has the quality of natural feminism and who was born on a lotus flower, resides! Oh one who is the consort of śrī bhūmip pirātti! For such an entity with all these qualities, is the quality of having motherly forbearance towards your followers an eminent one!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Your highness, during the time of deluge when the cētanas (those with knowledge) were under threat, protected them all in your small stomach manifesting your quality that you are the omniscient. For your highness, is saying ‘You have affection towards your followers’ a matter of eminence?” The implied meaning is that in the previous pāsuram, the quality of āśrita pakśapātam (partiality towards followers) mentioned was a natural one and not something which is to be looked at as being great.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**paṇḍum inṟum mēlāy** – *paṇḍu* refers to the time prior to creation; *inṟum* refers to the present time of creation; *mēl* refers to the time of deluge, which will follow. cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 says “*sadēva sōmya idamagra āsīt ēkamēva advitīyam*” (Oh child! brahmam, which is also called as sat \[that which exists\] (which is now seen with names and forms for all entities) was only one (since it was without names and forms for entities) during the time of deluge; (and since it was without an identical entity) it was without a second entity). subālōpaniśat 2 says “*tama*: *parē dēva ēkībhavati*” (primordial matter, called as *tamas*, subsides in paramātmā). In line with the above-mentioned authentic texts, during the time prior to creation, when cētanas were without names and forms, when they were similar to acētana entities, in a sorrowful state, emperumān protected them by keeping them within his stomach. During the time of creation, he creates them as per their karmas (deeds in previous births) and protects them in line with that. Thus, at all times, since he controls them fully, the term *āy* is used to refer to this.

**ōr bālanāgi** – like a new born infant, without any match.

**jñālam ēḻum uṇḍu maṇḍi** – Though he protects them when danger comes in the form of deluge, by keeping them in his stomach, the underlying opinion here is that he does as if to satisfy his hunger. By controlling all the worlds in his small stomach, he carries out impossible-to-carry-out-tasks and reveals that he is the sarvēśvaran, through this amazing ability.

**ālilait tuyinṟa ādi dēvanē** – Oh one who reclined in a just then sprouted tender banyan leaf, revealing that you are causative factor for the worlds!

**vaṇḍu kiṇḍu taṇ tuḻāy alangalāy** – the rest of the pāsuram says – īsn’t he, the supreme entity who reclines alone in the deluge, with no one present to show affection for him! Oh one who wears the divine tuḷasi garland, which is invigorating and which is always fresh such that beetles will keep splitting the tuḷasi for honey, all the time! The word *kiṇḍal* refers to tearing or splitting. This verse reveals that since he wears tuḷasi, he is the sarvēśvaran.

**kalanda sīrp puṇḍarīga pāvai sēru mārba** – since pirātti is the one who confirms the svarūpam (basic nature) of emperumān, she is always with him; she has lotus as her place of birth; she has the natural feminism. Oh one who has such pirātti, who has been praised by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 as “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (I will not separate from emperumān even for a moment) on your divine chest!

**bhūmi nādanē** – Oh one who is the consort of śrī bhūmip pirātti (bhū dēvi)! Through these two qualifications, just as it has been said in puruśa sūktam 2-5 “*hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau*” (Oh supreme entity! śrī dēvi and bhūmi dēvi are your consorts), it says that emperumān has the greatness of having lakshmi, who is the presiding deity for wealth and bhūmi dēvi, as his wives.

The underlying opinion is that such a supreme entity is reclining in the ocean of deluge, without anyone to shower affection on him, lying all alone by himself.

Next, we will consider the 23rd pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 23. Vanirattor siyamay

*vāniṟattor sīyamāy vaḷainda vāḷ eyiṟṟavanūniṟattu ugirttalam aḻuttināy ulāya sīrnāl niṟatta vēda nāvar nalla yōgināl vaṇangupāl niṟak kadal kidanda paṟpanāban allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāl niṟattu – having a white complexion; or – (due to mingling with human) a unique; sīyamāy – incarnating as a lion; vaḷainda – curved; vāḷ – having radiance; eyiṟṟavan – hiraṇyan, who had the teeth; ūniṟattu – in the vital spot \[chest\]; ugirttalam – nails; aḻuttināy – Oh one who pressed; ulāya – famous throughout the world; sīr – having the greatness of being authentic; nāl niṟam – having four types of tunes such as udāttam etc; vēdam – vēdam (sacred texts); nāvar – great followers of vēdams, who keep chanting; nalla – being great; yōgināl – through the means; vaṇangu – being attained by; pāl – being white, like milk; niṟa – having complexion; kadal – in the ocean; kidanda – one who is reclining; paṟpanāban – padmanābhan; allaiyē – are you not?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who incarnated as a unique lion with a whitish complexion, pressing the demon hiraṇyan, who had curved radiant teeth, on his chest, with your nails! You are attained by great followers of vēdam which have four types of tunes such as udāttam etc and which are famous throughout the world as being authentic. Are you not the one who is reclining on the milky ocean, as padmanābhan?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) During the time of deluge, to remove the danger which befell cētanas, you had swallowed the worlds, keeping them safely in your stomach, and reclined on a tender banyan leaf, manifesting your aghatitaghatanā sāmartyam (ability to bind together entities which cannot be bound at all). Furthermore, just as it has been said in brahmapurāṇam 103-61 “narasyārdatanum krutvā simhasyārdatanum tatā” (narasimha was born, keeping the (lower) portion of his divine form as human and (top) portion of his divine form as lion), you appeared, even no one had prayed for it, with the lower portion of your divine form as human and top portion as lion, thus combining two different species into one, unable to bear the danger posed by hiraṇya kaśyap on his son prahlādan. Recalling this aghatitaghatanā sāmartyam, āḻvār says “Who can estimate this quality!”*

**vāl niṟattor sīyamāy** – as a unique lion, with a whitish complexion. The word *vāl* refers to white colour. balarāma (elder brother of kriṣṇa), who was white in colour, was referred to as *vāliyōn*. āḻvār is amazed that puruśōttaman (emperumān, the best among all souls), for the sake of his devotee \[prahlāda\] not only transformed himself to look like a lion, but changed his complexion too, from his naturally dark hue to white!

**or sīyamāy** – āḻvār calls this incarnation as *or* – unique, since, among the countless incarnations which emperumān had taken, just as it has been mentioned in puruśa sūktam uttarānuvākam “*ajāyamānō bahudā vijāyatē tasya dīrā*: *parijānanti yōnim*” (though (paramapuruśan, the supreme entity) is without a birth, he incarnates in many ways. Only the learned people will know his incarnations) as well as in bhagavat gītā 4-5 “*bahūnimē vyatītāni janmāni tava cārjuna*” (Oh arjuna! For both you and me, several *janmas* (births) have taken place), there has been none which had two different species mingling into one. Alternatively, just as this āḻvār had said in nānmugan tiruvandādi pāsuram 22 “*aḻagiyān tanē*” (emperumān is the boundary for beauty), since he is in the form of human mixed with lion, he is said to be unique in his divine form. *sīyam* – lion.

**vaḷainda vāḷ eyiṟṟavan** – hiraṇyan who had white, radiant teeth which were curved. *vāḷ* – radiance. This refers to the strength of his physical form, which is not a part of the strength of the boons which he had obtained.

**Un niṟattu ugirttalam aḻuttināy** – oh one who pressed on the vital part of hiraṇyan’s form, the chest, with your nails! The word *niṟan* would refer to a vital part, and here, specifically, it refers to the chest. By the usage of the term “*ugirttalam aḻuttināy*“, this narrates how emperumān effortlessly plucked out the life of hiraṇyan with his nails, without harming the boon which hiraṇyan had obtained that he cannot be killed with a weapon.

**ulāya sīr nālniṟatta vēda nāvar** – those who have vēdam in their mouths, vēdam being praised all over the world as the most authentic text and having four types of tunes such as *udāttam, anudāttam, svaritam* and *pracayam*. The word *niṟam* refers to form. Here, this word refers to the four tunes which are vēdam’s form. Through this verse, it is said that those who worship emperumān establish *tatva hita puruśārtam* (truth, means to attain it and the benefit after one attains it) through the pramāṇam (authentic proof) of *śabdam*, which is vēdam, instead of through other proofs such as *pratyakśam* (seen through the eyes) and *anumānam* (based on guess).

**nalla yōgināl vaṇangu** – this could be construed as referring to either distinguished devotion through which his followers worship him, since it lays importance on meditation based on affection or distinguished act of surrendering to emperumān since he is the natural means for attaining him. The word *yōgu* is a shortened form of *yōgam* which refers to means.

**pāl niṟak kadal kidanda paṟpanābhan allaiyē** – āren’t you padmanābhan, reclining on tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) whose white colour adds beauty to the bluish divine form of yours, with the divine navel on your form indicating to the world “He is the causative factor for the world”? The vyākyātā (commentator) of this prabandam, periya vāccān piḷḷai, now explains the previous term “*nalla yōgināl vaṇangu*“.  
Usage of the term “*nalla yōgināl*” distinguishes those who follow vēdam fully over *māyāvādis* (those who say that everything in the world is an illusion; there is only one entity which is jīvan (sentient soul) whose knowledge is covered by ignorance and when that layer of ignorance is removed, he attains liberation) for whom knowing the meaning of the *mahāvākyams*  (three most important terms in vēdam) such as “*tatvamasi śvētakētō*” is the means leading to liberation, and over *bhēdābhēdavādis* (those who see the world as a mixture of oneness and manifold) who say that the union of karma (deeds) and jñāna (knowledge) is the means to liberation. These people, who misinterpret the meanings of vēdams, are referred to as *kudruśtis*.

The vyākyātā now explains the term “*ulāya sīr*” as seen in the verse “*ulāya sīr nāl niṟatta vēda nāvar*“. In the explanation above for this verse, the term *ulāya sīr* was considered as an adjective for vēdam. If it is considered as an adjective for *vēda nāvar* (those who recite vēdams), the meaning will change to – those who have the greatness of seeing the truth as it exists, in vēdams, which are the most authentic texts in the world. Alternatively, the phrase *ulāya sīr* could be combined with the last word *paṟpanābhan*. In this case, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 21-20 when sītāppirātti tells rāvaṇa “*viditas sa hi darmajñas śaraṇāgata vatsala*: l *tēna maitrī bhavatu tē yadi jīvitum iccasi* ll” ((Oh rāvaṇa!) śrī rāma is famous for knowing *darmam* (righteous path) and for having motherly forbearance towards those who take refuge under him. If you have the desire to live, let friendship develop with him), it refers to emperumān, with his auspicious qualities, being the causative factor for the world.

We will take up the 24th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 24. Gangai nir payanda

*gangai nīr payanda pāda pangayattu em aṇṇalēangai āḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndināysingamāya dēva dēva tēnulāvu menmalarmangai manni vāḻu mārba vāḻi mēni māyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

gangai nīr – the waters of river gangai; payanda – one who created; pāda pangayattu – having enjoyable divine feet which are like lotus; em aṇṇalē – Oh our lord!; am – beautiful; kai – in the divine hands; āḻi – divine disc; śangu – śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch); taṇḍu – kaumōdaki, the divine mace; villum – along with śrī śarngam (divine bow); vāḷum – and nāndakam (divine sword); ēndināy – Oh one who held!; singamāya – incarnating in the form of narasimha (human and lion); dēva dēva – Oh the lord of nityasūris!; tēn – honey; ulāvu – dripping from; mel – being soft; mangai – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); manni – without leaving even for a moment; vāḻum – residing radiantly; mārba – Oh one who has divine chest!; āḻi – like the invigorating ocean; mēni – having divine form; māyanē – Oh one with amazing activities!

**Перевод:**

**Oh our lord who created the waters of river gangai through your enjoyable, divine feet which are like lotus flowers! Oh one who holds the divine weapons of disc, conch, mace, bow and sword in your divine hands! Oh the lord of nityasūris, and who incarnated as narasimha! Oh one who has a divine chest in which periya pirāttiyār, who incarnated on a soft lotus flower from which honey is dripping, resides radiantly, without leaving for a moment! Oh one who is having a divine form which is like the invigorating ocean, and who is with amazing activities!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār once again immerses himself in the greatness of narasimha avatāram. He says to emperumān “Among the pious entities which purify others, you are the greatest; you are an unparalleled, dear entity; you have the divine weapons which protect your followers, who enjoy you. Such an entity like you transformed yourself in order to protect your devotee, prahlādāḻvān by changing your aprākruta (not made of primordial matter), divine, auspicious form into one with a mixture of human and lion forms. Could anyone, other than nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam) and pirātti know about this?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**gangai nīr payanda pāda pangayattu em aṇṇalē** – rudra plucked the head of his father, brahmā and committed a cruel sin. emperumān created the river gangai so that its sacred water would cleanse rudra of his sin and make him Siva \[the word *rudra* refers to one who cries and the word *Siva* refers to one who is auspicious\]. Oh my svāmy (lord) who is dear to those who are pure! śrī pāncarātram ślōka says “*ahamasmyaparādānām ālaya*:” (I am the reservoir for all the offences). The opinion is that emperumān has divine feet which purify samsāris who are full of deficiencies and he is dear to nityasūris who do not have any contact with samsāram at all and to muktātmās (who were in samsāram earlier, but who have got liberated and have attained śrī vaikuṇṭam). Why did āḻvār call emperumān as *aṇṇalē*? This is because emperumān revealed this truth to āḻvār on his own, with āḻvār not having carried out any act on his own, to know it.

**angai āḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndināy** – Oh one who holds the divine, beautiful five weapons in order to remove the hurdles for those who attain your such divine feet! *am kai* – beautiful hand. Since the divine hand is, by itself, a decoration for emperumān, holding the divine weapons in those beautiful hands is dear to āḻvār, just like emperumān’s divine feet.

**singamāya dēva dēva** – even though emperumān had those divine weapons, hiraṇya had got such boons from brahmā that he would not meet his end through those weapons. Hence, emperumān took the form of a lion which has nails as weapon and through which he annihilated hiraṇyan. Through this act of partiality towards his followers, he enslaved nityasūris. Only nityasūris, who know the beauty of emperumān’s divine, auspicious form, will know the extent of partiality towards his followers when he transforms himself to protect them. Hence, the word *dēva dēva* will be applicable only to nityasūris and not to celestial entities such as indra et al.

**then ulāvum men malar mangai manni vāḻu mārba** – periya pirātti incarnated on a lotus which had just blossomed so that it is full of honey. It is also soft. pirātti, being in the age of mangai \[young girl, around the age of 14\], is dear to emperumān. Oh one who has the divine chest in which such pirāttiyār resides permanently! Here, this does not refer to the generally known position of pirātti not leaving emperumān even for a moment, just as it has been said by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēṇ iṟaiyumenu*” (I will not leave this divine chest even for a moment). Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 30-40 “*tam druśtvā śatru hantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham* l *babhūva hruśtvā vaidēhī bhartāram pariśasvajē* ll” (Looking at her husband, who had annihilated the enemies and who made the sages joyous, vaidēhi became happy. She embraced her husband tightly), sītāp pirātti lost out to the valour of her husband śrī rāma and embraced him. Similarly, this narrative describes as to how periya pirāttiyār lost out to the valour of emperumān. This narration thus says as to how periya pirāttiyār, who is closer to emperumān than nityasūris, became enslaved by emperumān due to his quality of partiality towards his followers such as prahlādan. īsn’t emperumān ahead of even pirāttiyār when it comes to partiality towards followers, as brought out by periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 4-9-2 “*tannadiyār tiṟattagattut tāmariyāḷāgilum sidaguraikkumēl, ennadiyār adu seyyār seydārēl nanṟu seydār enbar pōlum*” (emperumān tells pirāttiyār when she complains to him about the bad deed of vibhīśaṇāḻvān “My follower will not carry out any harmful action towards you; even if he carries out, it will be only for your good”)

**āḻi mēni māyanē** – Oh one who is with amazing activities and who is like an ocean in removing the ennui of beholders! This implies that the divine form of emperumān \[as ṇarasimha\] which appeared highly threatening to hiraṇyan such that he could not win over him, appeared like an invigorating form to emperumān’s followers such as prahlādan.

We will take up the 25th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 25. Varattinil sirattai

*varattinil sirattai mikka vāḷeyiṟṟu maṟṟavanurattinil karattai vaittu ugirttalattai ūnṟināyiratti nī idenna poy iranda maṇ vayiṟṟuḷēkaratti un karuttai yāvar kāṇa vallar kaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇanē – Oh kriṣṇa!; varattinil – in the boons granted by brahmā; sirattai mikka – having lot of faith; vāḷ – like a knife; eyiṟṟu – one who has canine teeth; maṟṟavan – hiraṇyan, the enemy of your follower (prahlādan); urattinil – on the chest; karattai­ – divine hands; vaittu – kept; ugir talattai – the region of your nails; ūnṟināy – you firmly anchored and killed; nī – you, who are omnipotent and without unfulfilled desires; iratti – begged mahābali for earth; idu – this act of seeking earth as alms from mahābali; enna poy – what an amazing lie!; iranda – taking as alms from mahābali; maṇ – earth; vayiṟṟuḷē – in the divine stomach; karatti – you hid and protected; un karuttai – your thoughts; yāvar kāṇa vallar – who is capable of seeing?

**Перевод:**

**Oh kriṣṇa! hiraṇyan, the enemy of your follower, with his canine teeth like knives, had lot of faith in the boons granted by brahmā. You placed your divine hands on the chest of that hiraṇyan, firmly anchored your nails and killed him. You, who are omnipotent and without unfulfilled desires, begged mahābali for earth. What an amazing lie is this act of yours, seeking alms from mahābali! You hid the earth, which you took as alms from him, in your stomach, and protected. Who is capable of knowing your thoughts?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) prahlādan was a follower of emperumān, who did not desire anything other than emperumān himself. āḻvār once again refers to annihilation of prahlādan’s enemy, in this pāsuram. Also, he speaks about how emperumān made himself as a mendicant, for the sake of indra, who was inferior to prahlādan, since he was desiring other benefits, as well as about how emperumān protected samsāris, who are inferior to even that indra, by keeping them in his stomach during the deluge. āḻvār wonders as to who will know this quality of emperumān by which he helps everyone in times of danger.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**varattinil sirattai mikka** – one who kept lot of faith in the boons granted to him by brahmā. hiraṇyan fought with the creator of brahmā, while keeping faith on the boons granted by brahmā. ḍidn’t hiraṇyan ask his son, prahlādan, just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-17-21 “*kōyam viṣṇu*: *sudurbhuddē yam pravīśi puna*: *puna*: l  
*jagatām īśvarasyēha purata*: *prasabham mama* ll” (Oh fool! I am īśvaran (one who controls) for all the world. In my presence, you keep talking again and again about viṣṇu. Who is he?) He firmly believed the boons which he had sought, not realising that you are capable of taking a form negating all the boons that he had obtained.

**vāḷeyiṟṟu** – one who had his teeth, like sword. Here, reference to the strength of his teeth is symbolic of the strength of his shoulders, thus referring to his bodily strength. He did not realise that just as emperumān had taken a form transcending his boons, he, who is omnipotent, was also capable of destroying his bodily strength.

**maṟṟavan** – one who is the enemy. While both prahlādan and hiraṇyan had the same relationship with emperumān, the reason for referring to hiraṇyan as the enemy is because he is the enemy of his follower, prahlādan.

**urattinil** – on the chest. Just as those who eat meat would dig out and discard decayed part of meat, emperumān dug out the chest of hiraṇyan who had a terrible ego.

**karattai vaittu** – your hands were desired by akrūra, just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-28 “*apyēśa pruśtē mama hasta padmam kariśyati śrīmad anantamūrti*: l *yasyānguḷi sparśa hatākilāgai*: *avāpyatē siddirapāstatōśā* ll” (All the sins in people are removed through contact with the divine fingers of the divine hands of emperumān and they attain mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). Will kriṣṇa, whose form is without an end, keep his divine lotus-like hands on my back and shower his mercy on me?). nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi 10-3-5 says “*adicciyōm talaimisai nī aṇiyāy”* (You should keep (your lotus-like divine hand) on our heads, your servitors). Thus, you kept your divine hand, which is the means for uplifting your followers, and which is sweet to them as well, on the firm chest of one who had evil nature.

**ugirttalattai ūnṟināy** – transcending his boon of not being killed by any weapon, you annihilated him effortlessly with the region of your nails, which are the means to kill him. The word *talam* refers to both region and petal. Here, it means region.

**iratti nī** – unlike hiraṇyan, mahābali, who came in his clan, had the quality of magnanimity. Hence, instead of destroying him, you made yourself as a mendicant and carried out your work.

**iratti nī** – being omnipotent and having all your desires fulfilled, you begged with the hand which is used to giving only, as has been praised by tirumangai āḻvār in his tirunedundāṇḍakam 6 as *“alamburinda nedum tadakkai*” (long and huge divine hand that keeps giving). For hiraṇyan, you changed your divine form. For the sake of indaran, you changed your unique nature of magnanimity.

**idenna poy** – your seeking alms will be termed as true only if you sought something which did not belong to you. Only if mahābali gave something which belonged to him as alms, will his magnanimity be termed as true. Since you sought as alms something which belonged to you, your seeking is not true. This is the implied meaning here. Instead of saying *idu poy* (this is false), saying *idenna poy* (what a falsity is this) implies that this cannot be termed as true or false. In other words, while narrating the deed of trivikrama which happened the moment vāmana was granted what he had sought, vēdam says as in yajur – aśta 2-8-16 “*tṟiṇi padā vicakramē”* (trivikrama occupied (the world) with his three steps). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-30 says “*yatrāmbu vinyasya balir manōjjñān avāpa bhōgān vasudātalasta*: l *tatāmaratvam tridarādipatyam manvantaram pūrṇamapētaśatru*: ll” (With which divine hand, mahābali gave the *dāna jalam* (water as symbolic of giving alms) and attained all the pleasures in this word itself, and in a similar manner attained the status of being a celestial entity, the status of being dēvēndran (lord of celestial entities) and *pūrṇamanvantra kālam* (long, complete longevity) after getting rid of his enemies …), confirming that mahābali attained the position of indra as the benefit for this magnanimity. Thus, looking at pramāṇams (authentic texts) this cannot be termed as false. Since vāmanan sought his own worlds, treating them as mahābali’s, and since mahābali treated emperumān’s possessions as his own and donated them, this cannot be termed as true too. Hence, āḻvār says *idenna poy* – what sort of falsity is this!

**iranda maṇ vayiṟṟuḷē karatti** – Since it was your possession which you had painstakingly obtained as alms, when it encountered the danger of deluge, you hid it in your divine stomach and protected it.

**un karuttai yāvar kāṇa vallar** – who can understand your divine mind? There was a huge difference among prahlādan, who considered you alone as the benefit, indra, who considered other worldly pursuits as the benefit and samsāris who did not even bother about you. However not considering the difference, you had protected them all, since they were all your possessions. Who could know this thought of yours?

**kaṇṇanē** – you had come down from paramapadam to tirvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) as kaṇṇan, incarnating amidst herd-people who did not know the difference between left hand and right hand. You made them incapable of living without you. You rendered yourself to be tied down and beaten up by them. You have to give the answer to this doubt yourself.

Next, we will take up the 26th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 26. Aninodu pennumagi

*āṇinōdu peṇṇūmāgi allavōdu nallavāyūṇodōsai ūṟumāgi onṟalāda māyaiyāypūṇi pēṇum āyanāgip poyyinōdu meyyumāykkāṇi pēṇu māṇiyāyk karandu senṟa dēvanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āṇinōdu peṇṇumāgi – being the dwelling soul for both men and women, controlling them (in order to increase the populace); allavōdu – being the neuter gender too; nallavāy – being the controller for those who are beneficial, among them; ūṇ – the sense of taste; ōsai – the sense of sound; ūṟumāgi – the sense of touch, being the controller of all these; onṟalāda – evolving, in this manner, in all entities; māyaiyāy – being the controller for primordial matter; pūṇi – cows; pēṇum – one who supports; āyanāgi – being the covherd boy; poyyinōdu – being unfavourable towards duryōdana et al; meyyumāy – being favourable towards the pāṇḍavas; kāṇi – earth; pēṇum – one who supports; karandu senṟa – Oh one who hid himself!; kaḷvanē – Oh the deceitful one!

**Перевод:**

**You are the indvelling soul for men, women as well as neuter genders, controlling them. You are the five senses among all entities, evolving in them all. You are the controller of primordial matter. You protect cows, being born in the clan of covherd people. You are truthful towards pāṇḍavas and untruthful towards duryōdana et al. You protect the earth, as a learner, vāmana. Oh the deceitful one, who hid himself, in this process.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “You control all the entities. With the divine form of yours which is enjoyed by nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam), for the sake of your followers, you incarnated in this world as vāmana, one among the celestial entities, as well as kriṣṇa, one among the herd-people. Who can comprehend such amazing activities?” Since there is commonality between vāmanāvatāram and kriṣṇāvatāram with regard to beauty and fitting well with everyone, without any difference, āḻvār speaks about both the incarnations together, in this pāsuram.*

**āṇinōdu peṇṇūmāgi** – you remain as the indvelling soul for men and women, controlling and conducting them towards each other so that the world will increase in its population, through their union.

**allavōdu** – you are the indvelling soul of neuter gender too, who cannot support this increase in population, they being neither men nor women.

**nallavāy** – conducting the distinguished among men, women and neuter. The distinction for these people is to be engaged with one of the four ultimate benefits, namely righteousness, material, worldly pursuits and liberation from samsāram. Conducting them is remaining as the means for their attaining their ultimate goal, just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 10-10 “*tēśām sattayuktānām bhajatām prītipūrvakam* l *dadāmi buddi yōgam tam yēna mām upayānti tē* ll” (I affectionately shower intelligence to those who desire to be with me all the time and who worship me such that they can use it as a path to attain me).

**ūṇodu ōsai ūṟumāgi** – for those who are not interested in any of the ultimate benefits, he remains as the controller of the five senses such as taste, sound, touch etc which are enjoyed by them. Reference to only three of the five senses is symbolic for the other two too. This is applicable mostly to animals and those who are similar to animals for whom there is no engagement with the ultimate benefits of righteousness, material etc.

**onṟalāda māyaiyāy** – among these five sensory perceptions, apart from being the cause for their functioning, emperumān is also the controller of primordial matter which evolves into various entities in samsāram, without distinction between what has been said and not said in śāstrams (authentic texts). śvētāsvatara upaniśat 4-10 says “*māyāntu prakrutim vidyān māyinantu mahēśvaram*” (Since prakruti (primordial matter) is the cause for amazing creation, it is called as *māyai*; one who impels it to act is *mahēśvaran*, the supreme entity). Primordial matter is referred to as māyai in upaniśat, since it evolves into countless entities this way. The word *māyai* also refers to amazement. Thus, instead of controlling only a few entities, as mentioned earlier, emperumān conducts all entities appropriate to their karma (past deeds). Hence, he is said to have amazing characteristics.

**pūṇi pēṇum āyanāgi** – you conduct everything through your *sankalpam* (thought or vow). In order to protect your followers, in this world, you transform your *aprākruta* (not made of primordial matter) divine form into the divine form of a covherd, just as you did in kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation as kriṣṇa). Instead of protecting yourself, you were protecting the cows. *pūṇi* refers to cows.

**poyyinōdu meyyumāy** – when you incarnated and protected, you were not true to people like duryōdana et al, who did not consider you as their refuge while you were true to people like pāṇḍavas who took refuge under you. ḍidn’t nammāḻvār too say in tiruvāimoḻi 9-10-7 “*meyyanāgum virumbit toḻuvārkkellām poyyanāgum puṟamē toḻuvārkkellām*” (he will fulfil the desires of those who take refuge under him, and shine, manifesting his ultimate form of being the goal; he will grant the wishes of those who pray for other benefits from him, without desiring him, and keep himself away from them)!

**kāṇi pēṇum māṇiyāy** – when the earth was taken possession of by mahābali, you protected it so that it did not suffer, while you did not bother about your own form.

**karandu senṟa kaḷvanē** – oh the deceitful one, who hid yourself! Hiding himself refers to his taking a form by which none would think that he is the *sarvēśvaran* (lord of all) and going to mahābali, accepting that it belonged to mahābali only and asking for it, as alms. Being deceitful refers to his ensuring that the words of mahābali’s guru (mentor) did not enter mahābali, through the beauty of his form and speech, bringing mahābali under his spell, showing his small foot to mahābali for taking three steps with that foot, and once mahābali granted him, growing into a huge form to measure with his large foot. The last line *karandu senṟa kaḷvanē* should be added to the last line of the previous pāsuram, *ninnai yāvar kāṇa vallar*, to get the meaning “who can know you, when you hide yourself, in a deceitful way?”

We will next take up the 27th pāsuram of this prbandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 27. Vin kadanda sodiyay

*viṇ kadanda sōdiyāy viḷangu jñāna mūrtiyāypaṇ kadanda tēsamēvu pāva nāsa nādanēeṇ kadanda yōginōdu irandu senṟu māṇiyāymaṇ kadanda vaṇṇa ninnai yār madikka vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇ – mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) which is huge; kadanda – going beyond that; sōdiyāy – having a basic nature which is enlightening to itself; viḷangu jñānam – jīvan (soul) who has knowledge which enlightens itself as the form; mūrtiyāy – having as form; paṇ – vēdam which has tune as its predominant character; kadanda – going beyond that; tēsa – with radiance; mēvu – appearing effulgently; pāva nāsa – being the opposite of lowliness; nādanē – Oh lord of all!; eṇ – count; yōginōdu – along with the mingling of auspicious qualities; irandu – taking alms; senṟu – going (to mahābali); māṇiyāy – as a brāhamaṇa student, vāmana; maṇ – earth; kadanda – measured; vaṇṇam – having the quality; ninnai – you; madikka vallar Ar – who is capable of estimating?

**Перевод:**

**You have enlightening yourself as your basic nature, going beyond the huge, primordial matter. You have jīvan, who has self-enlightening knowledge, as your form. Having a radiance which appears effulgent, you are beyond vēdam, which has tune as its predominant character. Oh lord of all! You, having countless, auspicious qualities, went to mahābali, as a brāhmaṇa student, and measured this earth, after taking it as alms. Who is capable of estimating you, having such qualities?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Your eminence is far higher than the cētanācētana (with and without knowledge) entities. Even if one were to know that, who could know the eminence of your divine form and auspicious qualities with which you measured this earth, impelled by your quality of simplicity?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**viṇ kadanda sōdiyāy** – you have mūlaprakruti (primordial matter), which has permeated all the places below paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), within yourself, as your *svarūpam* (basic nature); \[Despite having this mūlaprukruti, which is an acētana entity, lacking in knowledge\] emperumān is a *svayam prakāśa vastu* (entity which enlightens itself). The term *viṇ* refers to mūlaprakruti, just as it has been referred to by nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 1.1.8 “*surar aṟivaru nilai viṇ*” (celestial entities will not know about prakruti which is the causative factor for ākāśam, ethereal layer). The reason for āḻvār saying like this is due to the fact that bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat says in 5-8-6 “*ākāśa Eva tat ōtanca prōtanca*” (in mūlaprakruti, which is called as ākāśam, all the entities are interwoven in both warp (longitudinal) and weft (lateral) directions), in which the word *ākāśa* refers to mūlaprakruti \[ākāśa refers to *viṇ* in tamiḻ\]. This confirms that emperumān pervades mūlaprakruti, which is a huge entity.

**viḷangu jñāna mūrtiyāy** – emperumān has as his *śarīram* (physical form) the jīvātma tatvam (living entity) which enlightens itself \[just like paramātmā, the supreme entity\] and which has knowledge as its *svarūpam*. This confirms that he is even more subtle than the jīvātma entity.

Thus, with these two verses, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 9-1 “*aṇōraṇīyān mahatō mahīyān ātma guhāyām nihitō’sya jantō*: l *tamakratum paśyati vītaśōkō dātu*: *prasādān mahimānamīśam* ll” (Smaller than the minutest, larger than the largest, paramātmā resides in the heart of this jīvan, which is like a cave. paramātmā is without any bonding with karma and is glory personified. jīvan is able to see that paramātmā with the mercy of paramātmā and is rid of his sorrows), emperumān is shown as being larger than the largest entity and smaller than the smallest.

**paṇ kadanda tēsa mēvu pāva nāsa nādanē** – Oh one who has radiance which cannot be estimated by vēdams as your *svarūpam*, who is the opposite of all that is lowly and who annihilates the sins of sāmsāri jīvans who attain you! Since vēdam has *svarams* (tunes) such as *udāttam, anudāttam* etc, the word *paṇ* which denotes *svaram*, refers to vēdam in this context. Though emperumān permeates both the cētana and acētana entities, as brought out in the first two verses of the pāsuram, their deficiencies will not touch him in any way. Also, he removes the deficiency in cētana entity brought about by its constant contact with acētana entity. Hence, āḻvār calls him as *pāva nāsa nādanē*.

**eṇ kadanda yōginōdu** – being together with countless auspicious qualities. *yōgu* is yōgam which means being together. Here, it refers to being together with auspicious qualities. bhagavat gītā 11-8 also calls *yōgam* as auspicious qualities: “*na tu mām śakśyasē draśtum anēnaiva svacakśuśā* l *divyam dadāmi tē cakśu*: *paśya mē yōgaiśvaram* \|\|” (You cannot see my form with your physical eyes; I shall grant you *aprākruta* (not made of primordial matter) eye. You could see the cluster of my auspicious qualities which are divine).

**irandu senṟu māṇiyāy** – going to mahābali, in the form of a student of vēdam, vāmana, seeking alms, making it appear as if his body had developed scars seeking alms.

**maṇ kadanda vaṇṇam** – the manner in which you measured earth. In other words, using the reason that “I have to get back the three kingdoms lost by indra to mahābali” as an excuse, you had kept your divine feet on the heads of people in the fourteen worlds (seven worlds below earth, earth and six worlds above the earth).

**ninnai** – you, who are distinct from the cētana and acētana entities, having eminence of your *svarūpam* which cannot be measured even by vēdams, going to mahābali after transforming yourself as a dwarf, seeing the quality of magnanimity in mahābali, impelled by your quality of simplicity.

**Ar madikka vallarē** – who is capable of measuring your qualities? Could vēdams estimate you? Could *vaidikas* (those who follow vēdams) who are eminent, know you? Could your eminence be known? Could your simplicity be known? Among your qualities of greatness, who can measure, which quality?

There is another way to get the meanings of this pāsuram, as written mercifully by the vyākyātā, periya vāccān piḷḷai.

**viṇ kadanda sōdiyāy** – superior to paramapadam, having *divyātma svarūpam* (divine, basic nature of the soul) which is self-illuminating. Here, the word *viṇ* refers to paramapadam. This verse refers to the greatness of emperumān’s svarūpam which imbibes within it, even paramapadam, which is thrice as large as leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm).

**viḷangu jñāna mūrtiyāy** – having a divine form manifesting knowledge, which is contained in the earlier mentioned *divyātma svarūpam*. The word *jñānam* mentioned in the verse is representative of the six divine qualities of *jñāna, bala, aiśvarya, vīrya, śakti* and *tējas* (knowledge, strength, opulence, valour, power and effulgence). ahirbuddnya samhitai 6-25 says “*sadā paśyanti tē dēvā*: *puruśam puśkarēkśaṇam* l *śādguṇya vigraham dēvam tādruśyā ca śriyā yutam* ll” (nityasūris constantly enjoy that paramapuruśan (supreme entity) whose divine form manifests the six auspicious qualities of *jñāna, bala, aiśvarya, vīrya, śakti* and *tējas*, who is together with śrīdēvi and who has lotus-like eyes).

**paṇ kadanda dēsa mēvu pāva nāsa nādanē** – Oh lord of all, dwelling permanently in paramapadam which cannot be estimated by vēdam which has tune as its predominant feature, and who is the opposite of lowliness, capable of removing the deficiencies in samsāri jīvātmās. Here, the word *dēsam* refers to paramapadam.

**eṇ kadanda yōginōdu** – just as it has been mentioned in śvētāśvatara upaniśat 6 “*parā’śya śaktir vividaiva śrūyatē svābhāvikī jñāna bala kriya ca*” (it is known that this paramātmā (supreme entity) has manifold, superior power, natural knowledge, power and deeds (such as creation etc)), you have amazing strengths which are manifold and which cannot be estimated.

**māṇiyāy irandu senṟu** – transforming the divine form which is beyond one’s senses and which is inestimable, to the form of a mendicant, which is limited and which is visible to mahābali’s eyes, and going to mahābali for getting alms

**maṇ kadanda vaṇṇam** – having the quality of (a) asking for three steps of land, measuring all the worlds below and above \[the earth\] through two steps, (b) showing a small foot while asking for alms and an enormous foot while measuring.

**ninnai** – having an immeasurable greatness of *svarūpam,* having a divine form which manifests the auspicious qualities in that *svarūpam*, having paramapadam, which cannot be estimated by vēdam, as the abode and coming down to earth, impelled by your amazing power, such an entity like you.

**Ar madikka vallarē** – could anyone measure even one among all such greatness?

We will next take up the 28th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 28. Padaitta par

*padaitta pār idandu aḷandu uṇḍu umiḻndu pauva nīrpadaittu adaittu adil kidandu mun kadainda peṟṟiyōymidaitta māli mālimān vilangu kālan ūr pugappadaikkalam vidutta pal padait tadakkai māyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pauva nīr – ocean which is the basis for the universe; padaittu – creating it; padaitta – thus created; pār – earth; idandu – piercing and taking it; aḷandu – measuring it (after taking it as alms from mahābali); adu – that earth; uṇḍu – keeping in your stomach during the dangerous time of deluge; umiḻndu – (later) spitting it out; pauvanīr – huge water \[ocean\]; adaittu – built a dam on it; mun – earlier; kadainda – churned; peṟṟiyōy – Oh one who has great majesty!; midaitta – one who became angry; māli – monster known as māli; māli mān vilangu – sumāli, who had the strength of an animal; these monsters; kālan – yaman’s; Ur – town; puga – to enter; padaikkalam – weapons; pal – many; padai – having many weapons; tada – huge; kai – having divine hands; māyanē – Oh one with amazing activities!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is with amazing activities! You created the ocean which is the basis for habitation. Having created the word, you dug it out during creation; you measured it; you kept it in your stomach during deluge and later, spat it out; you built a dam on the ocean created; earlier, you reclined on that ocean; you had also churned it. You made the monsters māli, who was angry, and sumāli, who had the strength of an animal, to enter the town of yama, by directing your weapons at them, through the many weapons which you have in your huge, divine hands.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār tells emperumān “With your casual sankalpam (thought; vow), you are capable of carrying out all the actions of creation, including creating ocean which is the basis for creation. In the world thus created, in order to protect your followers, you lower your eminence, undertake many incarnations and eliminate the enemies of your followers through your divine weapons. No one is capable of knowing these wondrous activities”. This pāsuram is to be combined with the verse “oruttarum ninādu tanmai innadenna vallarē” which will appear in the next pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**padaitta pāridandu** – the verse should be rearranged as “*pauva nīr padaittu pār idandu*“. As a prelude to creating the universe, you created the huge ocean, just as it has been mentioned in manu smruti 1-8 “*sō’bhidyāya śarīrāt svāt sisrukśur vividā*: *prajā*: *l* *apa Eva sasarjādau tāsu bhījam (vīryam) avāsrujat* ll” (Desiring to create many species of prajās (creations) from his divine form, that paramātmā (supreme entity) through his *sankalpam* (thought), created water first; in that water, he planted the seed (of his power)). From that, you created universe and then brahmā, the head of universe. You dug out earth, which was created by brahmā, which had got immersed in the waters of deluge, by transforming your divine form, which is enjoyed by nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam) into a wild boar, from the walls of the universe. For one who created the universe through your *sankalpam* itself, it would have been possible to take it out from the deluge through your *sankalpam* itself. However, in order to nurture faith among your followers who would think “It is emperumān who would remove us from the deluge of samsāram”, you took the form of a boar.

**aḷandu** – further, when mahābali usurped earth, you incarnated as vāmana (dwarf) and retrieved it, after measuring it fully. This activity was also to nurture faith among your followers “to get back his possession, emperumān would even come as a mendicant”

**aduṇḍumiḻndu** – during deluge, incarnating as *ālilaikkaṇṇan* (infant on a tender banyan leaf), you kept the three worlds in the small stomach, protecting them, ensuring that the worlds did not suffer while inside, and then spitting them out, at the end of deluge. While this activity too could have been carried out through *sankalpam*, it was done to make followers to have the faith that emperumān has all the amazing powers, capable of re-creating materials which had been destroyed.

**pauva nīr padaittu** – verse to be rearranged as “*pauva nīr padaittu adaittu*“. This refers to your building a dam along the ocean for the sake of sītāp pirātti. While you had created oceans through your *sankalpam*, you chose one ocean to surrender to varuṇa (lord of water), raised an army \[of monkeys and bears\] and then closed it \[by breaking the end of the dam\]. All these were carried out due to separation from your beloved \[sītāp pirātti\] and as a manifestation of your anguish.

**mun adil kidandu** – with the thought “When celestial entities such as brahmā, Sivan et al are troubled by demons such as hiraṇyan, rāvaṇan et al, we should not be at too far a distance”, as well as to enable devotees to meditate on you, you came down from paramapadam and reclined in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-12-38 says “*jyōtīmśi viṣṇur bhuvanāni viṣṇur vanāni viṣṇur girayō diśaśca* l *nadyas samudrāśca sa Eva sarvam yadasti yannāsti ca vipravarya* ll” (Oh the greatest among brāhmaṇas! All the radiances are viṣṇu only; all the worlds are viṣṇu only; all the forests, mountains and directions are viṣṇu only. Rivers and oceans are only viṣṇu. jīvans (souls) which as considered as ‘existing’ (since they are without any change) and acētana entities (those without knowledge) which are considered as ‘not existing’ (since they keep changing constantly) are also viṣṇu only). While you have pervaded all the creations, and are close-by, you have incarnated with a divine form only to manifest that you keep striving to protect your followers.

**kadainda peṟṟiyōy** – oh one who has the eminence of churning the ocean in order to retrieve all the wealth lost by the celestial entities due to sage durvāsar’s curse! The word *peṟṟi* refers to inherent nature. While there was no compulsion that you had to churn the ocean in order to retrieve the lost wealth, this churning was done only in order to manifest your quality of striving to remove the hurdles faced by your followers.

**midaitta māli** – māli, the monster, who was angry

**māli mān vilangu** – sumāli, another monster, who was very strong like an animal, but was insignificant. It could also be considered that since he had the conduct of an animal, he was considered as one.

**mān māli** – (due to strength) sumāli was considered as the great māli. Since the name *māli* has already been considered and since emperumān did not kill *māḷiyavān,* here the word *mān māli* should be taken as referring to *sumāli*.

**kālan Ur pugap padaikkalam vidutta** – emperumān had used his cakrāyudam (divine disc) in order to send māli and sumāli to yama’s town \[hell\]. Through this, it has been said that when it comes to removing the enemies of his followers, he engages in direct conflict with them, instead of killing them through his *sankalpam* and uses his weapons to annihilate them.

**pal padait tadakkai māyanē** – Oh the amazing entity who holds many divine weapons in your well rounded, large, divine hands so that you could destroy the enemies of your followers at later points of time!

We will next consider the 29th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 29. Parattilum parattaiyadi

*parattilum parattaiyādi pauvanīr aṇaikkidanduurattilum orutti tannai vaittu ugandadanṟiyumnarattilum piṟatti nāda jñāna mūrti āyināyoruttarum ninādu tanmai innadenna vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

parattilum – more than the three classes of ātmās, which are superior to prakruti; parattaiyādi – you have distinguished svarūpam (basic nature); urattilum – on your divine chest too; orutti tannai – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) who is unique and the main consort; vaittu – making her reside permanently; ugandu – enjoying the togetherness with her; pauva nīr – water of the ocean; aṇai – on the mattress; kidandu – reclining in the form of anirudda; adanṟiyum – further; narattilum – (despicable) human species too; piṟatti – you incarnated in the forms of Sr rāma, kriṣṇa et al; nāda – Oh lord, without any reason!; jñānamūrti āyināy – Oh one who has knowledge as svarūpam (basic nature); oruttarum – none among even vēdams or those who are with knowledge, and who follow vēdams; niṇadu tanmai – your one nature of mercy; innadenna – to estimate and say that it is ‘like this’; vallarē – is capable?

**Перевод:**

**Oh lord, \[who is the lord\] without any reason! Oh one who has knowledge as basic nature! You have a distinguished svarūpam which is beyond the three classes of ātmās (baddātmās, those who are bound to samsāram, muktātmās, who were earlier in samsāram but have got liberated from it and reached śrī vaikuṇṭam and nityātmās, the permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam, who have never had any contact with samsāram). You have periya pirāttiyar, who is unique and your main consort, permanently on your divine chest, enjoying her proximity. You recline on the waters of tiruppāṟkadal, as a mattress, in the form of anirudda. You have incarnated as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al in the human species, which is despised by celestial entities. No one, among vēdams and those who follow vēdams and are knowledgeable, could estimate your quality of mercy and say that ‘You are like this’**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, it was said, just as it had been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-22-15 “manasaiva jagat sruśtim samhāranca karōti ya: l tasyāripakśakśapaṇē kiyānudyamavistara: ll” (For one who is able to create and destroy the world through his mind, what great effort needs to be taken to kill his enemies?) that emperumān is capable of creating and destroying the world through his sankalpam (thought or vow) and that such emperumān will take many avatārams (incarnations) even for easy deeds due to his mercy towards his followers. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that the reasons for his doing this are: (a) the completeness brought about by his paratvam (supreme eminence), (b) the simplicity brought about by being the consort of tiru (śrī mahālakśmi) and (c) the strong relationship \[with his followers\] which cannot easily be given up. He says that due to emperumān’s such mercy, he takes many incarnations in vyūha (emperumān’s state in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) and vibhava (his state on earth) states and that none can estimate that his mercy is “like this”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**parattilum parattai ādi** – Just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-10 “*para*: *parāṇām parama*: *paramātmātma samstita*: l *rūpavarṇādi nirdēśa viśēśaṇa vivarjita*: ll” (paramātmā (supreme entity) who is greater than the greatest, who is without anyone superior to him, does not have the distinctions which are common to *prākruta* (made from primordial matter) entities such as colour, form etc and name), emperumān is more eminent than the three types of ātmās (badda, mukta and nitya) which are, in themselves, superior to mūla prakruti which is an acētanam (without knowledge). This \[being superior to all\] is emperumān’s svarūpam.

Next, we should consider the verse *urattilum orutti tannai vaittu ugandu*. īsn’t being the consort of periya pirāttiyār, a greater eminence to emperumān’s svarūpam than the ātma svarūpam mentioned in the first part!

**urattilum** – through the usage of *um* (also), it is shown that periya pirāttiyār is together with emperumān in his svarūpam also. Since she is the *svarūpa nirūpaka bhūtai* (the entity who identifies emperumān’s basic nature), periya pirāttiyār’s svarūpam will be together in all the contexts of emperumān’s svarūpam. Further, for the other consorts of emperumān, residing permanently in his divine chest is not mentioned and is mentioned only for periya pirātti. Hence, the term *urattilum* manifests periya pirāttiyār’s exclusive distinction, which is not seen with other consorts.

**orutti** – unparalleled. Just as nammāḻvār has mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-10 “*vadiviṇai illā malar magaḷ*” (śrī mahālakśmi, born on a lotus flower, is without match in her form), among the consorts of emperumān, she is without match in her divine form and basic nature. Since she is the head of the three classes of ātmās and is the reigning consort of emperumān, she is referred to as *orutti* (unique).

**tannai vaittu** – She resides permanently in the divine chest of emperumān without leaving even for a moment due to emperumān’s sweetness, just as it has been said by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (I will not leave \[emperumān\] even for a moment). Since she is so totally engaged with him, he too cannot separate from her even for a moment and hence keeps her in his chest always.

**ugandu** – just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-5 “*tulya śeelā vayōvruttām tulyābhijanalakśaṇām* l *rāghavō’rhati vaidēhīm tam cēyam asitēkśaṇā* ll”  
(śrī rāma is well suited to sītāp pirātti with her well-matched character, age and conduct; with well-matched lineage and characteristics, this black-eyed sītā is also suited to him), you have enjoyed togetherness with periya pirātiyār who has well-matched auspicious qualities, age and conduct.

**pauva nīr aṇaik kidandu** – protecting cētanas, who are to be protected, happens as an outlet for the happiness which results from affection towards periya pirāttiyār. Thus, you have incarnated as anirudda and reclined on tiruppāṟkadal, using the water of the ocean as your mattress. Just as viṣṇu darmam 41-30 says “*andakārē’titīvrē ca narasimham anusmarēt* l *taratyakhila du*:*kkhāni tāpārtīō jalaśāyinam* ll” (In pitch darkness, let the cētana think of narasimha perumāḷ. When suffering from sorrows, let him think of the supreme entity who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal; (by thinking this way), he crosses all hurdles), this talks about the great quality of reclining in tiruppāṟkadal, even when had desired it, to be the refuge for those who are suffering from sorrows. Reference to anirudda here is representative of śankarśaṇa and pradyumna too, who are the other two incarnations in vyūham (tiruppāṟkadal).

**adanṟiyum** – when cit (with knowledge) entities were lying together with acit (without knowledge) entities during deluge, without any knowledge, you had given them forms and names, when creation started. Through your mercy, you gave them śāstras (sacred texts which tell them what they should and what they should not do). When brahmā, Siva et al were troubled by monsters and demons, you removed their troubles. Thus, not being satisfied with protecting them through your three vyūha forms, going beyond….

**naṟattilum piṟatti** – cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says of emperumān “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of the good fragrances and all the good tastes). You, having such an *aprākruta* divine form, incarnated in the form of a human being, a species detested by brahma et al, as one among them, with the divine forms of śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al. Reference to human species is representative of celestial, animal and plant species too. kūrattāḻvān \[one of the prominent disciples of śrī rāmānuja\] mercifully says in his varadarāja stavam 17 that emperumān has incarnated in many species: “*iyam vai vaiyūhī stitrata kilēccāvihrutayē* *vibhūtīnām  madyē suranaratiraścāmavataran* l *sajātīyas tēśāmiti tu vibhavākyāmapi bhajan* *karīśa*! *tvam pūrṇō varaguṇagaṇais tān stagayasi* ll”  
(Oh varadarāja! The regulation of your auspicious qualities is apt only for your vyūha state. Beyond that, when you incarnate as celestial, human, animal species, in order to play just as you like, with a name of vibhavāvatāram (incarnating as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al), even though you are complete with your auspicious qualities, you hide those qualities since you want to be one among the respective celestial, human or animal species).

**nāda** – Oh one who has all the entities with you! The implied meaning is that you incarnate in various species since the owner cannot give up on his possessions.

**jñāna mūrtiyāyināy** – you have jñānam as your svarūpam. This speaks about the abundance of knowledge in him, in order to know the path for protecting cētanas, mentioned earlier. Even if one were the owner, if he does not know the way to protect his possessions, could he protect them? Is it so difficult to know the path to protect the cētanas? Since the tastes of the cētanas are varied, in order to correct them, emperumān has to deploy several methods such as giving them śāstram, instructing them through an ācāryan (guru, preceptor), manifesting his divine beauty, manifesting his auspicious qualities etc. These could be carried out only by emperumān and by no one else.

**oruttarum ninādu tanmai innadu enna vallarē** – could vēdams or the knowledgeable ones who follow such vēdams, measure and say that your mercy is to this extent? The implied meaning is that it is impossible for anyone to know the extent of emperumān’s mercy when he undertakes lowly incarnations, which are incongruent with his eminence, in order to protect the samsāris, when they destroy their svarūpam of *śēśatvam* (being servile to emperumān) and sever their relationship with emperumān.

Next, we will take up the 30th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 30. Vanagamum mannagamum

*vānagamum maṇṇagamum veṟpum ēḻ kadalgaḷumpōnagam seydu ālilait tuyinṟa puṇḍarīganētēnagam sey taṇṇaṟu malarttuḻāy nan mālaiyāykūnagam pugatteṟitta koṟṟa villi allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vānagamum – svarga lōkam (heaven); maṇṇagamum – bhūmi (earth); ēḻ veṟpum – seven anchoring mountains; ēḻ kadalgaḷum – seven oceans; pōnagam seydu – swallowing them; tuyinṟa – one who reclined; puṇḍrīganē – Oh one with lotus-like eyes!; tēn – honey; sey – filled with; agam – having the internal region; taṇ – being cold \[comfortable\]; naṟu – having fragrance; malar tuḻāy – made of tuḷasi flowers; nal – good \[beautiful\]; mālaiyāy – Oh one who has divine garland!; kūn – hunchback; agam puga – to disappear inside the body; teṟitta – drawing the bow with balls made of clay; koṟṟa – victorious; villi – one who has the bow; allaiyē – aren’t you?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who has eyes like a lotus flower! You swallowed heaven, earth, the seven anchoring mountains, the seven seas and then reclined on a tender banyan leaf. Oh one who has a cool, fragrant, nice, divine garland filled with honey! āren’t you the one who drew your victorious bow and shot the clay balls on the hunchback and made it go into the body?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, the term narattilum piṟatti referred to his incarnations in human species, in general. In the pāsuram prior to that (28th pāsuram), through the verse aduṇḍu umiḻndu his incarnation as ālilaikkaṇṇan (infant on a tender banyan leaf) was spoken. Combining these two, in this pāsuram, āḻvār engages with the innocence, beauty and power of ālilaikkaṇṇan and in the playful and valorous deed of śrī rāman, who incarnated as a human.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vānagamum maṇṇagamum** – svargam (heaven) along with the celestial entities such as indra et al, the materials which they enjoy, the implements (means) to enjoy those materials and the places where they enjoy these materials; similarly, the cētana and acētana entities on bhūmi (earth). Here, the words *vānagam* (heaven) and *maṇṇagam* (earth) refer to the people who inhabit these places, just as it is mentioned in the saying “*manjā*: *krōśanti*” which literally means that the bed is crying while figuratively, it refers to the people who are lying on the bed, who are crying. Only those who are in these worlds suffer during the time of deluge. Thus, this verse says that celestial entities such as indra et al, who are vorśipped by those who are on earth and who grant them the benefits desired by the people on earth, and the people on earth who worship the celestial entities, are all uniformly protected by emperumān, without any difference between them, during the threatening time of deluge.

**veṟpum** – the seven anchoring mountains (such as mēru etc) which sustain earth. Here too, this implies that during the time of deluge, without any distinction, earth, which is sustained and the seven anchoring mountains which sustain earth, are together protected by ālilaik kaṇṇan (emperumān on tender banyan leaf) alone. The term *veṟpu* denotes all the immovable botanical entities.

**ēḻ kadalgaḷum** – The seven oceans which separate the seven *dvīpams* (land masses) such as *jambhu dvīpam, plakśa dvīpam* etc. The term *kadal* also includes all the creatures which inhabit the oceans. Thus, emperumān is the protector for entities such as indra et al who are vorśipped, people who worship them, the mountains which sustain earth, earth which is sustained, oceans and the creatures which inhabit the oceans, without any distinction.

**pōnagam seydu** – swallowing them. For a protector, the act of protection is as desired as food is desired by a person who is hungry. katōpaniśat 1-2-25 says “*yasya brahma ca kśatram cōbhē bhavata ōdana*: *mrutyur yasyōpasēcanam*” (for that brahmam for whom brāhmaṇa and kśatriya people become food and yaman (lord of righteousness) becomes the pickle); brahma sūtram 1-2-9 says “*attā carācaragrahaṇāt*” ((in the aphorism *yasya brahma* ca) one who is spoken of as eating, is paramātmā only; this is because the cētana and acētana entities, which are moving and stationary, are considered as food). Thus, these two authentic texts too spoke of these entities as being food for paramātmā.

**ālilait tuyinṟa** – reclining on a tender banyan leaf, apt for the form of infant.

**puṇḍarīganē** – Oh one who has limbs which are like lotus flower! Just as tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-1 says “*senganivāy sengamalam*” (the divine reddish fruit-like mouth is like a reddish lotus), tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-4 says *“kaṇ pādam kai kamalam*” (the divine eyes, feet and hands are like lotus flower). Thus, since emperumān’s divine mouth, divine eyes, divine feet and divine hands are like lotus flower which had just then blossomed, he is apt to be referred to as *puṇḍarīgan*. Alternatively, this could be considered as emperumān whose entire divine form is similar to a blossomed lotus flower. The implied meaning is that the happiness felt by him since he is keeping the worlds in his divine stomach and protecting them manifests itself in his divine form, in a radiant manner.

**tēnagam sey** – *tēn* could be considered as referring to beetles, in which case this will refer to beetles which are carving out the inner regions of tuḷasi. Alternatively, *tēn* could be considered as referring to honey, in which case this will refer to tuḷasi flower which is full of honey.

**taṇṇaṟu malart tuḻāy** – just then blossomed tuḷasi flower which is invigoratingly cool and is fragrant.

**nan mālaiyāy** – this could refer to the divine garland which is more beautiful than his divine chest. ōr, it could refer to ‘distinguished garland’ which tells all, that he is the one who protects everyone. Thus, until now, we have enjoyed the amazing power which is natural to emperumān, the beauty of his limbs which are like lotus flower and the beauty brought about by the decoration with tuḷasi garland.

**kūn agam pugat teṟitta** – one who shot the balls of hardened clay from his bow in order to push back the hump on the back of the body, into the body. When śrī rāma was a child, he shot the balls of clay, playfully, at the hunched back of kūni \[kaikēyi’s servant\]. This shows that a playful act, indulged in when he was young, became the reason for annihilating rāvaṇa et al in later years \[kūni stopped the coronation of śrī rāma because she wanted to take revenge for his shooting clay balls at her\]. When he is the entity to protect everyone, everything with him becomes a protecting device.

**koṟṟa villi allaiyē** – when śrī rāma held the bow, demons such as rāvāna et al, who saw his bow and his divine hand, had their stomachs churning. Such was his valour! The word *koṟṟam* refers to victory and strength. While “*vata daḷa śāyi*” (one who reclined on a tender banyan leaf) who was referred to as puṇḍarīganē \[in the earlier part of the verse\] and śrī rāma, who was referred to as *koṟṟa villi* are different incarnations, since they denote the same emperumān, they appear as one to āḻvār, with one being in the stage of child and another, in the stage of youth.  Starting from this pāsuram, until the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, for five pāsurams, the verse *māyam enna māyam* which appears at the end of the 34<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, appears as the one denoting an activity.

Next, we will take up the 31st pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
