## 31. Kalanemi kalane

*kāla nēmi kālanē kaṇakkilāda kīrtiyāyjñālam ēḻum uṇḍu paṇḍōr bālanāya paṇbanēvēlai vēva vil vaḷaitta velsinatta vīranē ninpālarāya pattar cittam mutti seyyu mūrtiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kālanēmi – for the monster called as kālanēmi; kaṇakku – a count; ilāda – without; kīrtiyāy – Oh one who has fame!; jñālam ēḻum – worlds such as earth; paṇḍu – at the end of the previous kalpa (day time of brahmā, equal to 1000 catur yuga, with each catur yuga being composed of 4.32 million years); ōr bālanāya – a unique child; paṇbanē – Oh one who has the nature!; vēlai – ocean waters; vēva – for it to boil over; vil – bow; vaḷaitta – one who drew; vel – to be victorious; sinatta – having anger; vīranē – oh warrior!; nin pālarāya – those who are with you; cittam – mind; mutti – without attachment (towards anything else); seyyum – one who carries out; mūrtiyē – Oh one who has the greatness!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who became yama for the monster, kālanēmi! Oh one who has unlimited fame! Oh one who has the nature of a unique child who swallowed all the worlds, at the end of the previous kalpa! Oh the warrior who victoriously drew the bow, in anger, such that the ocean waters boiled over \[in fear\]! Oh one who has the greatness of removing the attachment towards worldly pursuits in the minds of your devotees!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār enjoyed the protective deeds carried out by emperumān during his incarnations. In this pāsuram, he enjoys the beneficial deeds carried out by emperumān for the sake of those who attain him, during those incarnations*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kalanēmi kālanē** – Oh one who was the lord of death for the monster kālanēmi too! Through this it is implied that for those who desire liberation and attain you \[referring to emperumān\], you remove the sins which are hurdles in attaining you, just like you removed kālanēmi. Sins are the manifestation of emperumān’s anger \[when devotees do not obey śāstras\] and which, emperumān endures due to pirātti’s (śrī mahālakśmi’s) *puruśakāram* (recommendatory nature). Since āḻvār was fearful of those sins, he makes kālanēmi as a simile for them.

**kaṇakkilāda kīrtiyāy** – Oh one who has the fame of having countless auspicious qualities such as *saulabhyam* (simplicity), *sauśeelyam* (easy approacability) etc which help your devotees to attain you! nammāḻvār, in his tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-7 says “*kudandai em kōvalan kudiyadiyār*” (those who have been servitors for generations to my kaṇṇan who is reclining in tirukkudandai (present day kumbakōṇam)), confirming that the qualities such as *saulabhyam* are famous in śrī vaikuṇṭam too! emperumānār (bhagavad rāmānujar) too, in his śaraṇāgati gadyam , soon after mentioning *śrī vakuṇṭanāta* (Oh the lord of śrī vaikuṇṭam), says “*apāra kāruṇya sauśeelya vātsalya audārya aiśvarya saundarya mahōdadē*” (Oh one who is like an ocean of auspicious qualities such as mercy, approacability, forbearance, magnanimity, opulence, beauty etc), implying that these qualities are present in śrī vaikuṇṭam too.

**jñālam ēḻum uṇḍu paṇḍōr bālanāya paṇbanē** – this mentions the benefits given by emperumān to cētanas after they attain him. Oh one who reclined on the tender banyan leaf, after swallowing earth with its seven dvīpams (Islands) such as jambu, plakśa etc, at the end of the previous kalpam, having an amazing power as your unique nature! *paṇbu* – natural quality. By referring to *jñālam ēḻum*, this includes the three worlds, viz. nether, earth and upper. This implies that emperumān has the power to make the samsāri (dweller of samsāram, the materialistic realm) who has as his nature, going after worldly pursuits, to become a servitor to emperumān as his nature. emperumān has the power to transform a person such as rāvaṇa, who had said, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam tu kasyacit* l *ēśa mē sahajō dōśa*:*svabhāvō duratikrama*: ll” (Even if split into two, I will not bow down to anyone like this. This is my fault, from birth. One’s nature cannot be crossed) to a person who had said, just like lakśmaṇa did, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 53-31 “*na ca sītā tvayā hīnā na cāhamapi rāghava* l *muhūrtamapi jīvāvō jalānmatsya vivōdrutau* \|\|” (Oh rāghava! Once sītā separates from you, she will not exist; I too (when separated from you) will not have existence. (even if we exist) just like a fish which has been separated from water, we will be alive for just one muhūrtam (48 minutes))

**vēlai vēva vil vaḷaitta vel sinatta vīranē** – Oh warrior, who has the anger which will win over enemies, who drew the bow in such a way that ocean would burn to ashes just like a stack of hay! This implies that he will casually remove the hurdles of his followers which prevent them from enjoying him.

**nin pālarāya pattar cittam mutti seyyum mūrtiyē** – for those devotees who expect no benefit from you, other than you, you have the greatness of removing from their minds thoughts such as following other means or seeking other benefits, just as it has been mentioned in paramasamhitai ślōkam “*yēna yēna dātā gaccati tēna tēna saha gaccati* l *tadyatā taruṇa vatsā vatsam vatsō vā mātaram* \| *cāyā vā satvamanugaccēt tatāprakāram* ll” (Just like a cow which has a tender calf follows the calf, just as the calf follows the cow, just as a shadow follows a person, in the same way, (muktātmā) follows parama puruśan (supreme entity) wherever he goes). We have to add the sequence “How amazing is this!”. āḻvār says that emperumān makes those who think of these rare acts of emperumān, to think of only emperumān and nothing else.

Next, we will take up the 32nd pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 32. Kurakkinap padai kodu

*kurakkinap padai kodu kurai kadalin mīdu pōyarakkar angu aranga venjaram turanda ādi nīirakka maṇ koduttavaṟku irakkam onṟum inṟiyēparakka vaittu aḷandu koṇḍa paṟpa pādan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kurakku – monkeys’; inam – troop; kodu – taking it; kurai – boisterous; kadalin – ocean’s; mīdu – on top; pōy – going, after building a divine dam; arakkar – demons such as rāvaṇa et al; angu – there; aranga – to hide in fear; vem – fiery; saram – divine arrows; turanda – shot; nī – only you; irakka – to go for taking alms; maṇ – earth; koduttavaṟku – for mahābali, who gave; irakkam – a place to reside; onṟum inṟiyē – not having even a foot; parakka – (your divine foot) spread widely; paṟpa – like a lotus; pādan – one who has divine feet; allaiyē – aren’t you?

**Перевод:**

**You are the great warrior who took an army of troops of monkey on top of the boisterous ocean, after building a dam on it. You shot divine, fiery arrows on demons there such that they hid themselves in fear. āren’t you the one with divine feet which are like lotus, with which you measured the land given by mahābali by keeping them spread out in such a manner that there was no place for mahābali to reside?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “When emperumān defeats enemies of his followers and carries out their act, there are instances when he carries out a righteous war just as he did like cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāma, the son of emperor daśarata) defeating demons; there are also instances when he went to mahābali as a mendicant and won in a deceitful manner. There is no compulsion for him to do only in a particular manner in destroying the enemies of followers”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kurakkinap padikodu** – taking monkey troops as the army

**kurai kadalin mīdu pōy** – the troops of monkey were in fear, wondering “How do we cross this ocean?” since the ocean was boisterous and in a state of agitation. A dam was built on top of the ocean with stones, over which the monkeys walked across. This implies that emperumān is capable of controlling strong entities such as ocean with weak entities such as monkeys.

**arakkar angu aranga** – demons were confident that “Due to the strength of boons that we have acquired as well as the strength of our shoulders, there is none matching us”. However, after seeing this impossible-to-achieve task \[of crossing the ocean\], they were in a state of fear, not knowing as to what they should do. The word *arakka* refers to hiding; hiding out of fear. The word *arakka* has got modified as *aranga*, with the sound ‘k’ transforming to the sound ‘ng’, thus *arakka* became *aranga*.

**venjaram turanda** – one who shot arrows which were cruel and fiery, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 46-22 “*dīpta pāvaka sangkāśai*: *śitai: kāncanabhūśaṇai*: l *na tvāmiccāmyaham draśtum rāmēṇa nihatam śarai*: ll” (I do not wish to see you killed by śrī rāma whose arrows are like burning fire, sharp and decorated with gold).

**ādi nī** – you are the leader among warriors. Since śrī rāma, in this avatāram (incarnation) considered himself as a human being, till the end, the term *ādi* cannot be taken as referring to his being the causative factor. We have to consider the verse to mean “You filled up ocean which was a protective layer for lankā, destroying the protection, made the demons fear in their hearts, destroyed them with your arrows; with all these rare activities, you are the leader of the warriors”. The word *ādi* refers to leadership, here.

**irakka maṇ koduttavaṟku** – When you went to seek alms, mahābali did not say “I will not grant”; he did not pay heed to the words of his preceptor śukran who said “Do not grant”. For mahābali, who granted you earth

**irakam onṟu inṟiyē** – without leaving even a small space \[to reside\]

**parakka vaittu aḷandu koṇḍa** – you showed your small foot, asking for three steps of land with that foot. \[Once he granted that\] you spread the foot wide and measured all the worlds under his control, with two steps.

**paṟpapādan allaiyē** – you have a divine name in śrī viṣṇu sahasranāmam ’’*puśpahāsa*’’ referring to a garden of lotus flowers. With such soft feet, aren’t you the one who measured rough forests, streams, hills, valleys etc? You did not look at your soft feet; you did not look at mahābali’s magnanimity; you did not bother about indra who was desiring something else and not you; you only considered that “ḍidn’t this indra ask *me* for help?” and carried out his task. We have to add the hidden words “How amazing is this!” to get the full meaning.

Next, we will consider the 33rd pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 33. Min nirattu eyirru

*minniṟattu eyiṟṟarakkan vīḻa venjaram turandupinnavaṟkaruḷ purindu arasaḷitta peṟṟiyōynanniṟattor insolēḻai pinnai kēḷva mannusīrpponniṟatta vaṇṇanāya puṇḍarīgan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

min – like lightning; niṟattu – having radiance; eyiṟu – having teeth; arakkan – rāvaṇan; vīḻa – to perish; vem – cruel; saram – arrows; turandu – shot; pinnavaṟku – vibhīśaṇāḻvān, brother of that rāvaṇan; aruḷ purindu – showering mercy; arasu – kingdom; peṟṟiyōy – one who has the nature!; nal – appearing nice; niṟattu – having complexion; or – being unique; in – sweet; sol – having words; ēḻai – one who is desirous (towards you); pinnai – for nappinnaip pirātti; kēḷva – one who is dear!; mannu – permanent; sīr – having qualities such as magnanimity; pon – desirable like gold; niṟatta – beauty of limbs; vaṇṇanāya – beauty of entire form; puṇḍarīgan – one with lotus like eyes; allaiyē – are you not?

**Перевод:**

**The demon rāvaṇan had his teeth with the radiance of lightning. You destroyed such rāvaṇan with your cruel arrows and gave the kingdom to his younger brother, vibhīśaṇan. Oh one who has such a nature! nappinnaip pirātti was unique, having a nice complexion, sweet words and was desirous of you. Oh one who was dear to her! āren’t you the one with permanent qualities such as magnanimity etc, with beauty of limbs which are like gold and with beauty of entire form, having lotus like eyes?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār said that when it comes to protecting followers, emperumān does not have any compulsion about the path in which he would protect them. In this pāsuram, he says that when it comes to attaining emperumān, there is no rule that only those who are in a particular class could attain him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**minniṟattu eyiṟṟarakkan** – rāvaṇan who has teeth which are radiant like lightning. This implies that he has the pride of having power in his shoulders. The term *arakkan* refers to his being born in the clan \[of demons\] who use that power, only to harm others. Only the good people’s shoulder power would be a refuge for those who have noble qualities.

**vīḻa venjaram tuṟandu** – shooting cruel arrows which are fiery in order to destroy that rāvaṇan.

**pinnavaṟkaruḷ purindu arasaḷitta peṟṟiyōy** – Oh one who has the affable quality of showering your mercy on vibhīśaṇan, brother of rāvaṇan, and gave the kingdom to him! You considered only the surrendering act of vibhīśaṇan and not that he was the younger brother of rāvaṇan. vibhīśaṇan, when he came to surrender to śrī rāma, said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-16 “*sōham paruśitastēna dāsavaccāvamānita*: l *tyaktvā putrāmśca dārāmśca rāghavam śaraṇam gata*: ll” (Reviled and humiliated by him (rāvaṇa) like a slave, I, leaving my children and wife, have come to surrender to śrī rāma). He did not consider the words of sugrīva et al who said, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*vadyatām badyatāmapi*” ((let this vibhīśaṇan) be beaten up; be tied down), but showered his affection and mercy, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “*mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana* l *dōśō yadyapi tasya syāt satāmēta dagarhitam* ll” (By any means, I do not desert anyone who arrives with a friendly appearance, even if he has a defect. His acceptance is irreproachable, in the eyes of good men). Further, śrī rāma says in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33 “*sakrut Eva prapannāya tavāsmīti ca yācatē* l *abhayam sarvabhūtēbhyō dadāmyētat vratam mama* ll” (For one who surrenders to me once, with the pathos of a friend and says ‘I am your servitor’ I give the assurance of protection from all creations. This is my solemn pledge). Though vibhīśaṇan was not interested in the kingdom, merely because of the reason for which vibhīśaṇan came to him \[just as mentioned in the first ślōkam under this sub-heading\], śrī rāma gave the kingdom to vibhīśaṇan. āḻvār is amazed with this great quality. The word *peṟṟi* refers to quality.

**nanniṟator insol ēḻai pinnai kēḷva** – you are dear to nappinnaip pirātti who has a good complexion and sweet words, and who is much engaged with you. In the first of the pāsuram, emperumān’s involvement with vibhīśaṇan was mentioned. In the third line, emperumān’s involvement with nappinnaip pirātti has been mentioned. Since both have been mentioned together, just as nappinnaip pirātti’s *rūpa guṇam* (qualities of her form) and *ātma guṇam* (qualities of her ātmā) are dear to emperumān, śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān is also dear to emperumān.

**mannu sīr ponniṟattu vaṇṇanāya puṇḍarīgan allaiyē** – aren’t you the one with permanent qualities such as magnanimity, with a beautiful divine form and with lotus-like divine eyes, just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6 and 1-7 “*ya ēśō’ntarādityē hiraṇmaya*: *puruśō druśyatē hiraṇyaśmaśrur* *hiraṇya kēśa āpraṇakhāt sarva Eva suvarṇa*: l *tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvamakśiṇī* ll” (This supreme entity who is seen amidst sūrya (solar galaxy) with a beautiful, golden hued form, has a beautiful, golden moustace and golden hair; he has two divine eyes which are like lotus, blossomed by sun), as well as it has been said in manu smruti 12-122 “*praśāsitāram sarvēśāmaṇīyāmsamaṇīyasām* l *rukmābham svapnadīgamyam vidyāttu puruśam param* ll” (It should be known that parama puruśan (supreme entity) controls all entities, that he has radiance like melted gold and that he is apt to be known through knowledge similar to the (amazing) intelligence gathered in dreams). We should add the world “How amazing is this!” here. \[Normally, the words *niṟam* and *vaṇṇam* would refer to complexion\]. Here, the word *niṟam* would refer to form and the word *vaṇṇam* to complexion. The implied meaning is that emperumān is with the complexion of a material in golden form. *puṇḍarīgan* refers to the beauty of his eyes and *ponniṟatta vaṇṇan* refers to the beauty of the divine form which is the beauty of his complexion.

Through this pāsuram, it has been mentioned that just as the *rūpa* guṇam and *ātma guṇam* of emperumān, in his incarnation as sūryanārāyaṇan, residing in the midst of the sūrya (sun) galaxy are enjoyable for his consorts such as nappinnaip pirātti and others, śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān was enjoyable for emperumān.

Next, we will consider the 34th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 34. Adiyadi adi ni

*ādiyādiyādi nīyor aṇḍamādi ādalālsōdiyāda sōdi nī adu uṇmaiyil viḷangināyvēdamāgi vēḷviyāgi viṇṇinōdu maṇṇumāyādiyāgi āyanāya māyam enna māyamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ādiyādiyādi – the one who is the material, efficient and instrumental causative factor; nī – you alone, are the one; or – being uniform; aṇḍam – oval shaped universe; ādi – you become; nī – you alone; ādalāl – Since you are the causative factor for all; sōdiyāda – without the need to analyse; sōdi nī – you are the supreme effulgence; adu – like that; uṇmaiyil – in vēdam which is forever; viḷangināy – Oh one who is splendorous!; vēdamāgi – being the controller of vēdam; vēḷviyāgi – as one who is vorśipped in the rituals which are mentioned in those vēdams; viṇṇinōdu – along with paramapadam; maṇṇumāy – being the controller of leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) too; ādiyāgi – being the controller of the taste which develops in attaining \[you\]; āyanāya – incarnated as a covherd boy; māyam – this wondrous deed; enna māyam – how amazing is it!

**Перевод:**

**You alone are the material, efficient and instrumental causative factors. You alone are the uniform group of universes. Thus, being the causative factor, you are the supreme effulgence, not requiring any analysis. Oh one who is splendorous in the vēdams which exist forever! How amazing is it that you, who are controller of the vēdams, who are vorśipped in all the rituals mentioned in the vēdams, who are the controller of both the leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) and nitya vibhūti (spiritualistic realm), who control the development of taste in attaining you, incarnated in the clan of covherds!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār enjoyed śrī rāmapirān’s great quality of showering his mercy on vibhīśaṇan, without looking at his lowliness of having been born as a demon. In this pāsuram, he says “How amazing is it that you, who are controlling the causative and effective entities, the authentic texts which cultivate good knowledge, the supreme entity who is known through those authentic texts, incarnated as a covherd boy, kaṇṇan, in order to create interest among covherd people who do not have interest in any of the four ultimate benefits of righteousness, opulence, worldly pursuits and liberation!”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ādi ādi ādi nī** – Just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-1 “*sadēva sōmya idamagra āsīt ēkamēva advitīyam*” (Oh child! brahmam, which is also called as sat \[that which exists\] (which is now seen with names and forms for all entities) was only one (since it was without names and forms for entities) during the time of deluge (and since it was without an identical entity) it was without a second entity), you are the causative factor in three ways: similar to a potter (one who determines) making pots with clay (material), using rod and wheel (implements), you are the materialistic cause (similar to clay), efficient cause (similar to the potter) and implement cause (similar to the rod and wheel), for all the *prākruta* (primordial) entities. Just as śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-2-53 says “*mahadādyā viṣēśāntā hi aṇḍam utpādayanti tē*” (twenty-three entities starting with mahān (great), the five senses etc create the universe), for all the entities which are derived from *prakruti*, you, who are together with *prakruti*, are the materialistic cause, through your solemn vow, the efficient cause and through your knowledge and power, the implement cause. This has been seen in cāndōjña upaniśat 6-2-3 “*tadaikśata bahusyām prājāyēyēti* l *tattējō’srujata*” (that para brahmam (supreme entity) took a *sankalpam* (vow) – let me become manifold; let me be born; it created effulgence).

**or aṇḍamādi** – you, who are together with these entities such as mahān etc, become the collection of universes. Just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-7-27 “*aṇḍānāntu sahasrāṇām sahasrāṇyayutāni ca* I *īdruśānām tatā tatra kōti kōti śatāni ca* ll” (The essence is “In that prakruti, there are several hundred crores of universes like this”), aren’t these universes uniform? While *oraṇḍam* (one universe) refers to the universe in singular, here it means the collection of universes. The implied meaning is that you create, from a brahmā to an ant, in each of these universes, permeate them and control each of them.

**ādalāl sōdiyāda sōdi nī** – Since you are the causative factor for all the worlds, you are the splendorous entity to be meditated upon, without the need for an analysis. The implied meaning is that unlike brahma sūtram which had to contend with a counter hypothesis that the *trivida mahābhūtam* (ethereal layer, fire and wind) of fire or lord of fire or āditya (sun) or vaiśvānarāgni (or the ethereal layer or wind) is the splendorous entity, before confirming that paramātmā alone, who controls all these, is that splendorous entity, we do not have to determine anything like that. cāndōjña upaniśat 3-13-7 says “*ata yadata*: *parōdivō jyōtir dīpyatē*” (the splendorous entity who has mercifully taken residence in paramapadam). muṇḍakōpaniśat 2-2-10 says “*hiraṇmayē parēlōkē virajam brahma niśkaḷam* l *taccubhram jyōtiśām jyōtistadyad ātmavidō vidu*: ll” (There is a brahmam (supreme entity) without *rajōguṇam* (quality of passion) and without limbs in the golden hued world which is above. It is sacred. *paramātmajñānis* (sages who are knowledgeable about the supreme entity) know that most splendorous of all splendorous entities). taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 says “*nārāyaṇa param brahmatatvam nārāyaṇa*: *para*: l *nārāyana parōjyōtirātmā nārāyaṇa*: *para*: ll” (nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme brahmam; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme entity; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme effulgence; nārāyaṇan alone is the supreme soul). In line with these authentic texts, are you not the most splendorous entity, more than the brightest entity?

**aduṇmaiyil viḷangināy** – Since that splendorous entity is mentioned by vēdams, which are without any fault, you are more distinguished than all the rest.

**vēdamāgi** – It was you who took a solemn vow that vēdams will be forever and will be without any fault. In other words, even when all the rest are destroyed during deluge, the *vēdākśarams* (words from vēdam) remain in your divine thoughts and are indestructible. Just as it has been said in śvētāsvatara upaniśat 6-18 “*yō brahmāṇam vidadāti pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai* l *tam ha dēvam ātmabuddi prasādam mumukśurvai śaraṇam aham prapadyē* ll” (I, desiring to attain mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), surrender to that supreme entity, who initially created brahmā and who instructed him all the vēdams), you were one who instructed vēdams at the beginning of creation, to brahmā. Hence, you are the reason for the permanency of vēdams.

**vēḷviyāgi** – Oh one who is the controller of the first part of vēdams too, which deals with conduct of yāgams (rituals)! In other words, you are the one, vorśipped through the yāgams and you are the one, giving the benefits too, for those rituals, just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 9-24 ślōkam “*aham hi sarvayajñānām bhōktā ca prabhurēvaca* l *na tu māmabhijānanti tatvē nātaś cyavanti tē* ll” (I am the enjoyer of all the yajñams; I shower all the benefits of such yajñams; those who are firmly engaged with other deities, do not know me truly; hence, they slip away from me).

**viṇṇinōdu maṇṇumāy** – you are the controller for *mōkśam* (śrī vaikuṇṭam) which is the ultimate benefit for those who have detachment towards worldly pursuits and worship you through yāgams; you are also the controller of the *bhōgabhūmi* (earth which is to be enjoyed) which is the ultimate benefit for those who have attachment towards *svargam* (heaven) and other worldly pursuits and who worship you through yāgams. The term *viṇ* refers to paramapadam while the term *maṇ* refers to the fourteen worlds enjoyed by the jīvan (soul). Would the same yāgam be the single means for contradicting benefits of *mōkśam* and *bhōgabhūmi*? If the yāgam is conducted with the true knowledge about jīvan and paran (soul and supreme entity, respectively), without attachment towards worldly pursuits, it would lead to *mōkśam*. If it is conducted without that knowledge and with attachment towards worldly pursuits, it will lead to *bhōgabhūmi*. Hence there is no contradiction here.

**ādiyāgi** – these two benefits of *mōkśam* and *bhōgabhūmi* will accrue to one who has taste in these. Hence, emperumān acts to create that taste in the jīvan. Since he is the cause for creating the interest, the word *ādi* refers to taste, here.

**āyanāya māyam enna māyamē** – you transformed your *aprākruta* (not made of primordial matter), divine, auspicious form into the form of a covherd, kaṇṇan. You created interest in those who did not have interest in *mōkśam* and *bhōgabhūmi* \[covhered people\] through instructing bhagavat gītā etc. How amazing is your incarnation as a covherd!

Next, we will consider the 35tpāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 35. Ambulavu minumagi

*ambulāvu mīnumāgi āmaiyāgi āḻiyārtambirānumāgi mikkadu anbu mikka adanṟiyumkombarāvu nuṇ marungul āyar mādar piḷḷaiyāyembirānumāya vaṇṇam enkolō em īsanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

em īsanē – Oh one who is our natural lord!; āḻiyār – (being the indicator of lord of all) divine disc’s; tambirānumāgi – being the lord; ambu – in water; ulāvu – roaming around; mīnum āgi – incarnating as a fish too; āmaiyāgi – incarnating as a tortoise too; mikkadu – gaining greatness; anbu mikku – being highly affectionate; adanṟiyum – beyond that; kombu – like a stick; nuṇ – slender; marungul – one who had waist; āyar mādar – yaśodāp pirātti, the herd-lady’s; piḷḷaiyāy – as her son; empirānum āya vaṇṇam – the quality of being our lord; enkol – how is it!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is our natural lord! You are the lord of the divine disc. You incarnated as a fish too, roaming in water. You incarnated as a tortoise too, being affectionate and being great. Beyond that, you also became the son of yaśōdā, the herd-lady, who had a slender waist like a stick or like a snake. How did you do this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had enjoyed the greatness of emperumān incarnating as kriṣṇa, transforming his aprākruta (not made of primordial matter) divine form into the form of a covherd boy, creating taste in mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) and bhōgabhūmi (earth which offers worldly pleasures) among people who did not have any taste in these. In this pāsuram, he engages with the greatness of the quality of kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation of emperumān as kriṣṇa) once again, recalling how he brought himself under the control of a covherd lady, yaśōdāp pirātti, such that she could tie him up with a rope or release him at her will, as well as beat him up.*

**vyākhyānam (Commenary)**

The first two lines of the pāsuram should be rearranged as *āḻiyār tambirānumāgi – ambulāvu mīnumāgi āmaiyāgi – mikkadu* to get cogent meanings.

**āḻiyār tambirānumāgi** – Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 114-15 “*vyaktamēśa mahāyōgī paramātmā sanātana*: l *anādimadya nidanō mahata*: *paramo mahān* ll *tamasa*: *paramo dātā śankhacakragadādara*: ll” (This śrī rāma is paramātmā, the ancient and mahāyōgi entity. He is without a beginning and end. This eminent entity is more eminent than tamas and mahān \[these are entities derived from prakruti, the primordial matter\]. He dons the divine disc, conch and mace), he is the supreme entity, having the divine disc in his divine hand.

**ambulāvu mīnumāgi** – incarnating as a fish which dwells in water. The word *appu* refers to water. a*mbu* is a variation of *appu*. Alternatively, the samskrutam word *ambu* which means water, could be considered here. Just as it has been said in periya tirumoḻi 11-4-1 “*mudugil malaigaḷai mīdu koṇḍu varum mīnai*” (emperumān, who is in the form of a fish, bringing mountains on his back), emperumān is moving about as a fish, in his natural habitat of water, in order to remove the danger of celestial entities who were worried since they lost their habitation to the deluge. The *um* (also) in the term *mīnum* (also as fish) indicates that apart from incarnating as upēndran (vāmanan), a celestial entity, and as śrī rāma and kriṣṇa, human entities, emperumān also incarnated as a fish, an animal, manifesting his greatness.

**āmaiyāgi** – incarnating as a tortoise in order to bear the weight of mantara mountain, during churning of the milky ocean. yajur āraṇyakam 1-23 says “*sō’pām antarata*: *kūrmam bhūtam* *sarpantam tamabravīt”* (that brahmā told that eminent tortoise which was roaming in the middle of the stream). In line with this, it could be said that emperumān incarnated in the form of tortoise and told brahmā about the sequence of creation, during the time of deluge.

**mikkadu** – being great. The implied meaning is that taking incarnation in lowly species, in forms which are normally taken by those who have committed lot of sins, and which are incongruent to his eminence, adds greatness to him.

**anbu mikku** – did he not take incarnations like this only because of his affection for jīvans, who were in dire straits! It was not because of karma (past deeds) or to beget someone, that he incarnated. Did not nammāḻvār too say that he took several incarnations because of his affection for cētanas, in his tiruvāimoḻi 1-8-8 “*mīnōdu ēnamum tānānān ennil tānāya sangē*” (he himself incarnated as fish, boar etc because of his affection for me).

**anṟiyum** – more than that. āḻvār wonders “How is he transforming the divine form which is enjoyed by nityasūris into a tortoise which dwells in water!” Due to this involvement with such transformation, he said “*āmaiyāgi āḻkadal tuyinṟa ādidēva”* (in pāsuram 14) and “*pāsam ninṟa nīril vāḻum āmaiyāna kēsavā*” (in pāsuram 20). He continues that involvement in this pāsuram too. Further, he states through the second half of the pāsuram as to how the eminence of kriṣṇāvatāram, which is higher than in kūrmāvatāram (incarnation as tortoise), made him engage more with emperumān.

**kobmarāvu nuṇ marungul āyar mādar piḷḷaiyāy** – as the son of yaśodaip pirātti who had a slender waist, like a stick or like a snake. When he incarnated as fish or tortoise to protect cētanas, there was none to control him. ḍidn’t this incarnation \[as kriṣṇa\] have the eminence of his being under the control of a covherd lady, who tied him down and beat him up! parāśara bhatar (son of kūrattāḻvān, the eminent disciple of rāmānujar) says in śrī guṇaratna kōsam 28 “*svarūpam svātantryam bhagavata idam candravadanē* *tvadāślēśōtkarśāt bhavati kalu niśkarśasamayē* l *tvamāsīr māta*: *śrī*: *kamituridamintantvavibhavas* *tadantarbhāvāt tvām na prutagabhidattē śrutirapi* ll” (Oh mother mahālakśmi who has a face like the moon! During the time of establishing supremacy of the supreme entity, did not the *svarūpam* (basic nature) of bhagavān’s independence come about because of your embracing him? You are the distinguished attribute for your consort, emperumān, explaining his basic nature and basic characteristics. Since you are together with him (identifying his basic nature), vēdam too does not treat you separately). In this incarnation \[as kriṣṇa\], even that independence was destroyed. In other incarnations, you transformed yourself but did not give up your independence!

**empirānumāya vaṇṇam enkolō** – how amazing that you manifested your quality of simplicity by which you let them to tie you down and to beat you! By using the plural term in *empirān* (our benefactor) \[instead of *en pirān* which will be in singular, referring to my benefactor\], āḻvār is including the other āḻvārs who also were engaged with this quality of simplicity of emperumān.

**emmīsanē** – we understood that your transforming your divine form and divine nature is only because you considered our happiness and sorrow as your own.

We will next take up the 36th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 36. Adagatta pun mulai

*ādagatta pūṇ mulai yasōdai āycci piḷḷaiyāysādu udaittu ōr puḷḷadāvi kaḷḷadāya pēy magaḷvīda vaitta veyya kongai aiyapāl amudu seyduādagakkai mādar vāy amudam uṇḍadu enkolō*

**Пословный перевод:**

ādagatta – with lustrous gold; pūṇ – having chain; mulai – having bosom; asōdai āycci piḷḷaiyāy – as the son of herd-woman yaśōdā; sādu – the monster śakatāsuran; udaittu – punishing him with his divine foot; ōr puḷḷadu – (one which troubles children) a bird’s; āvi – having the thought; kaḷḷa tāya – deceptive mother; pēymagaḷ – the demon; vīda – (for you) to be destroyed; veyya – cruel; kongai – in the bosom; aiya – subtle; pāl – milk; amudu seydu – mercifully drinking; kai – having hands; mādar – covherd girls’; vāyamudam – nectar of their saliva; uṇḍadu – mercifully drank; en kol – how amazing!

**Перевод:**

**You incarnated as the son of herd-woman yaśōdā, who had her bosom decorated with lustrous gold chain. You punished the monster śakatan with your divine foot. You drank milk subtly, from the bosom of the demon \[pūtanā\] who came with cruel thought of killing you, just like birds, in the form of your mother. You mercifully drank the nectar-like-saliva of the covherd girls, who were wearing golden bangles on their hands. How amazing is this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had mentioned about emperumān showing pāratantryam (dependence) towards his followers. He repeats that and engages with the amazing activities of emperumān during his incarnation as kriṣṇa.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ādagatta pūṇ mulai yasōdai āycci piḷḷaiyāy** – āḻvār had said in the previous pāsuram “*kombarāvu nuṇmarungul āyar mādar piḷḷaiyāy*“, referring to the slender waist of yaśōdā. He is stating the same, with different words, here.

**ādagatta pūṇ mulai** – one who had her bosom decorated with a chain made of shining gold. Since it was the bosom from which kaṇṇan drank milk, she has decorated her bosom; āḻvār is enjoying that affection which she had towards kaṇṇan. In the previous pāsuram, he had said, in a general way, *āyar mādar piḷḷaiyāy* (son of the covherd lady). Here, he is specifically saying that he is the son of the covherd lady, yaśōdā*.* Since āḻvār is engaging with the quality of simplicity manifested by emperumān, who, being the controller of everyone, let a covherd lady, who calls him as “My son” to tie him up and to beat him, he is repeating what he had said in the previous pāsuram, enjoying the activity.

**sādudaittu** – an infant would flail its arms and legs, when it needs milk. kaṇṇan was flailing his legs for a similar reason, the leg touched the cart in which the demon śakatan had pervaded. He died due to that impact. āḷavandār, one of our preceptors, has said in his stōtra ratnam ślōkam 30 about the divine feet of emperumān: “*vilāsa vikrānta parāvarālayam namasyadārtikśapaṇē krutakśaṇam* l *danam madīyam tava pāda pankajam kadā nu sākśātkaravāṇi cakśuśā* ll” (When will I see with my eyes your divine feet, which casually measured the worlds where the eminent celestial entities and the lowly human entities live, which spend their time in removing the sorrows of those who worship them and which are like wealth for me?) nammāḻvār in his tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-6 says “*kōlamām en sennikku un kamalamanna kurai kḻalē*” (your lotus-like divine feet, having the valorous anklets, are a decoration for my head). āḻvār is amazed that emperumān is using his divine feet, which are the means for those who do not desire anything else from him, for destroying wicked people.

**ōr puḷḷadāvi kaḷḷa tāya pēy magaḷ** – the demon who was having the evil thoughts of a bird which is flying to frighten children, and who came in the guise of a mother with affection such that none would go away from her doubting if she were a ‘stranger’. The word *ōr puḷ* refers to a bird which creates evil deeds; *āvi* refers to thoughts. Just like the bird comes thinking of creating evil deeds for children, the demon came thinking of creating evil deed for kaṇṇan.

**vīda vaitta veyya kongai** – you are the one who sustains all the worlds. She came with the thought of harming you, keeping her cruel bosom in your divine mouth.

**aiyapāḷ amudu seydu** – drinking the subtle milk which was capable of pervading and destroying the subtle life of the demon. Due to his fear in thinking about that event, āḻvār states about the cruelty of the demon through the verse “*puḷḷadāvi kaḷḷa tāya pēymagaḷ*“; the cruelty of her bosom through the verse *veyya kongai* and the cruelty of the milk that she gave through the verse “*aiya pāl”*. The word *aiya* could also be considered as calling out ‘’my lord!”

**amudu seydu** – kaṇṇan drank the milk given by her, considering her to be his mother, desiring it a lot. Just as darkness gets destroyed in a place where brightness comes in, since kaṇṇan is of the nature of destroying evil people, she, who wanted to kill kaṇṇan, perished.

**ādagakkai mādar vāy amudu uṇḍhadu enkolō** – when he was like an infant, as described in the above-mentioned incident and just as periyāḻvār had said in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 1-5-8 “*unnaiyum okkalaiyil koṇḍu tamil maruvi*” (the covherd women would take kaṇṇan to their homes, keeping him on their waists) and in the same pāsuram “*unnōdu tangaḷ karuttāyina seydu varum kanniyarum magiḻa*” (taking the form of a youth, just as they desired and making them happy by uniting with them), āḻvār is amazed that emperumān enjoyed the nectar-like saliva of the covherd girls.

**ādagakkai mādar** – women whose hands were decorated with golden bangles. They decorated their hands, considering them as “These are the hands which he holds and these are the hands which embrace him”. The word “*en kolō*” seems to ask “For the poison given by pūtanā, is the nectar of their saliva, the antidote?” Sustaining everyone and not having anyone to sustain yourself, are the lives of enemies and the affection of dear ones, the sustenance? This could also be construed as: when you were a small child, you transformed yourself as youth for the sake of covherd girls; when you were a youth, you appeared as a small child for yaśōdāp pirātti. How amazing is this!

We will next take up the 37th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 37. Kaytta nil

*kāytta nīḷ viḷangani udirttu edirnda pūngurundamsāyttu mā piḷanda kaitta kaṇṇan enbarālāycci pālai uṇḍu maṇṇai uṇḍu veṇṇey uṇḍu pinpēcci pālai uṇḍu paṇḍu ōr ēnamāya vāmanā*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāytta – having sprouted completely; nīḷ – being eminent; viḷam – wood apple’s; kani – fruits; udirttu – making them scatter; edirnda – opposing; pūm – fully blossomed; kurundam – a type of tree; sāyttu – uprooted it; mā – kēśi, a monster, who had taken the form of a horse; piḷanda – tore him into two; kaittalatta – having hands; kaṇṇan enbar – the wise sages will say that he is kriṣṇa; āycci – herd-woman yaśōdā, her; pālai – milk; uṇḍu – drank; veṇṇey uṇḍu – ate butter; pēcci – pūtanā’s; pālai – milk from her bosom; uṇḍu – drank; maṇṇai – earth; uṇḍu – kept in the divine stomach; paṇḍu – at the beginning of kalpam; ōr – unique; ēnamāya – incarnated as wild boar; vāmanā – (to retrieve that earth from mahābali) oh one who incarnated as śrī vāmana!

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who incarnated as śrī vāmana! You scattered the fruits of the wood apple tree, which had sprouted throughout the tree. You uprooted the kurundam tree which had fully blossomed flowers. You tore the monster, kēśi, who had come in the form of a horse, into two. Wise sages would say that you carried out all these activities with your divine hands. You drank milk from the herd-woman yaśōdā, you ate butter, you drank milk from pūtanā’s bosom. At the end of kalpam, you kept the earth in your divine stomach and at the beginning of kalpam, you incarnated as a unique, wild boar.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār once again enjoys the activities of kriṣṇa as a child and as a youth. He also enjoys other incarnations such as ālilaikkaṇṇan (emperumān lying on a tender banyan leaf), śrī varāhap perumāḷ (wild boar) and vāmanāvatāram (vāmana, the dwarf) since they are alike kriṣṇāvatāram in terms of playful act, simplicity and connecting with everyone, without looking at the differences amongst them.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kāytta nīḷ vilangani udirttu** – a demon, kapittāsuran, had pervaded a wood apple tree which had plenty of wood apple fruits. He was waiting for kaṇṇan, thinking “kaṇṇan will come desirously to eat the fruit. I would kill him then”. Another demon, vatsāsuran, pervaded a calf and waited for kaṇṇan to come near him, to kill him. kaṇṇan grabbed vatsāsuran and threw him at kapittāsuran, killing them both.

**edirnda pūngurundam sāyttu** – even after the demon who pervaded the wood apple tree was killed, another demon pervaded a kurundam tree which had blossomed well, waiting for the time when kaṇṇan will come near to pluck the flowers. kaṇṇan uprooted the tree and destroyed the demon. In these two cases, narrative about demons who disguised themselves as friendly entities to kill kaṇṇan, was mentioned. Next, āḻvār speaks about the demon who came as an enemy and was killed.

**mā piḷanda** – kēśi, the demon, came as an enemy, in the form of a horse, opening his mouth wide, threatening to swallow the town. kaṇṇan, very casually (with his bare hands) tore him up into two pieces, removing the danger to the town.

**kaittalatta kaṇṇan enbarāl** – knowledgeable sages such as vyāsar, parāśarar et al will mention in itihāsams (epics) and purāṇams (narratives which are old but which appear new, each time it is read) that emperumān did not use his weapons for these amazing activities, but used his bare hands, casually.

**āycci pālaiyuṇḍu maṇṇaiyuṇḍu veṇṇeyuṇḍu pin pēycci pālaiyuṇḍu** – *āycci pālaiyuṇḍu veṇṇeyuṇḍu* – mercifully consuming the milk from yaśōdaip pirātti and butter. cāndōjña upaniśat 8-7-1 says *ya “ātmā apahatapāpmā vijarō vimrutyur viśōkō vijighatśō‘pipāsas satyakāmas satyasankalpas sōnvēśtavya*:” (That ātmā who is without sins, without old age, without death, without sorrow, without hunger, without thirst, without desire unfulfilled and who has solemn vow, is apt to be searched for). Unlike this when emperumān was without hunger, \[during kriṣṇāvatāram\] it appeared as if he could not sustain himself without eating the food touched by his followers.

**pēycci pālaiyuṇḍu** – consuming the milk from the bosom of pūtanā, who came to kill him after taking the form of his mother, he \[kaṇṇan\] killed her. Usage of the term *uṇḍu* (consuming) indicates that just like yaśōdā’s bosom milk, even pūtanā’s bosom milk along with her life which came with it, sustained him.

**pin maṇṇaiyuṇḍu** – as soon as āḻvār enjoyed the activities of kriṣṇa as a child, he enjoys the activity of ālilaip perumān (emperumān on the tender banyan leaf) another child, matching kriṣṇa. Did not the activity of emperumān convey that it was an innocent act of a child who did not know that if he were to swallow the entire world in his small stomach, it will not hold?

**pāndōr ēnamāya vāmanā** – During the starting of ādivarāha kalpam (the time when you incarnated as a wild boar), even when none had prayed to you, you incarnated as śrī varāhap perumān, digging out earth and protecting her. Later, when that earth was seized by mahābali, oh one who incarnated as vāmana, measured the earth and redeemed it!

Since emperumān took the forms of varāha \[a wild boar\] and vāmana \[a mendicant\], which were totally incongruent with the form of his *paratvam* (the characteristic of being supreme), these two incarnations, for their sheer simplicity, match kriṣṇāvatāram. Without any distinction between the eminent and lowly, since he connected with everyone \[through his divine foot\] when he measured the worlds as trivikrama, a quality of simplicity manifested by vāmana, this avatāram matches kriṣṇāvatāram. For these reasons, āḻvār is enjoying these incarnations together.

We will take up the 38th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 38. Kadam kalanda

*kadam kalanda vankari marupposittor poygai vāyvidam kalanda pāmbin mēl nadam payinṟa nādanēkudam kalanda kūttanāya koṇḍal vaṇṇa taṇ tuḻāyvadam kalanda mālai mārba kāḷanēmi kāḷanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadam – with the rutting water; kalanda – being together with; van – being strong; kari – \[kamsan’s\] elephant, kuvalayāpeedam, its; maruppu – tusks; osittu – broke it; or – unique; poygai vāy – in the middle of the deep point; vidam – with poison; kalanda – together with; pāmbin mēl – atop the hoods; nadam – dance; payinṟa – one who carried out; nādanē – Oh svāmy (lord)!; kudam kalanda kūttanāya – one who was dancing, carrying pots (on the head); koṇḍal vaṇṇa – Oh one who has the complexion of cloud!; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – tuḷasi’s; vadam – with garland; kalanda – together with; mālai – having vanamālai (a divine garland); mārba – one who has the divine chest!; kāla nēmi – for the monster kālanēmi; kālanē – oh one who became the yaman (lord of death)!

**Перевод:**

**Oh the lord who broke the tusks of the elephant, kuvalayāpeedam, which was strong and which was rutting, and oh one who danced on the hoods of the poisonous snake kāliyan, in the deep point of river! Oh one who danced with pots on the head and who has the complexion of cloud! Oh one who has a divine chest, together with the cool, tuḷasi garland and the divine vanamālai! Oh one who became the lord of death for the monster, kālanēmi!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Just as emperumān had said in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 4-8 “paritrāṇāya sādūnām vināśāya ca duśkrutām ldarma samstāpanārtāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē ll” (I incarnate in every yugam (epoch) in order to protect the good people, to annihilate the bad ones and to establish righteous path), āḻvār enjoys the divine activities of emperumān which he had carried out during kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation as kriṣṇa) when he had killed the unfavourable ones and made happy, the favourable ones.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kadam kalanda van kari maruppu osittu** – kamsan had made his elephant kuvalayāpeedam to get into rut such that it had the waters of the rut all over its body. kriṣṇa snapped the tusks of that elephant, which was very strong. Since several adjectives have been used for the elephant (rutting, strong) the meaning implied is that kaṇṇan casually destroyed that elephant which had strength by its nature complimented further by the strength it acquired since it was in rut. The word *kadam* refers to rutting. *kadam kalanda* refers to the elephant which had the rutting water all over its body.

**ōr poygai vāy vidam kalanda pambin mēl nadam payinṟa nādanē** – *ōr poygai vāy* – śrī viṣṇu purāṇam describes the effect of the poison emitted by the snake kāḷiyan, in ślōkam 5-7-4 “*viśāgninā prasaratā dagdātīrmahīruham* l *vātāhatāmbu vikśēpasparśa dagda vihangamam* ll” (The trees on the banks of the river \[inside which the snake kāḷiyan had taken residence) went up in flame due to the poisonous fire spreading everywhere; birds got burnt due to the moisture from the air around falling on them. (kaṇṇan saw that deep portion of the river where kāḷiyan had taken residence)). The deep portion of the river was unique because of the grave danger that it posed. Even the pond in which crocodile caught the leg of elephant gajēndran was nothing compared to this place.

**vidam kalanda pāmbin mēl nadam payinṟa nādanē** – kāḷiyan, the snake, was poisonous to such an extent that it appeared that poison was present all over his body \[instead of only the sac where it is normally present\]. You \[kaṇṇan\] stood on the hoods of that snake and danced on it like an expert in dancing, changing the positions of your divine feet on its hoods. Through that, you controlled him and drove him away from river yamunā such that everyone could resume using that river once again, without fear. Oh one who manifested your lordship through this act! Was this not mentioned in śrī viṣṇū purāṇam 5-7-82 too! “*kriṣṇam akliśta karmāṇam anyē vismitacētasa*: l *tuśtuvur muditā gōpā druśtvā śivajalām nadīm* ll” (Covherds became happy on seeing this, and with their hearts in amazement, seeing that river yamunā had become purified, praised kaṇṇan who casually carried out this act)

Thus, through the first part of the pāsuram, the way emperumān controlled the cruel people was mentioned. In the latter part, it says about the way that good people were protected.

**kudam kalanda kūttanāya koṇḍal vaṇṇa** – Just as the divine disc was fitting well in your divine hand, the pot was also fitting well on your hand. Dancing with the pots, typical of the herd-people, oh one who manifested the beauty of your divine form which is like a bluish cloud! Alternatively, oh one who has the quality of the cloud which showers rain on land and water, without seeing any difference! ōr, it could be considered that this is speaking of the bluish complexion of his divine form like a dark cloud, when he was dancing.

**taṇ tuḻāy vadam kalanda mālai mārba** – one who performed the dancing style of lowly covherd is also the lord of all, who is complete in all aspects, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*avākyanādara*:” (paramātmā (supreme entity) is one who does not speak to anyone, due to his majestic nature and who is without any regard (towards any entity)). āḻvār is exclaiming that he is the lord of all since he is with the garland of tuḷasi and the vanamālai vaijayanti, on his divine chest. *vadam* – garland which has been strung together. *mālai* – the vanamālai \[divine garland\] which is permanently seen on his divine chest.

**kālanēmi kāḷanē** – Oh one who became yama (lord of death) by destroying kālanēmi who troubled celestial entities, who fell at your feet! Through this, it is emphasised that while he is the lord for both celestial and demonic entities, he considers the enemies of those who fall at his feet, as his enemies.

We will take up the 39th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 39. Verpeduttu velai nir

*veṟpeduttu vēlai nīr kalakkināy adanṟiyumveṟperuttu vēlai nīr varambu katti vēlai sūḻveṟpedutta inji sūḻ ilangai kattaḻitta nīveṟpeduttu māri kātta mēgavaṇṇan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veṟpu – mantara mountain; eduttu – took it; vēlai nīr – water of the ocean; kalakkināy – churned it; adanṟiyum – further; veṟpu – mountains; eduttu – took them through the troop of monkeys; varambu – divine dam; vēlai – through the moat of ocean; sūḻ – being surrounded; veṟpu – in trikūta mountains \[a mountain range\]; edutta – established (by viśvakarmā); inji – through fortified walls; sūḻ – surrounded by; ilangai – lankā; kattu – protection; azitta – destroyed by; nī – your highness; veṟpu – the mountain gōvardanam; māri – from rains; kātta – protected (cows, covherd boys and covherd girls); mēgavaṇṇan allaiyē – are you not the one with the divine form of dark clouds?

**Перевод:**

**You took the mantara mountain and churned the ocean water; further, with the help of troop of monkeys, you built a divine dam across the ocean waters. You had destroyed the protection of lankā, established by viśvakarmā on the trikūta mountain range and which had the ocean as a protective moat and further by fortified wall surrounding the city. āren’t you the one with the divine form of dark clouds who had held a mountain gōvardana and protected cows, covherd boys and covherd girls!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār enjoyed emperumān protecting the righteous people and punishing the evil ones. In this pāsuram, he speaks about how he annihilated the group of demons who were inimical towards celestial entities such as indran \[the lord of celestial entities\], who did not have the affection which covherds, who were mentioned in the previous pāsuram, had towards him and how emperumān got back their worlds. He also speaks about the affable quality of emperumān when he spared indran and protected the covherd people on the occasion when indran, who had received so much of help from emperumān, turned against the covherds because he was not offered food by them.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**veṟpeduttu vēlai nīr kalakkināy** – sage durvāsar had given indran a garland gifted to him by śrī mahālakśmi. However, indran did not pay due respect to the garland. Sage durvāsa cursed indran in anger saying “Let your wealth, the reason for your arrogance, be destroyed”. indran et al lost their entire wealth as a result of the curse and took refuge under emperumān, just as it has been stated in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-65 “*praṇāma pravaṇā nāta daitya sainya parājitā*: l *śaraṇam tvāmanuprāptā*: *samastā dēvatā gaṇā*: ll” (Oh lord! We, the assembly of all the celestial entities, who were won over by the demonic entities, were engaged only with bowing down to you and have taken refuge under you). As a result of their taking refuge under you (emperumān), you had churned the ocean with mantara mountain in such a way that all their wealth was regenerated in it. Going past the nature of ocean which is famous in the world as *ratnākaram* (dwelling place for gems), *matsyākaram* (dwelling place for fish), you made it as the place of creation of materials which are in svargam (heaven). ḍidn’t kūrattāḻvān too mercifully say this in his atimānuśastavam 26? “*ētat katam yanmatitas tvayāsau hitvā svabhāvam niyamam pratitam trilōkyām* l *aśvāpsarō viśa sudā vidu pārijāta lakśmyātmanā pariṇatō jaladir bhabhūva* ll” (This milky ocean, which was churned by you, going beyond its natural custom (of giving materials such as curd, ghee, fish, gems etc), gave horse, *apsaras* (celestial women), poison, nectar, candran (moon), pārijātam (a type of jasmine) tree, lakśmī dēvi and the like; how did this happen? You only have to mercifully declare this). Not looking at the lowliness of indran, who desires only other benefits, not looking at your eminence, that you grant only mōkśam (liberation), not looking at his old activities when, with the arrogance that “I am the head of celestial entities” indran had refused to give pārijāta tree \[to kriṣṇa\] and indulged in opposing you, you saved him merely because he took refuge under you.

**adanṟiyum** – “This activity alone is enough to make your followers to believe in you as the refuge. However, how many more activities did you indulge in, for the sake of your followers!” exclaims the āḻvār, listing other activities.

**veṟpeduttu vēlai nīr varambu katti** – you built a dam on the ocean to enable the troops of monkeys to walk on \[in order to reach lankā\], by throwing mountains that they could lay their hands on. īsn’t this an amazing activity, beyond the power of human beings! The mountains did not sink into the unfathomable ocean; the sand from the dam did not get eroded due to the agitating waves.

**vēlai sūḻ veṟpedutta inji sūḻ ilangai kattaḻitta nī** – lankā had the ocean as its protective moat and was established ite range of trikūta mountains by viśvakarma. It had the protection of fortified wall surrounding it as another protection. You destroyed this casually, just as it has been said in “*yatā saikatamambhasi”* (just like a house made of sand dissolves in water). The term i*nji* refers to fortified wall. The term *kattu* refers both to protection and natural quality. When it is taken as referring to natural quality, the meaning will transform to “You changed the natural quality of lankā where righteousness is non-existent”. The implied meaning is that emperumān made vibhīśaṇa who has righteousness as his natural quality as the king of lankā and through that made righteousness to return to lankā, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 17-24 *“vibhīśaṇastu darmātmā na tu rākśasacēśtita*:” (vibhīśaṇa is the epitome of righteousness; he is without the activities of demon)

Now, the pāsuram speaks about how emperumān protected his followers without killing indran when he, who is a favourable person, created harm to his followers.

**veṟpeduttu mārikātta mēgavaṇṇan allaiyē** – indran became angry that “The covherd people, listening to kriṣṇan, have changed the tradition of offering food to me and have insulted me”. He ordered the clouds which normally pour heavy rain during the time of deluge, to create hailstorm on tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam). For this heinous act, while it was appropriate to destroy such indran, thinking mercifully in your divine mind “Due to his anger of having been denied his food, he is angry; fighting with us is not his nature”, did you not lift the gōvardana mountain and remove the harm caused by indran, with this act of magnanimity! You did this only because, unlike rāvaṇan, who had said, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam tu kasyacit* l *ēśa mē sahajō dōśa*:*svabhāvō duratikrama*: ll” (Even if split into two, I will not bow down to anyone like this. This is my fault, from birth. One’s nature cannot be crossed), indran did not have this obstinacy of not bowing to anyone.

**mēgavaṇṇan** – you have the quality of doing good to the world, just like clouds, without expecting anything in return. Alternatively, it would refer to your divine form which will remove the ennui of beholders, like a dark, monsoon cloud. The implied meaning is that while the cloud of deluge was waiting to harm the cow-herd people, the cloud of kriṣṇa negated that harm.

We will take up the 40th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 40. Anai kattu

*ānai kāttor ānai konṟadanṟi āyar piḷḷaiyāyānai mēytti āneyuṇḍi anṟū kunṟam onṟinālānai kāttu maiyarikkaṇ mādarār tiṟattu munānai anṟu senṟadartta māyam enna māyamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ānai – śrī gajēndarāḻvān \[the elephant\]; kāttu – protected; āyar piḷḷaiyāy – being the son of the entire clan of covherds; ānai – kuvalayāpeedam \[the elephant\]; konṟu – killed it; ānai – cows; āney – cow’s ghee; uṇḍi – you ate; anṟu – when indran brought in a hailstorm; kunṟam onṟināl – with a mountain (at hand); ānai kāttu – protected cows; mai – decorated with black pigment; ari – having reddish lines; kaṇ – having divine eyes; mādarār tiṟattu – for the sake of nappinnaip pirātti; anṟu – earlier; mun senṟu – going in front of nappinnaip pirātti

**Перевод:**

**You protected the elephant, gajēndrāḻvān. Further, as the son of the entire clan of covherds, you killed the unique elephant, kuvalayāpeedam. You grazed cows; you ate cow’s ghee. On that day, when indran showered a hailstorm, you protected cows by lifting the mountain gōvardanam. For the sake of nappinnaip pirātti, who had decorated her eyes, which had reddish lines, with black pigment, you killed seven bulls by walking in front of her. How amazing are these amazing activities!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about how emperumān protected celestial entities who think of themselves as īśvaran (one who controls everyone). In this pāsuram, he enjoys emperumān’s activities when he protects those who do not expect any benefit from him; emperumān does not differentiate between his consorts who are above nityasūris and cows, which are below jīvāns (souls) and protects them only because they cannot sustain themselves without him, removing their enemies.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ānai kāttu** – you protected gajēndrāḻvān, despite his being an animal by birth and despite not having good conduct since he was going around with female elephants, just as it has been mentioned by poygai āḻvār in mudal tiruvandādi 97 “*pidi sēr kaḷiṟu*” (male elephant going around, uniting with female elephants), by looking only at his quality of not looking for any benefit other than emperumān. Instead of describing what sort of elephant gajēndran was, āḻvār is merely saying elephant, since the danger which befell the elephant and the way emperumān came running with disheveled dress and hair, were famous. viṣṇu darmam 69-46 says “*gaja ākarśatē dīrē grāham ākarśatē jalē* l *tayōr dvandvasamam yuddam divyam varśasahasrakam* ll” (gajēndran was pulling the crocodile towards land; crocodile was pulling the elephant towards water. This equal fight between them went on for thousand years by dēvamānam (one dēvamānam is equivalent to 360 earth years). parāśara bhattar (son of kūrattāḻvān) says in rangarājastavam 2-57 “*atantrita camūpati prahitahastam asvīkruta* *praṇīta maṇipādukam kimiti cākulānta:puram* l *avāhana pariśkriyam patgarājamārōhata*: *karipravarabrumhitē bhagavatas tvarāyai nama*: ll” (As soon as you heard the outcry of gajēndrāḻvān, you did not bother about the hand held out by sēnai mudaliyār (viśvaksēnar) and did not accept the ruby studded sandals; your divine consorts were perplexed exclaiming ‘Oh what happened?’; you did not wait for your vehicle \[garuda\] to be decorated; I salute that speed of emperumān who mounted garuda), bringing out the urgency in emperumān’s action in order to protect the elephant. The manner in which emperumān protected gajēndrāḻvān is famous, as brought out by toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār in his tirumalai pāsuram 44 “*tavam seydār veḷgi niṟpa viṇṇuḷār viyappa vandu ānaikku anṟu aruḷai īnda*” (you had mercifully come to the bank of the pond in order to protect the elephant \[gajēndrāḻvān\] and showered your mercy on him such that those who were engaged in carrying out penance \[in order to reach you\] put their heads down in shame \[despite the penance which they were carrying out for a long time, they could not have your darśan\] and nityasūris were surprised \[that emperumān was going with such a hurry in order to save an animal\])

**adanṟi āyar piḷḷaiyāy** – in order to have belief in taking refuge under you, this narrative of your protecting the elephant alone is enough. Going beyond the above-mentioned narrative, emperumān incarnated as the son of the entire clan of covherds such that he could be nominated as their representative for everything. In the 36<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, āḻvār had spoken of “*aśōdai āycci piḷḷaiyāy”*. In this pāsuram he changes that line and says “*āyar kulattiṟkē piḷḷaiyāyī*” (son of the entire clan of covherds)

**ōrānai konṟu** – Looking at śrī rāma and rāvaṇa, many said that this was a battle between unequals, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 103-5 “*bhūmau stitasya rāmasya ratastasya ca rākśasa:* l *na samam yuddamityāhur dēva gandahrva dānavā*: ll” (dēvas, gandarvas and demons said that the battle between śrī rāma, who is on the ground, and rākśasa, who is on a chariot, is not one among equals). In a similar way, śrī vasudēvar, dēvaki pirātti, the ladies in śrī maturā et al who were favourable to emperumān were afraid that the elephant kuvalayāpīdam which was incited to be in rut and which was kept to kill kaṇṇan, was posing grave danger to kaṇṇan; however, he killed it casually. The term *ōrānai* refers to the elephant which had its natural strength, along with the strength that it acquired due to its rutting. Thus, it was unmatched in its strength. ḍidn’t this āḻvār himself say “*kadam kalanda vankari*” in the 38<sup>th</sup> pāsuram?

**adanṟi ānai mēytti** – further, in line with your clan of covherds, you protected the cows during grazing. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 6-2-4 “*viḷaiyādu sūḻalaic cūḻavē ninṟu kāli mēykka vallāy*” (Oh lord who is capable of grazing cows in the broad grounds which are the playing arenas for them), you had protected the cows by manifesting your divine beauty to them such that their stomachs became full.

**āney uṇḍi** – you ate the ghee obtained from the cows. Though cāndōjña upaniśat 8-7-1 says of emperumān *satyakāma*: … *vijighātsa*: (he has no unfulfilled desire and that he has no hunger), you desirously ate the materials which were touched by your followers (such as ghee, butter etc) as if you could not sustain yourself without eating them.

**anṟu kunṟam onṟināl ānai kāttu** – further, when indran made the cloud bank called as *samvarta gaṇam* to destroy āyppādi (śrī gōkulam) \[on being denied food\], you took hold of a mountain which was lying near your hand and protected the cows. The word *An* appearing at the beginning of the 2<sup>nd</sup> and 3<sup>rd</sup> lines refers to cows. In the previous pāsuram too, this narrative was mentioned through the verse “*veṟpeduttu māri kātta*“. What is the reason for repeating this narrative here? In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that indran was spared from death since opposing emperumān is not a natural characteristic for him and that kaṇṇan lifted the mountain gōvardanam, thus praising emperumān’s divine quality. Here, this refers to the nature of the cows which cannot sustain themselves in separation from emperumān. Thus, there is no fault of repeating a narrative.

**maiyarikkaṇ mādarār tiṟattu** – for the sake of nappinnaip pirātti who had her eyes, which were with reddish lines, decorated with black pigment.

**mun ānai anṟu senṟadartta māyam** – when nappinnaip pirātti was waiting to embrace kaṇṇan, after decorating herself, he went in front of her, controlled the seven bulls simultaneously. How amazing is this! Here, the term *An* refers to bull.

**enna māyamē** – this action is more amazing than the ones mentioned earlier! The term *enna māyamē* reveals wonder. It could also be considered to express a doubt. In this case, the meaning would be – he had incarnated only to control the seven bulls and to marry nappinnaip pirātti, according to the preceptor who had given the meanings of difficult terms in the prabandam.

Next, we will consider the 41st pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 41. Ayanagi ayar mangai

*āyanāgi āyar mangai vēya tōḷ virumbināyāya ninnai yāvar vallar ambarattodu imbarāymāya māya māyai kol adanṟi nī vaguttalummāyamāyam ākkināy un māyam muṟṟum māyamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

āyanāgi – incarnating as a herd-boy; āyar mangai – nappinnaip pirātti’s; vēya – looking like a bamboo; tōḷ – shoulder; ambarattodu – being together with celestial entities in the upper worlds, such as brahmā et al; imbarāy – among the human entities on earth; yāvar – who; āya ninnai – you, who are the omnipotent; āya vallar – those who can analyse; māya – Oh one who has amazing powers!; māya – having been caused through connection with parakruti (primordial matter); māyai kol – was this done because of ignorance?; adanṟi – apart from that; nī – your highness; vaguttalum – though created classifications (starting from mahat (derivative of prakruti) and ending with physical forms \[of jīvātmās\]); māya – to be destroyed (jīvātmās, instead of going through the proper way, with the physical form and sensory organs given to them by you, going for other benefits); māyamākkināy – you made the jīvas to subside in prakruti (primordial matter) (thinking that it is better for them to be destroyed); un – your highness’; māyam muṟṟum – vows etc; māyamē – they are wondrous only

**Перевод:**

**Incarnating as a covherd, you desired the bamboo-like shoulders of nappinnaip pirātti. Who, among the entities such as brahmā et al in the upper worlds as well as the people on earth, is capable of analysing you, the omnipotent? Oh one who has amazing powers! Was this done due to ignorance resulting from connection with prakruti? (No) Apart from this, even though your highness had created various classifications starting with mahat and ending with physical forms of jīvātmās, the jīvātmās, instead of using the physical forms and sense-organs to go through the correct path (and reaching emperumān) use these to attain worldly pursuits. You made the jīvātmās to subside in prakruti, deciding that destruction for them is a safe option. The sankalpam (vow) of your highness is indeed amazing!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that if one were to analyse the methods by which emperumān incarnates in the clan of those who desire him and protects them in line with their nature of servility and the manner in which he protects through his sankalpam (vow) the samsāris who do not have engagement with emperumān, in line with their karmas (deeds), these would be beyond anyone’s ability to measure and know.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**āyanāgi āyar mangai vēya tōḷ virumbināy** – incarnating in the clan of covherds, you embraced the divine shoulders of fresh, youthful nappinnaip pirātti, which were like fresh, long bamboo.

**āyanāgi …. virumbināy** – you incarnated, leaving the divine, aprākruta \[not composed of primordial matter\] form in paramapadam, to take up a prākruta-like form of a covherd, and showed deep love for nappinnaip pirātti. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “*ānai anṟu senṟadarttu*” mentioning the narrative of marriage with nappinnaip pirātti. Will not saying that here too, be a repetitive narration here? The focus in the previous pāsuram was removing hurdles (bulls) in attaining nappinnaip pirātti. The focus in this song is in transforming his divine form, which is sweet to nityasūris in paramapadam, to a form similar to those in the clan of covherds. Protecting nappinnaip pirātti is symbolic of protecting all his followers who do not consider any benefit other than him. Thus, there is no repetitive narration here.

**āya ninnai yāvar vallar ambarattodimbarāy** – Among all the entities in celestial worlds such as brahmā et al as well as among the wise people on earth, who is there to analyse and know about you, the omnipotent entity?

**āya ninnai** – Who can estimate and know about the feature of making the aprākruta divine form to match the prākruta form of a covherd? The underlying opinion is that none is capable.

**māya** – Oh the amazing entity who has amazing activities! The word *māya* would refer to amazing materials. The term *māya* used in several instances in this pāsuram refers to each amazing entity.

**māya māyai kol** – just as it has been mentioned in bhagavad gītā 7-14 “*daivī hyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā* l *māmēva yē prapadyantyē māyām ētām taranti tē* ll” (This māyai \[prakruti, the primodial matter\], which has been created by me, the lord and which has three guṇas \[of satva, rajas and tamas, viz. noble, passion and ignorance respectively) is difficult to cross over. Those who surrender to me, will be able to cross over this), is it because of ignorance born out of your connection with this prakruti, an acētana entity that you incarnated as kaṇṇan, and married nappinnaip pirātti? No! The word of query *kol* here, means ‘no’. The first term *māya* here refers to prakruti, which is the cause for the amazing creation. The second term *māyai* here (as in *māyai kol*) refers to the ignorance which results in the man due to connection with prakruti. Is there any proof to confirm that the word *māyā* refers to ignorance? The lexicon for vēdas says *māyā vayūnam jñānam* (the words māyā, vayūnam and jñānam have the same meaning). Thus, the word *māyā* refers to jñānam (knowledge) which has amazing characteristics. Since *ajñānam* (ignorance) is wrong knowledge, this word is also taken to be referred to by the word *māyā* \[There is no separate meaning for the world *abhāvam* (non-existent); similarly, ignorance will also refer to wrong knowledge\].

*ambarattodu* could also be considered as referring to nityasūris who are residents of paramapadam, which is above the prākruta lōkams (worlds which are under primordial matter). The meaning for the verse would then be – could even nityasūris, who are without any connection with prakruti, understand the affable quality of emperumān who manifests his aprākruta, divine auspicious form, which is beyond the senses derived through prākrutam, to those who are in samsāram?

**ambarattodu imbarāy yāvar kāṇa vallar? māya māyai kol?** – we could also consider this to mean, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*avākyanādara*:” (paramātmā (supreme entity) is one who does not speak to anyone, due to his majestic nature and who is without any regard (towards any entity)), that emperumān, who does not have any regard towards anyone, and who does not need anything to attain fulfilment, has a desire in the physical form of a herd girl. Who, in the two vibhūtis (nitya vibhūti and leelā vibhūti) can estimate and understand this affable quality of emperumān?

**adanṟi** – apart from the greatness which has been mentioned in the incarnation, thus far. The pāsuram now says that none can estimate the benefits which you (emperumān) had carried out for the cētanas, through activities such as creating the worlds, etc.

**nī vaguttalum** – parāśara bhattar has said in śrī rangarāja stavam 2-41 “*acidaviśēśitān pralayasīmani samsarata*: *karaṇa kaḷēbarair ghatayitum dayamānamanā*: l *varada nijēccayaiva paravānakarō*: *prakrutim*  *mahadabhimānabhūta karaṇāvaḷi kōrakiṇīm* \|\|” (Oh varada! During the time of pralayam (deluge) the jīvans were like acētana entities \[without knowledge\]; you decided in your divine mind that they will be together with sensory organs and forms; being led only by your own sankalpam (vow), you transformed mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) into mahān (great entity), ahankāram (ego), the five elements, the sensory organs \[both knowledge-led and activities-led\]). Just as it has been said in this ślōkam, you had taken pity on the pitiable condition of samsāra jīvans during the time of deluge, had given them knowledge such that they will conduct their lives in the path of righteousness in order to attain you. Despite this…

**vaguttalum** – the classification starting from mahān (the great entity, which is the first derivative of mūla prakruti) and ending with the five elements, the further classification of the universes which formed from this group, the further classification of fourteen worlds in each of these universes, with seven worlds below earth and seven worlds above \[including the earth\], the further classification of four types of entities such as celestial, human, animal and plant, in each of these universes. Despite creating these ….

**māya** – to get destroyed. The jīvans, who were thus created, instead of utilising their forms and knowledge to seek good things for themselves, engaged with worldly pursuits, starting with *śabdam* (sound, form, taste etc), thus leading to the destruction of their basic nature. Here, the word *māya* comes in the meaning of destruction.

**māyam ākkināy** – even at this stage, instead of giving up on the jīvans, you considered that destroying their physical forms is in their betterment, thus making them subside in *māyā* which is mūla prakruti (primordial matter) which is part of your divine form and which is amazing.

**un māyam muṟṟum māyamē** – knowledge which is a manifestation of your vow is truly amazing! Here, the first word *māyam* refers to the knowledge which comes from emperumān’s vow and the second *māyam* refers to the amazement which results from that knowledge.

We shall take up the 42nd pāsuram of this prabandam, next.
