## 42. Verisainda cekkar meni

*vēṟisainda cekkar mēni nīṟaṇinda pun sadaikkīṟu tingaḷ vaittavan kai vaitta van kabāl misaiūru sengurudiyāl niṟaitta kāraṇandanaiēṟu senṟadartta īsa pēsu kūsam inṟiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēru – distinguished; isainda – appropriate for (the activity of destruction); cekkar – reddish (due to anger); mēni – having a form; nīṟu – ashes; aṇinda – one who has applied; pun – entangled and being thin; sadai – one who has matted hair; kīṟu – having a single streak \[moon on the first day\]; tingaḷ – moon; vaittavan – rudra, who has adorned (on that matted hair); kai – in the hand; vaitta – held; van – not letting go; kabāl misai – in the skull; ūru – secreted (in the divine form); sem – reddish; kurudiyāl – with blood; niṟaitta – filled up; kāṟaṇandanai – the cause; kūsam inṟiyē – without any shyness; senṟu – falling over; ēṟu – bulls; ādartta – one who killed; īsa – Oh lord of all!; pēsu – (you yourself) should mercifully state

**Перевод:**

**rudran has a reddish, distinguished form (due to his anger) which is appropriate for his activity of destruction. He applies ashes on his body and has an entangled, matted hair, along with moon with a single streak (moon as it appears on the first day after new moon day) on that matted hair. Oh, the lord of all! You had filled up the skull which that rudran held in his hand, and which was inseparable from his hand, with reddish blood. You, who had killed seven bulls falling over them, should mercifully state the reason for this, without feeling shy.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “nī vaguttalum māya māyamākkināy” (you had created and destroyed the worlds). In line with the saying “ēka Eva rudrō na dvītiyāya tastau” (Only Siva was present; there was no second entity) and in line with the authentic text taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 16 “sarvō hyēśa rudra:” (all these \[entities\] are only rudra), only rudra carries out the activity of destruction. Moreover, it has been mentioned in several authentic texts that brahmā carries out the activity of creation. emperumān reminds āḻvār that when such authentic texts say that other deities carry out such activities, how is he saying that only emperumān has the greatness of carrying out the three major tasks \[of creation, protection and destruction\]? āḻvār responds, saying “In order to remove the danger which occurred to rudra, you had incarnated in a lower status, amidst them \[rudra and brahmā\]. Can’t you clarify this doubt yourself?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vēṟisainda cekkar mēni** – having a reddish form which is appropriate for his distinguished activity (of destruction). *cekkar* – red. Red – symbol of anger. The term *vēṟisainda*, meaning fitting in a distinguished way, is used as an adjective here. Instead of referring merely to a colour, reddish, it refers to anger, which the colour red symbolises. While emperumān engages with the activities of creation, protection and destruction, he carries out creation of all entities in the universe, through brahmā. During the time of deluge, he carries out the activity of destruction of the entities in the universe, through rudran. He also protects all the entities such that they do not get harmed during the time of deluge, by keeping them in his divine stomach. emperumān carries out the three activities of creation, protection and destruction, because of his mercy. brahmā carries out creation through rajō guṇam (quality of passion) and rudran carries out destruction through tamō guṇam (quality of laziness, ignorance).

**nīṟaṇinda punsadai** – having a physical form smeared with ashes and having matted hair which is entangled. *punmai* – lowliness, viz. having entangled hair. It is clear that he is carrying out atonement for having plucked the head of his father, brahmā, since he has smeared ash all over his physical form. Since he has his hair in an entangled manner, it is clear that he is observing a *sādanam* (means) like sages.

**kīṟu tingaḷ vaittavan** – having moon with a single streak on his entangled hair. Thus, through usage of the term *cekkar mēni* it is clear that he has the activity of destruction which results from anger; through the term *pun sadai*, it is clear that he follows a means as he is under the control of śāstram (authentic texts) and has lowly matted hair; through the term *kīṟu tingaḷ vaittavan*, it is clear that he engages with prākruta bhōgam (enjoyment with worldly pursuits).

**kai vaitta van kabāl misai** – in the brahmā’s skull which is inseparable from his hand. The term *kabāl misai* makes it clear that he has incurred a curse since he had plucked brahmā’s head. The term *van* indicates the strength of that curse in the sense that he will not be liberated from the curse until the skull is filled through alms. Since he is called as *kapāli* by his followers, the strength of this curse is emphasised. Being with a sin is applicable to only the jīvans who are bonded to samsāram and who are under the control of śāstram. From the narrative of liberation from curse of the skull, it is implied that brahmā too is a bonded jīvan.

**ūṟu sengurudiyāl niṟaitta kāraṇandanai** – the reason for filling (that skull) with blood-like water which secreted from your \[emperumān’s\] divine form.

**pēsu** – you tell me if the reason for that is different from the fact that your are the *īśvaran* who controls him \[rudran\] and that he is controlled by you. Is there any reason other than the fact that you are the protector and that he is protected by you? In this pāsuram it has been mentioned through the usage of the term *sengurudi* that the material which emperumān offered as alms from his divine chest is blood. In this prabandam, in the 113<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, āḻvār would say *alangal mārbil vāsa nīr koduttavan* confirming that the material offered as alms from his divine chest is fragrant water. What is the reason for this difference? In this pāsuram, it is said that emperumān gave reddish water, which was like blood, from his *aprākruta* divine form which he made appear like a *prākruta* form. Just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 which says of emperumān that he is “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes), since he transformed his *aprākruta* divine form which is the repository of all good fragrances into a *prākruta* form, even blood, which came out of his *aprākruta* divine form was fragrant. It is this fragrance which has been cited in pāsuram 113.

**kūsam inṟiyē ēṟu senṟadartta īsa pēsu** – without feeling shy of the cruel nature of the seven bulls and the softness of your divine form, oh emperumān, you had fallen on the bulls and proved that you are the īśan (one who controls everything) by controlling those bulls! You only have to mercifully tell me about this.

The meaning for this is as follows: Despite incarnating in the clan of covherds who are under the control of śāstrams, you had killed seven bulls and were not affected by that sin and remained as the *īśvaran*. rudra was with the name of *īśvaran* and even when he was still with the divine nature, he attained the stature of a sinner after plucking the head of brahmā. You had incarnated as *viṣṇu* to be one among brahmā and rudra and removed the sin of rudra even when you had lowered yourself to their level. Could this fact of your being the *īśvaran* and rudra being a jīvan under the control of karma, be destroyed by even celestial entities, should they turn against you as your enemies? We could also unite the terms *kūsaminṟiyē* with *pēsu* and get the meaning – mercifully tell me, without any shyness.

Not feeling shy of hiding your eminence, and standing together with lowly people, just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-12 “*nāham dēvō na gandarvō na yakśō na ca dānava*: l *aham vō bāndavō jātō naitaccintyamitō’nyatā* ll” (I am not a dēvan; not a gandarvan; not a yakśan; not a demon (these are all different types of celestial entities). I am born as your relative; my birth is not to be considered (by you people) in any other manner), you should mercifully tell me, who knows the aprākruta nature of your divine form just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 4-9 “*janma karma ca mē divyam ēvam yō vētti tatvata*: l *tyaktvā dēham punar janma naiti māmēti sō’rjuna* ll” (Oh arjuna! One who truly knows like this, my *aprākruta* (not relating to primordial matter) birth and activities, does not have another birth after relinquishing his body in that birth. He attains me).

We shall next take up the 43rd pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 43. Venjinatta velam

*venjinatta vēḻa veṇmaruppositta uruttamākanjanaik kadindu maṇṇaḷandu koṇḍa kālanēvanjanattu vanda pēycci āvi pāluḷ vāngināyanjanatta vaṇṇanāya ādi dēvan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vem – cruel; sinatta – having anger; vēḻam – the elephant kuvalayāpeedam’s; veṇ – whitish; maruppu – tusks; osittu – broke; urutta – one who became angry; mā – the strong; kanjan – kamsan; kadindu – destroyed; vanjanattu – with deceit; vanda pēycci – pūtanā, who came; āvi – vital air; pāluḷ – in her milk; vāngināy – one who usurped; maṇ – earth; aḷandu koṇḍa – measured (with three steps); kālanē – Oh one who has divine feet!; anjanatta – dark; vaṇṇanāya – having complexion; ādi – being the causative factor; dēvan – one who is radiant; allaiyē – aren’t you?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who broke the white tusks of the elephant kuvalayāpeedam which had a cruel anger and one who killed the angry, strong kamsan! Oh one who usurped the life of the deceitful putanā, along with her milk! Oh one who has divine feet with which you measured the world with three steps! āren’t you the one who is radiant and the causative factor for the worlds?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that apart from removing the sin of rudran, just as it has been mentioned in the previous pāsuram, since you had incarnated as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) and annihilated kamsa as well as his companions such as ariśtan, dēnukan, kēśi, pralambhan, narakan, bāṇan, pūtanā, śakatan et al, since you proclaimed that you are the lord of all by keeping your divine feet on the heads of celestial entities such as that rudran et al as well as on worms and insects without any distinction, by incarnating first as vāmana and later on transforming yourself as trivikrama, you are the causative factor for the worlds.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**venjinatta vēḻa veṇ maruppu osittu** – (you) casually broke the white tusks of kuvalayāpeedam which had cruel anger. *venjinam* – cruel anger; very angry. While it was sufficient to describe the elephant as having cruel anger, what was the need to describe the colour of its tusks? Just as the anger of the elephant and its tusks which were capable of killing those who opposed the elephant caused fear in āḻvār \[because of the harm that it could do to kaṇṇan\], even the white colour of its tusks created fear in him. This implies the overflowing affection which āḻvār has towards kaṇṇan.

**urutta mā kanjanaik kadindu** – the moment he heard that his elephant kuvalayāpeedam had been killed, the naturally strong kamsan, got up in anger, with the thought of killing kaṇṇan. Killing that anger and killing kamsan, along with that anger. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-4-4 says that kamsan was talking to his assistants about kaṇṇan: “*kimindrēṇālpa vīryēṇa kim harēṇaikacāriṇā* l *harina vāpi kim sādyam cidrēśvasuraghātinā* ll” (What can indra, with his limited power, do? What can Siva, who has the strength of his penance, do? Whan can hari, who kills monsters at his appropriate time, do?)

While the pāsuram next describes the activity of trivikrama, since in the third line the verse talks about another activity of kaṇṇan, we could rearrange the verse as *vanjanattu vanda pēycci āvi pāluḷ vāngināy! maṇṇaḷandu koṇḍa kālanē* and consider the meaning.

**vanjanattu …. kālanē** – emperumān drank the life along with the poisoned milk of pūtanā, who came disguising herself in the form of a mother, unlike kuvalayāpeedam and kamsan, who came as enemies of emperumān. She came to offer milk to emperumān, in the form of a mother; he too desirously drank that milk, in the form of a son. kaṇṇan had the nature of killing wicked people; due to his nature, her thought of killing him resulted in her own thought killing her. āḻvār says that due to the amazing activity of taking pūtanā’s life through her bosom and killing his enemies as a natural act instead of waiting for the thought of “These wicked people must be killed”, emperumān is superior to everyone. Has it not been said in harivamśam 53-3? “*tasyā: stanam papau kriṣṇa*: *prāṇai*: *saha nanāda ca* l *stanyam tadaviśa sammiśram rasyamāsīt jagadgurō*: ll” (kaṇṇan killed pūtaṇa by drinking her milk from her bosom along with her vital air; he also roared. The milk from her bosom was tasty for him, the lord of the world)

If the text were *uruttu* instead of *urutta* (*uruttu mā kanjanai …*) considered, it would mean that kaṇṇan had uncontrolled anger, even after killing kuvalayāpeedam.

**maṇṇaḷandu koṇḍa kālanē** – apart from killing kamsan, oh one who has the divine feet which you kept on the heads of brahmā et al, as a manifestation of your being the lord of the worlds! The word *kālanē* could also be taken to mean one who was the yama (lord of death) for inimical persons.

**anjanattu vaṇṇanāya ādi dēvan allaiyē** – aren’t you the one with the most distinguished *aprākruta* divine form, creating the world and being radiant, because of that? taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 says “*tasya madyē vahniśikā aṇīyōrdvā vyavastita*: l *neelatōyada madyastā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā* ll” ((paramātmā’s divine auspicious form) agniśikhā is present amidst that lotus-like heart, with a minute form, looking upwards; it shines like lightning which has imbibed bluish cloud)

The opinion is that just as being the causative factor is a symbol for emperumān for being the lord of universe, having an *aprākruta* divine form is also a symbol.

We shall take up the 44th pāsuram of this prabandam, next.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 44. Palinirmai

*pālinīrmai sembonīrmai pāsiyin pasumbuṟampōlunīrmai poṟpudait tadattu vaṇḍu viṇḍulāneelaneermai enṟivai niṟainda kāla nāngumāymālineermai vaiyagam maṟaittadenna nīrmaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pāḷin nīrmai – the whiteness of milk; sem – reddish; pon – gold’s; nīrmai – the quality of being red; pāsiyin – moss’; pasumbuṟam pōlum nīrmai – the quality of freshness on the outer layer of greenish complexion; poṟpudai – being beautiful; tadattu – in the pond; vaṇḍu – beetles; viṇḍu – spreading their wings; ulā – well spread out; neela – the colour of purple lily; nīrmai – black complexion; enṟivai – these complexions; niṟainda – seen (on his divine form) without any shortcoming; kālam nāngumāy – the one who is the controller of the four epochs of kruta etc; mālin nīrmai – the simplicity of emperumān; vaiyagam – those who dwell on earth; maṟaittadu – not caring about; enna nīrmaiyē – how horrible?

**Перевод:**

**The whiteness of milk, the redness of gold, the freshness of green complexion seen on the outer side of moss and the dark complexion seen when beetles spread out their wings in the pond are seen in the divine forms of emperumān who is the controller of the four epochs of kruta, trētā, dvāpara and kali, respectively. How horrible is it that those who dwell on this earth have become indifferent to the simplicity of such emperumān!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, the manner in which cētanas (those with knowledge) announced their servility through the verse “maṇṇaḷandu koṇḍa kālanē” and the manner in which those who follow this servility knew the way in which they could meditate on the aprākruta divine form through the verse “anjanatta vaṇṇan” were mentioned. During the four yugams \[epochs\] of kruta, trēta, dvāpara and kali, due to the excess of noble, passion etc qualities in jīvans, they will desire the colours of white, red, green and black, respectively. Due to this, emperumān changed his complexion from the black colour which was mentioned in the previous pāsuram and took upon the complexion of white, red etc, and manifested his divine form with the colour desired by them. Despite that, the jīvans were indifferent to emperumān. Seeing this, āḻvār detests the samsāris and exclaims ‘’How strong is their impression of previous births!’’*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pālin nīrmai** – since those who inhabit the world during kruta yugam have excess of satva guṇam (noble qualities) and desire white colour, emperumān will make his divine form to appear in white colour. The word *nīrmai* refers to *svabhāvam* – basic characteristic. Here, it refers to the complexion. Both tirumangai mannan in his tirunendundāṇḍagam – 3 “*kadalamudam koṇḍa kālam vaḷaiyuruvāyt tigaḻndān*” (in kruta yugam, when he churned the milky ocean to obtain nectar \[for celestial entities\] emperumān had a white complexion, like a conch) and this āḻvār in tiruccanda viruttam 15 “*sangavaṇṇam anna mēni*” (divine form like a white coloured conch), had mercifully referred to his white complexion during kruta yugam. Here, while mentioning the colour of that yugam, reference to *pālin nīrmai* is to show that the divine form of emperumān has all the good tastes, including the taste of white coloured milk, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarvaganda*: *sarvarasa*:” (emperumān is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes))

**sembon nīrmai** – in the second yugam of trētā yugam, people will have a lower level of satvaguṇam and a higher level of rajō guṇam (qualities of passion, anger etc), desiring reddish colour. emperumān will make his divine form to reflect this reddish colour just as it has been said in manu smruti 12-122 “*praśāsitāram sarvēśāmaṇīyāmsamaṇīyasām* l  
*rukmābham svapnadīgamyam vidyāttu puruśam param* ll” (It should be known that parama puruśan (supreme entity) controls all entities, that he has radiance like melted gold and that he is apt to be known through knowledge similar to the (amazing) intelligence gathered in dreams) as well as by nammāḻvār who had said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-1-2 “*sutturaitta nanpon un tirumēni oḷi ovvādu*” (even pure gold which has been melted in fire and polished on a grinding stone will not match your divine complexion). While referring to the complexion of emperumān, tirumangai āḻvār had said in tirunedundāṇḍagam 3 “*tirēdaikkaṇ sēyan enṟum*” (emperumān has reddish colour in trētā yugam). āḻvār’s reference to gold while explaining his reddish complexion while saying “*sembon nīrmai*” is to emphasise that emperumān’s divine form with that colour is as invaluable as gold.

**pāsiyin pasumbuṟam pōlum nīrmai** – during dvāpara yugam, emperumān has the divine complexion which is similar to the freshness on the outer surface of moss, which removes the ennui of beholders.

Next, the pāsuram speaks about the complexion in kaliyugam.

**poṟpudait tadattu vaṇḍu viṇḍulā nīrmai** – in a beautiful pond, beetles lie close to each other and drink honey from flowers. Due to the resultant happiness \[of drinking honey\] they flap their wings and roam around, together. emperumān has the complexion of bluish colour which is seen during that action by beetles. Since adjectives have been provided for the bluish coloured flower, it is implied that he has the complexion of such bluish flower which removes the ennui of those who look at it. The term *poṟpu* refers to beauty. *viṇḍal* refers to blossoming. Here, this would refer to spreading the wings. Unlike other yugams, during kaliyugam, emperumān does not covet any specific colour. Since, in kaliyugam, there is none who desires him irrespective of the colour which he takes, he remains with his original bluish complexion. Did not tirumangai āḻvār too say in his periya tirumoḻi 4-9-8 “*muḻudum nilai ninṟa pinnai vaṇṇam koṇḍal vaṇṇam*” (during kaliyugam, emperumān has dark complexion of cloud)? Hasn’t the great sage parāśarar too said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-1-50 that during kaliyugam, people will not engage with emperumān? “*kalau jagadpatim viṣṇum sarvasraśtāramīśvaram* l *nārcayiśyanti maitrēya pāśaṇḍōpahatā janā*: ll” (Oh maitrēya! During the time of kaliyugam, people, who have been befuddled by *pāśaṇḍis* (those who have contrary views to established authentic texts) will not worship viṣṇu, who is the lord of the worlds, who is the creator and who is the lord of all).

**enṟivai niṟainda kālam nāngumāy** – being the four yugams when emperumān takes up the complexions which are without any deficiencies, as desired by his followers. In other words, being the controller of the four yugams. He controls the people in those four yugams, taking up the complexions in his divine form.

**mālin nīrmai** – the simplicity of emperumān, who is the lord of all, who is like this. *māl* – an eminent person. Despite being eminent, he has the simplicity to transform his complexion as desired by the yugapuruśas (those who live in those yugams).

**vaiyagam maṟaittadenna nīrmaiyē** – āḻvār is amazed that people in the world, looking at such simplicity manifested by emperumān, instead of attaining him, remain indifferent to him because of the same simplicity, due to their karmas which they have been carrying since time immemorial. Just as the term *manjākrōśanti* (the bed is crying) refers to the people who are lying on the bed, the term *vaiyagam* (world) refers to the people who are inhabiting the world. The term *maṟaittadu* refers to the character of people remaining indifferent to him due to their connection with the three guṇams (satva, rajas and tamas), just as it has been said in jitantē ślōkam 1-3 “*ēkastavamasi lōkasya sraśtā samhārakas tatā* l *adyakśaścānumantā ca guṇamāyā samāvrutā*: ll” (You are the only one who created all the worlds (without expecting any assistance); similarly, for destruction too, you are the only one; you are the one awaiting the time to protect (without stopping those who engage with deeds which lead to bonding further with samsāram), allowing them, being hidden by primordial matter which is composed of the three guṇams of satva, rajas and tamas). The implied meaning is that cētanas’ deeds which have been gathered over time immemorial are much stronger than the mercy of emperumān who transforms himself, as per the wishes of his followers.

We shall next take up the 45th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 45. Mannulay kol

*maṇṇuḷāy kol viṇṇuḷāy kol maṇṇuḷē mayangi ninṟueṇṇumeṇṇagap padāy kol enna māyai nin tamarkaṇṇuḷāy kol sēyai kol anantan mēl kidanda empuṇṇiyā punanduḻāy alangalam punidanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇṇuḷāy kol – haven’t you (an aprākrutan (not composed of anything related to primordial matter)) incarnated in leelā vibhūti, in the same species as those in leelā vibhūti?; viṇṇuḷāy kol – (having incarnated like this) aren’t you the controller of nityasūris who are in paramapadam?; maṇṇuḷē – on the earth; mayangi ninṟu – remaining in a bewildered state; eṇṇum – (samsāris) thinking; eṇ – thoughts; agappadāy kol – aren’t you outside?; nin tamar – for your followers; kaṇṇuḷāy kol – aren’t you in their eyes?; sēyai kol – aren’t you too far away (from the enemies of your followers)?; anantan mēl kidanda – reclining on top of tiruvanandāḻvān (ādiśēśan); em puṇṇiyā – oh one who is my means!; punanduḻāy alangal – with the garland made of divine tuḷasi; am – being beautiful; punidanē – oh one who is sacred!; enna māyai – how amazing is this!

**Перевод:**

**Haven’t you incarnated in the prākruta species of those who inhabit leelā vibhūti, even though you are an aprākrutan? Are you not the controller of nityasūris who are in paramapadam (even after incarnating in leelā vibhūti)? āren’t you outside the thoughts of those who remain on the earth, who are in a bewildered state? āren’t you in the eyes of your followers? āren’t you too far away for those who are inimical to your followers? Oh one who is lying atop tiruvanandāḻvān, as my means! Oh one who is beautiful and who is the sacred entity with the garland made of divine tuḷasi! How amazing is this!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had spoken about the loss which samsāris incurred due to impressions of their past deeds which had exceeded emperumān’s simplicity. In this pāsuram he says “I am the lowest among the samsāris. You are manifesting your eminence as well as simplicity to me; you are also manifesting your simplicity to your followers and the quality of being difficult to attain for others; through all these, you have made me exist for you. How amazing is this!”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṇṇuḷāy kol** – being full in all aspects, just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*avākyanādara*:” (paramātmā (supreme entity) is one who does not speak to anyone, due to his majestic nature and who is without any regard (towards any entity)) you incarnated in order to make samsāris to attain you, after transforming your divine *aprākruta* form to a *prākruta* form, visible to samsāris. By using the term *‘kol’* āḻvār asks that while incarnating, since emperumān manifests his eminence as simplicity, how are these two fitting within you? In other words, while he incarnated as śrī rāma, even as he was in a state of sadness after being separated from sītāp pirātti, how did he grant mōkśam (paramapadam) to a bird, jatāyu? Hasn’t kūrattāḻvān too mercifully stated this in atimānuśastavam 17? “*pruccāmi kincana yadā kila rāghavatvē māyāmrugasya vaśagōmanujatvamaugdyāt* l *sītā viyōgavivaśō na ca tadgatijjña*: *prādās tadā paragatim hi katam kagāya* ll ” ((Oh empirān!) I want to ask you one question: you had incarnated as śrī rāma; with the lower intelligence of humans, you came under the influence of the illusory deer, got separated from sītāp pirātti and became unconscious. You did not know as to where she had gone. Even in that state, how did you grant mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) to a bird?)

**viṇṇuḷāy kol** – you have an eminence which cannot be estimated even by nityasūris who have no connection with samsāram. Here, the word ‘*kol’* is used with the meaning that while he is the controller of nityasūris, he is affable with guhan, śabarī, sugrīva et al. How do these two qualities coexist?

**maṇṇuḷē mayangi ninṟu eṇṇum eṇṇagappadāy kol** – you are beyond the thoughts of samsāris who are unable to see clearly your qualities of eminence as well as simplicity due to their contact with their physical bodies on earth and who seek worldly pursuits.

*enna māyai* has been explained at the end, as a verb.

**nin tamar kaṇṇuḷāy kol** – for those who take refuge under your divine feet, without considering any other benefit, just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 4-8 “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām vināśāya ca duśkrutām* l  
*dharma samstāpanārtāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē* ll” (I incarnate in every yugam \[epoch\] in order to protect the good people, to annihilate the bad ones and to establish righteous path) you make them see you, just as you are.

**sēyai kol** – for those who are inimical to your followers, you are at a very great distance, without knowing anything about you and to get destroyed fighting you. The reference seen in bhagavat gītā 4-8, seen just now, explains this too. The word ‘*kol*‘ signifies that while emperumān has a relationship which is the same for both his followers and those who are inimical to his followers, he is benevolent towards his followers while he is beyond the reach of their enemies. How do these coexist?

From the beginning of this pāsuram, the contrasting qualities which emperumān manifests towards his followers and towards their enemies, along with the authentic texts cited as proof, make it clear that these cannot be brushed aside as false. Since the qualities are in contrast, they cannot be considered as true too. How amazing is this!

**anantan mēl kidanda em puṇṇiyā** – let these remain as contradictory. By showing the beauty of your divine form as you reclined on tiruvanandāḻvān (ādiśēśan), you have made it clear to me that you have the nature of mingling with your followers and that I am totally subservient to you!

**puṇṇiyā** – As per etymology, “*pumstvam nayatīti puṇya*:” (that which makes the basic nature clear is puṇyam). āḻvār says that emperumān is the means for clarifying his basic nature and āḻvār’s basic nature.

**punanduḻāy alangal punidanē** – mahābhāratam uttara parvam 42-35 says “*yō’nyatā santamātmānam anyatā pratipadyatē* l *kim tēna na krutam pāpam cōrēṇātmāpahāriṇā* ll” (What sin has not been committed by a person who considers ātmā, which is emperumān’s possession, as his own?) Oh the great purifier, who, by manifesting your shoulders and the garland decorating the shoulders, removed my taste from other benefits and removed impurities such as considering ātmā (soul) as one’s own \[while it is owned by emperumān\]! *punidan* – one who purifies others.

**enna māyai** – how amazing is this! The implied meaning is that the great deeds which emperumān mercifully did for āḻvār could not be measured.

We shall next take up the 46th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 46. Todu perra

*tōdu peṟṟa taṇḍuḻāy alangal ādu senniyāykōdu paṟṟi āḻi ēndi anjiṟaippuḷ ūrdiyālnādu peṟṟa nanmai naṇṇam illaiyēnu nāyinēnvīdu peṟṟu iṟappodum piṟappaṟukkamā solē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tōdu peṟṟa – petals spreading out; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – made of tuḷasi; alangal – divine garland; ādu – tilting (towards his followers); senniyāy – Oh one who has divine crown!; kōdu – śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch); paṟṟi – holding; āḻi – divine disc; ēndi – holding; am – beautiful; siṟai – having divine wings; puḷ – garuda; ūrdi – you conducted; Al – alas!; nādu – people (who were present in those times); peṟṟa – enjoyed; nanmai – benefit (of these activities); naṇṇam illaiyēnum – even if I had not got; nāyinēn – lowly person like a dog; vīdu – śrī vaikuṇṭam; peṟṟu – attaining; iṟappodum – getting liberated from this physical form; piṟappu aṟukkum – not taking another physical form; A – means; sol – you should mercifully tell me

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who has the garland made of cool tuḷasi with spread out petals and a divine crown which tilts towards your followers! Holding the divine conch and the divine disc, you conduct garuda. Alas! I did not enjoy the benefits which people in those times had enjoyed. You should mercifully tell me the means by which I, like a lowly dog, could get rid of this physical form and attain you, without the need of taking birth again.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had mentioned that emperumān was the natural means for him by addressing him as “anantan mēl kidanda em puṇṇiyā“. He then thought of the incarnations made by emperumān both in vyūham (milky ocean) and in vibhavam (leelā vibhūti) as well as going on his garuda to places where his followers were residing, in order to protect them. He says that he has lost out on all these. He tells emperumān that he must mercifully show him the way by which āḻvār could attain him by getting rid of hurdles such as repeated births and deaths.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tōdu peṟṟa taṇḍuḻāy alangal ādu senniyāy** – Oh one who has the cool garland made of tuḷasi, with its spread-out petals and divine crown which tilts towards your followers! *tōdu* – petals of a flower. The implied meaning is that due to constant contact with emperumān, tuḷasi is cool and blossomed. Since he is welcoming his followers and tilting his crown towards them, they get relieved from their troubles in samsāram. The connection with emperumān is the cause for cētanas to blossom and to rid themselves of their troubles. Through these, it is affirmed that emperumān has the beauty in his form to create an interest in those who come to him and to look at them affably.

**kōdu paṟṟi āḻiyēndi** – *kōdu* – divine conch; holding *pāncajanyāḻvān* (the divine conch), who removes the sorrows of followers through his roaring. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 10-6-8 “*karudumidam porudu kainninṟa cakkarattan*” (one who holds the divine disc in his hand in order to defeat the enemies of his followers by going to their places and destroying them), emperumān holds the divine disc too, in his hand. Apart from looking at his followers affably, emperumān also holds the divine weapons to destroy their enemies.

**anjiṟaippuḷ ūrdiyāl** – you come to the places where your followers reside, through garuda, your vehicle, whose wings get beauty through your contact. The word *am* (beauty) is used here as an adjective for *siṟai* (wings). If it is to be used as an adjective for *ūrdi* (vehicle) the meaning would change to – having garuda as the vehicle is a decoration for emperumān. This shows emperumān’s simplicity of going to his followers in samsāram on his vehicle to remove their sorrows and protect them during times of danger.

**Al** – this term shows the excess of āḻvār’s sorrows. He feels sad that he has missed out on the times when emperumān had shown himself up, to his followers.

**nādu peṟṟa nanmai naṇṇam illaiyēnum** – I did not have the fortune to enjoy the manner in which you had travelled on garuda as your vehicle in such a way that all the people who had been on this earth could see you. The implied meaning is that while he had lost out on times when emperumān had incarnated on this earth, should he still lose out when emperumān has incarnated in *arcai* (idol) form now?

**nāyinēn vīdu peṟṟu** – just like a dog would be at the doors of a person who had given it food on one day, I too should attain paramapadam since I do not have any refuge other than you.

**nāyinēn** – there is another explanation for āḻvār calling himself this way. Just as a dog would enter various places which are open and be driven away, āḻvār says that he too had gone to various philosophies and been insulted. He opines that just as a dog brings impurity to a person who touches it, he too is lowly.

**vīdu peṟṟu** – I, who am like this, should get the benefit attained by nityasūris; I, who am without any goodness in me, should get the benefit which nityasūris, who are complete in all aspects, get.

**iṟappodum piṟappu aṟukkumā solē** – I should not get another form after this physical form is destroyed; you should mercifully show me the way by which I get fully liberated from this samsāram. While *vīdu peṟudal* (getting liberated) will follow after birth is severed, why is āḻvār talking of liberation first and then about severing from the cycle of birth and death? If he speaks about severing the cycle of birth, it could lead to *kaivalyam* (ātmā enjoying itself instead of carrying out service to emperumān and enjoying the happiness that it brings to emperumān). In order to prevent the occurrence of *kaivalyam*, āḻvār is first talking of attaining emperumān and then severance from birth. When a prince is imprisoned by his enemy, those who are favourable to him, after defeating the enemy, will first place the crown on his head while he is still inside the prison and only after that will they bring him out of the prison. In the same way, emperumān should first take a *sankalpam* (vow) of making them attain him first and after that only, should he sever them from their births.

We will next take up the 47th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 47. Karodotta meni

*kārodotta mēni nangaḷ kaṇṇa! viṇṇinādanēnīridat tarāvaṇaik kidatti enbar anṟiyumōridattai allai ellai illai enbar ādalālsērvidattai nāyinēn terindu iṟainju māsolē*

**Пословный перевод:**

karodu otta – matching monsoon clouds; mēni – having a divine form; nangaḷ kaṇṇa – Oh our kriṣṇa!; viṇṇin nādanē – Oh the lord of nityasūris!; kidatti – you are reclining; enbar – vēdams and those who follow vēdams will say; anṟiyum – apart from that; ōridattaiallai – you are not in any one particular place (you are present everywhere); ellaiyillai enbar – (for the abodes where you can be attained such as dahara vidyai etc) they will also say that there is no count; ādalāl – since those places are not approachable; nāyinēn – I, who am very lowly; sērvidattai – the place to attain; terindu – to know; iṟainjumā – the way to attain; sol – you should mercifully tell me

**Перевод:**

**Oh our kriṣṇa who has a divine form matching dark clouds of monsoon! Oh the lord of nityasūris! vēdams and those who follow vēdams will say that you recline on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān in tiruppāṟkadal. Apart from that, they will also say that you do not have a specific place and that there is no limit to the places where you could be attained. Since all these places, which have been mentioned so far, are not approachable \[for me\], you should mercifully tell me the place as well the way for a very lowly person like me, to attain you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “adiyēn vīdupeṟṟup piṟappaṟūkkum vaḻiyai aruḷic ceyyavēṇum” (you should mercifully show me the means to attain paramapadam and to sever connection with my births). emperumān told him “There are many abodes such as param (śrīvaikuṇṭam), vyūham (tiruppāṟkadal, the milky ocean), vibhavam (places of divine incarnations such as śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al) to attain me. You could reach any of these places and attain me”. āḻvār responds to emperumān in this pāsuram “It is not possible for me to attain you in these places. You should now mercifully tell me a place where I can know you and attain you”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kārodotta mēni nangal kaṇṇa** – Oh kriṣṇa, who has a divine form matching the colour of dark clouds, attracting the minds of those who behold, and who made it as the wealth of your followers! Through this, āḻvār hints that he does not have the means to uplift himself by attaining kaṇṇan who had incarnated a long time back and who is not present in his time. emperumān asks him “But you could attain the divine form which is in paramapadam which does not have such a deficiency, but is available at all times”. āḻvār responds mercifully to that, saying …

**viṇṇin nādanē** – that divine form is apt to be enjoyed by nityasūris. It is not possible for me, who is at a far distance, to attain.

When asked “Unlike kriṣṇa who was present at a long time back or paravāsudēvan (emperumān at paramapadam) who is at a very long distance, is it not possible to attain the divine form at tiruppāṟkadal, which is present in leelā vibhūti and uplift yourself?”  he says ……

**nīridattu arāvaṇaikkidatti** – even though the deficiency of being at a far away place or at a far away time is not seen in the emperumān at tiruppāṟkadal, even that divine form is apt to be seen at some point of time or the other, by people in śvētadvīpam (a world similar to our earth). It is not possible for people like us to attain that place or to see that divine form.

**enbar** – we have only heard this from vēdams and from the people who have the knowledge about vēdams; we have not seen this ourselves. Just as it has been said in puruśa sūktam uttarānuvākam ‘’*ajāyamānō bahudā vijāyatē*’’ (emperumān does not have any birth at all; he keeps taking many births \[he does not have birth due to any past deeds; but he takes births as per his vow\]) as well as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 4-11-4 “*yadōr vamśam nara*: *śrutvā sarvapāpai*: *pramucyatē* l *yatrāvatīrṇa*: *kriṣṇākya*: *param brahma narākruti* ll” (after hearing from the yadu kulam in which the supreme entity kriṣṇa incarnated in human form, a person gets liberated from all his sins), we have only heard about these from śrī viṣṇu purāṇam and not in any other way. Even in the case of emperumān in paramapadam, we have only heard through rig vēdam – viṣṇu sūktam, and have not seen it. “*tad viṣṇō*: *paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya*: l *divīva cakśurātatam* ll  
*tadviprāsō vipanyavō jāgruvāmsas samindatē viṣṇōr yat paramam padam* ll” (nityasūris constantly keep looking at that superior abode of viṣṇu. That supreme abode is effulgent like sūriyan (sun) who reveals everything, who is in the sky, who is like the eyes of the world. In that paramapadam, greatly wise people, people who keep constantly vorśipping, ever alert nityasūris shine brightly). harivamśam 113-62 says “*ēśa nārāyaṇa śrīmān kśīrārṇava nikētana*: l *nāgaparyankam utsrujya hyāgatō madurāmpurīm* ll” (this śrīman nārāyaṇan himself, who has reclined in tiruppāṟkadal, has left the mattress of anantāḻvān and mercifully reached madurā). Thus, that emperumān has mercifully taken residence in the abode of tiruppāṟkadal is known only to wise sages and not seen by ourselves.

**nīridattu** – from ocean; from tiruppāṟkadal.

**arāvaṇai** – the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān.

**anṟiyum** – further

**ōridattai allai** – you are not in one place. The underlying impression is that (as *antaryāmi*, the indvelling soul, with his divine form) he remains in all the places.

**enbar** – this is also affirmed by vēdams and those knowledgeable people who follow vēdams. kauśīdakī upaniśat 2-4 says *“sahōvāca prāṇōsmi prajñātmā tam mām āyuramrutamiti upāsva*” (that indran said – I, who am with the physical form, also exist as the soul form. Worship me as *āyus* (life) and as *amrutam* (that which is without death)). The same upaniśat in 2-8 says *“sa hō vāca māmēva vijānīhi ētadēvāham manuśyāya hitatamam manyē yanmām vijānīyāt*” (that indran said – worship only me (viz. that supreme entity who is my *antaryāmi* – I consider vorśipping me alone as the greatest benefit to the jīvan). In such instances, in the three types of *upāsanas* (worship) (vorśipping emperumān as the antaryāmi of acit (physical form, which does not have knowledge), cit (soul form, which has knowledge) and directly vorśipping) which have been mentioned in authentic texts, has it not been affirmed that the divine form which is auspicious and apt to be meditated upon, is apt to be vorśipped?

**ellaiyillai enbar** – they will also say that there is no limit to the abodes of worship such as dahara vidyai, śāṇḍilya vidyai, vaiśvānara vidyai, upakōsala vidyai etc which have been ordained in the vēdams for vorśipping emperumān. Just as it has been mentioned in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 5-7-22 “*yamātmā na vēda* …” (that supreme entity who the jīvātmā does not know….), since we cannot know the *antaryāmi* (indvelling soul) form of emperumān and since vorśipping emperumān through upāsanam (vorśipping as laid out in vēdams) is appropriate only for brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya classes of people, āḻvār says that he is not qualified to attain emperumān through these paths.

**ādalāl** – since parama padam is at too far a distance, since incarnations have been at too far back a time, since tiruppāṟkadal, though present in samsāram, is accessible only to entities from śvētadvīpam, since *antaryāmi* form is not known, and since the abodes of worship such as dahara vidyai, śāṇḍilya vidyai etc are apt only for the first three classes of people (brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya), āḻvār says that he cannot attain emperumān through these means.

**sērvidattai nāyinēn terindu iṟainjumā solē** – I am lowly like a dog and unqualified. You should mercifully tell me a place to attain you so that I will know “This place is appropriate to be attained”

We will next take up the 48th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 48. Kunrinirundu

*kunṟininṟu vānirundu nīḷkadal kidandu maṇonṟu senṟadonṟai uṇḍadonṟidandu panṟiyāynanṟu senṟa nāḷavaṟṟuḷ nalluyir padaittavark(ku)anṟu dēvamaittaḷitta ādi dēvan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kunṟil ninṟu – standing atop tirumalai; vān irundu – sitting in paramapadam; nīḷ – lengthy; kadal – in tiruppāṟkadal; kidandu – reclined; maṇ onṟu – the nonpareil earth; senṟu – measured; adu onṟai – that unique earth; uṇḍu – kept in the stomach; panṟiyāy – as wild boar; adu onṟidandu – dug out that unique earth; nanṟu senṟa nāḷ avaṟṟuḷ – in the varāha kalpam (a period of time) which went well; nal – having benefits; uyir – people; padaittu – created; avarkku – for those people; anṟu – when rajas and tamas (qualities of passion and ignorance, respectively) are dominant; dēvu – the class of celestials (appropriate to be attained); amaittu – created; aḷitta – one who protected; ādi – causative factor; dēvan – the lord, without any specific reason; allaiyē – aren’t you?

**Перевод:**

**You \[addressing emperumān\] are standing atop tirumalai hills; you are sitting in paramapadam. You reclined in tiruppāṟkadal. You measured the earth which is nonpareil. You kept that unique earth in your divine stomach.  You dug out that earth, as a wild boar, during the time of varāha kalpam. You created people with benefits; when the qualities of rajas and tamas became dominant in them, you created celestial entities who would be appropriate for those people to attain, and thus you protected them. āren’t you that causative factor, who did all the above, without a specific reason?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān says, subsequent to the previous pāsuram, “We have created many abodes in a general way, for followers to attain. Based on the particular abode and manner in which they have attained me, I can fulfil the desire which they seek. Is it my responsibility to specify the abode by which you could reach me?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “While you have the nature of fulfilling the desires of people whether they attain you or do not attain you, is it not your responsibility alone to fulfil my desire too?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kunṟil ninṟu** – in his nāṇmugan tiruvandādi 47<sup>th</sup> pāsuram, this āḻvār had said of tiruvēngadam “*kānamum vānaramum vēdum udai vēngadam*” (tiruvēngadam which has forests, monkeys and hunter tribes). You are standing in that tirumalai in order to shower your mercy on lowly people. Did anyone ask you to stand there?

**vān irundu** – you have the immeasurable eminence of sitting in paramapadam such that nityasūris, who do not have any connection with samsāram, could enjoy you all the time. Did you not come to tirumalai and stand there in order to grace lowly people who did not even ask you to come there?

**nīḷ kadal kidandu** – unlike monkeys and the people of tribes of hunters, celestial entities such as brahmā, rudra et al have ego and think “I am īśvaran” (I control everyone). When such celestial entities had not asked you to recline in tiruppāṟkadal, you had reclined there, just as it has been said by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 8-10-8 “*tāḷum tōḷum mudigaḷum samanilāda pala parappi*” (spreading out his matchless divine feet, shoulders and crowns), such that they could come there and beseech you to help them in times of danger, in order to hear their voice, even when they had not asked for it.

**maṇṇonṟu senṟu** – indran had lost three worlds (bhū, bhuva: and suva:, namely earth, intermediate and heaven) to mahābali. mahābali too had taken over only these three worlds from indra. Yet, when none had prayed for it, you went ahead and measured all the worlds up to *brahmalōkam* (the abode of brahmā), keeping your divine feet on the heads of everyone. Even though emperumān had measured all the worlds, āḻvār says “*maṇ onṟu*” (unique earth) since the importance here was only for earth. The word *senṟu* here refers to measuring the worlds, by keeping his divine feet. Alternatively, we could consider this to refer to each and every incarnation which emperumān undertook, going to various places on earth for this purpose.

**adu onṟai uṇḍu** – when the waters of deluge were about to swallow that earth, did you not keep it safely in your divine stomach and protect it, when no one prayed to you for doing it?

**adu onṟu idandu panṟiyāy** – digging out that unique earth which was to be swallowed by deluge, as a great boar.

**nanṟu senṟa nāḷavaṟṟuḷ nalluyir padaittu** – created beneficial people during the good times. āḻvār is celebrating the great time to see varāha tirukkōlam (the auspicious form of wild boar) when he says “*nanṟu senṟa nāḷ*“. Since human beings have the worthiness to follow what had been ordained in vēdams, unlike animals and plants, āḻvār is referring to them as *nalluyir*. śrī bhāgavatam 11-2-29 says “*durlabhō mānuśō dēhō dēhinām kśaṇabhangura*: l *tatrāpi durlabham manyē vaikuṇṭha priyadarśanam* ll” (It is very rare for a person to get the physical form of a human being which gets destroyed in a moment; more than that, I think that it is more difficult to see a person devoted to emperumān), thus affirming that it is very difficult to get a human birth.

**avarkku anṟu dēvamaittu** – when people have predominance of rajō and tamō guṇam, and they do not engage with emperumān, creating celestial entities such as brahmā, rudran et al so that they can attain them.

**aḷitta ādi dēvan allaiyē** – even when none desired, according to the status in which they were remaining, are you not the causative factor who grants them benefits? Since you create the people who worship as well as the celestial entities who are vorśipped by them, and since you are the natural lord, being the causative factor for all the worlds, are you not appropriate to be vorśipped by everyone? Has it not been said in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 *“sahasraśīrśam dēvam*” (nārāyaṇan, who has thousands of heads and who is denoted by the word *dēvan*) as well as in *“sākśāt dēva*: *purāṇō’sau*” (this supreme entity, who is ancient, is the natural lord)? The implied meaning is that since I am (reference to āḻvār) totally subservient only to you, it is your (reference to emperumān) responsibility to tell me the abode which I should attain, as per my desire.

Next, we will take up the 49th pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 49. Kondai konda

*koṇḍai koṇḍa kōdai mīdu tēnulāvu kūni kūnuṇḍai koṇḍu aranga ōtti uḷ magiḻnda nādan ūrnaṇḍai uṇḍu nārai pēra vāḷai pāya neelamēaṇḍai koṇḍu keṇḍai mēyum andaṇīr arangamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

koṇḍai – on the hair, on head; koṇḍa – having; kōdai mīdu – on the garland; tēn – beetles; ulāvu – roaming; kūni – mantarā’s (servant of kaikēyi); kūn – hump (on her back); aranga – (to make the hunch) to disappear inside; ōtti – shot; uḷ magiḻnda – felt happy; nādan Ur – the abode where cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāma) is dwelling; naṇḍai – crab; uṇḍu – ate; nārai – crane; pēra – as it walked; vāḷai – a scabbard fish; pāya – as it jumped (after seeing the crane); neelamē – purple water lily; aṇḍai koṇḍu – keeping as a protection; keṇḍai – fresh water carp fish; mēyum – (without fear) foraging; am – beautiful; taṇ – cool; nīr – having flowing water; arangamē – it is only śrī rangam

**Перевод:**

**Beetles were roaming on the garland donned on her head by mantarā, who has a hump. (śrī rāma) shot balls of clay at her hump such that it went inside (her back) and felt happy. śrī rangam is the divine abode where cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāma) is dwelling. In that abode, crane would eat a crab and walk away. Seeing that, a scabbard fish would jump into the water, while a fresh water carp fish would forage for food in the flowing water, keeping a purple water-lily as a protective layer (to escape from the crane).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had prayed to emperumān to tell him the manner in which he could attain emperumān. emperumān showed him the greatness of reclining in the big temple in śrī rangam, where he is manifesting his affable qualities of simplicity etc which he exhibits during his incarnations, while at the same time exhibiting the qualities of eminence, on this earth itself. Seeing that, āḻvār enjoys that in this pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**koṇḍai koṇḍa kōdai mīdu tēn ulāvu kūni** – kūni, who was attracting people through the beetles which were roaming around the garland that she had decoratively worn on her head. In the *svāpadēśam* (interpretation through inner meaning), kūni represents a bonded jīvan (soul) \[in samsāram\]. Her hump represents the deficiency of the soul thinking that the body is the soul. By saying “*koṇḍai …. tēn ulāvu*” it signifies that the svabhāvam (natural characteristic) of the jīvan is much desirable for emperumān, just like the śrī kaustubha gem which he dons on his divine chest.

**kūni kūn** – the term *kūn* (hump back) which is together with her name kūni and which does not gel with the form and decoration mentioned earlier. Through the term *kūn*, āḻvār is referring to ego (namely dēham is ātmā), being opposed to one’s svarūpam (basic nature), being together with the name as if identifying with terms such as “I am dēvan (celestial entity), I am manidan (human entity)” etc, causing deficiency to one’s svarūpam.

**uṇḍai koṇḍu aranga ōtti** – when śrī rāma was a child, he kept balls of clay on his bow and shot them playfully on her hump back such that it will go into her body. With respect to the inner meaning, it could be said that āḻvār is referring to the power of emperumān who could remove the jīvan’s deficiency of ego in a casual manner.

**uḷ magiḻnda nādan Ur** – being happy that he could remove ego, the deficiency in his followers, in a casual manner, and through that act, he won me over as his servitor. The divine abode of such cakravartit tirumagan (son of emperor daśaratan). Through this, it is demonstrated that it is emperumān who is happy when deficiency in his followers is removed. Thus, through the first part of the pāsuram, āḻvār says that the *sauśeelya guṇam* (a person of high stature moving in an affable manner with one of low stature) of śrī rāma could be seen in namperumāḷ (the utsava idol of emperumān in śrī rangam).

**naṇḍai uṇḍu nārai pēra** – crane, which swallowed the crab, due to the weight of the crab, had to move slowly, as if like a mountain, taking short steps; through the term *pēra*, the greatness of crab posing a hurdle to the crane in moving swiftly has been highlighted.

**vāḷai pāya** – the scabbard fish thought that the crane was coming to grab it and hence jumped in fear.

**neelamē aṇḍai koṇḍu keṇḍai mēyum** – looking at the assembly of cranes and scabbards, the fresh water carp fish, out of fear, hid behind the dark shadow of purple water-lily flowers which had blossomed throughout the surface of the water body. It felt safe under the flowers, just like how people living inside a fort feel, and foraged for food, fearlessly. In the consideration of inner meaning, crab refers to the worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound, form) etc. Crane, which eats those crabs, refers to samsāri jīvātmās who enjoy such pursuits. The way the cranes move, refers to their ego that identifies body as soul. Scabbard fish, which is scared of this, indicates those who follow other means such as bhakti yōgam (path of devotion), being fearful of samsāris’ ego and trying on their own efforts to attain mōkśam. The act of carp fish, hiding behind purple-coloured flowers and foraging for food, indicates the manner in which prapannas (those who have surrendered to emperumān), being fearful of how both the ego and self-efforts of samsāris in attaining emperumān would bring undesired results, take refuge under the bluish complexioned emperumān and enjoy his auspicious qualities as their pastime, without any fear.

**andaṇīr arangamē** – the huge temple in śrī rangam which is beautiful and which has water bodies which remove the ennui of everyone. jīvātmās suffer from *ādyātmikam* (suffering brought about by one’s physical form), *ādidaivikam* (suffering brought about due to emperumān’s deeds) and *ādibhaudikam* (suffering brought about by other creatures). śrīrangam removes these three sufferings of jīvātmās. Through the term *neelamē aṇḍai koṇḍu*, since the carp fish takes refuge under the purple-coloured water-lily which is dark and fragrant, it is implied here that for those who engage with the beauty of periya perumāḷ (emperumān), he remains as the means for uplifting them and the sweet entity for enjoying him. While outwardly, all the terms in the verse appear to refer to naturally occurring events \[crane eating crab, fish jumping in fear etc), is it acceptable to give inner meanings of each of these events? If āḻvār’s divine heart had not engaged with these inner meanings of events, there would have been no purpose behind living in śrī rangam, a divine abode mercifully desired by emperumān to dwell, and describing only the naturally occurring events. Hence, looking at inner meanings of the events and considering them for uplifting ourselves is appropriate.

Next, we will take up the 50th pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 50. Ven tiraik karum kadal

*veṇḍiraik karungadal sivandu vēva munnornāḷtiṇḍiṟal silaikkai vāḷi vitta vīrar sērumūreṇḍisaik kaṇangaḷum iṟainjiyādu tīrttanīrvaṇḍiraitta sōlaivēli mannuśir arangamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veṇ – being white; tirai – having waves; karu – being dark; kadal – ocean; sivandu – (due to the fire emanating from arrows) becoming red; vēva – to be burnt; munnornāḷ – during the 28th catur yugam (four yugams, together add up to 43,20,000 years); tiṇ tiṟal – being very strong; vāḷi – arrows; vitta – one who shot; vīrar – cakaravartit tirumagan, the masculine entity; sērum – dwelling; Ur – abode; eṇ tisai – in eight directions; kaṇangaḷum – assemblies of people; iṟainji – vorśipping divine feet; ādum – taking bath; tīrta – having purity; nīr – having water; vaṇḍu – beetles; iraitta – humming loudly; sōlai – gardens; vēli – having all around; sīr – (sacred and enjoyable) qualities; mannu – being seen permanently; arangamē – it is only śrī rangam

**Перевод:**

**cakravartit tirumagan shot very strong arrows during the 28th catur yugam, the fire emanating from the arrows burning the dark ocean with white waves. śri rangam is the divine abode where that śrī rāman dwells; it is the abode to which assemblies of people from eight directions come to take a bath in its water which has high purity; it is surrounded by gardens all around, with beetles humming loudly; it is only śrī rangam where \[emperumān’s\] sacred and enjoyable qualities are seen permanently.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār spoke about the greatness of śrī rāmāvatāram which removes the inner enemy of ego and also about the greatness of the divine abode śrī rangam which is the refuge where one can live without fear even if enemies were in proximity. In this pāsuram, he speaks about the greatness of śrī rāmāvatāram which removes enemy who is outside and about the greatness of śrī rangam which is apt to be attained by all.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**veṇḍiraik karungadal** – dark ocean which has whitish waves; this speaks about the abundance of waves since they overshadow the darkness of the ocean with their white complexion.

**sivandu vēva** – both the dark ocean and the white waves, without distinction, became reddish from the arrows shot by śrī rāma and got burnt. Through this, it affirms that since varuṇan (deity of water) was a hurdle for his protecting his followers such as sītāp pirātti and celestial women \[who were imprisoned by rāvaṇa earlier\], śrī rāma agitated ocean which was the dwelling place for varuṇan.

**munnor nāḷ tiṇ tiṟal silaikkai vāḷi vitta vīrar sērum Ur** – during the 28<sup>th</sup> catur yugam, śrī rāma took his bow, śrī śārngam, and aimed arrows at varuṇan, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 21-22 “*cāpamānaya saumitrē śarāmścāśi viśōpamān* l *sāgaram śōśayiśyāmi padbhyām yāntu plavangamā*: ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! Bring the bow and arrows which are like snake’s poison. I will make the ocean to go dry such that monkeys could reach (laknā) on their feet). śrī rangam is the dwelling place of such a great warrior, śrī rāma. In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned of this abode, “*uḷ magiḻnda nādanūr*“, where emperumān considered protecting his followers as his benefit. In this pāsuram, it mentions about the power of emperumān which controls the enemies of his followers. Through this, it is implied that emperumān is residing permanently in śrī rangam only in order to remove the engagement of his followers with worldly pursuits such as *śabda, sparśa, rūpa, rasa* and *ganda* (sound, touch, form, taste, smell respectively) which are hurdles for their enjoying him. Usage of the term *tiṇ tiṟal* wherein two words which refer to strength have been used together implies that the bow is a very strong one.

**eṇḍisaikkaṇangaḷum iṟainjiyādu tīrtta nīr** – the abode has water body which has the piousness of removing all the sins which are hurdles for realisation of desires for assemblies of people who come from the eight directions, worship the divine feet of emperumān and take a dip in the vaterbody. īsn’t the divine abode described as “*sarva śakti mayam dāma sarvatīrta mayam sara*: l *sarvapuṇyamayō dēśa*: *sarvadēvamayō hari*: ll” (The divine temple has all the powers; the divine pond has all the piousness; the divine abode has all the virtues; emperumān is the antaryāmi (indvelling soul) for all the celestial entities)! Since the verse says *tiṟtta nīr* in a general way, it refers to divine kāviri, chandra puśkariṇi and all the other divine water bodies in the abode. Since the verse says *gaṇangaḷum*, this refers to people of different classifications such as those who desire opulence, those who desire mōkśam, those who are eminent by way of their birth and activities, those who are lowly by the same count, etc.

**vaṇidraitta sōlai vēli** – having gardens all around where beetles, having drunk honey, hum loudly as a result of the happiness that they had attained due to drinking honey.

**mannu sīr arangamē** – the divine, big temple in śrī rangam which has greatness such as this piety, sweetness etc, at all times. Just as the divine abode makes those who do not have good qualities as pious \[due to its piousness\], it is sweet to those who have good qualities.

Next, we will take up the 51<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 51. Sarangalait turandu

*sarangaḷait turandu vil vaḷaittu ilangai mannavansirangal pattu aṟuttu udirtta selvar mannu ponnidamparandu pon nirandu nundi vandalaikkum vārpunalarangam enbar nānmugattayan paṇinda kōyilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vil – śrī śārngam (bow); vaḷaittu – drew; sarangaḷai – arrows; turandu – shot; langai – for lankā; mannavan – rāvaṇa, the king, his; sirangaḷ pattu – ten heads; aṟuttu – severed; udirtta – felled; selvar – rāma, who has the wealth of valour; mannu – dwelling forever; pon – desirous; pon nirandu nundi – pushing gold, without ever defaulting; parandu vandu – coming, in a widespread manner (in all places); alaikkum – with agitating; vār punal – having flowing water; nānmugattu – catur mukhan \[four-faced\]; ayan – brahmā; paṇinda – vorśipping; kōyilē – only this temple

**Перевод:**

**śrī rāma, drew his bow, śrī śārgnam and shot arrows at the ten heads of king of lankā, rāvaṇa and made them to fall down. People will say that it is only this temple, called as tiruvarangam, where that śrī rāma desirously dwells for ever. It is the place where water flows copiously, in a widespread manner, pushing gold, without ever defaulting and where brahmā, with four faces, worships.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(īntroeduction) In the previous pāsuram, the manner in which ocean was agitated and made to be subservient was mentioned. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that it is only in the divine abode of śrī rangam that śri rāma, who subsequently reached the shore of lankā and destroyed rāvaṇa, resides for ever and where brahmā, who created the universe with fourteen worlds, vorśipped in order to obtain his position \[as creator\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sarangaḷait turandu vil vaḷaittu** – drawing the bow śārngam and shooting arrows. t*hurattal* – shooting \[arrows\]. Being the lord of all, instead of killing rāvaṇa through his *sankalpam* (thought or vow), he killed the enemy of his followers with his hands, feeling happy that “I have got a matching enemy”, treating it as his benefit.

**ilangai mannavan** – lankā was such solid that even women, if they had this place, could win over enemies. rāvaṇan, the king of that lankā, was capable of winning against his enemies, even outside of lankā. Did not hanuman tell his companions, before flying into lankā, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 1-39? “*yatā rāghava nirmukta*: *Sara*: *śvasanavikrama*: l *gaccēt tadvad gamiśyāmi lankām rāvaṇa pālitām* ll” (Just like the arrow, shot by śrī rāma, moves like one’s breath (without anyone being aware of it), I will enter lankā which is protected by rāvaṇa)

**sirangaḷ pattaṟuttu udirtta selvar** – even after severing his ten heads, due to the strength of (rāvaṇa’s) boon, they reappeared repeatedly. śrī rāma, who was complete with the wealth of valour, kept severing them repeatedly, scattering them on the ground.

**mannu ponnidam** – the gold-like sweet place, where such great perumāḷ dwells forever; the place where followers search for the protector and where the protector seeks those to be protected, desirously. The implied meaning is that this is the place where emperumān dwells in order to destroy the minds along with the sensory organs of followers, who came much after his incarnation as śrī rāma, and who do not desire any benefit other than emperumān, such that their minds and sensory organs will not dwell on worldly pursuits. Has it not been said in sātvata samhitai 12-51 “*daśēndriyānanam ghōram yō manōrajanīcaram* l  
*vivēkaśarajālēna śamam nayati  yōginām* ll” (emperumān destroys the ten heads, which are like ten sensory organs, of the horrible minds of ascetics)? bhagavat gītā 6-5 too says that the mind which engages with worldly pursuits is inimical to jīvan “*uddarēd ātmanātmānam nātmānam avasādayēt* l *ātmaiva hyātmanō bandurātmaiva ripur ātmana*: ll” (with a mind (which does not engage with worldly pursuits) one should elevate the ātmā; with a mind (which engages with worldly pursuits), one should not lower the ātmā. (the earlier mentioned) mind is a close relative for ātmā; (the latter mentioned) mind alone is an enemy for ātmā).

**parandu pon nirandu nundi vandalaikkum vār punal** – river kāvēri which, due to its affection for arangan (śrī ranganātan), spreads out on both sides, pushing gems such as gold, rubies, pearls etc towards śrī rangam and flows with waves.

**vār punal** – water which floods; here, it refers to kāvēri.

**arangam enbar nānmugattayan paṇinda kōyilē** – brahmā is firmly engaged with two bhāvanas (pathos) – (1) karma bhāvanai (pathos of deeds) through activities such as creation; (2) brahma bhāvanai (pathos of devotion) through meditating on supreme entity. Such brahmā, in order to remove enemies who come after his position \[of creating the worlds\] and to enable him to attain mōkśam as the benefit of meditating on emperumān, such that he destroys the pride of being greater than anyone else on account of (a) having created the fourteen worlds (seven up to earth and seven above earth) (b) having four faces through which he recites the four vēdams simultaneously (c) having got the name of *ajan* (1. not being born like the others and 2. Having been born from śrīman nārāyaṇan who is referred to as akāravācyan (one who is denoted by the syllable of akāram)), attains the temple which sages, who have seen purāṇams, call as śrī rangam. Hasn’t the greatness of śrī rangam been mentioned in purāṇams too, just as in “*kśīrābdēr maṇḍalād bhānōr yōginām hrudayādapi* l *ratim gatō harir yatra rangamityabhidīyatē* ll” (That place where bhagavān attains *rati* (happiness) which is higher than tiruppāṟkadal, higher than the sūrya galaxy and higher than the hearts of ascetics, is called as rangam)? As per etymology, *ratim gata*: *iti rangam* – since this is the place where one feels happy it is called as rangam; thus, the greatness of śrī rangam has been mentioned in purāṇams too.

Next, we will take up the 52<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 52. Porrai urra

*poṟṟaiyuṟṟa muṟṟal yānai pōredirndu vandadaippaṟṟiyuṟṟu maṟṟadan maruppositta pāganūrsiṟṟeyiṟṟu muṟṟal mūngil mūnṟu daṇḍar onṟinaraṟṟapaṟṟar suṟṟi vāḻum andaṇīr arangamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

poṟṟai – atop rock; uṟṟa – standing; muṟṟal – very strong; yānai – kuvalayāpeedam; pōredirndu – opposing in battle; vandadai – one which came; uṟṟu – going towards it; paṟṟi – catching hold of; maṟṟu – beyond that; adan – that kuvalayāpeedam’s; maruppu – tusk; ositta – one who broke; pāgan – kriṣṇan, who looked like a mahout (elephant-keeper); Ur – the divine abode where he resides; siṟṟu – small; eyiṟṟu – nodes which looked like teeth; muṟṟal – being strong; mūngil – made of bamboo; mūnṟu daṇḍar – holding trident staff; onṟīnar – being deeply involved (with matters relating to bhagavān); aṟṟapaṟṟar – sanyāsis (ascetics) who have renounced their attachment towards everything; suṟṟi – thinking (of living in the temple in all manner as worth living); vāḻum – living forever; am – being beautiful; taṇ – cool; nīr – having water bodies which are full of water; arangam – it is only the huge temple

**Перевод:**

**śrīrangam is the divine abode where, kriṣṇa, who looked like a mahout and who caught hold of and broke the tusks of the elephant kuvalayāpeedam, which stood atop a rock, and came as if it were in a battlefield, resides. It is also the abode where ascetics, who hold a strong, bamboo trident staff having small nodes which look like teeth, and who are deeply involved with matters relating to bhagavān, think that living in the temple forever is a life worth living. It also has beautiful, cool, water bodies which are full of water.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the three previous pāsurams, āḻvār showed as to how emperumān had helped those who were born after his rāmāvatāram, by mercifully residing in the huge temple at śrī rangam. Now, in this as well as the next pāsuram, he shows as to how he helped those who came after his kriṣṇāvatāram. What is the connection between the earlier pāsuram and this? Unlike the previous pāsuram, wherein emperumān remained to be attained by brahmā, who is the head of all the prākruta worlds (those made with the help of primordial matter) and who is the most knowledgeable, in this pāsuram āḻvār says that emperumān remained in śrī rangam temple in order to be enjoyed by those who did not expect any benefit other than himself and who did not consider any means other than himself.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**poṟṟai uṟṟa muṟṟal yānai** – since it is standing atop a rock, it is a strong elephant. āḻvār hints that if it had been made to stand on sand, during battle, it would not have had firm legs; hence kamsan made it to stand atop a rock. The term *poṟṟai* refers to rock. Alternatively, since *poṟṟai* could also refer to a place in forest where elephants inhabit, without any contact with humans, this could mean that kamsan brought kuvalayāpeedam from the forest, due to which it became bewildered on looking at human beings and became angry enough to harm.

**pōr edirndu vandadai** – unlike the vaśerman who came perchance opposite kriṣṇan \[and got killed\], since this elephant was brought only to wage a battle with kriṣṇa, it came running, as if in a battle.

**paṟṟi uṟṟu** – without bothering about it, kriṣṇan went towards it on his own. Alternatively, it could mean that he went between its legs, caught hold of its body.

**maṟṟadan maruppositta pāgan Ur** – beyond that, he casually broke its tusks, which were meant for killing its enemies by piercing their bodies with them. The abode śrī rangam is the place where such kriṣṇan, who was like an elephant-keeper, dwells. Since the term *pāgan* has been used, which refers to one who keeps the elephant and looks after it by knowing its left and right sides \[viz. knowing fully about it\] and who controls it by harming it if required, this verse refers to kriṣṇa, who, acting like an elephant-keeper, hurt it in its most critical place and destroyed it.

Through this, it is implied that for those who came after kriṣṇāvatāram, periya perumāḷ would remove the hurdles which prevent his followers from enjoying him, just as he removed kuvalayāpeedam. ḍidn’t kuvalayāpeedam stand as a hurdle for śrī vasudēvar, dēvakip pirātti and the women of maturā who had assembled near the stage which kamsan had arranged for demonstrating one’s prowess in shooting arrows from bows, in looking at kaṇṇan? śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-20-54 says “*sakhya: paśyata kriṣṇasya mukhamatyaruṇēkśaṇam* l *vikāsiśāradarambhōjamavaśyāya jalōkśitam* l *paribhūya stitam janma saphalam kriyatām druśa*: ll” (Oh friends! Look at the eyes of kriṣṇa which have won over \[the beauty of\] lotus of autumn season, which has been sprinkled with dew. Make use of having been provided with eyes). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-20-52 says “*mahōtsavamivāsādya putrānana vilōkanam* l *yuvēva vasudēvō’būt vihābhyāgatām jarām* ll” (śrī vasudāvar, in order to look at the lotus-like face of his son, got rid of his old age and became a youth). Just as it has been mentioned in these ślōkams, didn’t kriṣṇa give himself to them for their enjoyment?

**siṟṟeyiṟṟu muṟṟal mūngil mūnṟu daṇḍar** – the ascetics, who were holding in their hands, firm bamboo trident staff which had small nodes, looking like teeth. Usage of the term *tridaṇḍar* distinguishes the ascetics from *brahmācāris* (those who are fit to learn vēdams and who are not yet married) who hold *palāśadaṇḍam* (a staff made of Indian coral tree) and *gruhastas* (married people) who hold single *daṇḍam* (staff). The term *eyiṟu* refers to teeth; here, it refers to the nodes present in the bamboo staff. *muṟṟal mūngil* refers to strong bamboo. śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 56-23 says “*digbalam kśatriya balam brahmatējō balam balam* l *ēkēna brahmadandēna sarvāstrāṇi hatānimē* ll” (Is the strength of being a king, real strength? The strength derived due to *brahmatējas* (the splendour of vorśipping the supreme entity) is the real strength. Due to the *brahmadaṇḍam* of vasiśtar, all my weapons were destroyed). viśvāmitra says that all his weapons were destroyed by the single staff of sage vasiśtar. āḻvār reminisces the strength of *tridaṇḍam* destroying all the weapons of others and not being destroyed by any of the other weapons. He shows that the strength of *tridaṇḍam* comes from the ascetics who hold them, through the next two terms.

**onṟinar** – we could construe this as those whose hearts were firmly entrenched with emperumān or as those who came to śrīrangam, considering it to be the ultimate benefit, leaving aside all the other divine abodes of emperumān.

**aṟṟa paṟṟar** – without attachment towards *aiśvaryam* (opulence) and *kaivalyam* (ātmā enjoying itself), without attachment towards other means such as *karma, jñāna* and *bhakti yōgams* (paths of deeds, knowledge and devotion, respectively), and considering that perumāḷ (emperumān) alone is the means and the ultimate benefit. The terms *onṟinar* and *aṟṟapaṟṟar* could be considered as stating the greatness of ascetics who were referred to by the term *mūnṟu daṇḍar* or they could be considered as describing all the *varṇāśramams* (those in the specific varṇam (brāhmaṇa, kśatriya etc) and specific āśramam (brahmacāri, gruhasta etc). In the former instance, these terms would convey the meaning “ascetics who hold the trident, who have hearts which are firmly entrenched with emperumān and who consider matters other than emperumān as being equivalent to grass”.

**suṟṟi vāḻum** – in all manner, living in the huge temple alone as life worth living. The term *suṟṟi* refers to being firm in all manner. Alternatively, *suṟṟal* could also refer to thinking. Thinking of the distinction of the temple, all the time, considering that alone as the sweetest way to live.

**andaṇīr arangamē** – śrī rangam huge temple alone is beautiful and has water flow which is invigorating.

Next, we will take up the 53<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.
