## 53. Modiyodu ilaccaiyaya

*mōdiyōdu ilaccaiyāya sābameydi mukkaṇānkūdu sēnai makkaḷōdu koṇḍu maṇḍi venjamattuōda vāṇan āyiram karam kaḻitta ādimālpīdu kōyil kūdu nīr arangam enṟa pēradē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ilaccaiyāya – (those with noble qualities) feel embarrassed to even look at her; sābam – curse; eydi – attained; mōdiyōdu – with kāḷi; makkaḷōdu – with sons; kūdu – assembly; sēnai – army; koṇḍu – taking them; venjamattu – in the battle; maṇḍi – very rapidly; mukkaṇān – Sivan, with three eyes; ōda – to run away; vāṇan – bāṇāsuran’s; āyiram karam – thousand hands; kaḻitta – one who severed; ādi – causative factor; māl – kriṣṇa’s; pīdu kōyil – huge temple; kūdu nīr – having abundance of water; arangam enṟa pēradu – has the divine name of śrī rangam

**Перевод:**

**kaṇṇan made Sivan, who has three eyes, to run away from the battle field along with kāḷi, who, people with noble qualities feel embarrassed even to look at, as well as with his sons and the assembly of army, rapidly. He also severed the thousand hands of bāṇāsuran. The huge temple, with abundant water bodies, where that kaṇṇan, who is he causative factor (for all the worlds), resides, has the divine name of śrī rangam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(īntroeduction) Just as kaṇṇan removed bāṇāsuran and others, who were strong and who were inimical to the desires of his grandson aniruddan, today, for removing hurdles for the desires of his followers, the divine abode where that kaṇṇan resides forever is the huge temple in śrī rangam, says āḻvār.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ilaccaiyāya sābam eydi mōdiyōdu** – for those who have *sātvika* guṇam (noble qualities), seeing her or talking about her is a matter of embarrassment. She has incurred the curse of not being cherished by them. *mōdi* is a name for kāḷi. Since she has tamō guṇam (qualities of laziness, ignorance etc) and since she has the bad habit of accepting animal sacrifices such as sheep, roosters etc, she is not favoured by those with noble qualities. Thus, āḻvār qualifies her by saying *ilaccaiyāya sābam eydi*. Alternatively, the verse could be rearranged as *sābam eydi ilaccaiyāya mukkaṇān*, in which case, the qualification \[of being with shame\] would shift to Sivan. In that case, just as it has been mentioned in mātsya purāṇam -182 “*brahmā: – yasmādanaparādasya śiraścinnam tvayā mama* l *tasmāccāpasamāviśta*: *kapālee tvam bhaviśyasi* ll” (brahmā said: since my head, which is without any deficiency, has been plucked by you, you will be seized by my curse and will become a *kapāli* (one who has skull in his hand)), since brahmā cursed him, he is the one with three eyes, who attained shame. Through śaivāgamam (rules and regulations governing establishment of temples for Sivan) Sivan instructed that he is sarvēśvaran (lord of all) and donned moon on his head. Through these, he manifested his strength, showered people who think that the body is soul (those with ego), with whatever they had asked for, without looking at whether that would lead to beneficence or munificence and making them to worship him. Due to the curse of brahmā, that he is a jīvan who is governed by the śāstrams (authentic, traditional texts) became known to everyone. Should one speak about the shame that this had caused him?

**kūdu sēnai makkaḷōdu koṇḍu maṇḍi venjamattu ōda** – along with murugan, the son of Sivan, who was spoken of, as in tiruvāimoḻi 7-4-8 “*nēr sarindān kodikkōḻi koṇḍān*” (subramanṇyan, who has rooster as his flag, got defeated, being unable to fight), along with several other sons, along with a huge assembly of army, kāḷi, agni et al, Sivan ran away from the battle, unable to cope with the skills of kaṇṇan and balarāman.

**vāṇan āyiram karam kaḻitta** – one who severed the thousand hands of bāṇāsuran, through his cakrāydam (divine disc). ḍidn’t periyāḻvār too say in his tiruppallāṇḍu 7 “*māyapporupadai vāṇanai āyiram tōḷum poḻi kurudi pāyac cuḻaṟṟiya āḻivallān*” (one who severed the thousand shoulders of bāṇāsuran with his divine disc such that the asuran was bleeding profusely and his army was destroyed)! emperumān destroyed the pride of rudran who had vowed that he will protect bāṇāsuran. He severed the shoulders of bāṇāsuran who was prideful of the strength of his shoulders.

**ādimāl** – the lord of all, who is the causative factor for the worlds. Was it not said in mahā bhāratam sabhā parvam 38-23? “*kriṣṇa ēvahi lokānāmutpattirapi cāpyaya*: l *kriṣṇasya hi krutē bhūtamidam viśvam carācaram* ll” (kriṣṇa is the causative factor for the creation, sustenance and destruction of all the worlds. The universe with stationary and mobile entities was created only for kriṣṇa).

**pīdu kōyil** – huge temple. The term *pīdu* refers to greatness.

**kūdu nīr arangam enṟa pēr adē** – having the divine name of tiruvarangam (śri rangam), surrounded by water gathered from the starting point in sahyādri mountain range and from the rains coming from regions to the east of that mountain range.

The verse should be rearranged as *sābam eydi ilaccaiyāya mukkaṇān mōdiyōdu makkaḷōdu kūdu sēnai koṇḍu venjamattu maṇḍi ōda vāṇan āyiram karam kaḻitta ādimāl kūdunīr arangam enṟa pēr adāna pīdu kōyil*.

The essence of the two pāsurams 52 and 53 is – irrespective of whether it is an animal such as kuvalayāpeedam elephant or the lord of celestial entities, rudran, emperumān removes them seeing only whether they are inimical to his followers. Since kuvalayāpeedam came to kill kaṇṇan, he mercifully killed that elephant. Since rudran came with the thought “Let me defeat kaṇṇan, protect vānan and make him victorious”, kaṇṇan made him to run away from the battle field, in fear. He severed the shoulders of vāṇan who thought, just as in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-33-2 “*kaccinmamaiśām bāhūnām sāpalya janakō raṇa*: l *bhaviśyati vinā yuddam bhārāya mama kim bhujai*: ll” (for my multifold pairs of hands, will battle, which gives benefit \[of being victorious\], take place? If there is no battle, what is the purpose of having so many hands which are a burden for my body?) Thus, the thoughts of his enemies would return to haunt them, is the essence of these two pāsurams.

Next, we will take up the 54<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 54. Ilaittalai

*ilaittalaic caram turandu ilangai kattaḻittavanmalaittalaip piṟandu iḻindu vandu nundu sandanamkulaittalaittu iṟutteṟinda kungumak kuḻambinōdualaittoḻugu kāviri arangamēya aṇṇalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ilai – like a leaf; nuni – having edge; saram – divine arrows; turandu – shot; ilangai – lankā; kattu – fortress; aḻittavan – rāmapirān who destroyed; malai – in sahya parvatam (mountain range of sahyādri region); talai – on the peak; piṟandu – having its origin; iḻindu vandu – flowing down; nundu sandanam – sandalwood trees which are pushed; kulaittu – pushing (creepers of saffron); alaittu – pulling; iṟuttu – snapping; eṟīnda – letting them out; kungumam kuḻambinōdu – along with the waters of saffron; alaittoḻugu – pulling these; kāviri – having divine kāvēri; arangamēya – one who is permanently residing in the divine temple; aṇṇalē – is the lord only

**Перевод:**

**śrī rāma shot arrows which had sharp edges, like leaves, to destroy the fortress of lankā. River kāvēri flows down from the peak of sahyādri mountain range, pushing sandalwood trees as well as creepers of saffron plants, snapping them as they are pushed and pulled. The divine temple at śrī rangam where such kāvēri flows into, is the place where śrī rāma resides permanently.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) lankā had a fortress which made it very difficult for good people who had sātvika guṇam (noble qualities) to go to lankā. cakravartit tirumagan destroyed that protection through his arrows. In order to destroy the fortress of ego which is the hurdle for his followers to attain him as well as to remove the ennui of tiredness, which resulted from the killing of rāvaṇa, by having his divine feet stroked by the divine hands of kāvēri, emperumān mercifully reclined in śrī rangm, says āḻvār in this pāsuram. In the previous two pāsurams, the way emperumān destroyed the outer enemies was mentioned. In this pāsuram, it is mentioned that emperumān, just as he had destroyed the fortress of lankā, destroys the inner enemy of svātantryam (being independent of emperumān\] which makes a follower to think, when he enjoys emperumān, that the happiness resulting from that enjoyment is his \[the follower’s\].*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ilaittalaic caram turandu ilangai kattaḻittavan** – cakravartit tirumagan shot divine arrows which had sharp edges like leaf and destroyed the fortress of lankā.

**ilaittalaiccaram** – arrow which had their edges resembling a leaf.

**kattu** – fortress; the narrative of rāmāvatāram is hinted here. Just as rāmapirān (śrī rāma) destroyed the ego of sūrppaṇakā who wanted to enjoy śrī rāma for her happiness, he mercifully reclines in śrī rangam in order to destroy the *svātantryam* (independence) of followers who come there to enjoy emperumān.

**malaittalaippiṟandu iḻindu vandu nundu sandanam** – having its origin on the peak of sahyādri mountain range, coming down the range with sandalwood trees which are uprooted by the heavy rains

**kulaittalaittu iṟuttu eṟinda kungumak kuḻambinōdu alaittu oḻugu kāviri** – saffron plant is on the ground with its creepers and flowers. The flowing kāvēri, once it reaches the ground level, pushes and snaps the creepers of saffron plant. Due to this, with the powders of saffron flowers, the entire area becomes muddy. Along with this muddy water, kāvēri pushes the sandalwood trees mentioned earlier. The great materials pushed down by kāvēri as it reaches śrī rangam have been described by parāśara bhattar in his śrīrangarāja stavam pūrva śatakam 21 “*dugdābdir janakō jananyahamiha śrīrēva putrī vara*: \| *śrī rangēśvara ētadarhamiha kim kuryāmitīvākulā* \|\| *sanjaccāmara candra candana mahāmāṇikya muktōtkarān* \| *kāvērī laharīkarair vidadatī paryēti sā sēvyatām* \|\|”  
(“tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) is the father; I (the wife of that ocean) am the mother; this śrī ranganāyaki is our daughter; aḻagiya maṇavāḷan (the utsavar at śrī rangam temple) is the son-in-law; what gifts can I get which will be appropriate for this daughter and this son-in-law?” kāvēri river, which was bewildered with such thoughts, brought the splendorous cāmaram \[fly flapper used as a fan for emperumān\], medicated camphor, sandalwood, eminent rubies, pearls etc, holding them with waves which are like her hands. We should get the fortune of having a holy dip in that kāvēri). Through this, it has been mentioned that kāvēri, impelled by her affection towards emperumān, and in order to remove the tiredness that he has due to destroying lankā, cools him with sandalwood and saffron, and carries out servitude to him.

**arangamēya aṇṇalē** – the lord who permanently resides in the great temple in śrī rangam. kāvēri comes down from sahyādri mountain to śrī rangam in order to serve śrī ranganātan. emperumān came down from tiruppāṟkadal to śrī rangam in order to be served by kāvēri. Even though the distance covered by emperumān to come to śrī rāngam is much longer than that covered by kāvēri, due to his partiality towards his followers, he treats the distance travelled by kāvēri to carry out servitude to him as the same as the distance travelled by him.

**aṇṇal** – lord. The opinion is that he is the svāmy (lord) who creates affection in the hearts of those who do not have affection for him, makes those who have affection for him to attain him and removes the ego which those who attain and who enjoy him thinking “I am enjoying emperumān for my happiness”.

Next, we will take up the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 55. Mannu mamalar

*mannu māmalark kiḻatti vaiya mangai maindanāypinnum āyar pinnai tōḷ maṇam puṇarndadanṟiyumunna pādam enna sindai manna vaittu nalgināyponni sūḻ arangameāya puṇḍarīgan allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mā – distinguished; malar – on a lotus flower; mannu – one who incarnated; kiḻatti – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); vaiyamangai – śrī bhūmip pirātti, for these two; maindanāy – as consort; pinnum – more than that; āyar – one who incarnated as cow-herd girl; pinnai – nappinnaip pirātti, who is neeḷā dēvi, her; tōḷ – shoulders; maṇam puṇarndu – embraced; adanṟiyum – beyond that; unna pādam – your divine feet; enna sindai – in my heart; manna vaittu – kept, without separating ever; nalgināy – you gave a great benefit; ponni – by the divine river kāvēri; sūḻ – surrounded; aranga mēya – one who resides permanently in the huge temple; puṇḍarīgan allaiyē – aren’t you the one with divine limbs which are like lotus?

**Перевод:**

**You are consort of śrī dēvi, who incarnated on a distinguished lotus flower, as well as śrī bhūmip pirātti. More than that, you embraced the shoulders of nappinnaip pirātti, who is neeḷā dēvi, and who was born as a cow-herd girl. Beyond these, you kept your divine feet in my heart, without ever separating from it, thus providing me with a great benefit. āren’t you the one with divine limbs which are like lotus, who is permanently residing in the huge temple in śrī rangam, which is surrounded by the divine river kāvēri?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān, after reclining in śrī rangam for the sake of his followers, despite being the consort of śrī dēvi, bhū dēvi and neeḷā dēvi, accepted him \[āḻvār\] too, such that he would not forget.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**mannu mā malark kiḻatti** – periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi), who is the queen consort, and who incarnated on a distinguished lotus flower which has softness, coolness and fragrance. amarakōśam 2-6-5 says “*krutābhiśēkā mahiśi bhōginyō’nyā nrupastriya*:” (one who is crowned along with the king is called as the queen consort; the other wives of the king are pleasure givers). Thus, since she is emperumān’s queen consort, when he protects the worlds, she is together with him. The term *kiḻatti* refers to one who is fit to be crowned.

**vaiya mangai maindanāy** – being the dear consort of śrī bhūmip pirātti (bhū dēvi). Just as the saying goes “*yuvatiśca kumāriṇī*” (being more in the stage of an adolescent, with a little bit of youthfulness), being appropriate for her stage of being a *mangai* (one who is around 12 to 13 years of age), he \[emperumān\] is in the stage, as said in rig vēda aśtakam 2-2-25 “*yuvā’kumāra*:” (one who is more in the stage of youth, with a little bit of adolescence), a consort in the stage of a *kāḷai* (one who is around 16 to 17 years of age).  The term *maindan* refers to one who is young.

**pinnum** – beyond that. Since śrī mahālakśmi, who is the presiding deity for wealth, is his queen consort, it is clear that he is the supreme entity, above all the other deities. Since bhūmip pirātti is the repository for all the enjoyments, it is clear that he has all the enjoyments. Beyond this, he also has neeḷā dēvi as his consort, says the pāsuram. yajur samhitai 4-41-1 says “*asyēśānā jagatō viṣṇu patnī*” (one who is the controller of the world and who is the consort of viṣṇu); puruśa sūktam 2-25 says “*hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau*” (Oh the supreme entity! śrī and bhū dēvis are your consorts); tiruvāimoḻi 8-1-1 says “*dēvimārāvār tirumagaḷ bhūmi*” (śrī dēvi and bhū dēvi are your consorts). Thus, haven’t samskruta and tamiḻ vēdams called śrī dēvi and bhū dēvi as his consorts? The usage of the syllable *ca* in *lakśmiśca* (as said in the above-mentioned ślōkam *hrīśca tē lakśmīsca)* indicates that neeḷā dēvi has also been included in this.

**āyar pinnai tōḷ maṇam puṇarndu** – having the enjoyment of embracing the divine shoulder of nappinnaip pirātti who was born in the eminent clan of herd-people.

**adanṟiyum** – while there has been no deficiency in your enjoyments, further to these

**unna pādam enna sindai manna vaittu nalgināy** – just as it has been said in tirukkuṟundāṇḍagam 7 “*amarar sennippū*” (the divine feet of emperumān would be like a flower on the neck of nityasūris), you had kept your divine feet, which are enjoyed by nityasūris, in my heart, even as I had been toiling in samsāram since time immemorial, not leaving my heart even for a moment, thus showing your partiality towards me. The quality of *vātsalyam* which emperumān has, makes him to consider the faults in followers as good qualities, not allowing him to look at his eminence and the lowliness of his followers!

**ponni sūḻ arangamēya puṇḍarīgan allaiyē** – āren’t you the one, having limbs which are like lotus flower, living in śrī rangam, which is surrounded by river kāvēri? tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-1 says “*sendāmarait tadam kaṇ senganivāy sengamalam sendāmarai adikkaḷ*” (your divine wide eyes are like reddish lotus, your fruit-like divine mouth is like a red lotus, your divine feet are like lotus); tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-3 says “*kaṇ pādam kai kamalam*” (your divine eyes, divine feet and divine hands are like lotus); as seen in these pāsurams, since emperumān’s divine eyes, hands, feet, mouth etc are like lotus, āḻvār calls emperumān as *puṇḍarīgan* (one who is like a lotus). taittirīya upaniśat bhruguvalli 7 says “*ānandō brahmēti vyajānāt*” (he knew that brahmam (supreme entity) is happiness); taittirīyavalli upaniśat bhruguvalli 6 says “*vijjñānam brahmēti vyajānāt*” (he knew that brahmam is knowledge). Just as upaniśats have said that the supreme entity is bliss and knowledge (since he has happiness and knowledge in abundance), through the latter part of the pāsuram, āḻvār says that more than the freshness that emperumān’s divine limbs attained due to residing in śrī rangam which removes ennui, they attained freshness through contact with him.

Next, we will take up the 56<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 56. Ilangai mannan

*ilangai – for lankāmannan – king rāvaṇa’saindodaindu – tenpaindalai – heads, vhic are strongnilattu – on eartuga – to fall dovnkalanga – (rāvaṇa to) get bevilderedanṟu – at an earlier point of timesenṟu – vent to his dvelling placekonṟu – destroyed himvenṟi koṇḍa – one vho became victoriousvīranē- ōh varrior!vilangu – on te bodynūlar – having te yajjñōpavītam (sacred tread)vēda – vēdas (sacred texts)nāvar – having on teir tonguesnīdiyāna – honestkēḷviyār – brāhmaṇas, vho give instructions, teirvalam – circumambulationskoḷa – to acceptkudandaiyuḷ – mercifully reclined inside tirukkudandaimālum allaiyē – aren’t you te lord of all!*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh warrior, who became victorious by going to the dwelling place of rāvaṇa, the king of lankā, and destroyed the bewildered king by making his ten heads, which are strong, to fall on earth! āren’t you the lord of all, who mercifully reclined inside tirukkudandai and accepted the circumambulations of brāhmaṇas who have sacred thread on their physical forms, sacred vēdas on their tongues and who give honest instructions!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Starting from this pāsuram, for six pāsurams, āḻvār is enjoying the manner in which emperumān is reclining in tirukkudandai (present day kumbakōṇam). Through this first pāsuram, he enjoys the manner in which emperumān is lying down in tirukkudandai, after destroying rāvaṇa, the enemy of his followers, such that he could remove the hurdles for his followers who came in later years in attaining him,and who consider him alone as the benefit and to accept their salutation and circumambulation activities which manifest their love towards him. ilangai mannan aindodaindu paindalai nilattugakkalanga anṟu senṟu konṟu venṟi koṇḍa vīranēvilangu nūlar vēda nāvar nīdiyāna kēḷviyārvalam koḷak kudandaiyuḷ kidanda mālum allaiyē Word-for-Word Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ilangai mannan** – just as wasps which have the habit of harming others stay together in an apiary, demons, who have the habit of harming people, lived together in lankā, for which rāvaṇa was the king. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-12 “*rāvaṇō nāma durvruttō rākśasō rākśasēśvara*: l *tasyāhamanujō bhrātā vibhīśaṇa iti śruta*: ll” (There is a person, rāvaṇa, who is a demon, having bad conduct and who is the leader of demons. I, who am his brother, am known by the name vibhīśaṇa), vasn’t rāvaṇa the one under whom all the demons stayed?

**aindodaindu paindalai nilattuga** – the ten heads, which are difficult to be cut off, got severed and fell on the ground.

**paindalai** – strong heads. Just as it has been said in sātvata samhitai 12-51 “*daśēndriyānanam ghōram yō manōrajanīcaram* l *vivēkaśarajālēna śamam nayati yōginām* ll” (emperumān destroys the ten heads, which are like ten sensory organs, of the horrible minds of ascetics), in the inner meaning for this verse, āḻvār hints that emperumān removes the hurdles of five sensory organs which are for deeds and five sensory organs which are for knowledge, of his followers such that they could attain him.

**kalanga anṟu senṟu konṟu venṟī koṇḍa vīranē** – rāvaṇa was one who did not know fear. When he was living at a time with the fame that celestial entities had given up their kingdoms to him and he was living fearlessly, śrī rāma made him fearful and bewildered, not knowing what to do. Oh the warrior, who is like a manly tiger, who annihilated his brothers kara and dūśaṇa, in janastānam (a place near citrakūtam, in present day madya pradēśam) along with their armies, built a dam across the ocean, went to lankā, killed that rāvaṇa and remained victorious!

**vilangu nūlar** – those who don the sacred thread on their physical forms as a proof of performing activities all the time, as mentioned in vēdas. The term *vilangu* refers to physical form.

**vēda nāvar** – since they recite vēdams all the time, reciting vēdam becomes a proof for their mouths.

**nīdiyāna kēḷviyār** – brāhmaṇas who have received instructions from good preceptors that emperumān, the lord for all, is apt to be vorśipped through all deeds.

**valam koḷak kudandaiyuḷ kidanda mālum allaiyē** – aren’t you the head of nityasūris, mercifully residing in tirukkudandai such that you could accept the salutations and circumambulations of those brāhmaṇas? *māl* – one who is greater than all. The term *valam* refers to circumambulation and is symbolic of all the favourable activities undertaken by followers of emperumān. Thus, through this pāsuram, āḻvār has said that he has enjoyed the masculine character of śrī rāma in śārngapāṇi \[name of emperumān lying in tirukkudandai\], in the divine abode of tirukkudandai.

Next, we will take up the 57<sup>th</sup>  pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 57. Sangu tangu

*sangu tangu munkai nangai kongai tangal uṟṟavanangamanga anṟu senṟadarttu eṟinda āḻiyānkongu tangu vārkuḻal madandaimār kudanda nīrpongu taṇ kudandaiyuḷ kidanda puṇḍarīganē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sangu – bangles; tangu – remaining; mun kai – having forearm; nangai – sītāp pirātti’s; kongai – in her divine bosom; tangal – to remain; uṟṟavan – rāvaṇa, who thought of; angam – physical form; manga – to be destroyed; anṟu – on a day, at that time; senṟu – went (to the place where he resided); adarttu – fought with; eṟīnda – severed his heads; āḻiyān – one who has a divine form which is invigorating like an ocean; kongu – fragrance; tangu – present always; vār – being long; kuḻal – having locks; madandaimār – women; kudainda – took a bath by dipping in; nīr – water; pongu – since it overflows; taṇ – being cool; kudandaiyuḷ – in divine tirukkudandai; kidanda – one who is mercifully reclining; puṇḍarīganē – it is only ārāvamudāḻvān (emperumān’s divine name in kudandai) who has limbs which are like lotus

**Перевод:**

**rāvaṇa wanted to embrace sītāp pirātti, who had bangles on her forearm. emperumān went to that rāvaṇa’s place and destroyed him by severing his head. āren’t you ārāvamudāḻvān, with divine limbs like lotus, who has a divine form like the invigorating ocean and who is mercifully reclining in tirukkudandai, where women, with fragrant hair, take a dip in the water which is cool due to that!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned that emperumān removes hurdles for his followers such that they could attain him and that he accepts their servitude. In this pāsuram, āḻvār enjoys the way emperumān is reclining in tirukkudandai in order to remove the hurdles for his followers so that they could attain him, just as he removed the hurdle for pirātti, who never separates from him, for her bhagavadanubhavam (enjoyment with emperumān) by destroying rāvaṇa.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sangu tangu mun kai** – sītāp pirātti whose bangles will not come out of her forearm. When emperumān takes hold of her hand, it is the bangles which he praises. When he separates from her, it is the bangles which will slip out of her hands. Hence, āḻvār is referring to her bangles. *sangu* – bangles made of conch. Since the term *tangu* refers to the fact that the bangles will always be present on her forearm, it indicates that pirātti will be always together with emperumān.

**nangai** – one who is complete with the beauty of her divine form, with the auspicious qualities of her divine form such as softness etc and with the auspicious qualities of her divine soul such as mercy, patience etc. sītāp pirātti is one who is just as she has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-5 “*tulya śeelavayōvruttām tulyābhijanalakśaṇām* l  
*rāgavō’rhati vaidēhim tam cēyamasitēkśaṇā* ll” (for vaidēhi who has (with śrī rāma) matching qualities, age and conduct, śrī rāma is well suited; with her well-matched pedigree and characteristics, this black-eyed sītā is also well suited to him). In such sītāp pirātti’s case …

**kongai tangal uṟṟavan angam manga** – while it is appropriate to take refuge under the divine feet of mother, without even considering that she is the mother, rāvaṇa thought of embracing the bosom of such pirātti. To destroy the physical form of that rāvaṇa who thought of carrying out the most inappropriate act. *tangal* – to embrace. *uṟṟavan* – one who thought of. The reason for such a wicked thought is that rāvaṇa considered his body as soul. Hence, śrī rāma thought of destroying the body \[of rāvaṇa\] which was the case for his pride.

**anṟu** – this could be considered as – on that day when \[rāvaṇa\] separated perumāḷ and pirātti on either side of ocean, just like separating body and soul, or as – on that day when pirātti, reaching the extreme limit of her sorrow, wanted to end her life by hanging herself by knotting her hair around her neck.

**senṟu** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 1-29 “*abhiyātā prahartā ca sēnānayaviśārada*:: (śrī rāma is one who goes to enemy’s place to wage a war; he is an expert on the rules of war), since he was a great warrior, he went to rāvaṇa’s place.

**adartteṟinda āḻiyān** – laying siege on lankā, śrī rāma severed the head of rāvaṇa, and through that bliss, he has a divine form which would remove the ennui of the beholder. *eṟidal* – severing the head.

**kongu tangu vārkuḻal madandaimār** – women, with long hair, which had lasting fragrance.

**kudainda nīr pongu taṇ kudandaiyuḷ**– the water body in which they had a bath, flowed abundantly due to contact with them. Due to this, tirukkudandai was able to remove the ennui of everyone. With this verse, it is hinted that the distinguishing adjectives used for tirukkudandai could be applied to women who welcome followers who reach paramapadam, just as it has been mentioned in kauśīdakī upaniśat 1-34 “*tam pancaśatānyaprasarasām pratidāvanti śatam mālā hastā*: \| *śatam anjana hastā*: *śatam cūrṇa hastā*: *śatam vāsō hastā*: \|\| *śatam paṇa hastās tam brahmālanārēṇālankurvanti* \|\|” (500 celestial ladies hasten towards that muktātmā \[who has just reached parama padam\]; 100 proceed with garlands in their hands; hundred with black pigment in their hands, for the eyes; hundred with fragrant body powder in their hands; hundred with divine dresses in their hands; hundred with ornaments in their hands; they decorate him with the decorations of brahmam \[supreme entity\]). This verse is also applicable to women who carry out servitude to ārāvamudan here \[in tirukkudandai\].

**kidanda puṇḍarīganē** – emperumān is reclining in tirukkudandai like a forest of lotus flower having blossomed on the entire surface of a pond, with his divine limbs of eyes, hands, legs etc resembling lotus in such a way that his followers could enjoy him.

Thus, through the 56<sup>th</sup> and 57<sup>th</sup> pāsurams, āḻvār has enjoyed the valour and beauty of cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāman, the son of emperor daśaratan) in the divine form of ārāvamudan (the presiding idol in tirukkudandai). In the next two pāsurams, he enjoys the valour and beauty of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) in the divine form of ārāvamudan.

Next, we will take up the 58<sup>th</sup>  pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 58. Maram keda nadandu

*marangeda nadandadarttu mattayānai mattagattuturangedap pudaittor kombosittu uganda uttamāturangam vāy piḷandu maṇṇaḷanda pāda vēdiyarvarangoḷak kudandaiyuḷ kidanda mālum allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

maram – the twin maruda maram (a species of tree called as Indian laurel); keda – to be destroyed; nadandu – crawled; matta – being in rut; yānai – the elephant, kuvalayāpeedam; adarttu – controlled; mattagattu – (on its) forehead; uram – strength; keda – to be crushed; pudaittu – hit; or – unique; kombu – tusks; osittu – broke; uganda – felt happy; uttamā – Oh puruśōttama (best among souls)!; turangam – \[demon\] kēśi’s; vāy – face; piḷandu – tore; maṇṇaḷanda pāda – (as śrī vāmana, standing with mahābali) one who has divine feet which measured the earth; vēdiyar – experts in vēdam; varam koḷa – to obtain boons (from you); kudandaiyuḷ – in tirukkudandai; kidanda – one who has mercifully reclined; mālum allaiyē – aren’t you the dear one!

**Перевод:**

**You crawled between the twin maruda trees such that they were destroyed. Oh the best among all souls! You controlled the elephant kuvalayāpeedam, which was in rut, hit it on its forehead such that its strength was destroyed, broke its unique tusks and felt happy. You tore the face of the demon kēśi. You have the divine feet with which you measured the earth. āren’t you the one who is dear towards your followers, reclining in the divine abode of tirukkudandai such that they could get boons from you!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Haven’t you, who destroyed enemies such as yamaḷārjunas, for the sake of your followers, in order to help those who came after that time, reclined in tirukkudandai due to your great affection for your followers!”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maram keda nadandu** – just as it has been said by periyāḻvār in his periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-1-3 “*poymmāya marudāna asuraraip ponṟuvittu*” (you made two maruda trees in which two demons, who were deceitful and treacherous, had pervaded, to fall down by tearing into them), two celestial entities, naḷakūbara and maṇigrīva, due to a curse, were turned into trees, inside which two demons, ordered by kamsan, had pervaded, waiting to kill kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa). You crawled between those two trees, releasing the two celestial entities from their curse and killing the two demons, by felling the trees. The word *nadandu* refers to crawling and taking baby-steps. ḍidn’t tirumangai āḻvār too mercifully say in periya tirumoḻi 11-4-9 “*iṇaimarudu iṟṟu vīḻa nadai kaṟṟa teṟṟal*” (the intelligent child learnt how to walk by making the pair of maruda trees to fall down, after being snapped)!

**adarttu mattayānai** – kamsan had made his elephant kuvalayāpeedam to be in rut; kaṇṇan controlled that elephant by destroying its pride of being in rut.

**mattagatturam kedap pudaittor kombosittu** – the verse should be rearranged as “*or kombosittu, uram keda mattagattup pudaittu*” to get a cogent meaning – breaking the unique tusks and hitting it on its forehead such that its strength was destroyed; kaṇṇan went among its four legs such that its pride of being in rut was destroyed. Thus, āḻvār explains the way it was destroyed.

**uganda uttamā** – Oh puruśōttama, who felt ecstatic that your followers’ enemy was killed! Since for the word *uttamā*, the term *uganda* has been used as a distinguishing feature, it is implied here that emperumān, who is superior to everyone else through his nature, is superior to everyone else through his auspicious qualities too by way of being partial towards his followers. īsn’t it through the greatness of one’s qualities that an entity earns its greatness?

**turangam vāy piḷandu** – tearing the mouth of the monster kēśi, who came in the form of a horse, during dusk time, in order to destroy śrī gōkulam.

**maṇṇaḷanda pāda** – Oh one who has the divine feet which you placed on the heads of everyone, as śrī vāmana! Since kriṣṇāvatāram and vāmanāvatāram resemble each other closely, as soon as āḻvār mentioned the valorous activity of kaṇṇan through the destruction of kēśi, he mentions the measurement of worlds too as an activity of kaṇṇan. Through the act of touching everyone, without making any distinction between the eminent and the lowly, as well as melting the hearts of enemies through their divine beauty, both kriṣṇāvatāram and vāmanāvatāram resemble each other.

**vēdiyar varangoḷak kudandaiyuḷ kidanda mālum allaiyē** – āren’t you the one who has deep affection towards those who know the inner meanings of vēdams such that they could seek their desires from you and attain them! *māl* – one who has deep love towards his followers. emperumān destroyed the deceitful monsters who hid themselves inside the maruda trees through his child-like activity \[of crawling\]. He casually destroyed kuvalayāpeedam and kēśi who came revealing themselves as his enemies. At a time when mahābali and the people of the world did not know the proper order \[that he is the lord and they are his possession\], he kept his divine feet on everyone’s head and made them realise that he is the lord, and *puruśōttaman* (the best among all souls). āḻvār says that tirukkudandai is the divine abode where such emperumān, in order to help those who came after his divine incarnations, through his love, mercifully reclined.

Next, we will take up the 59<sup>th</sup>  pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 59. Sali veli

*sāli vēli taṇ vayal tadam kidangu pūmboḻilkōlamāda nīdu taṇ kudandai mēya kōvalākālanēmi vakkaran karan muran siram avaikālanōdu kūda viṟ kunitta viṟkai vīranē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sāli – crop of reddish paddy; vēli – having all around; taṇ – cool; vayal – fields; tadam – (within it) being expansive; kidangu – tank; pūm – (within it) blossoming; poḻil – orchards, having all these; kōla – beautiful; nīdu – eminent; mādam – having residences; taṇ – cool; kudandai mēya – residing permanently in the divine abode of kudandai; kōvalā – Oh kriṣṇa!; kālanēmi – demon kālanēmi; vakkaran – dantavakran (brother of śisupālan); karan – cruel; muran – demon muran, all these people’s; siramavai – (severing) heads; kālanōdu – with yama (the deity of death); kūda – to join; vil – bow; kunitta – one who drew; vil kai vīranē – oh one who is valorous with bow!

**Перевод:**

**The divine abode of tirukkudandai is surrounded by fields having crop of reddish paddy, having expansive tanks within them. These moats have blossoming orchards. Cool, beautiful, eminent residences are also present. Oh kriṣṇa, who has taken permanent residence in this divine abode of kudandai! Oh one who is valorous with the bow! You severed the heads of demons kālanēmi, dantavakkaran and muran so that they could join yama, through your bow.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) From the day he incarnated in dvāpara yugam, kaṇṇan was destroying enemies of his followers with his great strength. He made his followers, through his beauty and auspicious qualities, to carry out servitude towards him. āḻvār enjoys in this pāsuram saying that such kaṇṇan mercifully reclined in tirukkudandai which is complete in all respect, for those born in kali yugam to get rid of their sorrows.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sāli vēli taṇ vayal tadam kidangu pūmboḻil** – having fields of reddish paddy, which remove the ennui of beholders, all around, having expansive moats within those fields, and having blossoming orchards within that. Through all these, it is hinted that the divine abode is sweetly appropriate for the tenderness of ārāvamudan who has mercifully taken residence in the temple inside kudandai.

**kōlamāda nīdu taṇ kudandai mēya kōvalā** – Oh kaṇṇa, who lives permanently inside tirukkudandai which has mansions which are tall and which are great to look at! Through this, it is mentioned that it is an appropriate place of enjoyment for the appreciative character of emperumān. The reason for identifying ārāvamudan as kōvalā (one who tends to cows) is because we see the qualities of beauty, simplicity and affability which are associated with kaṇṇan, in ārāvamudan. Since he is matching kriṣṇāvatāram in all aspects, instead of calling him after any other incarnation, we have to call him as kaṇṇan only. Hasn’t nammāḻvār too, in his tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-7 referred to those who welcome muktātmās in śrī vaikuṇṭam as “*kudandai em kōvalan kudiyadiyār*” (servitors, for generations together, of the protector of cows who is mercifully reclining in tirukkudandai)?

**kālanēmi vakkaran karan muran siram avai** – severing the heads of kālanēmi, dantavakran and the cruel muran. We have to add the word ‘severing’ to get the cogent meaning.

**kālanōdu kūda vil kunitta** – Oh one who killed them with your bow instead of your *sankalpam* (vow)! yama, the deity of righteousness, would be frightened on hearing their names; you made him to hand out punishment to them and for them to run in fear.

**viṟkai vīranē** – to destroy the enemies of followers, there was no need even for drawing the bow. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkintā kāṇḍam 3-9 “*simha viprēkśitau vīrau simhātibalavikramau* l *śakracāpanibhē cāpē gruhītvā śatrunāśanē* ll” (those who have vision like a lion’s; warriors; those who have more strength and valour than a lion; those who hold the bow which destroy enemies and which match the bow of indra’s), once the enemies see emperumān holding bow in his hands, they will get destroyed – one with such masculinity.

Next, we will take up the 60<sup>th</sup>  pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 60. Selum kolum

*seḻungoḻum perum pani poḻindida uyarnda vēyviḻundularndu eḻundu viṇ pudaikkum vēngadattuḷ ninṟueḻundirundu tēn porundu pūm poḻil taḻaik koḻumseḻundadak kudandaiyuḷ kidanda mālum allaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

seḻum koḻum – being huge; perum pani – misty rain; poḻindida – to pour copiously; uyarnda vēy – tall bamboo; viḻundu – (due to the weight of that misty rain) leaning towards the ground; ularndu – (later, due to the rays of sun) getting dried; eḻundu – rising; viṇ – sky; pudaikkum – will hit; vēngadattuḷ – in tiruvēngadam (divine hill of tirumalai); ninṟu – standing; tēn – beetles; eḻundu – rising far; irundu – again coming downwards; porundu – living; taḻai – thriving; koḻu – being fertile; pūm – blossoming; poḻil – having orchards; seḻum tadam – (within that) having fertile ponds; kudandaiyuḷ – inside tirukkudandai; kidanda – one who mercifully reclined; māl allaiyē – aren’t you the dear one?

**Перевод:**

**When misty rainfall pours down the hills of tiruvēngadam, the tall bamboo trees will bend down and lean towards the ground. After the rain stops and sun’s rays fall on them, the same bamboo trees will dry up and rise to the sky. In tirukkudandai, beetles will rise far above and then come down again, in thriving and fertile orchards which are blossoming, which has fertile ponds. Are you not the one who has love towards your followers, reclining mercifully in that tirukkudandai?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(īntroruction) emperumān, instead of thinking “The abodes where I reside in arcā (idol) form are for the followers to attain me and the only place where they can enjoy me is paramapadam”, just as it has been said in periya tirumoḻi 6-9-8 “nilaiyāra ninṟān” ((emperumān) stood forever), showed his standing posture in tirumalai for those who are involved with his posture of standing. He showed his reclining posture, just as it has been said in tirumālai 23 “kidandadōr kidakkai” (the manner in which emperumān reclined) in tirukkudandai for those who are involved with his posture of reclining. āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān has made his followers to enjoy his beauty here \[tiruvēngadam\] itself, only because of his great love towards them.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**seḻum koḻum perum pani poḻindida** – thick, misty rain, as it falls down copiously. s*eḻumai* – greatness; *koḻumai* – richness. Thus, through these two words it says that the thick mist is falling down continuously, without any break. *seḻumai* could also be taken as referring to magnanimity. āḻvār is describing mist in this way since everything related to tirumalai attracts him deeply.

**uyarnda vēy viḻundu** – this says that the tall bamboo trees slant down due to the dense mist. Instead of taking the words *seḻum* and *koḻum* to refer to mist, as said earlier, if we assume them to refer to *uyarnda vēy* (tall bamboo), then it would mean that the tall bamboo trees which are thick, have been nurtured very carefully. We could consider that the earnestness of tiruvēngadamudaiyān (emperumān’s divine name in tirumalai) resulting from his distinguished mercy is functioning as water and manure for bamboo, to make them grow thick.

**ularndu eḻundu viṇ pudaikkum vēngadattuḷ ninṟu** – since mist is driven away by the rays of sun, the bamboo trees, as mentioned earlier, will rear their heads up once the mist dries out and stand tall on the peaks of tirumalai which rise up to the sky. Here, the result of bamboo trees leaning towards ground and rising up to the sky is symbolic of all entities in tirumalai which will hold their heads high with the distinguished mercy of tiruvēngadamudaiyān and lower their heads in its absence.

**eḻundirundu tēn porundu pūmboḻil taḻaikkoḻum** – beetles will initially rise up flying, spreading themselves and then come down towards the flowers to stay lor long, drinking honey. Since the orchards too have been nurtured through the distinguished mercy of emperumān, they are also dense and blossom everywhere. The term *tēn* refers to beetles. Since it has been said that they rise up and then come down, this indicates that they are unable to live forever on the flowers due to the excessive secretion of honey in the orchard, while at the same time, they are unable to let go of the flowers forever.

**seḻum tadam kudandaiyuḷ** – on the inner side of the orchards, ponds are located, in such tirukkudandai. Due to the usage of these adjectives, it is indicated that in this divine abode, even entities which do not have distinguished knowledge \[such as bamboo, orchards, ponds and beetles\] attain what they desire and live happily and that the divine abode removes the ennui suffered by people due to the three sufferings of *ādyātmikam* (that which results from one’s own actions), *ādi daivikam* (that which results due to  emperumān) and *ādi bhaudikam* (that which results from other entities in the world).

**kidanda mālum allaiyē** – aren’t you one with great beauty who is reclining with deep affection towards your followers, with the thought “Who is going to involve with the beauty of our reclining posture and is going to enjoy this?”

Next, we will take up the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 61. Nadanda kalgal

*nadanda kālgaḷ nondavō nadunga jñālam ēnamāyidanda mey kulangavō vilangu māl varaic curamkadanda kāl paranda kāvirik karaik kudandaiyuḷkidandavāṟu eḻundirundu pēsu vāḻi kēsanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nadanda – measured the worlds (alternatively, walked in the forest); kālgaḷ – divine feet; nondavō – did they suffer?; jñālam nadunga – when bhūmip pirātti trembled (not seeing the protector); ēnamāy – as a great boar; idanda – dug her out (from the walls of universe); mey – divine form; kulungavō – was it the tiredness due to the jolt?; vilangu – being a hurdle; mālvarai – huge mountains; suram – forests, all these; kadanda – crossed; kāl paranda – having widespread flow; kāviri – divine kāvēri \[river\]; karai – on the bank; kudandaiyuḷ – inside tirukkudandai; kidandavāṟu – the reason for reclining; eḻundirundu – standing up; pēsu – you should mercifully say; kēsanē – Oh kēsavanē!; vāḻi – let auspicious things happen (to you)

**Перевод:**

**Did your divine feet suffer when they measured the worlds (or, when you walked in the forests)? Did your divine form become tired due to the jolt of digging out bhūmip pirātti (from the walls of the universe), in the form of a great boar, when she trembled in fear (at not seeing you)? Oh kēśava! You should stand up and mercifully tell me the reason for your reclining like this in tirukkudandai which is on the bank of river kāvēri, which is flowing past the hurdles of huge mountains and forests. Let auspicious things happen to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) As mentioned in the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had started enjoying at the divine feet of ārāvamudāḻvār (emperumān) in tirukkudandai, which is the abode for enjoyment. He saw that emperumān was not speaking to him or embracing him. Due to his deep affection for emperumān, āḻvār had a doubt “Perhaps, with his soft divine form, since he had undertaken very difficult to perform deeds such as measuring the worlds etc, he had become tired; he is reclining to get rid of that tiredness”. He says in this pāsuram “You should mercifully state the reason for reclining like this and remove my fear”. In tiruvehkā (a divine abode in kāncīpuram), emperumān, in his arcāvatāra form, had done what āḻvār had asked him to do. āḻvār thinks that this perumāḷ too, though being in arcāvatāra form, should respond to the question raised by him and hence says “eḻundirundu pēsu” (you should get up and speak).*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nadanda kālgaḷ nondavō** – Just as it has been said by nammāḻvār in tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-10 “*vadiviṇaiyillā malarmagaḷ maṟṟai nilamagaḷ pidikkum melladi*” (the soft divine feet gently massaged by śrī dēvi and bhū dēvi, both of whom have unmatched divine forms), your divine feet are such soft that they will become red even if your divine consorts touch them. Are you reclining because you had measured the worlds with such soft feet which suffered due to that activity of measuring? ōr, as śrī rāma, you had walked for many years through forests and mountains, with those soft feet. Unable to bear the suffering due to that, have you reclined? emperumān’s divine feet are such soft that daśarata cakravarti had to cry out, just as it has been mentioned by kulaśēkharap perumāḷ in his perumāḷ tirumoḻi 9-2 “*evvāṟu nadandanai emmirāmāvō*” (Oh rāma! How did you walk on those rough lands with your soft feet?) āḻvār is asking emperumān whether his divine feet suffered during trivikramāvatāram or rāmāvatāram so that he could massage the divine feet to get rid of their suffering.

**nadunga jñālam ēnamāy idanda mey kulungavō** – ōr else, when bhūmip pirātti, who is the esteemed deity for earth, was hidden by hiraṇyākśan in the nether world, when she was trembling as to how she was going to be lifted up, you had taken an incarnation as the great boar, dug out the fifty-crore mile wide earth from the walls of the universe. Due to the tiredness in your divine form, did you recline here? āḻvār is asking emperumān through his divine mind so that he could massage emperumān’s divine form to rid him of his tiredness.

**vilangumāl varaiccuram kadanda kāl paranda kāvirikkarai** – on the bank of river kāvēri which flows past the hurdles of mountains and forests, branching out severally due to the excessive flow of water. The term *suram* would refer to forest as well as to an insurmountable narrow path. Through this verse, the way by which river kāvēri came, with several tributaries, after crossing several hurdles such as mountains and forest, in order to carry out servitude to ārāvamudan through cool water and breeze, to manifest her great affection for him, has been mentioned.

**kudandaiyuḷ kidandavāṟu** – the way by which emperumān has reclined manifesting his fatigue, so that kāvēri could carry out servitude to him in the above-mentioned way. Since āḻvār saw river kāvēri carrying out servitude through her cool water and cool breeze, since emperumān did what āḻvār had asked him to do in the divine abode of tiruvehkā, just as it has been said in paramasamhitai ślōkam “*yēna yēna dātā gaccati tēna tēna saha gaccati* l *tadyatā taruṇa vatsā vatsam vatsō vā mātaram* *cāyā vā satvamanugaccēt tatāprakāram* ll” (Just like a cow which has a tender calf follows the calf, just as the calf follows the cow, just as a shadow follows a person, in the same way, (muktātmā) follows parama puruśan (supreme entity) wherever he goes), and since emperumān showed himself up to āḻvār just as he had said in the 55<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam “*unna pādam enna sindai manna vaitu nalgināy*” (you kept your divine feet in my heart, without separating from it, and benefitted me greatly), āḻvār thought in his divine mind that the reason as to why emperumān was not mingling with him in tirukkudandai and was merely reclining there, must be his fatigue only. ārāvamudāḻvār too, in order to enjoy the boundary for affection of this āḻvār, who is called as *tirumaḻisaippirān* (the vo*rd pirān* refers to one who carries out a benefit) as well as due to the sweetness of the place, is only reclining there, instead of mingling with āḻvār.

**eḻundirundu pēsu** – if you tell me the reason for lying down like this, in the posture of lying down, it will not remove my fear. In order to remove my fear, you have to stand up and mercifully tell me “I am not fatigued”. My fear should disappear after seeing the beauty of your getting up \[from the reclining posture\] and after hearing your sweet voice when you open your mouth to tell me. Just as jatāyu maharājar (the bird jatāyu) had praised śrī rāma with long life when he said as in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 67-15 “*yāmōśadimivāyuśman*! *anvēśasi mahāvanē* l *sā dēvī mama ca prāṇā rāvaṇēnōbhayam hrutam* ll (Oh one with long life! That sītā dēvi, who you are searching for like a medicine in this forest, as well as my life, have been seized by rāvaṇa), āḻvār too sings in praise of emperumān such that no shortcoming would come to his beauty.

**kēsanē**– Oh kēsava! The last syllable in *kēśava* has got shortened and has become *kēsan*. ōr, we can consider this word to refer to one who has beautiful hair (kēśam). Through both, it would mean the divine hair which is glossy and dark, and is curled. Just as it has been said in “*narakē pacyamānastu yamēna paribhāśita*: l *kim tvayā nārcitō dēva*: *kēśava*: *klēśanāśana*: ll” (a jīvān (soul) who was being tortured in narakam (hell) was asked by yama, the lord of righteousness “Oh! Haven’t you vorśipped kēśava dēvan (emperumān) who has beautiful hair and who removes all the grief?”) āḻvār is beseeching emperumān to decorate his divine hair and to remove his grief.

Next, we will take up the 62<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 62. Karandam adu

*karaṇḍa mādu poygaiyuḷ karum perum panaip paḻampuraṇḍu vīya vāḷai pāy kuṟungudi nedum tagāytiraṇḍa tōḷ iraṇiyan sinam koḷ āgam onṟaiyumiraṇḍu kūṟu seydu uganda singam enbadu unnaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

karaṇḍam – water-crows; ādu – walking; poygaiyuḷ – in the pond; karum – being dark; puraṇḍu vīḻa – to fall down, in the water; vāḷai – carp fish; pāy – (mistaking the palm fruits for crows) jumping in fear; kuṟungudi – mercifully residing in tirukkuṟungudi; nedum tagāy – Oh one with great eminence!; tiraṇḍa – being thick; tōḷ – having shoulders; iraṇiyan – hiraṇya kaśyap’s; sinam koḷ āgam onṟaiyum – unmatched physical form which manifests anger; iraṇḍu – two; kūṟu seydu – tearing into halves; uganda – one who became happy; singam enbadu – speaking about as narasimha; unnaiyē – is that about you?

**Перевод:**

**Huge, dark palm fruits fall into water pond, inside tirukkuṟungudi, where water-crows walk about. Mistaking the palm fruits for water-crows, the carp fish jump in fear. Oh one who is mercifully residing in tirukkuṟungudi with great eminence! People speak about narasimha, who tore the unmatched physical form of demon hiraṇya kaśyap, which manifested anger, into two halves, and felt happy. Is that you?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**karaṇḍam ādu …. kuṟūngudi nedum tagāy** – on the bank of the pond in tirukkuṟūngudi, water crows will keep walking about. Huge black palm fruits, from palm trees, will fall into the water in pond. Carp fish in the pond, mistaking the palm fruits for the water-crows, will jump in fear. Oh emperumān who has the qualities of beauty, tenderness, simplicity and eminence with radiance, in such tirukkuṟūngudi! *karaṇḍam* – water crow (coot); *ādu* – this word could refer to either walking or bathing. *tagai* refers to the natural, auspicious qualities of tirukkuṟungudi nambi (emperumān’s divine name in tirukkuṟūngudi). The fear which the carp fish felt looking at the palm fruit is symbolic of the people in that divine abode needlessly fearing about the safety of emperumān there, wondering “Oh, what will happen to him?”

**tiraṇḍa tōḷ iraṇiyan** – hiraṇyan who had thick (well-rounded) shoulders. The shoulders manifest the huge strength which he had. Alternatively, we could consider this to mean the shoulders which were well rounded in order to control celestial entities. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam says through 1-17-3 ślōkam, about the way hiraṇya kaśyap controlled the celestial entities “*indratvamakarōt daitya*: *sa cāsīt savitā svayam* l *vāyuragnirapām nāta*: *sōmaścābhūnmahāsura*: ll” (that iraṇiyan held the post of indra (as head of celestial entities); he was himself sūryan; that great demon was vāyu (deity for wind), agni (deity for fire), varuṇan (deity for water) and candran).

**sinangoḷ āgam** – physical form which was full of anger; the anger in his heart was visible clearly in his physical form.

**onṟaiyum iraṇḍu kūṟu seydu** – with the tender form \[which he was manifesting in tirukkuṟungudi\], emperumān tore the physical form of hiraṇyan, who had unmatched strength, into two parts

**uganda singam enbadu unnaiyē** – is that you, with this tender form, who, the authentic texts praise as one who felt happy that “The enemy of son prahlādan has been destroyed”! nruśimhatāpinī – nrusimha mantram says “*ugram vīram mahāviṣṇum jvalantam sarvatōmukham* l *nrusimham bhīśaṇam bhadram mrutyumrutyum namāmyaham* \|\|” (I worship the one who is full of fury, valour, mahāviṣṇu, effulgent, who has faces everywhere, \[present in all places\], who is narasimhan (combination of human and lion forms), who is terrifying, who is beautiful and is death for death itself). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-20-60 says  
“*kva yauvanōnmukhībhūta sukumāra danur hari*: l *kva vajra katinā bhōga śarīrō’yam mahā’sura*: \|\|” (Where is hari who has the divine form of an adolescent, just entering the stage of youth and where is the great demon who has the form which is hardened like diamond and which is broad?) The opinion here is that your \[emperumān’s\] tenderness was amazing while it was not fitting with the incarnation of narasimha who carried out a harsh task. emperumān, who is superior to everyone else and who is tender, is manifesting himself to samsāris, who do not desire him, in tirukkuṟungudi. This made the people in that divine abode to get bewildered, thinking “What will befall him?” This manifestation \[of emperumān\] becomes the reason for the fear in the minds of the people of the abode and for āḻvār to wonder about the tenderness of emperumān.

Next, we will take up the 63<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 63. Nanrirundu

*nanṟirundu yōganīdi naṇṇuvārgaḷ sindaiyuḷsenṟirundu tīvinaigaḷ tīrtta dēva dēvanēkunṟirunda māda nīdu pādagattum ūragattumninṟirundu vehkaṇaik kidandadenna nīrmaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nanṟu – being proper; irundu – sitting (on a seat appropriate for practising meditation); yōga – the path of meditation; nīdi – through the means; naṇṇuvārgaḷ – those who are desirous of attaining (you); sindahiyuḷ – in the hearts; senṟu – reaching; irundu – mercifully residing (without detesting); tīvinaigaḷ tīrtta – removing cruel sins; dēva dēvanē – Oh the controller of nityasūris!; kunṟu – like mountains; irunda – remaining; mādam – having houses; nīdu – being long; pādagattum – in tiruppādagam \[a divine abode in kāncīpuram\]; ūragattum – in ūragam (which is similar to that) \[another divine abode in kāncīpuram); ninṟirundu – in sitting and standing postures; vehkaṇai – in tiruvehkā \[yet another divine abode in kāncīpuram\]; kidandadu – mercifully in reclining posture, all these; enna nīrmai – what a simplicity!

**Перевод:**

**Oh the controller of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam)! You are residing in the hearts of those who are desirous of attaining you, through the path of meditation, by sitting on a seat which is appropriate for such meditation. What a simplicity is your dwelling in the posture of sitting and standing in the divine abodes of tiruppādagam and ūragam, which have houses which are like mountains as well as in the posture of reclining in the divine abode of tiruvehkā!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār is amazed with emperumān, with the thought “We can accept to an extent that you had, without considering the tenderness of your divine form, prompted by your affection for śrī prahlādan, destroyed hiraṇyan; however, for the sake of samsāris who do not desire you, without considering your eminence, you have mercifully taken residence in various temples, manifesting your divine form in standing, sitting and reclining postures; isn’t this simplicity amazing!”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**nanṟirundu** – It has been said in bhagavad gītā 6-11 and 6-12 “*śucau dēśē pratiśtāpya stiramāsanamātmana*: \| *nātyuccritam nātinīcam cēlājinakuśōttaram* ll *tatraikāgram mana*: *krutvā yadacidēndriyakriya*: \| *upaviśyāśanē yujñād yōgamātma viśuddayē* ll” (One should observe the activity of meditation in a sacred place, sitting on a seat, which is stable, neither too high nor too low, made of darbha grass, deer skin and cloth, spread one over the other, controlling the activities of one’s mind and sensory organs and focussed on meditation). Just as it has been described above, one should sit in a sacred place, on a seat which is composed of kuśa grass \[a type of grass which is sacred and appropriate for carrying out activities as mentioned in vēdams\], skin of a deer and silk cloth, spread one over another, in this sequence. One should carry out meditation with a focussed mind, just as it has been said in brahmā sūtram 4-1-1 “*āsīna*: *sambavāt*” (in sitting posture (one should meditate); (only if one were sitting) mind will be focussed).

**yōganīdi naṇṇuvārgaḷ** – those devotees who intend to attain you through the means of *aśtānga yōgam* (the path of yōgam or meditation, which is composed of eight parts). The word *nīdi* here refers to “means (path)”

**sindaiyuḷ senṟirundu tīvinaigaḷ tīrtta dēva dēvanē** – Oh one who has the natural characteristic of getting into the hearts of your followers, without looking at your eminence of being as said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1 “*ayarvaṟūm amarargaḷ adipati*” (lord of nityasūris who do not have deficiency of forgetting (that you are the lord and they are your servitors)) or their lowliness of thinking of worldly pursuits since time immemorial, with a divine auspicious form which is appropriate for their meditation, removing their inner and outer hurdles which impede their enjoying you, as if you were sitting on a throne which always showers only victory and not leaving their hearts! Even though they are people who hold on to a means (path of meditation) other than you, you go into their hearts considering the fact that they do not consider anything other than you as their benefit. You remain there firmly, without detesting the fact that they had hearts which had been involved with worldly pursuits all along. The implied meaning here is that it is possible only for emperumān to remove the hurdles of *ajñānam* (ignorance), *karmam* (the two deeds of virtues and sins), *vāsanai* (impression of worldly pursuits) *ruci* (repeated taste in worldly pursuits) etc. Has it not been mentioned in viṣṇu sahasranāmam *“yōgō yōga vidām nētā*” (emperumān is the means for attaining him through the path of devotion and is the guide for those who follow this means)? He does not consider their lowliness of attaining him through other means instead of through himself. He goes to their hearts considering only their distinction that they do not consider anything other than himself as the benefit. āḻvār says through the latter part of the pāsuram that while it is possible to understand the magnanimity of emperumān in trying to make them attain him \[even through other means\], he is not able to understand his simplicity of trying to correct samsāris even when they have hatred towards him.

**kunṟirunda māda nīdu pādagattum ūragattum** – in tiruppādagam where houses are like mountains which are together, as well as in ūragam which is similar to it.

**ninṟirundu** – in the divine abode of tiru ūragam, you were standing, expectantly awaiting a follower who would look at you; even when none came, you went to the divine abode of tiruppādagam, where you sat mercifully.

**vehkaṇaikkidandadu enna nirmaiyē** – even when you were sitting mercifully in tiruppādagam, seeing that none approached you, you went to tiruvehkā and remained in reclining posture, thinking “At some point of time when they develop the taste \[in matters relating to emperumān\], they will develop an involvement in the beauty of our reclining posture which is better than the standing and sitting postures”. āḻvār says that this simplicity of emperumān’s is incomprehensible to our knowledge. We could understand your offering yourself to nityasūris, who are incapable of separating from you, in order to enjoy you permanently. We could also understand your greatness of falling head over heels on your devotees who understand your distinction, correcting them and enabling them to enjoy you. However, how will we understand your simplicity of remaining in standing, sitting and reclining postures, expecting samsāris who do not have any desire for you, to turn towards you?

Next, we will take up the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 64. Ninradu endai

*ninṟadendai ūragattu irundadendai pādagat(tu)anṟu vehkaṇaik kidandadu ennilāda munnelāmanṟu nān piṟandilēn piṟandapin maṟandilēnninṟadum irundadum kidandadum en nenjuḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

endai – one who is my svāmy (lord); ūragattu – in tiru ūragam; ninṟadu – when he mercifully stood; endai – one who is my svāmy; pādagattu – in tiruppādagam; irundadu – when he mercifully sat; anṟu – that time (when I had not developed taste in the two divine abodes mentioned); vehkaṇai – in tiruvehkā; kidandadu – when he mercifully reclined; ennilāda – when I had turned my face away \[from emperumān\]; munnelām – all the time preceding it; anṟu – during that time; nān – I; piṟandilēn – remained as not having been born (since I did not have knowledge about emperumān); piṟanda pin – after I gained knowledge (about emperumān); maṟandilēn – I did not forget; endai – one who is my lord; ninṟadum irundadum kidandadum – all the activities which he did in the three divine abodes mentioned above; en nenjuḷē – he does inside my heart

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram that emperumān, with his simplicity, whom he had enjoyed in the previous pāsuram, mercifully resided in divine abodes such as tiru ūragam etc in order to create an interest in the heart of āḻvār. Once āḻvār developed the interest, due to his great affection for āḻvār, he shifted to āḻvār’s heart. āḻvār says that emperumān’s simplicity which he had mentioned in the previous pāsuram, fructified in his case.*

**ninṟadendai ūragattu** – As trivikraman, emperumān had measured all the worlds, gently stroking every one such that his magnanimity was manifested. With that divine form, he mercifully stood in the divine abode of tiru ūragam. The implied meaning is that emperumān, through measuring the world, wanted to indicate the rule that he is the owner and that the entire world is his possession. He manifested his beauty to āḻvār such that he would accept that emperumān is his lord and to create a taste in āḻvār towards him. Alternatively, even during the time that āḻvār had not agreed to “He is my lord”, the reason for emperumān coming to tiru ūragam and standing there is to announce that the owner will not let go of his possession.

**irundadendai pādagattu** – Since emperumān found that āḻvār had not developed an interest in him despite coming to tiru ūragam, he showed his beauty in the divine abode of tiruppādagam such that āḻvār would accept the fact that “You are the lord; I am your servitor” and mercifully remained in sitting posture in tiruppādagam. This is just like the way lakśmaṇa told hanuman, when hanuman came to enquire about them, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 4-12 *“ahamasyāvarō bhrātā guṇair dāsyamupāgata*:” (Through his (śrī rāma’s) thoughts, I am his younger brother; (factually) through (his) auspicious qualities, I am his servitor).

**anṟu vehkaṇaik kidandadu** – Since āḻvār did not develop an interest towards emperumān despite standing in tiru ūragam and sitting in tiruppādagam, displaying his beauty, he reclined in the divine abode of tiruvehkā, manifesting his beauty, such that āḻvār would develop an interest towards him. āḻvār explains the boundary before he came to these divine abodes.

**ennilāda munnelām** – During the long time when I (referring to āḻvār) did not exist. The implied meaning is – during the time when I had not developed an interest towards emperumān. How is *ennilāda* to be treated as *nānilāda* (when I was not)? The vyākyātā, periyavāccān piḷḷai, quotes nammāḻvār’s tiruvāimoḻi 7-9-4 “*appanai enṟu maṟappan? ennāgiya tappudalinṟit tanaik kavi tān solli*” (Is it possible to forget that emperumān? He sang hymns on himself through me such that there will be no deficiency), where a similar usage is seen. He, who is the lord of paramapadam, which is nitya vibhūti (permanent realm) and of leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm), stood, sat and lay down in order for a samsāri (dweller in leelā vibhūti) to develop an interest towards him! While even my existence is only through his mercy, is he longing for my glance! When asked as to what he was doing then, āḻvār says ……

**anṟu nān piṟandilēn** – during the time of creation, even though emperumān had given a form and name, I (āḻvār) had not bowed down to his divine feet since I had interest in other matters. Despite having been born, I remained as one who had not been born.

**nān piṟandilēn** – The opinion here is that since birth for ātmā happens only when it gains knowledge, despite having taken many births, I was without knowledge. Has this not been said in upanaiśats too, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6?

“*asannēva sa bhavati* l *asat brahmēti vēda cēt* l *asti brahmēti cēd vēda* l *santamēnam tatō vidu*: ll (Since the soul does not have knowledge about brahmam (supreme entity) he is considered as not existing; if it has (such knowledge), for that reason alone, he is considered to be existing). Has it not been said in smruti too that only he, who had created knowledge about brahmam, is the greatest father? *utpādaga brahmapitrōr garīyān brahmada*: *pitā* (Between the biological father and the father who gave instructions about brahmam, it is only he, who gave instructions about brahmam, is considered as eminent)

**piṟandapin maṟandilēn** – due to emperumān’s efforts since time immemorial, I have not forgotten him after I received knowledge about him. Just as it has been said in bhagavad gītā 15-15 “*sarvasya cāham hrudi sanniviśtō mattas smrutir jñānamapōhananca* l  
*vēdaiśca sarvair ahamēva vēdyō vēdāntakrut vēdavidēva cāham* ll” (I am sitting in everyone’s heart; it is only through me that memory, knowledge and forgetfulness emerge; only I am known through all the vēdas; it is only I who grants the benefits as mentioned in vēdas and it is only I who knows vēdas), isn’t it through emperumān that soul gets knowledge? Since the knowledge obtained through emperumān’s efforts transforms into *bhakti* (devotion), just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi, *madinalam* (knowledge with devotion), there is no scope for forgetting it. Thus, it is firmly established that one would exist only if one becomes interested about emperumān and that without that interest, he becomes non-existent.

**ninṟadum irundadum kidandadum en nenjuḷē** – after I developed interest towards emperumān such that I will not forget, he carried out his activities of standing, sitting and reclining, which he was carrying out in those divine abodes, in my heart, as if he had forgotten about those divine abodes.

It appeared that emperumān was desirous, since time immemorial, to attain āḻvār. He made āḻvār, who was non-existent, to exist, creating devotion in āḻvār which he will not forget, made himself as the target for that devotion and fulfilled his desire. In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “*sindaiyuḷ senṟirundu tīvinaigaḷ tīrtta*“, implying that emperumān had entered the minds of his followers and removed only their sins. In this pāsuram, he says “*ninṟadum irundadum kidandadum en nenjuḷē”* emphasising that emperumān, staying in his mind, enjoyed him in many ways. Through these two pāsurams, it is made clear that āḻvār mentions the superiority of attaining emperumān through himself instead of attaining him through other means (devotion).

Next, we will take up the 65<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 65. Nirpadumor verpagattu

*niṟpadum or veṟpagattu iruppum viṇ kidappadumnaṟperum tiraik kadaluḷ nānilāda munnelāmaṟpudan anandasayanan ādi bhūdan mādavanniṟpadum iruppadum kidappadum en nenjuḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

or – unique; veṟpagattu – in tirumalai (tiruvēngadam); niṟpadum – standing mercifully; viṇ – in the sky, which is paramapadam; iruppum – sitting mercifully; nal – being good; peru – being dense; tirai – having waves; kadaluḷ – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kidappadum – reclining mercifully; nān ilāda munnelām – during earlier times, when I was like asat (that which does not exist). Not knowing the proper way (of vorśipping emperumān); aṟpudan – being an amazing entity (due to jñāna, śakti (knowledge, power) etc; ananda sayanan – lying atop anantan (ādiśēśan); mādavan – śrīman nārāyaṇan; niṟpadum iruppadum kidappadum – carrying out the activities of standing, sitting and reclining (now); en nenjuḷē – these are in my heart

**Перевод:**

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Since āḻvār had not forgotten the benefits which emperumān showered on him, he engages with emperumān further. In this pāsuram he says “emperumān mercifully stood in tiruvēngadam, sat in paramapadam and lay down in tiruppāṟkadal only during the time when āḻvār had not developed affection towards emperumān’s divine feet. Nowadays, is he not doing all these activities, staying in my heart?“*

**niṟpadum or veṟpagattu** – manifesting his divine posture of standing, atop the unmatched tirumalai. The uniqueness of tirumalai is that just as this āḻvār himself had said in nānmugan tiruvandādi 47 “*gānamum vānaramum vēdum udai vēngadam*“, tirumalai is the divine abode where emperumān is manifesting his simplicity to the trees, monkeys and hill-tribe people, who are present in tiruvēngadam, without differentiating among them. īsn’t he standing mercifully in tiruvēngadam in order to create an involvement among the trees, the monkeys and the people of the hill-tribes?

**iruppum viṇ** – for those who have an involvement with his divine feet, emperumān manifests the divine posture of sitting which he adopts at paramapadam and which he displays to nityasūris, who have no connection with samsāram (materialistic realm), such that they could enjoy him.

**kidappadum naṟperum tiraikkadaluḷ** – tiruppāṟkadal is clear, cool and has waves which gently stroke emperumān’s divine feet. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 8-10-8 “*tāḷum tōḷum mudigaḷum samanilāda pala parappi*” (spreading his unmatched, many divine feet, divine shoulders and divine crowns), emperumān spreads his expansive divine form in tiruppāṟkadal, which is expansive appropriately, in order to recline mercifully on it. The word *nal* (good) refers to tiruppāṟkadal having clear and cool water. This could be taken as an adjective either for the ocean or for its waves.

**nān ilāda munnelām** – during the time when I did not know the relationship with emperumān and appeared to be non-existent, he was carrying out the activities of standing, sitting and reclining, in those abodes. Through the second half of the pāsuram āḻvār says “I became favourable towards him, his heart was not in those abodes; he carries out those activities in my heart, now. īsn’t he, who shows his affection for me, the one who is complete in all aspects!”

**aṟpudan anandasayanan ādibhūdan mādavan**– emperumān is complete with auspicious qualities of amazing knowledge, power etc. Since he is reclining atop anantan (ādiśēśan), he is spoken of as para brahmam (supreme entity) in sleeping posture, just as it has been said in kauśīdaki upaniśat “*amitaujā*: *paryanka*:”. He is the causative factor for the worlds. Apart from being the lord of the two vibhūtis (materialistic and spiritual), isn’t he having the greatness of being the consort of lakshmi too! We could also consider that emperumān attained all these greatness after attaining āḻvār.

**niṟpadum iruppadum kidappadum en nenjuḷē** – just as nammāḻvār had said in periya tiruvandādi 68 “*kallum kanaikadalum vaigunda vānādum pullenṟu oḻindanakol ēpāvam*” (Oh how sad! tirumalai, the roaring tiruppāṟkadal and paramapadam appear to have grown grass, (after emperumān left these places to enter my mind)), after attaining my heart, emperumān did not bother about those divine abodes; all his experience of standing, sitting and reclining in those divine abodes took place in my heart. I am the leader of all the lowly persons; after attaining me, he left tirumalai which is the boundary for simplicity and resided in my heart. After he announced to me about the sweetness of his divine feet, he shifted from paramapadam, the abode where he was mercifully sitting and considered my heart as his *bhōgastānam* (the place where he was enjoying all the enjoyments). Just as viṣṇu darmam 41-30 says “*andakārē’titīvrē ca narasimham anusmarēt* l *taratyakhila du*:*kkhāni tāpārtō jalaśāyinam* ll” (In pitch darkness, let the cētana think of narasimha perumāḷ. When suffering from sorrows, let him think of the supreme entity who is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal; (by thinking this way), he crosses all hurdles), by manifesting his reclining posture in tiruppāṟkadal and removing my sorrows in samsāram, he shifted from his abode of tiruppāṟkadal and reclined in my heart, getting rid of his longingness. āḻvār says that he could not forget the immense love which emperumān showed towards him.

Next, we will take up the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
