## 66. Inru sadal

*inṟu sādal ninṟu sādal anṟi yārum vaiyagat(tu)onṟi ninṟu vāḻdal inmai kaṇḍu nīsar enkolōanṟu pār aḷanda pāda pōdai onṟi (unni) vānin mēlsenṟu senṟu dēvarāy irukkilāda vaṇṇamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

inṟu sādal – dying the moment one is born; ninṟu sādal – dying after completing a hundred years; anṟi – apart from these two; yārum – however great, such as brahmā et al, one may be; vaiyagattu – in this world; onṟi ninṟu – staying firmly; vāḻdal – living; inmai kaṇḍum – even after seeing directly that it does not occur; nīsar – ignorant people; anṟu – on that day (when mahābali had usurped the world); pār – all the worlds; aḷanda – measured; pāda pōdai – divine feet which are like lotus flower; unni – meditating upon; senṟu senṟu – reaching paramapadam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) through the path of arcis, i.e. light) etc; vānin mēl – in paramapadam; dēvarāy – matching nityasūris; irukkilāda vaṇṇam – reason for not being \[like that\]; en kolō – for what?

**Перевод:**

**Life in this world is such unstable that one may die on the day one is born or die after living for a hundred years. Even for great persons such as brahmā et al, it does not happen that they live here forever. After seeing all these, why are ignorant people not meditating upon the lotus feet of emperumān who measured the worlds on that day (when mahābali usurped the lands) and not reaching paramapadam, through the path of arcis etc and match nityasūris who dwell there?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār is wondering that while attaining emperumān is this simple, while worldly life, which is samsāram, has many evident defects such as insignificant, unstable etc, and while knowing that by attaining emperumān one need not return to this world but enjoy the great eminence of being in śrī vaikuṇṭam, for what reason are samsāris not attaining emperumān?*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**inṟu sādal** – dying in the womb immediately after the embryo is formed upon the union of a man and woman; alternatively, dying immediately after being born in the world, after knowing about himself / herself. In the former case, how could it life be counted during the stay in womb? Just as it has been said in āpastamba dharma sūtram 1-1-1-19 “*garbhāśtamēśu brāhmaṇamupanayīta*” (for a brāhamaṇa boy, upanayanam (wearing of sacred thread) should be conducted in the eighth year from the time that conception takes place in the womb), since longevity is calculated from the time of conception, the time of stay in the womb is also considered as part of life.

**ninṟu sādal** – dying after completing the longevity of 100 years which has been considered for human beings. Alternatively, this could be considered as the period beginning with kalpam and ending with kalpam for celestial entities such as brahmā et al (kalpam is 4.32 billion years). Has not emperumān mercifully brought out separately the features of birth and death among people and celestial entities, as said in bhagavat gītā 2-20? “*na jāyatē mriyatē vā kadācit nāyam bhūtvā bhavitā vā na bhūya*: l *ajō nitya*: *śāśvatō’yam purāṇō na hanyatē hanyamānē śarīrē*: ll” (This ātmā never takes birth nor does it ever die. This entity of soul (which is inside the physical form of brahmā et al) does not originate (at the beginning of kalpam) and does not disappear (at the end of kalpam). ātmā is without birth; it is present forever; even though it has been around for long, it is enjoyable like a new entity; even when the body \[within which it is present\] is killed, it does not die).

**anṟi** – even in this state of impermanence, though there are differences between various classes of people, there is none who has remained forever. nammāḻvār too had said in tiruvāimoḻi 4-1-6 “*anṟu mudal inṟaṟudiyā vāḻndārgaḷ vāḻndē niṟpar enbadillai*” (it has not happened that from the time of creation of brahmā, people have lived life in much the same way). Has it not been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-52? “*garbhē vileeyatē bhūyō jāyamānō’stamētica* l *jātamātrē ca mriyatē bālabhāvē’ta yauvanē* ll *madyamam vā vaya*: *prāptē vārdakē vā druvā mruti* ll” ((jīvātmā) perishes while inside the womb; perishes during the time of birth too; perishes during the time of infancy, during childhood and during youthful states; (even otherwise) during middle age or during old age, death is certain),

**ārum** – even jīvans such as brahmā et al who have eminent knowledge and who rule the fourteen worlds

**vaiyagattu onṟi ninṟu vāḻdalinmai kaṇḍum** – even after knowing that there is none who lives in the fourteen worlds, permanently, in the same manner. The term *vaiyagam*, though refers to world, here it refers to the fourteen worlds, comprising earth and the six upper worlds as well as the seven nether worlds. The term *vāḻdal* would ironically mean “Is his life anything worth to be spoken of?” Alternatively, it could also mean that samsāris would consider this life as something great and is hence referring to it as something significant.

**nīsar** – those without knowledge. They are the people who look up to others for instructing them to know about the life which they are experiencing. This could also be taken to mean that these people have committed such sins that they are unable to give up the worldly pursuits even after knowing through vēdams and through the instructions of those who follow vēdams that entities which are derived from prakruti (primordial matter) are lowly. The term *nīsar* would refer to those who have attained lowly births due to excessive sins committed by them.

**anṟu** – on that day when this world had been seized by mahābali, as a result of which the quality of being the svāmy (lord) for the souls as well as the quality of servitude had been lost on the jīvātmā.

**pāraḷanda pāda pōdai** – divine feet which are like lotus and which emperumān had kept, without seeing any distinction between the eminent and lowly, on the heads of all the cētanas (those with knowledge) in all the worlds and manifested that all are his possession. The term *pār* refers to all fourteen worlds. The term *pōdu* while referring to flower in general, refers to lotus here since they \[divine feet\] are sweet, even if they are rare to attain.

**unni** – thinking deeply about. It signifies that there is no difficulty in attaining emperumān; merely meditating on him is sufficient. īsn’t meditating upon, easy for everyone, just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 9-1-7 ”*siṟṟa vēṇḍā sindippē amaiyum*” (there is no need for engaging with difficult activities; having deep faith alone through the mind is sufficient \[to attain emperumān\])? The text is also written as *onṟi* \[instead of *unni*\] – it would then mean engaging firmly. If attaining emperumān is easy, vouldn’t the benefit accrued due to that also be that great? āḻvār says that this is not the case.

**vānin mēl senṟu senṟu** – going in the path of arcis, reaching the land of paramapadam from where one never returns. Through the two terms *senṟu senṟu*, the path through which one goes and the final destination of paramapadam are indicated. Alternatively, both terms could be considered to refer to the path itself since going in the path is in itself, very sweet and hence the term is repeated to emphasise this.

**dēvarāy irukkilāda vaṇṇam … enkolō** – what is the reason for not attaining the eminence of being on a par with nityasūris? The term *dēvar* refers to nityasūris who are without any fatigue. Matching nityasūris would mean that they will be without any shortcoming in their knowledge and would match them through their divine form and enjoying brahmam (supreme entity).

**enkolō** – what is the reason for this? (1) Is it because there is an antidote for the deficiencies seen prominently in samsāram? (2) Is there any shortcoming in the great happiness derived from attaining emperumān as mentioned in śāstrams? (3) Is there any deficiency to the simplicity of emperumān who has been attained? (4) Is there any difficulty in attaining him? (5) Is there any lowliness in the paths for attaining him? Since the pāsuram says *inṟu sādal…..vāḻdalinmai kaṇḍum* the first mentioned option is not the reason. Since the pāsuram says *dēvarāy irukkilāda* the second option is also ruled out. Since the pāsuram says *anṟu pāraḷanda pāda pōdai* the third reason is also eliminated. The fourth option is also incorrect due to the usage of the term *unni*. Similarly, due to the usage of the term *senṟu senṟu*, the fifth option is also eliminated. āḻvār says that he has not seen any reason other than the sin due to which one engages with *mūlaprakruti* (primordial matter) which is an acētana entity (without knowledge) and its effect such as *dēham* (physical form) etc as well as the sin due to which one does not engage with emperumān, for ending up with this great loss.

Next, we will take up the 67<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 67. Sanda mandalattin

*saṇḍamaṇadalattin ūdu senṟu vīdu peṟṟu mēlkaṇḍu vīdilāda kādal inbanāḷum eyduvīrpuṇḍarīga pāda puṇya kīrtti num sevi madut(tu)uṇḍu num uṟuvinait tuyaruḷ nīngi uymminō*

**Пословный перевод:**

saṇḍamaṇḍalattin – through the solar galaxy which is difficult to attain; ūdu senṟu – going amidst it; vīdu peṟṟu – attaining paramapadam; mēl – one which comes after that; vīdilāda – being without any break; kādal inbam – the joy of carrying out service through devotion; kaṇḍu – seeing that; nāḷum eyduvīr – Oh those who think constantly of attaining (that); puṇḍarīga pāda – having divine feet which are like lotus flower; puṇya kīrtti – in the fame of means; num – your; sevi – ears; maduttu – engaging them; uṇḍu – enjoying (that); num – created by you; uṟu – that which remains with you (until you experience it); vinaittuyaruḷ – from the sorrow, which is the result of sins; nīngi – getting rid; uymminō – uplift yourselves

**Перевод:**

**Oh those who are constantly meditating upon reaching paramapadam by going through the solar galaxy, which is difficult to attain and who are thinking of carrying out eternal service through devotion, which results from reaching paramapadam! Uplift yourselves by engaging your ears with the fame of means represented by the divine feet \[of emperumān\], which are like lotus flower, enjoy it and get rid of the sorrow, which is the result of the sins which remain with you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār felt saddened, looking at the ignorance of samsāris in not realising the benefits that they had. Thinking that “If they get a taste in this (involvement with emperumān) they should not miss these benefits”, āḻvār starts instructing the samsāris over seven pāsurams, starting with this. In this pāsuram he says “Oh those who intend to get the indestructible experience of brahmānubhavam (enjoyment with emperumān) by reaching through the path of arcis (light)! Make sure that you attain emperumān who is both the means for attaining him and the goal for enjoying him, get rid of your hurdles and uplift yourselves”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**saṇḍamaṇḍalattin ūdu senṟu** – going through the path of solar galaxy which is difficult to attain. Just as it has been said in periya tirumadal 16 “*mannum kadum kadirōn maṇḍalattin nannaduvuḷ*” (right at the central region of sun whose rays are permanent and fiery), going through the path of solar galaxy which is difficult to attain for those who do not have desire in mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). Just as princes of a kingdom have the freedom to roam all over the kingdom without any hurdle merely because they were born in the clan, those who have connection with bhagavān too would go through the path \[of solar galaxy\] without any hurdle.

**vīdu peṟṟu** – attaining paramapadam.

**mēl vīdilāda kādal inbam – kaṇḍu – nāḷum eyduvīr** – the result of attaining emperumān like this is to carry out service to emperumān, which is indestructible, which is without any break and which is in line with the svarūpam (basic nature) of the ātmā. This service is the result of the desire arising out of enjoying emperumān. Oh those who have seen all these and who desire to carry out this service to emperumān until the ātmā exists {in other words permanently, since ātmā is indestructible).

**puṇḍarīga pāda puṇya kīrtti** – the divine feet of emperumān are sweet like the fresh lotus flower. These divine feet have the fame of purifying the jīvātmā and are the means for attaining mōkśam. Several examples have been quoted from itihāsams (epics) to substantiate that this is well known in śāstrams. śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 82-9 says “*pāvanas sarvalōkānām tvamēva raghunandana*” (Oh raghunandana (śrī rāma)! You are the one who purifies all the worlds); mahābhāratam vanaparvam 88-27 says “*pavitrāṇām hi gōvinda*: *pavitram paramucayatē* l *puṇyānāmapi puṇyō’sau mangaḷānānca mangaḷam* ll” (Among all the sacred entities, gōvindan (emperumān) is said to be the most sacred entity; among the virtuous entities, he is the most virtuous; among the auspicious entities, he is the most auspicious). śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-15 says “*lōkānām tvam parō darmō viśvaksēnaś caturbhuja*: l *śārngadanvā hruśīkēśa*: *puruśa*: *puruśōttama*: ll” (You are the most righteous, among all the worlds; you have the army which spreads everywhere; you are together with four shoulders; you are the controller of the bow, śārngam; you are one controlling all the senses; you are praised as puruśa and puruśōttama). mahābhāratam vana parvam 71-123 says “*yē ca vēda vidō viprā yē cādyātma vidō janā*: l *tē vidanti mahātmānam kriṣṇam darmam sanātanam*  ll” (brāhmaṇas who know vēdams and the elders who know the nature of ātmā know that kriṣṇa symbolises the ancient righteousness).

**num sevi maduttu** – you have been such that even in your dreams you had not had any connection with bhagavān; making your ears to listen to the instructions of an ācāryan. In order for emperumān to take you under his wings, making you to listen to your ācāryan’s instructions is important.

**uṇḍu** – enjoying the auspicious qualities of emperumān, which have been sweet from the time that one started to think and which do not expect anything else to be a means to attain him.

**num uṟuvinait tuyaruḷ nīngi uymminō** – sins are ones which you have created yourselves. Just as it has been said in brahmavaivartam prakruti 26-90 “*avaśyamanubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham* l *nābhuktam kśīyatē karma kalpakōti śatairapi* ll”  
(The results of deeds, both virtuous and sinful, which have been carried out by a person, have to be compulsorily experienced; unless experienced, karmas will not get destroyed, even after the lapse of a hundred crore kalpams \[a kalpam is 4.32 billion years\]) sorrows result from the collection of sins which are unavoidable. You, who are carrying out your lives under these sorrows, should get rid of such sorrows and attain the comfort of carrying out service to emperumān. Just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1 “*toḻudeḻu*“, you should uplift yourselves by bowing down to that emperumān, carrying out service to him.

Next, we will take up the 68<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 68. Muttirattu

*muttiṟattu – of tree types (distinguiśed by satva, rajas and tamas qualities)vāṇiyattu – among te benefitsiraṇḍil – among te tvo benefits vhic are appropriate vit rajas and tamas qualitiesonṟum – you people, vho have taste in itnīsargaḷ – tose vho are lovlyadil ittu – giving up tat benefit at te end of tat karmaiṟandu – giving up tat physical form vhic vas used for enjoyingpōndu – taking birt in eart (as mentioned in pancāgni Vidya, in śāstram)mattarāy – enjoying commodities obtained from primordial matter, tinking tat body is ātmāmayanguginṟadu – getting bevildered, being ignorant of te nature of ātma and paramātmā(Hence)ettiṟattum – by any meansuyvadōr upāyamillai – tere is no means for uplifting in any oter vayuyguṟil – if you viś to uplift yourselvestottiṟutta – strung denselytaṇ – being cooltuḻāy – having divine tuḷasinal – having auspicious qualities suc as simplicity etcmālai – one vho is superior to everyone elsevāḻtti – praying to himvāḻminō – be blissful, free from all burdens*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Lowly people who have an involvement with rajas and tamas qualities among the three qualities of satva, rajas and tamas, enjoy the benefits derived from these two qualities, and lose themselves in the materials obtained from prākrutam (primordial matter) due to their bewilderment that physical form is ātmā and everything should be done to satisfy the physical form. At the end of their lives, they leave behind the benefits, leave the physical forms and through the path of pancāgni vidyā, re-enter samsāram due to their ignorance. They do not have any means to uplift themselves. If you wish to uplift yourselves, worship emperumān who is simplicity personified and who wears the densely strung garland of tuḷasi and be blissful, leaving aside all your burdens.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) samsāris (dwellers in materialistic realm) have one of the three qualities of satvam (noble), rajas (passion) and tamas (ignorance) in excess over the other two. Since they \[normally\] have rajas and tamas in excess, they attain the corresponding deities and obtain the benefits given by them. āḻvār stops them, in this pāsuram, saying “Think of getting liberated from samsāram, attain parama puruśan \[emperumān, the supreme entity\] who is the greatest among all those who have noble qualities and uplift yourselves” muttiṟattu vāṇiyattu iraṇḍil onṟu nīsargaḷmattarāy mayanguginṟadu ittadil iṟandu pōnduettiṟattum uyvadōr upāyamillai uyguṟiltottiṟutta taṇ tuḻāy nanmālai vāḻtti vāḻminō Word-by-Word Translation*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**muttiṟattu vāṇiyattu** – among the three types of benefits obtained after praying from deities, based on the dominant quality among sātvika, rājasa and tāmasa. *tiṟam* – classification; *vāṇiyam* refers to benefit, based on higher benefit obtained with lower efforts as in commercial activities. vēdas and śāstras identify the deity which a person has to worship appropriate to his quality, the method in which he has to worship and the benefit that he attains through this worship. Examples of such statements are:

manu smruti 3-76  
“*agnau prāstāhuti*: *samyagādityam upatiśtatē* l  
*ādityājjāyatē vruśtir vruttērannam tata*: *prajā*: ll”  
(āhuti (offering) which has been thrown into fire by one who conducts the ritual, reaches āditya (sun); from that sun, rain forms; from that rain food and from that food, progeny are formed).

bhagavat gītā 9-26  
“*patram puśpam palam tōyam yō mē bhaktyā prayaccati* l  
*tadaham bhaktyupahrutam aśnāmi prayatātmana*: ll”  
(I partake of a leaf or flower or fruit or water which has been offered with devotion by a devotee (as if it is rare to get one)).

bhagavat gītā 2-45 says “*traiguṇya viśayā vēdā nistraiguṇyō bhavārjuna*” (Oh arjuna! vēdas were created for people who have one of three qualities of satva, rajas and tamas, in excess; let you be one without satva guṇa which is admixed with rajas and tamas guṇas) \[in other words, let you have only satva guṇa, without any trace of rajas and tamas\]

**iraṇḍil onṟum nīsargaḷ** – lowly entities who, instead of being with satva guṇam and vorśipping sarvēśvaran (supreme entity) who is vorśipped by such people, reaping the benefit of attaining mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), are with the other two qualities of rajas and tamas, vorśipping deities who have such rajas and tamas guṇas and reaching lowly benefits such as worlds inhabited by celestial entities such as yakśas and demons or even lower benefits such as worlds inhabited by spoofs, corpses and ghosts. *nīsar* – those who nurture living in samsāram.

**mattarāy mayanguginṟadu ittadil iṟandu pōndu** – in order to attain the respective presiding deity et al for rājasa and tāmasa qualities and coming out of the benefits granted, which their deeds had been giving them. The word *ittadil* has to be changed as *adil ittu* to get the meaning – giving up that benefit.

**iṟandu** – at the end of the period for which they had been provided physical forms in order to enjoy the pleasures in the respective deity’s lands.

**pōndu** – descending at the end of the stipulated periods of enjoyment, from worlds such as svargam etc where they had gone to enjoy pleasures, and reaching ethereal layer, cloud, crops etc through *pancāgni vidyai*, ultimately reaching a woman’s womb. They take birth again, on earth, just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 9-21 “*tē tam bhuktvā svargalōkam viśālam kśīṇē puṇyē martyalōkam viśanti* l *ēvam trayīdarmam anuprapannā*: *gatāgatam kāmakāmā labhantē* ll” (They (jīvātmās) after enjoying in the expansive svarga lōkam (heaven), upon exhausting their reserve of virtues (which had been the reason for their enjoyment), re-enter the land of people \[earth\]. Thus, those who have desire in deeds which grant them their pleasures, follow the first part of vēdams \[which deals with carrying out deeds, unlike the second part which deals with emperumān and how to attain him\] and attain benefits which come their way, again and again).

**mattarāy** – since they think that the physical form itself is ātmā, they carry out activities which will nurture the physical form \[and not the ātmā\], attain their pleasures, and feel proud about it.

**mayanguginṟadu** – since they do not understand ātmā and īśvaran (emperumān), aren’t they always in a state of stupor? The opinion here is that the jīvātmās do not realise that their physical forms, when they enter worlds of enjoyment such as svargam etc, have a limited life only and that they have to return to earth, once they exhaust their bank of virtues and enter the womb of a lady once again. It is because they do not think about the hardships which they endure in order to come back here repeatedly that they involve with the worldly pursuits in every life cycle and remain in a state of bewilderment as well as pride.

**ettiṟattum uyvadōr ubāyamillai** – hence, there is no means to get liberated from samsāram, through one’s own efforts or through attaining other deities or through attaining emperumān for getting aiśvaryam (wealth).

**uyguṟil** – if you wish to get liberated from samsāram and attain mōkśam

**tottiṟutta taṇ tuḻāy nanmālai vāḻtti vāḻminō** – emperumān is decorated with densely strung, cool tuḷasi garland which points out firmly that he is the lord of all. Attain such emperumān who has the auspicious qualities of *sauśeelyam* (simplicity), *saulabhyam* (easy to approach), *vātsalyam* (motherly forbearance) etc and uplift yourselves. Just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 and other such references “*nigaril pugaḻāy ulagam mūnṟudaiyāy ennai āḷvānē nigaril amarargaḷ munikkaṇangaḷ virumbum tiruvēngadattānē*” (Oh one who has unparalleled fame! Oh one who is the lord of all cētana and acētana entities! Oh one who has made even this lowly person as your servitor! Oh one who is desired by nityasūris who are engaged with your auspicious qualities or who are involved with carrying out servitude to you! Through these statements, emperumān’s auspicious qualities of vātsalyam, svāmitvam, sauseelyam, saulabhyam are described, respectively), praise the divine qualities of emperumān and until the ātmā exists \[which is forever\], enjoy the eminent bliss of carrying out kainkaryam in paramapadam and uplift yourselves. īsn’t paramātmā alone the one who is capable of granting bliss to jīvātmā, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 7 “*ēśa hyēvānandayāti*” (is it not true that only paramātmā makes jīvātmā blissful?)

Next, we will take up the 69<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 69. Kanilum urupppolar

*kāṇilum uruppolār sevikkināda kīrttiyārpēṇilum varam tara midukkilāda dēvaraiāṇam enṟadaindu vāḻum ādargāḷ em ādi pālpēṇi num piṟappenum piṇakkaṟukka kiṟṟirē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāṇilum – even if seen; uru – in their physical forms; polār – being with wickedness; sevikku – for the ears; ināda – unworthy of hearing; kīrttiyār – being scornful; pēṇilum – even if one were to take refuge; varam – boons; tara – to give; midukkilāda – being powerless; dēvarai – celestial entities such as brahmā et al; āṇam enṟu – that they are protectors; adaindu – taking refuge under them; ādargāḷ – Oh those without any knowledge; em – for us, who are interested in mōkśam; ādi pāl – in the matter relating to emperumān who is the causative factor; pēṇi – being affectionate; num – your; piṟappu enum – termed as birth; piṇakku – weed; aṟukka kiṟṟirē – are you capable of severing?

**Перевод:**

**Even if one were to see the physical forms of celestial entities such as brahmā, rudra et al, they will have wickedness; their fame will be unworthy of hearing for the ears; they are powerless to grant boons even if one were to take refuge under them. Oh those without knowledge who think that they are protectors and take refuge under them! Are you capable of severing your repeated births in samsāram by taking refuge under emperumān, who is the causative factor for the worlds?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction Some people asked “It was said in the last pāsuram ‘ettiṟattilum uyvadōr upāyamillai’. How correct is this? By attaining other deities, one can attain mōkśam” āḻvār responds saying “It is difficult to attain other deities; even if one were to take refuge under other deities, they do not have the power to grant mōkśam. Hence, attain emperumān, who is the causative factor for the worlds and sever the correction with samsāram which is the result of engaging with sinful activities since time immemorial”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kāṇilum uruppolār** – *kāṇilum* – since they are deities with excessive tamō guṇam, they are not appropriate to be looked at. Hasn’t śāstra too said that celestial entities such as rudra et al, who have taken birth due to their karmas, are unfit to be vorśipped by sātvika entities? Two references have been quoted in the vyākhyānam to buttress this: pauśkara samhitai 36-257 “*skanda rudra mahēndrādyā: pratiśiddāstu pūjanē* l  
*jñātvaivam bhaktisāngkaryam na kuryādēvamēva hi* ll” (deities such as skandan, rudran, indran et al have been proscribed (by wise people) from matters relating to worship; knowing this, let one not carry out devotion in this matter). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-7-77 “*anyētu puruśavyāgra cētasō yē vyapāśrayā*: l *aśuddāstē samastāśca dēvādyā*: *karmayōnaya*: ll” (Oh leader of people! Since celestial entities such as brahmā, rudra et al, who are fit to be meditated upon through the mind, are born out of their karma (deeds), they are impure only). bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-5-6 says “*ātmā vā arē draśtavya*: *śrōttavya: mantavyō*: *nididyāsitavya*:” (ātmā (here the word ātmā refers to paramātmā, the supreme being) is apt to be heard, to be thought of and to be meditated upon; it is apt to be seen). Unlike paramātmā, these deities are not appropriate to be seen, as determined by upaniśat.

**uruppolār** – even though they are not apt to be seen, since they have excess of rajas and tamas guṇas, even if they are seen, since their physical forms carry garland of skulls and bones, third eye, ash from burning bodies etc, their forms will be grotesque. Unlike the lotus like eyes of emperumān which reflect the mercy in his divine mind, the physical forms of these entities will not attract beholders. Just as it has been mentioned in yajur vēdam 6-2 “*rudrō vai krūra*:” (rudra is cruel), the cruelty in their minds is reflected in their physical forms too.

**sevikkināda kīrttiyār** – apart from being unfit to be seen, they are also without fame which is worth listening to, as brought out by the quote seen earlier from bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-5-6. Even if one desires to listen to them out of affection for them, one would only hear statements which would make the ears to feel ashamed to hear, such as “he severed the head of his father brahmā; as a result of this, he roamed around in the three worlds, begging for alms \[to rid himself of his sin\]; he killed dakśan, his father-in-law; he caused harm to the wives of sages”. These statements are not sweet such as “He brought back to life the dead son of sage sāndīpani; he redeemed the sons of a vaidikan from paramapadam, a place from which no one could come back to earth; he gave eminent benefits and protected those who had surrendered to him irrespective of whether they were monsters, demons, animals such as elephants et al”.

**pēṇilum** – these deities are not appropriate to be attained; the narrative of siṟūttoṇḍar nāyanār \[one among 63 nāyanmārs, who are extolled by śaivaites) is evidence for this. Sivan, in the garb of *kāpālikan* (one belonging to a sect of vorśippers of Sivan) came to the house of siṟuttoṇḍar and told him and his wife that they should serve him their son, cooked. siṟuttoṇḍar, accepting the order of the kāpālikan, asked his wife to cook their son. His wife wanted to keep the head of her child and hence cooked the remaining parts of the child, leaving aside the head. When the food was served, the kāpālikan said that something was missing and asked that part too to be included. She then cut the head too and cooked it, for him. This narrative has been included by sēkkiḻār in his periya purāṇam (which includes the narratives of all the sixty-three nāyanmārs). Thus, these deities are not fit enough to be attained. However, due to your affection for them, even if you wish to attain them, tolerating their physical forms which are grotesque and their ill-fame which will be harsh on the ears, it will not be similar to attaining emperumān who does not expect anything from his devotees just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 9-26 which says “*patram puśpam phalam tōyam yō mē bhaktyā prayaccati* l *tadaham bhaktyupahrutam aśnāmi prayatātmana*: ll” (I partake of a leaf or flower or fruit or water which has been offered with devotion by a devotee (as if it is rare to get one)) and as said in bhagavat gītā 9-2 “*rājavidyā rājaguhyam pavitram idam uttamam* \| *pratyakśāvagamam darmyam susukam kartum avyayam* ll” (Among sciences and secrets, this \[knowledge about emperumān\] is the most eminent; among those which nullify sins, this is the most superior; it manifests me directly; it is the means for attaining me; it is easy to practise; (even after giving the benefit) it does not get destroyed).

**varam tara midukkilāda dēvarai** – if they are intent on harming someone, they will have the power to destroy the world itself; they do not have the ability to grant the great benefit of mōkśam. emperumān, on the other hand, grants mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) even to an apparition such as ghaṇṭākarṇan or a bird such as jatāyu. He praises those who come to him seeking something from him as magnanimous persons, as said in bhagavat gītā 7-18 “*udārā*: *sarva ēvaitē jñānī tvātmaiva mē matam*” (These people are magnanimous; those who are with knowledge are like my ātmā (they sustain me); this is my opinion). emperumān is praised as one who gives four *puruśārtams* (ultimate benefits) with his four hands, just as it has been said in viṣṇu darmam 43-47 “*dēvēndras tribhuvanam artamēka pingas sarvartim tribhuvanakānca kārtavīrya*: l *vaidēha*: *paramapadam prasādya viṣṇum samprāptas sakalapalapradō hi viṣṇu*: ll” (Praying to viṣṇu, dēvēndran obtained the three worlds; kubēran obtained opulence; kārtavīryan obtained fame spanning the three worlds and king janaka attained paramapadam. īsn’t viṣṇu bhagavān one who magnanimously gives everything to those who seek from him!). However, these deities do not have such greatness. When sage mārkaṇḍēya told Sivan “By your granting me the boon that I would remain at the age of sixteen forever, suffering in this samsāram has become a permanent feature for me; you must grant me mōkśam such that I could get liberated from this samsāram”, Sivan told him “I do not have the ability to grant you mōkśam; however, I will show you the way to attain it” and showed him emperumān.

**āṇam enṟu adaindu** – thus, even though these deities are not fit to be attained, people attain them since they are bewildered. *āṇam* refers to protection. They are not like paramātmā (emperumān) who had said that he does not give up those who attain him, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “*mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam katancana* l *dōśō yadyapi tasya syāt satāmētadagarhitam* ll” (I will not ever abandon this vibhīśaṇa who has come to me with the pathos of a friend; even if he has faults, let them be – it is not proper on the part of a good person to abuse if he \[visbhīśaṇan\] were accepted). They are not capable of telling a suffering samsāri “Do not grieve” just as emperumān had said in bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*sarvadarmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja* l *aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca*: ll” (Giving up all righteous paths (which are considered as means), along with their impressions, attain me alone as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins; do not grieve). This is because deities such as Sivan have excess of tamō guṇam (quality of ignorance and laziness) in them.

**vāḻum ādargāḷ** – Oh ignorant people who consider attaining such lowly deities as prosperity! The term *ādar* refers to blind people as well as to ignorant people. They are not the people who have attained emperumān and got rid of their fear just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 60-20 “*gajam vā vīkśya simham vā vyāgram vāpi varānana* l  
*nāhārayati santrāsam bāhū rāmasya samśritā* ll (Since sītāp pirātti was under the shoulder of śrī rāma, she did not fear elephant or lion or tiger; her face blossomed looking at them), or as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 34-31 “*bāhuccāyām avaśtabdō yasya lōkō mahātmana*:” (that mahātmā (great entity) under whose shoulder the entire world takes shelter) or as in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6 “*yadā hyēvaiśa ētasminnadruśyē’nātmyē’niruktē’nilayanē’bhayam pratiśtām vindatē* l *ata sō’bhayagatō bhavati* l\|” (That time when one attains, through devotion, this paramātmā who cannot be seen, who does not have a (primordial) physical form, who cannot be described through words (that he is a celestial or human entity), who does not have a prop for himself, in order to get rid of fear from samsāram, one becomes fearless about samsāram).

**em ādi pāḷ pēṇi** – being affectionate towards one who is the cause for us. What is the reason for calling one who is the causative factor for the whole world as being the cause for us? The reason for sarvēśvaran to create the worlds is with a desire to creating *mumukśus* (those who are desirous of being liberated and reaching śrī vaikuṇṭam). Since it is only through entities such as himself \[reference here is to āḻvār\] that one could say that emperumān’s effort in creating the world has fructified, āḻvār says mercifully that “emperumān is the cause for us”.

**num piṟappu enum piṇakku aṟukkakiṟṟirē** – are you capable of severing on your own, this bush called as birth in samsāram, which cannot be severed by anyone?

**piṇakku aṟukka** – *piṇakku* – shrub or bush. The reason for calling birth as bush is due to the fact that for one who has got entangled in it, it is impossible to come out; it is difficult to live inside it; it is difficult to find out the start and end for it.

Next, we will take up the 70<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 70. Kundamodu

*kundamōdu sūlam vēlgaḷ tōmarangaḷ taṇḍu vāḷbandamāna dēvargaḷ parandu vānagam uṟavanda vāṇan iṟainjjñūṟu tōḷgaḷait tuṇitta nāḷandavanda āgulam amararē aṟīvarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kundamōdu – along with the weapon called as savaḷam \[lance\]; sūlam – trident; vēḷgaḷ – many types of spears; tōmarangaḷ – many types of tōmarams (a type of dart); taṇḍu – mace; vāḷ – sword, all these; bandamāna dēvargaḷ – celestial entities who had them as protective gears; parandu – scattering in the four directions; vānagam – their abodes located in the ethereal layer; uṟa – after they attained; vanda – one who came to wage a battle; vāṇan – bāṇāsuran’s; iṟainjjñūṟu – thousand; tōḷgaḷai – hands; \|tuṇitta nāḷ – when destroyed; andavanda āgulam – the sorrows (suffered by rudran); amararē – the deities (who ran along); aṟīvar – would know

**Перевод:**

**Celestial entities, holding lances, spears, tridents, many types of darts, maces, swords as protective weapons, ran in different directions towards their abodes in the ethereal layers. bāṇāsuran, who came to wage a battle \[with kriṣṇa\], had his thousand hands destroyed. The celestial entities, who ran from the battlefield, knew the sorrows suffered by rudran \[in the battle\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When some people asked “Could we say about those deities as ‘varam tara midukkilāda dēvar’? Haven’t many people attained deities such as rudran and been granted their desires?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying “Those celestial entities who were nearby knew how bāṇāsuran suffered after going to rudran for help and how rudran suffered when he tried to protect him”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kundamōdu sūlam vēlgaḷ tōmarangaḷ taṇḍu vāḷ bandamāna dēvargaḷ** – celestial entities who came with different weapons such as lance etc, to rudra to help him, as his friends. *kundam* – a weapon, also known as *savaḷam* (lance); *tōmaram* – a type of spear which is thrown (at the enemy); *taṇḍu* – mace; *bandam –* friends, relatives. Alternatively, bandam could also be considered as hurdle, since when they were running away from the battlefield in fear, these weapons became hurdles for their running; hence they had to throw them away and run for their lives. Though kriṣṇa had said in bhagavat gītā 4-8 “*paritrāṇāya sādūnām vināśāya ca duśkrutām* l *darmasamstāpanārtāya sambhavāmi yugē yugē* ll”  
(I incarnate myself in every epoch in order to protect good people, to destroy wicked people and to establish righteousness as laid down in vēdas), instead of looking at kriṣṇa as one who came to destroy monsters and demons who are their enemies and to give them back their prosperity, the celestial entities looked at him as those monsters and demons and took to weapons to oppose him. Thus, these celestial entities are depicted as treacherous entities. Instead of merely saying that celestial entities came with weapons, by describing the different types of weapons that they brought with them, āḻvār is saying with anger that these entities were trying to harm emperumān who was trying to help them all along.

**parandu vānagamuṟa** – unable to stand up to kaṇṇan in the battlefield, these celestial entities, instead of running in the same direction, ran helter-skelter in various directions towards their abode.

**vanda vāṇan** – even after seeing that the celestial entities were running, after discarding their weapons, and after seeing that rudran, who is his lord, was in tears after losing out to kaṇṇan, bāṇāsuran came to the battlefield with pride in the strength of his thousand shoulders.

**īrainjjñūṟu tōḷgaḷait tuṇitta nāḷ** – on that day when emperumān severed five hundred shoulders on each side, with his divine disc. emperumān destroyed the power of shoulders which bāṇāsuran thought that he had; he made the celestial entities, who came with various weapons, to throw their weapons away and run away from the battle field; he made rudran, who was the leader of all these entities, to become incapacitated.

**anda anda āgulam** – rudran lost in the battle in such a way that his matted hair got diśevelled, with the flowers decorating his hair, such as *konṟai* (Indian laburnum), *tumbai* (white dead nettle, worn by soldiers in battlefield) falling off, and he was in sorrow. After seeing that he was helpless in facing up to emperumān, rudran became tearful and prayed to him in order to get his mercy. Has it not been said in harivamsam 125-8? “*pāravaśyam samāyāta*: *śuli jrumbhita tējasā* l *pāncajanyasya ghōśēṇa śārngaviśpūrjitēna ca* ll” (Sivan, who is wielding a trident, swooned and fell unconscious, after hearing the trumpeting sound of emperumān’s conch, śrī pāncajanyam, and the sound of emperumān’s bow, śrī śārngam) and in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-33-41? “*kriṣṇa kriṣṇa mahābāhō jānē tvām puruśōttamam* l *parēśam paramātmānam anādinidanam param* ll” (Oh kriṣṇa, with huge hands! I know that you alone are the best among all souls, the head of celestial entities, you are the supreme being, the one without a beginning and an end and superior to all)

**amararē aṟivarē** – celestial entities who ran in fear, for the two types of sorrows are the witness; do I have to state further?

Next, we will take up the 71<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 71. Vandulavu kodai

*vaṇḍulāvu kōdai mādar kāraṇattināl veguṇḍuiṇḍavāṇan iṟainjjñūṟu tōḷgaḷait tuṇitta nāḷmuṇḍanīṟan makkaḷ veppu mōdiyangi ōdidakkaṇḍu nāṇi vāṇanukku iranginān em māyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇḍu – beetles; ulāvu – (continuously) roaming around; kōdai – one who has garland; mādar kāraṇattināl – for the sake of his daughter, uśai; veguṇḍu – getting angry; iṇḍa – one who came close-by; vāṇan – bāṇāsuran’s; īrainjjñūṟu tōḷgaḷai – thousand shoulders; tuṇitta nāḷ – the day he severed; muṇḍan nīṟan – rudran, who has tonsured head and body smeared with ash; makkaḷ – sons such as skanda et al; veppu – deity for diseases such as fever; mōdi – kāḷi; angi – deity for fire, all these people; ōdida – as they ran away \[from the battlefield\]; kaṇḍu – looking at that; nāṇi – feeling ashamed; vāṇanukku – towards bāṇāsuran; iranginān – one who took pity; em āyanē – \[he\] is only our kriṣṇan

**Перевод:**

**For the sake of his daughter, uśai, around whose garland, beetles were roaming continuously, bāṇāsuran, with anger, waged a battle with kriṣṇa. When that bāṇāsuran neared kriṣṇan in the battlefield, kriṣṇan severed the thousand shoulders of bāṇāsuran. rudran \[who had come to help and protect his devotee bāṇāsuran\], with a tonsured head and ash smeared over his body, along with his sons skanda et al, the deity of diseases such as fever, kāḷi and agni, the deity of fire, ran away from the battlefield, leaving bāṇāsuran at the mercy of kriṣṇa. Looking at this, kriṣṇa, taking pity on bāṇāsuran since he felt ashamed at the action of rudran, let go of bāṇāsuran with his life.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says in this pāsuram “rudran had vowed that he would protect his devotee bāṇāsuran from kaṇṇan, and had taken the help of his sons as well as other deities such as kāḷi, agni et al. However, since he was not able to protect bāṇāsuran from kaṇṇan, rudran left him at the mercy of kaṇṇan and ran away from the battlefield. kaṇṇan felt ashamed that rudran could let go of someone who had taken refuge under him, took pity on bāṇāsuran and did not kill him. Thus, one could see clearly that kaṇṇan is merciful and rudran is not a protector. Does anyone need to explain this?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vaṇḍulāvu kōdai mādar kāraṇattināl** – for the sake of uśai who had decorated herself with a garland around which beetles were roaming continuously. With the usage of the phrase *vaṇḍulāvu kōdai mādar* (lady around whose garland beetles were roaming), it is implied that uśai was expectantly awaiting the arrival of anirudda, who she had seen in her dream and who was identified by her friend citralēkai and who was going to bring him to her and that with that expectation, uśai was constantly decorating herself with garland made from freshly blossomed flowers.

**veguṇḍu iṇḍa vāṇan** – bāṇāsuran, who, without realising that the one whom he was opposing was the lord of all, went head over heels in engaging in a battle with him.  The term *veguṇḍu* would refer to anger born out of displeasure. *iṇḍal* – to go nearby. Just as it has been mentioned in vihagēndra samhitai 22 as well as in śaraṇāgati gadyam “*pitaram mātaram dārān putrān bandūn sakīn gurūn* l *ratnāni danadānyāni kśētrāṇi ca gruhāṇi ca* ll *sarvadarmāmśca santyajya sarvakāmāmśca sākśarān* ll *lōkavikrānta caraṇau śaraṇam tē’vrajam vibhō* ll” (Oh bhagavān! Leaving aside father, mother, wife, children, relatives, friends, teachers, gems, wealth and foodgrains, fields, houses, all the means \[to attain you\] and all the benefits including *kaivalyam \[*where the ātmā enjoys itself\], along with any taste in these, I take refuge under your divine feet which had mercifully measured the worlds), bhagavān, who is the natural relative, is one who has to be attained leaving aside those with whom we have relationship through blood. Did not bāṇāsuran develop enmity towards that bhagavān, due to his wickedness? When his daughter, uśai, married anirudda through the method of *gāndarva vivāham* (a marriage in which a man and a woman get married without the consent of their parents), instead of feeling happy that he has become related to kaṇṇan, he became angry due to his pride and ego of having a thousand hands.

**īrainjjñūṟu tōḷgaḷait tuṇitta nāḷ** – since the reason for his waging the battle was the strength emanating from his thousand shoulders, kaṇṇan severed his thousand shoulders. On the day when this happened …

**muṇḍan nīṟan makkaḷ veppu mōdi angi ōdidak kaṇḍu nāṇi** – rudran, who had given refuge to bāṇan and who was bāṇan’s leader, saying that he will protect him, left him at the mercy of kaṇṇan and ran away from the battlefield, along with his sons and others.

**muṇḍan nīṟan** – rudran, who had tonsured his head and who had applied ash all over his body. From this, it is clear that he was practising means in order to attain kaṇṇan \[emperumān\]. rudran, who normally has a matted hair, also has a tonsured head. Both these forms indicate that he practises a means towards attaining emperumān. The opinion here is that without realising that he is under the control of kaṇṇan and that the entity with whom he had engaged in battle was his lord, rudran came with a tonsured head and ash-smeared body.

**makkaḷ veppu mōdi angi ōdidak kaṇḍu nāṇi** – rudran ran away from the battlefield along with his sons such as skanda, who is the head of the army of celestials, the deity for diseases such as fever, kāḷi and agni who is the leader of forty-nine types of fires. The term mōdi refers to *pidāri* (a village goddess); or it could refer to different forms of kāḷi. Seeing this, kaṇṇan felt ashamed. He felt ashamed because he had to fight against people who, instead of protecting one who had taken refuge under them, either by defeating the enemy or getting killed in the battle, ran away from the battle in fear. Alternatively, just as it has been said in the saying “*ārtō vā yadivā drupta*: *parēśām śaraṇāgata*: l *ari*: *prāṇān parityajya rakśitavya*: *krutātmanā* ll” (whether out of fear or without fear, if a person surrenders to an enemy, that person should be protected even if one were to give up his life), instead of protecting the person who had surrendered to one even if one were to give his life, how could he run away from the battlefield, leaving the person who had taken refuge, at the mercy of the enemy? Thus, kaṇṇan would have felt ashamed due to this reason too.

**vāṇanukku iranginān emmāyanē** – thus, bāṇan should have been protected \[by rudran\] since he had surrendered to rudran. \[Since he had been left alone in the battle\] considering his precarious situation, taking mercy on him, our leader, kaṇṇan, who had been born in the clan of covherd people, left only two shoulders of bāṇan and protected him. Thus, it is seen that some deities, even if they come forward to protect someone, will not render help during times of danger while sarvēśvaran (emperumān), even when he is angry, will protect and be a friend at the time of need. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38-35 “*sa tam nipatitam bhūmau śaraṇya*: *śaraṇāgatam* l *vadārhamapi kākutsta*: *krupayā paryapālayat* ll” (śrī rāmapirān, who is apt to be surrendered to, through his mercy, protected him \[kākāsuran\] who had fallen to the ground, though he was fit to be killed, since he surrendered to śrī rāma), isn’t emperumān one who protected even the great sinner, kākāsuran?

Next, we will take up the 72<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 72. Podin mangai

*pōdin mangai būdalak kiḻatti dēvi anṟiyumpōdu tangu nānmugan magan avan magan solilmādu tangu kūṟanēṟu adu ūrdi enṟu vēdanūlōduginṟadu uṇmai alladu illai maṟṟu uraikkilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pōdu – the flower lotus; il – having it as the place of birth; mangai – periya pirāttiyār, who is youthful forever; būdalam kiḻatti – bhūmip pirātti who is the representative deity for worlds such as earth; dēvi – are consorts (for sarvēśvaran, the lord of all); anṟiyum – further; pōdu – in the divine navel, which is like a lotus flower; tangu – residing permanently; nānmugan – brahmā, who has four faces; magan – (his) son; solil – if spoken of (based on statements from vēdas); mādu tangu kūṟan – one who has given half of his body to pārvati; ēṟadu ūrdi – Sivan, who has bull as his vehicle; avan magan – is the son of that brahmā; enṟu – like this; vēda nūl – śāstram (sacred text) such as vēdam; ōduginṟadē – only that meaning which is conveyed; uṇmai – is the truth; maṟṟu – contrary to it; uraikkil – (using āgama rules) if one tries to say; alladu – that, which is contrary to vēdam; illai – is a lie

**Перевод:**

**śrī dēvi, who was born in a lotus flower and who is young always, and bhūmi dēvi, who is regarded as the deity for all the worlds, are consorts of sarvēśvaran. Further, brahmā, who resides in the divine navel which looks like a lotus flower, is sarvēśvaran’s son. If one were to speak, based on the meanings conveyed in vēdas, Sivan, who has bull as his vehicle and who has given half of his body to his consort, pārvati, is the son of that brahmā. This is the truth, as conveyed by śāstram such as vēdas. If one tries to say anything contrary to this, it is a lie.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Some people asked āḻvār “We see in this world that some people take refuge under Sivan since he grants mōkśam. In śaivāgamam (principles governing temple worship for followers of Siva) and in tāmasa purāṇams \[treatises which praise tāmasic deities\], he is called as the supreme entity. When such is the case, how is it correct to say in the previous pāsuram that he is incapable of granting mōkśam?” āḻvār responds saying “vēdas have the greatness of not having been written by any person and of having permanence. It does not have the deficiencies such as ignorance, confusion, bewilderment etc seen in āgamams which have been written by people. Since these vēdas have classified Sivan as a jīvātmā, just as people lie about having seen an apparition in banyan tree, this \[saying that Sivan grants mōkśam\] is also a lie. Deceitful words as written in saivāgamam are also lies only”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pōdil mangai būdalak kiḻatti dēvi** – periya pirātti (śrī dēvi) who was born in a lotus flower and śrī bhūmip pirātti are the consorts of paramātmā (supreme soul, emperumān. This is widely known throughout, just as it has been mentioned in ślōkam 2-5 of puruśa sūktam which is the essence of all vēdams “*hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau*” (Oh supreme entity! śrī dēvi and bhūmi dēvi are your consorts). Has it not been mentioned in the 4<sup>th</sup> ślōkam of śrī sūktam “*padmē stitām padma varṇām tvāmi iha upahvayē śriyam*” (I beseech you, śrī dēvi, residing on a lotus flower and having the complexion of a lotus) that periya pirāttiyār resides on a lotus flower?

**anṟiyum** – that he is the consort of periya pirātti who has all the opulence, is alone sufficient to state that he is superior to everyone else and that he is apt to be attained by everyone. Is there any greater eminence than this, truly?

**anṟiyum** – apart from this, other features of emperumān’s greatness and lowliness of other celestial entities have been shown in vēdams.

**pōdu tangu nānmugan magan** – brahmā, who is also referred to as *kamalāsanan* (one who has lotus as his seat) since he was born in the lotus-like navel of the supreme entity, is his son. *vēdavākyams* (phrases from vēdams) such as mahābhāratam śanti parvam 47-56 “*ajasya nābhāvadyēkamarpitam yasminnidam viśvam bhuvanamadiśritam*” (in the divine navel of parama puruśan who is without a birth, a flower blossomed; all these worlds sustain through that flower) and yajur vēdam nrusimhatāpinī “*sa prajāpatirēka*: *puśkaraparṇē samabhavat*” (That brahmā, who is eminent, manifested from the petals of lotus flower) also say this.

**avan magan solil mādu tangu kūṟan ēṟadūrdi** – even though there are *vēdavākyams* such as “*ēka Eva rudrō na dvītiyāya tastau*” (Only Siva was present; there was no second entity) and  taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 16 “*sarvō hyēśa rudra*:” (all these \[entities\] are only rudra) and even though he is praised as the supreme entity in śaivāgamam etc, when looking at several *vēdavākyams* such as in sāmavēdam “*brahmaṇa*: *putrāya* *jyēśtāya*” (for rudran who is the eldest son of brahmā), it is clear that he is the son of brahmā. Since he has been mentioned as “*mādu tangu kūṟan*” (ardanāri or half-man, half-woman), unlike sanaka et al who were created by brahmā through his mind, rudra was born through the union with a woman and he is together with a woman. Since he is the first to be born through union with a woman, it is clear that he is a jīvan (soul like all the others). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-7-12 says “*bhrukutīkutilātdasya lalātāt krōdadīpitāt* l *samutpannas tadā rudrō madyāhnārkasamaprabha*: ll *ardanārīnaravapu*: *prasaṇḍō’tiśarīravān* ll” (From the curved forehead of brahmā which was aglow with the fire of anger, rudra came out with the splendour of sun at noon, having a form composed of half man and half woman, with huge anger and a fat form) while describing the creation of brahmā, confirming that rudra was created as an *ardanārī* (half-woman).

The reason for stating *ēṟadūrdi* is to convey rudran’s pride in having bull as his vehicle as opposed to garuda \[emperumān’s vehicle} who is described by vēda as *vēdamayan* (he is full of vēda).

**enṟu vēda nūl ōduginṟadu uṇmai** – the sayings of vēdam, which is authentic śāstram, that śrīdēvi and bhūdēvi are emperumān’s consorts, that brahmā is his son and that rudran is brahmā’s son are all true.

**alladu illai maṟṟu uraikkilē** – if an interpretation is given by āgamams or tāmasa purāṇams, which is contrary to what has been said in vēdam, it can only be a lie. Hasn’t it been said in mahābhāratam ānu parvam 43-35? “*yattatpadmamabhūt pūrvam tatra brahmā vyajāyata* l *brāhmaṇaścāpi sambhūta*: *Siva ityavadāryatām* ll *śivāt skandas sambhabhūva ētat śruśti catuśtayam* ll” (From the lotus (emanating from the divine navel of bhagavān) which was formed earlier, brahmā was born; it is to be known that Sivan was born from brahmā; from Sivan, skandan was born; These are the (initially formed) four creations)

Next, we will take up the 73<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 73. Maram poda

*maram podac caram turandu vāli vīḻa munnornāḷuram podac caram turanda umbarāḷi empirānvaranguṟippil vaittavarkku alādu vānam āḷilumnirambu nīdu bhōgam ettiṟattum yārkkum illaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnornāḷ – in an earlier trētā yugam \[an epoch which follows kruta yugam (1st epoch, lasting about 1.26 million years\]; maram – seven ebony trees; poda – to be pierced; saram – arrow; turandu – shot; vāli – vāli \[the monkey king\]; vīḻa – to fall down; uram – his chest; poda – to pierce; saram – arrow; turanda – one who shot; umbar Ali – as the lord of celestial entities; empirān – son of emperor daśaratan, and our lord, his; varam – distinguished; kuṟippil – in the divine mind; vaittavarkkalādu – other than those who have been kept; vānam āḷilum – even if one were to rule brahma lōkam (world) etc; yārkkum – for anyone; nirambu – without any deficiency; nīdu – permanent; bhōgam – the comfort of servitude; ettiṟattum – by any means; illaiyē – is not there

**Перевод:**

**emperumān incarnated as the son of emperor daśaratan, during one of the trētā yugams. He pierced seven ebony trees with his arrow. He brought down vāli, by piercing his chest, with his arrow. Entities, other than those who he keeps in his distinguished divine mind, do not have any possibility of carrying out servitude, which is without any deficiency and which is permanent, even if they were to rule brahmalōkam etc.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, it was mentioned that “Only emperumān is apt to be attained. Since brahmā and Sivan are badda jīvas (souls bonded in samsāram) they are not apt to be attained”. In this pāsuram, āḻvār says that the consort of mahālakśmi \[emperumān\], in order to shower his mercy on samsāris, incarnated as a human being and that he is capable of granting mōkśam (liberation) to brahmā, Sivan et al. Through this, āḻvār mercifully brings out the difference between emperumān, who is the lord of all celestial entities, and the other deities.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maram podac caram turandu** – shooting an arrow to pierce seven ebony trees in order to create confidence in sugrīva maharāja. Through this it is ascertained that emperumān has the quality to remove any lingering doubt that one may have regarding his capability to protect.

**vāli vīḻa munnor nāḷ uram podac caram turanda** – vāli had the strength to tie down rāvaṇa to his tail and go all over the worlds. In one of the trētā yugams which had passed, emperumān shot an arrow which pierced vāḷi’s chest and brought him down. There is a *svāpadēśam* (inner meaning) given for this action of creating confidence in sugrīva and killing vāli – for those who are favourable towards emperumān \[they accept that he is the supreme entity and that he is the means for attaining him\] and who do not have any quality which is unfavourable towards him, emperumān gives them the desire to enjoy him. He removes all the hurdles such as ignorance, karma (effect of past deeds), past impressions, taste, connection with *prakruti* (primordial matter) etc which come in the way of their enjoying him.

In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that other deities such as brahmā et al are *varam taramidukkilāda dēvar* (incapable of giving mōkśam). In this pāsuram, he shows that emperumān is the exact opposite of those deities.

**umbarāḷi** – The term *umbar* would refer to *nityasūris* who are permanent dwellers in the *nityavibhūti* (permanent or spiritual realm). Through this, it is implied that emperumān, who takes such nityasūris, who have no ignorance, under his wings as his servitors, also helps such lowly monkeys. Alternatively, it could also be considered that nityasūris are those who realise the simplicity of emperumān who removes the doubts in the minds of those regarding his ability to protect, by removing their enemies.

**empirān** – through this act of protecting sugrīva, he won me \[āḻvār\] over. Alternatively, the term *umbar* could be taken as referring to celestial entities such as brahmā et al. Then, the meaning for *umbarāḷi empirān* could be taken as – through the act of protecting sugrīva, emperumān is one who has won over both the celestial entities, who desire other benefits, and us \[entities such as āḻvār\] who do not desire other benefits, without any distinction The verse *uram podac caram turanda empirān* could be taken to imply that the help rendered by emperumān to sugrīvan is the help rendered by him to āḻvār.

**varam kuṟippil vaittavarkku alādu** – apart from those who have been given a place in the distinguished, divine mind of cakravartit tirumagan (śrī rāma, emperumān).

**varam kuṟippu** – distinction for the mind is to consider only good biding for others.

**vānamāḷilum** – even if they are distinguished with their supreme knowledge and rule over the worlds below and above the earth, such as brahmā et al.

**niramba nīdu bhōgam** – the experience which is without any shortcoming and which is permanent. In other words, the *bhagavad anubhavam* (experiencing emperumān) which occurs in mōkśam due to *paramabhakti* (ability to sustain oneself in the presence of emperumān and giving up life in his absence), which is complete, which is vivid and which is permanent.

**ettiṟattum yārkkum illaiyē** – it is not that it is not present in brahmā et al who desire other benefits. Even for the most eminent entities who do not desire any benefit other than emperumān, it will not occur without emperumān’s mercy. It will not occur without his mercy whether one observes other means such as *karma, jñāna, bhakti yōgams* (paths of deeds, knowledge and devotion, respectively) or one considers emperumān who is readily available, as the means to attain him. Irrespective of whether one considers him as the benefit despite having supreme knowledge, and consider him as both the means and the ultimate goal or one considers him as the means and benefit since one does not have any knowledge, for everyone, his mercy alone is the means for attaining mōkśam. The collective meaning of this pāsuram is that even for brahmā, Sivan et al who are in eminent positions, without the mercy of the son of daśaratan, there is no means for attaining the permanent mōkśam.

Next, we will take up the 74<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 74. Arindarindu

*aṟindaṟindu vāmanan adiyiṇai vaṇanginālseṟindeḻunda jñānamōdu selvamum siṟandidummaṟindeḻunda teṇḍiraiyuḷ mannu mālai vāḻttinālpaṟindeḻundu tīvinaigaḷ paṟṟaṟudal pānmaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāmanan – emperumān who incarnated as vāmanan, his; adiyiṇai – both the divine feet; aṟindaṟindu – knowing that they are the means and the ultimate goal; vaṇangināl – if one worships (them); seṟindu – strong; eḻunda – happened; jñānamōdu – with true knowledge (about ātmā, soul); selvamum – wealth of devotion; siṟandidum – will come in abundance; maṟindu – spread out; eḻiunda – being with agitation; teḷ – being clear; tirai uḷ – in kśīrābdi (milky ocean) which has waves; mannu – (residing) permanently; mālai – one who is affectionate; vāḻttināl – if one were to recite through the mouth; tīvinaigaḷ – sins (which are tied to ātma svarūpam); paṟindu eḻundu – getting out (from that); paṟṟaṟudal – and getting destroyed, with their impressions; pānmaiyē – is natural only

**Перевод:**

**If one worships the two divine feet of emperumān who incarnated as vāmanan, knowing that they are the means \[to attain him\] and the ultimate goal \[to enjoy after attaining him\], one would get strong, true knowledge about ātmā as well as the wealth of devotion, in abundance. If one recites the divine names of emperumān who is permanently residing in the milky ocean, which is clear, spread out and has waves, all the sins which are attached to the ātma svarūpam will get destroyed, naturally.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) If the mercy of emperumān, who is apt to be attained, is the means for getting mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), what should the person, who desires mōkśam, do, in order to get emperumān’s mercy? āḻvār says that vorśipping at the divine feet of śrī vāmanan and reciting the divine names which describe the simplicity of emperumān will bring the ultimate goal \[śrī vaikuṇṭam\] to this person.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**aṟindaṟindu vāmanan adiyiṇai vaṇangināl** – if one were to firmly believe that the divine feet of vāmanan, who came in the form of an alms-seeker in order to get back his possession, are the means and end goal and submit the responsibility accordingly. The reason behind saying *aṟindu* twice is to show that one should fall at the divine feet of emperumān after knowing that only his divine feet are the means to attain him and that they are also the benefit to be enjoyed after attaining him. Alternatively, it could also be construed as “By hearing through śāstram (sacred texts) and knowing from ācāryan’s instructions”. Another interpretation is: since the divine feet of vāmanan were kept on the heads of everyone, without seeing the distinction between the eminent and lowly, the divine feet are those of an entity who is simplicity personified. This phrase is an explanation for the term “*anṟu pāraḷanda pāda pōdu*” which we had seen in the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

**seṟindeḻunda jñānamōdu selvamum siṟandidum** – the term “*seṟinda jñānam*” refers to the true knowledge about ātmā who is inside the physical form of an entity. The term “*eḻunda jñānam*” refers to the fact that this knowledge will take the person till the stage where he sees the ātmā directly. True knowledge is knowing that this ātmā is subservient only to emperumān and to none else. Since knowledge about other entities outside is superficial knowledge, it cannot be considered to be true knowledge. Through the term “*selvam*“, the devotion that one would get through this knowledge is indicated. Did not poygai āḻvār too mercifully say in his mudal tiruvandādi 43 “*danamāya tanē kaikūdum*” (knowledge and devotion which are the wealth for a śrīvaiṣṇava will come on their own) emphasising that for a śrīvaiṣṇava, devotion is wealth? Since kainkaryam (carrying out servitude to emperumān) results from this devotion, this will be an auxiliary for bhagavat kainkaryam (service rendered to emperumān). Since the term “*sīrappu*” refers to abundance, the term *“sīrandidum*” indicates that the knowledge and devotion will be attained in full. Reference to knowledge and devotion here would also be symbolic of the kainkaryam which results from these two as well as to elimination of hurdles such as sins which is required for the fruition of knowledge and devotion.

**maṟindeḻunda teṇḍiraiyuḷ mannu mālai vāḻttināl** – āḻvār says that even those, who do not have the maturity to firmly believe that emperumān alone is the means and the end-benefit, could attain the same benefit by reciting the divine names of emperumān, without expecting any benefit. By reciting the divine names of the affectionate entity who is permanently residing in the divine milky ocean which has clear waves which rise in all four directions. The term “*maṟidal”* refers to being spread out everywhere. In order to clear the doubt “Would one get the benefit by merely reciting the divine names?” āḻvār refers to emperumān as *māl* – one who is affectionate.

**paṟindu eḻundu tīvinaigaḷ paṟṟu aṟudal** – emperumān will ensure that pāpa and puṇya (sinful and virtuous deeds), which are bonded inseparably to ātmā since time immemorial, and which are hurdles in attaining him, will be removed and destroyed without leaving any trace of their impressions on the ātmā,

**pānmaiyē** – since these deeds are incidental to ātma svarūpam \[basic nature of ātmā\], their leaving the ātmā could only be termed as natural. Just as we had seen earlier in the 18-66<sup>th</sup> ślōkam from bhagavat gītā “*sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi*” (I will relieve you from all your sins), if all these sins, which are incidental to the ātma, leave the ātmā, due to the natural characteristic of *śēśatvam* (ātmā being totally subservient to emperumān), *kainkaryam* (service to emperumān) will occur on its own. Just as in the first half reference to jñānam and bhakti (knowledge and devotion) were indicative of elimination of hurdles, in the second half, elimination of hurdles is indicative of that knowledge and devotion and the resultant servitude.

Next, we will take up the 75<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 75. Onri ninru

*onṟi ninṟu naṟṟavam seydu ūzi ūḻi tōṟellāmninṟu ninṟavan guṇangaḷ uḷḷi uḷḷam tūyarāyccenṟu senṟu dēvadēvar umbar umbar umbarāyanṟi engaḷ sengaṇmālai yāvar kāṇa vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

onṟi ninṟu – engaging the mind; nal tavam seydu – carrying out good penance, without expecting any benefit; ūḻi ūḻi tōṟellām – for many kalpams (a long duration of time); ninṟu ninṟu – gradually; avan guṇangaḷ – the qualities (of emperumān who is most pious); uḷḷi – meditating upon; uḷḷam tūyarāy – having a pure heart; senṟu senṟu – reaching in an orderly way till the status of meditation; umbar umbar umbarāy anṟi – other than reaching the states of parabhakti (knowing about emperumān), parajñānam (seeing emperumān) and paramabhakti (living only if together with emperumān, perishing otherwise); dēvadēvar – as the controller of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrī vaikuṇṭam); engaḷ – our (those who have surrendered to emperumān); sengaṇmālai – affectionate one with eyes which are like a reddish lotus; yāvar kāṇa vallar – who is capable of seeing

**Перевод:**

**Other than engaging the mind, meditating for a long time on the auspicious qualities of emperumān, and carrying out penance without expecting any benefit, with a pure heart, reaching in an orderly way the states of parabhakti (having knowledge about emperumān), parajñānam (seeing emperumān) and parama bhakti (living only in the presence of emperumān or perishing in his absence), it is not possible to attain emperumān. Who is capable of seeing that emperumān, who is the controller of nityasūris, who is affectionate towards his followers, who has eyes which are like reddish lotus and to whom we have surrendered?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “Either through surrendering to emperumān, considering him as the means and end-goal or through reciting his divine names, treating that \[the recitation\] itself as the end benefit, one can get rid of the hurdles which prevent his mercy from reaching him / her and one can easily attain emperumān”. Starting from this pāsuram, for seven pāsurams, he mercifully says “If one were to attain him through his / her efforts, it will be very difficult”. In this pāsuram, he says that starting with karma yōgam (path of deeds) until parama bhakti (ability to sustain oneself only in the presence of emperumān), it is difficult to attain emperumān through one’s efforts.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**onṟi ninṟu** – Just as arjuna had said, as in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 6-34 “*cancalam hi mana*: *kriṣṇa pramāti balavad drudam* l *tasyā’ham nigraham manyē vāyōriva suduśkaram* ll” (Oh kriṣṇa! Mind is unstable, bewildering, strong (towards worldly matters) and wavering. I think that it is difficult to be controlled just as it is difficult to control the flow of wind), since mind is deeply engaged with worldly matters such as *śabdam* (sound, form etc), it is difficult to retrieve it saying “Only emperumān is apt to be attained” and engaging with emperumān with that mind.

**nal tavam seydu** – the word *tapas* (penance) here is symbolic of deeds such as *yajñam* (rituals), *dānam* (bestowing to the needy) etc just as it has been said in bhruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-4-22 “*tamētam vēdānuvacanēna brāhmaṇā vividiśanti yajñēna dānēna tapasā’nāśakēna* l” (wise men who are knowledgeable about emperumān, desire to know about emperumān through reciting *vēdas* (sacred texts), *yajñam* (rituals), *dānam* (donating alms), *tapas* (penance (which does not expect any benefit in the result)); bhagavat gītā ślōkam 18-5 “*yajñō dānam tapa*: *karma na tyājyam kāryamēva tat* l *yajñō dānam tapa*: *karma bhāvanāni manīśiṇām* ll” (Deeds such as rituals, donation, penance are not apt to be given up; they are to be necessarily carried out. (This is because such deeds purify a person)). The word *naṟṟavam* refers to the karmayōgam (path of deed) which is carried out after giving up the thoughts that “This is my deed, the benefit for this deed belongs to me and this deed is carried out by me”, keeping in the forefront true knowledge about jīvātmā and paramātmā.

**ūḻi ūḻi tōṟelām** – just as it takes a long time for dirt to destroy a new dress, it would take a long time for removing the sins which act as hurdles in practising *bhakti yōgam* (path of devotion) through karmas (deeds). This is buttressed by lagvatri smruti ślōkam “*janmāntara sahasrēśu tapōjñāna samādibhi*: l *narāṇām kśīṇa pāpānām kriṣṇē bhakti*: *prajāyatē* ll” (After observing karma yōgam and jñāna yōgam during tens of thousands of births, (for those who have overcome hurdles which are inimical to the starting of bhakti yōgam) devotion towards kriṣṇa will happen)

**ninṟu ninṟu avan guṇangaḷ uḷḷi** – meditating on the auspicious qualities of emperumān who is the most pious and who destroys the sins of those who meditate on him, gradually.

**ninṟu ninṟu** – while describing the seven means towards engaging with devotion through firm meditation, brahmanandi vākyam says “*tallabhdi*: *vivēka-vimōha-abhyāsa-kriyā-kalyāṇa-anavasāda-anuddarśēbhya*” (devotion through stable meditation occurs through 1) *vivēkam* (cleanliness in body resulting from eating proper food), 2) *vimōham* (detachment), 3) *abhyāsam* (frequent meditation), 4) *kriya*i (carrying out karmas, deeds), 5) *kalyāṇa* (qualities of ātmā), 6) *anavasādam* (without mental fatigue) and 7) *anuddarśam* (without excessive mental happiness)). Through these seven steps and through continuous meditation, one should meditate on the auspicious qualities of ātmā.

**uḷḷam tūyarāy** – through karma yōgam mentioned earlier and through meditating on the auspicious qualities of emperumān, getting a purified mind just as it has been said in vyāsa smruti “*manasātu viśuddēna*” (only through a pure mind could (emperumān) be known) and as said in śrī bhāśyam “*manasā grāhya*:” (paramātmā is known through (pure) mind).

**senṟu senṟu** – gradually reaching till the stage of meditating on emperumān without any break, just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 7-26 2 “*āhāra śuddau satva śuddi*: *satva śuddau druvā smruti*: l *smruti lambhē sarvagrantīnām vipramōkśa*: ll” (by taking pure \[ordained\] food, mind gets purified; once mind becomes pure, firm thought about *ātmadyāna* (meditating on emperumān) stage is reached; once that happens, all the knots such as *avidya* (ignorance) etc get loosened) and just as it has been said in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 6-5-6 “*ātmā vā arē draśtavya*: *śrōtavya: mantavyō*: *nididyāsitavya*:” (ātmā (here the word ātmā refers to paramātmā, the supreme being) is apt to be heard, to be thought of and to be meditated upon; it is apt to be seen).

**dēvadēvarumbarumbarumbarāyanṟi** – unless one reaches gradually the stages of *parabhakti* (acquiring knowledge about emperumān), *parajñānam* (seeing emperumān) and *parama bhakti* (sustaining oneself only in the presence of emperumān and perishing in his absence), which have been mercifully showered on nityasūris by their svāmy (emperumān)

**engaḷ sengaṇ mālai** – one who has reddish lotus eyes, who is affectionate towards us who have surrendered to him, by treating him as our means. The term *engaḷ* could be considered as referring to the other āḻvārs or it could be considered as referring to people like kaṇikaṇṇan who have taken refuge under him, and who have taken the path of surrendering to emperumān. Since he has used the term *sengaṇmāl*, just as it has been said in jitantē ślōkam 1-1 “*jitantē puṇḍarīkākśa namastē viśvabhāvana* l *namastē’stu hruśīkēśa mahāpuruśa pūrvaja*: ll” (Oh one who has eyes which are like reddish lotus! (This ātma entity) has been won over by you; (this) is not mine; Oh one who created the worlds! Oh one who controls all the sensory perceptions! Oh one who is magnanimous! Let this (ātma entity) remain only for you and not for me), it is indicated here that emperumān, starting with developing an interest in him and ending with *parama bhakti*, creates involvement in them \[āḻvār and others\], through his distinguished mercy.

**yāvar kāṇa vallarē** – who could see him, just as we, who had nothing with us, had seen him only through his mercy? One should add the previous lines and get the meaning – other than gradually reaching the stage of *parama bhakti* which is the stage of nityasūris, a stage which they had been granted by emperumān, who could see our *sengaṇmāl* (affectionate entity with eyes which are like reddish lotus), who is the lord of nityasūris? Thus, it is indicated here that it is difficult to see emperumān by observing means such as karma yōgam and that even if one were to attain him through that means, it would not equal the state reached by āḻvār et al who had seen him through emperumān’s causeless mercy.

Next, we will take up the 76<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.
