## 76. Punpula vali

*pun pula vaḻi adaittu arakku ilaccinai seydunan pula vaḻi tiṟandu jñāna naṟcudar koḷeeienbil eḷgi nenjurugi uḷ kanindu eḻundadōranbil anṟi āḻiyānai yāvar kāṇa vallarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pul – being very lowly; pulan vaḻi – the path for senses to reach worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc; adaittu – blocking it; arakku ilaccinai seydu – sealing it (that path) with shellac such that even their scent cannot follow; nal pula vaḻi – senses in the apt bhagavat viśayam (matters related to emperumān); tiṟandu – opening that path; jñānam – knowledge; nal sudar – the good light; koḷeei – lighting it; enbil eḷgi – with the physical form, made of bones, wilting; nenju urugi – with the heart melting; uḷ – inside; kanindu – maturing; eḻundadu – rising in the form of parama bhakti (deep devotion); ōr – unique; anbil anṟi – without that devotion; āḻiyānai – one who is standing, holding the disc (for our sake); yāvar kāṇa vallar – who is capable of seeing?

**Перевод:**

**One should block the lowly path of senses in reaching worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc and seal it with shellac such that even their scent cannot follow. Then, one should open the path for senses to reach to the apt bhagavat viśayam to light the flame of knowledge. With the body wilting and the heart melting, maturing inside, paramabhakti will rise. Without that unique devotion, who is capable of seeing emperumān, who is standing, holding the disc?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) If one were to start with karma yōgam (path of deeds) initially, in order to carry out the deeds, lot of materials will have to be earned. During the process of earning as well as during activities such as war, yāgam (carrying out rituals) etc, blemish of hurting others will result. Due to this reason, instead of starting with karma yōgam which requires acting through the five senses, some people may decide to start with jñāna yōgam (the path of knowledge) which entails controlling the senses from acting on their own. āḻvār says in this pāsuram that even for those people who start with jñāna yōgam, it is difficult to see emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**punpula vaḻi adaittu** – blocking the way of senses from engaging with lowly worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc. Lowliness of matters such as śabdam comes from their not having great sweetness; even the sweetness that they have is meagre, not long lasting and creating hatred towards *bhagavat viśayam* (matters relating to emperumān) which is apt for our basic nature. Blocking their path refers to turning the senses away from them and taking them towards ‘*pratyāhāram*’ \[withdrawal or retreat\] as mentioned in *aśtānga yōgam* \[a method for practising meditation\].

**arakku ilaccanai seydu** – repeatedly recalling the lowliness of worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc and ensuring that even their scent will not follow one. Just as shellac is applied on the lock of a house to ensure that thieves et al do not enter, ensuring that even the scent of worldly pursuits will not enter. Just as manu smruti 2-215 says “*mātrā svasrā duhitrā vā na viviktāsanō bhavēt* l *balavān indriyagrāmō vidvāmsamapi karśati* ll” (A man should not even sit in the same seat with his mother or sister or daughter, while being alone. The group of senses, which are strong, will pull down even a strong-willed person), the scent of worldly pursuits will pull down even knowledgeable persons. Only in order to ensure that even this scent does not enter, that reference to stamping with shellac has been mentioned.

**nan pula vaḻi tiṟandu** – opening the path such that senses will go in the path of matters relating to ātmā and paramātmā (supreme being). Just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 9-2 “*rājavidyā rājaguhyam pavitram idam uttamam* \| *pratyakśāvagamam darmyam susukam kartum avyayam* ll” (Among sciences and secrets, this \[knowledge about emperumān\] is the most eminent; among those which nullify sins, this is the most superior; it manifests me \[emperumān\] directly; it is the means for attaining me; it is easy to practise; (even after giving the end benefit) it does not get destroyed), āḻvār says that showing the matters relating to emperumān which are sweet for anyone, during the stage of practising means, to the senses, is *nan pula vaḻi tiṟandu*.

**jñāna naṟcudar koḷeei** – creating the beneficial light of knowledge; the opinion here is to make the *svarūpam* (basic nature), *rūpam* (divine form), *vibhūtis* (spiritual and materialistic realms; emperumān’s wealth) etc belonging to emperumān to be the target for one’s knowledge.

**enbil eḷgi** – making the physical form to wilt by piercing the bones \[matters relating to emperumān should be imbibed such that they go to the extent of bones, making the body to wither\]. The term *eḷgal* refers to the body withering to a wilted stage.

**nenjurugi** – making the heart like a liquid, as if it were melting. The implied meaning is that through constant practise, knowledge is made to reach the stage of *parama bhakti*.

**uḷkanindu eḻundadōr anbil anṟi** – there is no other way to see emperumān than through making that fruit of parama bhakti to mature well inside and blossom as affection.

**āḻiyānai yāvar kāṇa vallarē** – in the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had referred to emperumān as “*engaḷ sengaṇmāl”* \[our affectionate entity with eyes which are like reddish lotus\]. Instead of that term, here he uses the term *āḻiyānai* \[one who holds the divine disc\]. Just as nammāḻvār said in periya tiruvandnhādi 87 “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān nammēl vinai kadivān*” (emperumān, who has the divine disc in his divine hand constantly, will remove our past deeds), emperumān removes through his divine disc, the sins which were the reason for engaging with worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc as well as sins which were hurdles in not engaging with emperumān. By manifesting the beauty of his divine hand and the divine disc which are joined together, emperumān creates devotion towards him. āḻvār asks as to who could see such *a cakradāri* (one who holds the divine disc) the way in which he had seen. Those who desire to see emperumān through their own efforts will have to first get rid of the engagement which their senses have towards worldly pursuits. Next, they have to keep matters relating to emperumān as the target for their senses. Then they have to transform the knowledge in matters relating to emperumān as knowledge which is the form of devotion, just like oil flows without any scattering, when opened from a tap. Then it has to mature as *parabhakti, parajñānam* and finally as *parama bhakti*. The opinion here is that it is indeed very difficult to achieve this stage.

Next, we will take up the 77<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 77. Ettum ettum ettumay

*ettum ettum ettumāy ōr ēḻum ēḻum ēḻumāyettum mūnṟum onṟumāgi ninṟa ādi dēvanaiettināya bēdamōdu iṟainji ninṟu avan peyaretteḻuttum ōduvārgaḷ vallar vānam āḷavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ettum ettum ettumāy – as three times eight viz. as twenty-four acētana (without knowledge) entities; ōr – important; ēḻum – as seven land masses; ēḻum – as seven anchoring mountains; ēḻumāy – as seven oceans; ettu mūnṟum onṟumāgi – as twelve ādityas (sun); ninṟa – one who is remaining; ādi dēvanai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all) who is the causative entity; ettināya bēdamōdu – with aśtānga praṇāmam (prostrating with eight limbs); iṟainji ninṟu – vorśipping him; avan peyar – among his divine names; etteḻuttum – tiruvaśtākśaram (a divine name which has eight syllables); ōduvārgaḷ – those who recite with their mouths; vānam – the sky of parama padam; āḷa vallarē – are capable of ruling over it

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran remains as the twenty-four acētana entities, the seven land masses, the seven anchoring mountains and the seven oceans. He is also the twelve ādityas. Those who prostrate before him with their eight limbs, worship him and recite his divine name of tiruvaśtākśaram, are capable of ruling over parama padam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous two pāsurams, āḻvār had mercifully said that through bhakti yōgam (path of devotion) which is achieved through karma yōgam (path of deeds) and jñāna yōgam (path of knowledge), one could attain emperumān. This was done through the mode of vēdāntam (the end portion of vēdas, also called as upaniśat). Starting from this pāsuram, over five pāsurams, āḻvār mercifully states the difficulty in attaining emperumān by practising devotion through the mode of itihāsams (epics) and purāṇams \[treatises on emperumān which are old, but which give new meanings each time that they are read\]. Among these five pāsurams, in this pāsuram, he says that those who carry out devotion by performing aśtānga praṇāmam to emperumān, who is the causative entity for all the worlds, dwelling inside all the ātmās as antaryāmi, and reciting tirumantram (one of the three esoteric meanings, composed of eight syllables), will have parama padam (śrī vaikuṇṭam) under their control.*

**ettum ettum ettumāy** – emperumān remains as the ātmā for twenty-four acētana entities and gives them existence. These twenty-four entities are: (1) mūlaprakruti (primordial matter), (2) mahān (huge mass, a derivative of mūlaprakruti), (3) ahankāram (ego), (4) mind, (5) to (9) five senses of knowledge, namely body, mouth, eyes, nose and ears, (10) to (14) five senses of deeds, namely speech, hands, legs, two excretory organs, (15) to (19) five tanmātras (rudimentary or subtle elements) namely śabdam (sound), sparśam (touch), rūpam (form) rasam (taste) and gandam (smell), and (20) to (24) five elements of ākāśam (ethereal layer), vāyu (wind), agni (fire), nīr (water) and nilam (earth). The reason for equating these twenty-four elements with emperumān by saying *ettum ettum ettumāy* is because they are the physical forms for paramātmā, who is their ātmā. Through this verse, samaśti sruśti (aggregate creation) in which mūla prakruti undergoes transformation, has been described. Further, the pāsuram describes vyaśti sruśti, in which the creation of individual entities derived from breaking down of aggregates takes place.

**ōr ēḻum ēḻum ēḻumāy** – the seven land masses, the seven anchoring mountains which hold these land masses and the seven oceans which are the boundaries for the land masses. Mentioning these includes all the other worlds within the universe, as well as all the entities which live in these lands, mountains and oceans.

**ettum mūnṟum onṟumāgi ninṟa** – emperumān remains as the antarātmā (in-dwelling soul) for the twelve ādityas (suns). Mentioning twelve ādityas here is representative of all the celestial entities who have as their heads, eleven rudras, eight vasus and the two asvinī dēvas as their heads.  Just as it has been said in manu smruti ślōkam 3-76 “*agnau prāstāhuti*: *samyagādityam upatiśtatē* l *ādityājjāyatē vruśtir vruttērannam tata*: *prajā*: ll” (āhuti (offering) which has been put into fire by one who conducts the ritual, reaches āditya (sun); from that sun, rain forms; from that rain food and from that food, progeny are formed), considering the prominence of āditya, he has been mentioned explicitly, while including the other celestial entities through him.

**ādi dēvanai** – one who is the efficient or instrumental cause, the material cause and the accessory cause \[when a potter makes a pot, the potter becomes the efficient or instrumental cause, clay becomes the material cause and the wheel and stick which are used in the making of pot, are the accessory cause\] and who is attained by all, including celestial entities, human entities et al. During the time of deluge, he \[emperumān\] remains as the causative entity having the subtle forms of cētana (those with knowledge) and acētana (those without knowledge) entities as his physical form; during the time of creation, he is the effective entity, having the gross forms of cētana and acētana entities as his physical form. Due to this, it is implied that he becomes the material cause for creation. Just as celestial entities are lord for human entities, emperumān becomes the lord for celestial entities and is attained by all entities. This is shown through the term *dēvan*.

**ettināya bēdamōdu iṟainji ninṟu** – attaining emperumān through prostrating before him with the eight limbs. Prostrating with eight limbs is: (1) falling down in front of him, without ego, (2) and (3) making emperumān as the focus for mind and intellect, (4) and (5) two legs, (6) and (7) two hands and (8) head, in the form of a tortoise, on earth, and vorśipping him. Hasn’t this been brought out in sātvata samhitai 6-187 too? “*manō bhudyabhimānēna saha nyasya darātalē* l *kūrmavacca tura: pādān śiras tatvai pancamam* ll” (along with mind, intellect, ego, prostrating with hands, legs and head is termed as aśtānga namaskāram)

**avan peyar etteḻuttum ōduvārgaḷ** – those who recite the distinguished tiruvaśtākśara mantram (eight syllabled mantram) which is superior to the śatakśari (six syllabled) mantram of viṣṇu and the dvādadaśākśari (twelve syllabled) mantram of vāsudēva, all of which have been hinted in the first two lines of the pāsuram, while speaking about emperumān remaining as antaryāmi. Just as it has been mentioned in the first two lines, those who meditate on the meanings of this mantram, recite the mantram through their mouth and prostrate before emperumān with their eight limbs, thus carrying out worship through all the three faculties of mind, speech and body, will control paramapadam. Just as tirumantram is taken as referring to his being the causative factor, authentic texts too \[as detailed below\] say that the entity who is the causative factor for the worlds is nārāyaṇan:  
manu smruti 1-10 “*āpō nārā iti prōktā āpō vai narasūnava*: \| *tā yadasyāyanam pūrvam tēna nārāyaṇa*: *smruta*: ll” (Water is referred to as nāram; did it not come out of bhagavān who is naran? That water became his dwelling abode. Hence, he is called as nārāyaṇan).  
mahābhāratam ānu parvam 186-7 “*narājjātāni tatvāni nārāṇīti tatō vidu*: l *tānyēva cāyanam tasya tēna nārāyaṇa*: *smruta*: ll” (All existing entities came from nārāyaṇan. Hence, people know them as nārā*:*. They became his dwelling abodes. Hence, he is meditated upon as nārāyaṇan).

Next, we will take up the 78<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 78. Sorvilada kadalal

*sōrvilāda kādalāl todakkaṟā manattarāynīrarāvaṇaik kidanda ninmalan nalangaḻalārvamōdu iṟainji ninṟavan peyar etteḻuttumvāramāga ōduvārgaḷ vallar vānam āḷavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nīr – in tiruppāṟkadal; arāvu – tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); aṇai – on the mattress; kidanda – one who is mercifully reclining; ninmalan – emperumān, who is faultless, his; nalangaḻal – divine feet which are beneficial; ārvamōdu – with devotion; iṟainji ninṟu – praying; sōrvu ilāda – without any lowliness; kādalāl – with affection; todakku aṟā – without any disruption in thoughts; manattarāy – with mind; avan – that svāmy’s; peyar – divine name of; etteḻuttum – the divine eight syllables; vāramāga – as refuge; ōduvārgaḷ – those who recite; vānamāḷa – to rule parama padam; vallarē – are capable of

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, who is without any fault, is mercifully reclining atop ādiśēśan in tiruppāṟkadal. Those who pray to his divine, beneficial feet, with affection which does not have any lowliness and recite the divine eight syllables of that svāmy without any disruption in the thoughts of their mind, are capable of ruling śrī vaikuṇṭam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that those who carry out devotion with tirumantram will attain parama padam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). To a question “For meditation which is in the form of devotion, is not a divine form, which is the auspicious refuge, required?” he responds in this pāsuram. emperumān has come down to recline in tiruppāṟkadal for the sake of followers in this world (here, the term world refers to entire leelā vibhūti, the materialistic realm\], created by him. Those who take refuge under the divine form of tiruppāṟkadal nātan (lord of milky ocean), knowing the relationship between them and emperumān through tirumantram, and have constant affection towards emperumān, will reach parama padam and surely attain the kingdom of servitude, says āḻvār.*

**sōrvilāda kādalāl** – during all times, having affection which is not diluted with other worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc. In other words, due to excessive affection, these people will be ecstatic while being with emperumān and will be despondent when separated from him.

**todakkaṟā manattarāy** – having a mind which will continuously follow emperumān. This refers to a state when stable meditation occurs, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 7-26-2 “*āhāra śuddau satva śuddi*: *satva śuddau druvā smruti*: l  
*smruti lambhē sarvagrantīnām vipramōkśa*: ll” (by taking pure \[ordained\] food, mind gets purified; once mind becomes pure, firm thought about *ātmadyānam* (meditating on emperumān) occurs; once that happens, all the knots such as *avidy*A (ignorance) etc get loosened).

**nīrāravaṇaik kidanda ninmalan nalangaḻal** – the sweet, divine feet of emperumān, who is without fault and who is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan in tiruppāṟkadal. This refers to the auspicious, divine form of emperumān which is apt to be attained, which creates affection that is in the form of meditation and which is the most distinguished entity for meditation.

**nīrarāvaṇaik kidanda** – one who is reclining on ādiśēśan in tiruppāṟkadal; through this verse, the tenderness of his form which requires spreading out ādiśēśan on water so that even water will not be an irritant to his divine form, his quality of being together with his followers who do not know anything other than himself, his divine qualities of saulabhyam (simplicity), sauśeelyam (an eminent person moving with lowly people), vātsalyam (motherly forbearance) etc with which he has come to tiruppāṟkadal much before celestial entities, who desire other benefits could come to him, in order to shower his mercy on them, have been mentioned.

**ninmalan nalangaḻal** – emperumān is without any deficiency; the sweet, divine feet of such emperumān are capable of destroying the sins of his followers. The implied meaning here is that the divine feet of emperumān, who is capable of removing the sins of his followers which are hurdles in their carrying out devotion towards him and who is the exact opposite of all faulty and lowly qualities, will create a desire in his followers towards emperumān.

**ārvamōdu iṟainji ninṟu** – being affectionate towards emperumān and taking refuge under him. muṇḍaka upaniśat 1-2-12 says “*parīkśya lōkān karmacitān brāhmaṇō nirvēdamāyāt nāstyakruta*: *krutēna*” (that brāhmaṇa person who is a *brahma jñān*i (knowledgeable about the supreme entity) and who sees the various worlds which are attained through karmas and who feels sad that paramātmā, who is ever present, cannot be attained through karmas which are evanescent…). Just as it has been mentioned in this, instead of attaining him like devotees who think “This world is transient; emperumān alone is permanent”, attaining him through the thought of love thinking “emperumān is very sweet and dear”.

**avan peyar etteḻuttum** – the divine name of tiruppāṟkadal nātan, with eight syllables. Is the divine name of nārāyaṇan, which is associated with parama pada nātan \[emperumān’s divine name in śrī vaikuṇṭam\] also applicable to tiruppāṟkadal nātan? harivamśam 113-62 says “*ēśa nārāyaṇa śrīmān kśīrārṇava nikētana*: l *nāgaparyankam utsrujya hyāgatō madurāmpurīm* ll” (this śrīman nārāyaṇan himself, who has reclined in tiruppāṟkadal, has left the mattress of anantāḻvān and mercifully reached madurā). Thus, as mentioned in this pramāṇam (authentic text), as said in tiruppāvai 1 “*nārāyaṇanē namakkē paṟai taruvān”* (nārāyaṇan alone will give us servitude to serve him) and as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-13 ślōkam “*apravīccruṇu mē rāma śatyam satya parākrama* l  
*bhavān nārāyaṇō dēva*:  *śrīmāmścakrāyudō vibhu*: ll” (Oh rāma, the true warrior! Listen to my words: You are the consort of divine mahālakśmi, the one who holds divine disc, and the lord nārāyaṇa who pervades everything and everywhere) for incarnations of paramapada nātan such as tiruppāṟkadal nātan, śrī rāma, kriṣṇa et al, the divine name nārāyaṇa is applicable. The implied meaning is that his divine and most important name of nārāyaṇan will follow in all the abodes where he incarnates.

**vāramāgal ōduvārgaḷ vallar vānamāḷavē** – just as it has been said in nāradīyam “*āsīnā vā śayānā vā tiśtantōyatra kutra vā* l *namō nārāyaṇāyēti mantraika śaraṇā vayam* ll”  
(We are those who hold on to the divine name of nārāyaṇa as the means, whether we are sitting or sleeping or standing somewhere), those who have engaged deeply in meditating on tirumantram, with its meaning, are capable of ruling over parama padam. The opinion here is that emperumān would have made parama padam to be just like what these people desire it to be. We could also construe the meaning for the term *vānamāḷa vallar* as those who attain the empire of servitude in which one wears the crown of the divine feet of emperumān on one’s head.

Next, we will take up the 79<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 79. Pattinodu pattumay

*pattinōdu pattumāy or ēḻinōdu onbadāyppattinān disaikkaṇ ninṟa nādu peṟṟa nanmaiyāypattināya tōṟṟamōdu orāṟṟal mikka ādi pālpattarām avarkkalādu mutti muṟṟalāgumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pattinōdu – along with the ten directions; pattumāy – as the dikpālas of (those who guard) these ten directions; orēḻinōdu – along with the unique saptasvaram (seven notes in music); oronbadāy – along with the nine pathos of dance starting with śringāram (love); pattinān disaikkaṇ ninṟa – fourteen types of; nādu peṟṟa nanmaiyāy – for the worldly pursuits enjoyed by the people; pattināya – ten types of; tōṟṟamōdu – coming with incarnations; or – unique; āṟṟal – through the quality of patience; mikka – having in abundance; ādi pāl – to the ādimūrti (emperumān, who has been there since the beginning); pattarām avarkkalādu – other than being engaged with him; mutti – the benefit of mōkśam (liberation); muṟṟal – to mature; āgumē – is it possible?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān is the antaryāmi (in-dwelling soul) of ten directions and those who guard these ten directions. He is the controller of the seven notes of music and the nine pathos of dance. He created the worldly pursuits to be enjoyed by the people in the fourteen worlds (seven from earth, above, and seven, below earth). In order to make the people to get engaged with him, he incarnated in ten different incarnations, with abundance of patience. Other than being engaged with such emperumān who is the ādimūrti, is it possible for anyone to attain the benefit of mōkśam?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) A query was posed to āḻvār “Meditating on tiruppāṟkadal nātan is possible only for those who live in śvēta dvīpam which is in proximity to that abode; how is it possible for others?” āḻvār responds saying “Only those who carry out devotion at the divine feet of one who came, without considering his eminence, from tiruppāṟkadal, which is the starting point of all incarnations, and carried out ten incarnations in this world, would attain mōkśam; it is not possible for the others”*

**pattinōdu pattumāy** – as the indvelling soul of the ten directions and the guards for these ten directions. The ten directions are: the four main directions of east, south, west, north, the four sub-directions of south east, south west, north west and north east, the direction on top and the direction at the bottom. In these directions, indran guards north, agni guards east, yaman guards south, nairrutan guards west, varuṇan, vāyu, kubēran and īsan guard south east, south west, north west and north east, respectively. brahmā guards the top direction from satyalōkam and anantan (ādiśēśan) guards the pātāḷa lōkam at the bottom.

**orēḻinōdu oronbadāy** – the seven notes of music and the nine pathos of dance, which are nonpareil. amarakōsam 1-7-1 says “*niśādarśabha gāndāra śatjamadyamadaivatā*: l  
*pancamaś cētyamī sapta tantrīkaṇṭōttitā*: *svarā*: ll” (The seven notes of niśādam, riśabham, gāndāram, śatjam, madyamam, daivatam and pancamam rise from the strings (of instruments) and the necks (of people)). Just as it has been said in  
*“śringāra hāsya karuṇa vīra raudra bhayānakā*: l *bībhatsātbhuta śāntāśca nava nātyarasā*: *smrutā*: ll” (love, laughter, sadness, valour, anger, fear, hatred, wonder and peace are the nine pathos which exist in dance), there are nine pathos which are mentioned in dance. Mentioning the notes of music and pathos of dance here is symbolic of all the matters such as śabdam (soul), sparśam (touch), rūpam (form), rasam (taste) and gandam (smell). Thus, in the first line of the pāsuram, it has been mentioned that emperumān has under his control, the ten directions, the leaders of guards in these ten directions as well as the worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc enjoyed by these leaders and all the other samsāris, without any distinction.

**pattinān disaikkaṇ ninṟa nādu peṟṟa nanmaiyāy** – to become the matters, pursued for their benefit, by people living in seven lōkams (worlds) which are above the earth, such as bhūlōkam, bhuvarlōkam etc and the seven worlds which are under earth such as atalam vitalam etc. Just as it has been said of emperumān in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes), isn’t he one who has all the aprākruta rasam (taste) and gandam (fragrance) on his divine form! The word *disai* here refers to type \[and not to direction\]. This is seen in the saying “*ēśā dik*” \[statement from those who write books\]. The meaning for this is – in this way or in this type (one can understand further). Thus, since those who write books use the word *dik* (disai) in the meaning of type, the verse *padinān disaikkan ninṟa nādu* will refer to the fourteen types of worlds and those who live in these worlds. In the first line, āḻvār had said *pattinōdu pattumāy* referring to the leaders together with the directions. He refers to the same matter in the second line through the verse *pattinān disaikkaṇ ninṟa nādu*. The worldly pursuits such as *śabdam* etc referred to in the first line *ēḻinōdoronbadāy* have been repeated in the second line through the verse *peṟṟa nanmai*. The term *āy* (as in *ēḻinōdoronbadāy* as well as in *nādu peṟṟa nanmaiyāy*) comes in the meaning of ‘to become’. Is there any authenticity to this? In tiruvāimoḻi 1-4-10 “*uyir mudalā muṟṟumāy”*, the term *āy* comes in the meaning of ‘to become’. For the samsāri cētanas in this world who keep pursuing the worldly materials for their enjoyment, emperumān, as said earlier in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” in order to make the samsāris to enjoy only him, becomes all the pursuits such as śabdam etc.

The pāsuram next talks about what emperumān does in order to make the samsāris, who are enjoying the worldly pursuits, to enjoy him.

**pattināya tōṟṟamōdorāṟṟal mikka ādi pāl** – emperumān, the *pūrvajan* (one who came at the beginning), who takes ten incarnations to appear in this world, tolerating the insults meted out by the samsāris to him and protecting them. The term *tōṟṟam* refers to emperumān’s incarnations where he, who is invisible to the senses, makes himself visible to their senses such as eyes.

**āṟṟal** – patience; emperumān’s putting up with the insults heaped on him by the samsāris, who do not know that he is he supreme entity, is termed as *āṟṟal* here.

**ādi pāl** – one who has been existing since the beginning. Just as it has been said in the jitantē ślōkam *mahā puruśa pūrvaja*: (one who is a great soul and has been existing since the beginning) and in puruśa sūktam “*pūrvō yō dēvēbhyō jāta*:” (one who came for the sake of celestials, even before they could seek from him), since there was no benefit if he remained in paramapadam when samsāri jīvans developed an interest in him, he incarnated before they had a desire and became *pūrvajan*.

**pattarām avarkkalādu** – Just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 4-9 “*janma karma ca mē divyam ēvam yō vētti tatvata*: l *tyaktvā dēham punar janma naiti māmēti sō’rjuna* ll” (Oh arjuna! One who truly knows like this, my *aprākruta* (not relating to primordial matter) birth and activities, does not have another birth after relinquishing his body in that birth. He attains me), other than his devotees who engage with his incarnations, truly knowing the benefits that he has carried out for them.

**mutti muṟṟalāgumē** – the *palam* (1. advantage, 2. fruit) of mōkśam will not mature. Just as it was mentioned in the last bhagavat gītā ślōkam above, the opinion here is that there is no possibility for hurdles such as connection with body etc to be removed and the benefit of attaining emperumān.

Next, we will take up the 80<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 80. Vasiyagi nesaminri

*vāsiyāgi nēsaminṟi vandedirnda dēnugannāsamāgi nāḷulappa nanmai sēr pananganikkuvīsimēl nimirnda tōḷin illaiyākkināy kaḻaṟkuāsaiyām avarkkalāl amararāgal āgumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vāsiyāgi – as a mule; nēsaminṟi vandu – coming without any affection; edirnda – one who opposed; dēnugan – the monster, dēnukan; nāsamāgi – making him suffer; nāḷ ulappa – to put an end to his life; nanmai sēr – being beautiful; pananganaikku vīsi – throwing it on top of palm fruit; mēl nimirnda – going above the capability of enemy; tōḷin – due to the strength of shoulders; illaiyākkināy – made that dēnukan to be non-existent; for such your; kaḻaṟku – to the divine feet; āsaiyāmavarkkallāl – other than those who have affection; amararāgal āgumē – is it possible to be on a par with nityasūris?

**Перевод:**

**The monster, dēnukāsuran, without any affection, came in the form of a mule and opposed you \[emperumān\]. You made him suffer and destroyed him by throwing him atop beautiful palm fruits. Due to the strength of your shoulders, you went far above the capability of your enemy. Other than those who have affection towards the divine feet of such an entity like you, is it possible to be on a par with nityasūris \[and enjoy you in paramapadam\]?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that keeping the avatāra rahasya jñānam (knowledge about the secrets of incarnation of emperumān) to the fore, carrying out devotion to him is a means for mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). In this pāsuram, he recalls that in one of his incarnations, as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa), emperumān has as his nature, destroying the enemies of his followers. He says that apart from those who proffer love to his divine feet, there is no possibility to enjoy emperumān by being together with nityasūris in paramapadam.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vāsiyāgi nēsaminṟi vandedirnda dēnukan** – unlike enemies such as kālayavanan who came in their original \[physical\] form, the monster dēnukan came in the form of *vāji*, a mule, in order to pounce on kaṇṇan when he was not attentive enough.

**nēsaminṟi vandedirnda dēnugan** – *nēsaminṟi* vandu (coming without affection) –  periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-6-9 says “*maruṇḍu mān gaṇangaḷ mēygai maṟandu mēynda pullum kadaivāy vaḻisōra*” (groups of deer, which are always bewildered, forgot grazing \[on hearing the sound of flute played by kriṣṇa\], and even the grass which they were chewing came out of the corners of their mouth). Thus, attracted by the activities of kaṇṇan, even animals got entranced due to affection and stayed quietly. However, dēnukan, being a wicked person, was unlike those animals, and came without affection. *edirndu vandu* – even if one were not without affection, one could stay neutral; however, he came only to cause harm.

**nāsamāgi nāḷ ulappa** – in order to make such dēnukan to lose his life and become a corpse. Since the term *nāḷ ulappa* refers to putting an end to his \[dēnukān’s\] life, the term *nāsam* here, instead of referring to destruction, refers to suffering.

**nanmai sēr pananganikku vīsi** – throwing him on top of palm trees so that the beautifully matured palm fruits will fall down. Hasn’t śrī viṣṇu purāṇam too said this in 5-8-9 and 5-8-10? “*kruhītvā bhrāmaṇē naiva sōmbarēgatajīvitam* l *tasminnēva sa cikśēpa dēnukam truṇarājani* ll *tata*: *palānyanēkāni talāgrān nipatan khara*: l *prutivyām pātayāmāsa mahāvātō ghanāniva* ll” (That bhagavān caught hold of dēnukāsuran who was in the form of a mule, whirled him around and threw him, who had lost his life when he was thrown to the sky, against the palm tree. The wicket demon, once thrown like that, just like a strong wind forces clouds to let go of the water held by them to fall as rain drops, hit against the tree and made many fruits to fall on the ground, from the top of the tree).

**mēl nimirnda tōḷin illaiyākkināy kaḻaṟku** – to the divine feet of yours, who had powerful shoulders whose strength was far superior to the enemy’s and with with which you destroyed the enemy. The implied meaning is that instead of killing him with his vow or any weapon, emperumān had caught hold of his enemy and destroyed him with the power of his shoulder.

**āsaiyām avarkkallāl amararāgal āgumē** – āḻvār asks whether it is possible for one other than those who meditate on your nature of destroying the enemies of your followers and are affectionate towards your divine feet, to be on a par with nityasūris and enjoy you in paramapadam

Next, we will take up the 81<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 81. Kadainda parkadal

*kadainda pāṟkadal kidandu kālanēmiyaik kada(di)nduudainda vāli tandanu(na)kku udava vanda irāmanāymidainda ēḻ marangaḷum adanga eyduvēngdamadainda māla pādamē adaindu nāḷum uymminō*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadainda – (for the same of ambrosia) churned; pāṟkadal – in kśīrābdi (milky Ocean); kidandu – reclining; kālanēmiyai – monster named kālanēmi; kadandu – defeated him; udainda vāli – vali, whose basic nature had got destroyed (due to contact with other deities), his; tandanukku – for sugrīva, his younger brother; udava – to help; irāmanāy vandu – incarnated as rāma, son of emperor daśaratan; midainda – standing close to each other; ēḻ marangaḷum – seven ebony trees; adanga – all these; eydu – pierced with arrow; vēngadam – at tirumalai; adainda – one who has mercifully reached; māla – affectionate one’s; pādamē – only his divine feet; adaindu – taking refuge under; nāḷum – permanently; uymminō – uplift yourselves; pāṟkadal – in kśīrābdi (milky Ocean); kidandu – reclining; kālanēmiyai – monster named kālanēmi; kadandu – defeated him; udainda vāli – vali, whose basic nature had got destroyed (due to contact with other deities), his; tandanukku – for sugrīva, his younger brother; udava – to help; irāmanāy vandu – incarnated as rāma, son of emperor daśaratan; midainda – standing close to each other; ēḻ marangaḷum – seven ebony trees; adanga – all these; eydu – pierced with arrow; vēngadam – at tirumalai; adainda – one who has mercifully reached; māla – affectionate one’s; pādamē – only his divine feet; adaindu – taking refuge under; nāḷum – permanently; uymminō – uplift yourselves

**Перевод:**

**emperumān reclined in the milky ocean, after churning it. He killed the monster kālanēmi. He incarnated as śrī rāma, the son of emperor daśaratan, in order to help sugrīva, the younger brother of vāḷi, whose basic nature had got destroyed and he pierced seven ebony trees. He is mercifully standing in tirumalai. Take refuge under the affectionate emperumān’s divine feet and permanently uplift yourselves.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsurams, āḻvār had mercifully said that we can attain kśīrābdinātan (emperumān who is the lord of milky ocean), vyūha avatāram, and kaṇṇan et al, vibhava avatārams, and uplift ourselves. A query arose “tiruppāṟkadal nātan is far from us due to distance and vibhava avatāams are far from us due to time; how could we attain them?” In this pāsuram he mercifully says “On this earth, at this time, emperumān is mercifully standing in tirumalai \[tiruvēngadam\], so that everyone can take refuge under him. Attain his divine feet and uplift yourselves” and brings to an end parōpadēsam (instructing others) which he had started in the 75th pāsuram.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kadainda pāṟkadal kidandu** – due to the curse of sage durvāsa, the wealth of celestial entities such as indra disappeared in tiruppāṟkadal. In order to redeem their wealth, emperumān churned that ocean and reclined in that ocean so that celestial entities such as brahmā et al could approach him there and seek redressal for any danger that may come their way.

**kālanēmiyaik kadandu** – defeating the monster kālanēmi who was harassing those celestial entities. *kadattal* – to be victorious

**udainda vāli tandanakku udava vanda irāmanāy** – this mentions the way emperumān helped the younger brother of vāli, who had a broken heart because of vāli, by incarnating as śrī rāma. Through the first line, the pāsuram mentioned the way emperumān helped celestial entities such as brahmā et al. Through the second line it mentions the way he helped monkeys such as sugrīva et al by incarnating as śrī rāma. The term *udainda* could be taken as an adjective for vāli, instead of for sugrīva. vāli had come to earth as indra’s *amśam* (representative part) in order to help śrī rāma. However, he became a devotee of Siva and befriended rāvaṇa. Since his *svarūpam* (basic nature) had changed, āḻvār calls him as *udainda vāḷi*. The term *tandanakku* could be taken to refer to vāli himself. Just as śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 17-8 says “*tadastram tasya vīrasya svarga mārga prabhāvanam* l *rāmabāṇā sanakśiptam āvahat paramām gatim* ll” (That arrow which was shot from śrī rāma’s bow, and which showed the path to parama padam, gave mōkśam to the valorous vāli), śrī rāma came to help vāli since those who die at his hands reach mōkśam. Alternative explanation – since sugrīva had taken refuge in *riśyasrungamukha parvam*, a mountain where vāli was barred from entering, vāli had a broken heart since he thought “What is the point in my ruling when my enemy is still alive?” To get rid of the danger to sugrīva from that broken-hearted vāli, śrī rāma came.

**udainda vāli tandanukku** – periyavāccān piḷḷai, the commentator for this prabandam, has quoted a tamiḻ grammar expert who was said to have written the explanation for this, in the following way: the term *tambi tanakku* (for younger brother) has got transformed as “*tandanu(na)kku*”, with the syllable ‘m’ in “tambi” replaced by the syllable ‘n’ and the last syllable ‘bi’ in ´tambi´ getting shortened fully; the resulting syllable ‘*than’* refers to *tambi*.

**midainda ēḻ marangaḷum adanga eydu** – Though śrī rāma had given a word to sugrīva that he would protect sugrīva from vāli, sugrīva had a doubt whether śrī rāma would be able to protect him. Hence, he asked śrī rāma, pointing to an ebony tree “In order to test his strength, vāli would shake this tree and check if the leaves are falling; could you pierce this tree with your arrow?” śrī rāma pierced all the seven ebony trees which were close together. Not only that, he also pierced seven mountains and the seven nether worlds, with his single arrow. Thus, to eliminate sugrīvan’s doubt completely, śrī rāma, apart from the seven trees, also targeted seven mountains and seven nether worlds.

**vēngadam adainda māla pādamē adaindu nāḷum uymminō** – the simplicity with which emperumān had helped celestial entities such as brahmā et al is not accessible to us since we cannot approach tiruppāṟkadal; in the same way, the simplicity with which he helped monkeys during his incarnation as śrī rāma is not accessible to us since we had come too far behind his time. In order to ensure that even we do not miss out on his simplicity, emperumān, who shows his simplicity to those who desire benefits other than himself as well as to monkeys and other animals, has mercifully taken residence in tiruvēngadam, manifesting his simplicity, due to his great affection for his followers. āḻvār tells us to attain the divine feet of tiruvēngadamudaiyān \[divine name of emperumān in tiruvēngadam\], without desiring any other benefit, and to uplift ourselves. By stressing with the long syllable E as in *pādamē*, āḻvār hints that “Even though he helps those who desire other benefits too, you take refuge under his divine feet, considering them alone as the benefit. That is the way to uplift yourselves”.  Instead of considering the verse as *nāḷum uymminō*, we can transpose the term *adaindu* towards the end of the verse, considering it to be *nāḷum adaindu uymminō*. The meaning will then become: attain the divine feet of tiruvēngadamudaiyān always and uplift yourselves.

**uymmin** – For a *śēśa bhūtan* (servitor), getting uplifted would refer to carrying out *kainkaryam* (service to emperumān). Hence the meaning for this term is “carry out service”. nammāḻvār too in tiruvāimoḻi 1-1-1 mercifully said “*sudaradi toḻudeḻu*” (worship his resplendent divine feet and rise), confirming that “*sudaradi toḻudu*” (vorśipping his resplendent feet) is *eḻudal* (kainkaryam).

**vēngadam adainda māla pādamē adaindu** – for his followers, truvēngadamudaiyān’s divine feet are the abode to be attained. For tiruvēngadamudaiyān, since tiruvēngadam enables him to get his followers, it is the place to be attained.

Next, we will take up the 82<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 82. Ettirattum

*ettiṟattum ottu ninṟu uyarndu uyarnda peṟṟīyōymuttiṟattu mūrinīr arāvaṇait tuyiṇra ninpattuṟutta sindaiyōdu ninṟū pāsam vittavarkkuettiṟattum inbam ingum angum engum āgumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ettiṟattum – in all species; ottu ninṟu – (along with cētanas who have forms such as celestial, human etc) incarnating in harmony; uyarnduyarnda – rising up, to a great extent; peṟṟiyōy – Oh one who has qualities!; muttiṟattu mūri nīr – in the expansive milky ocean which is filled with waters from river, rain and springs; arāvu aṇai – on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); tuyinṟa – one who has reclined; nin – in matters related to you; pattu – with devotion; uṟutta – abundantly; ninṟu – remaining; pāsam vittavarkku – those who have given up attachment in other matters; ettiṟattum – in all the ways; ingum – (with physical form) whether they remain here; angum – whether they are in paramapadam; engum – (leaving the physical form behind) while ascending the path to paramapadam; inbamē āgum – only the happiness of bhagavat anubhavam (experiencing emperumān) will occur

**Перевод:**

**Oh emperumān who has the qualities of incarnating in various species, in harmony with that particular species, and who rises up to a great extent! You are lying in the expansive milky ocean, which has waters coming from river, rains and springs, on the mattress of ādiśēśan. For those who have abundant devotion towards matters related to you, leaving aside attachment towards other matters, only the happiness of experiencing emperumān occurs to them, whether they are living in samsāram or they are in parama padam or while ascending the path towards parama padam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) With the previous pāsuram, āḻvār completed instructing others about emperumān. From now onvards, he sings many pāsurams where he engages in conversation with emperumān, later he engages in conversation with his heart and in the last pāsuram, he gives an indication about the benefit that he received. Through many pāsurams from now onvards, he tells emperumān “Those who have affection towards your divine feet will equal nityasūris”. The reason for this is that the devotion which he had instructed to others, from the 75th pāsuram, as mōkśa sādanam (a means to attain mōkśam) was not a means to him but was a very enjoyable benefit for him. parabhakti (knowledge about emperumān) which is mentioned as a means for attaining mōkśam, in śāstrams (authentic texts), is the last step for an upāsakan (one who reaches emperumān through the path of devotion) in that path. For a prapannan (one who reaches emperumān through the divine feet of emperumān), who considers emperumān as the means, this parabhakti is the first of the benefits which he enjoys on reaching mōkśam. Among the pāsurams which reflect a conversation between āḻvār and emperumān, in this pāsuram, āḻvār, meditating on the eminence of emperumān even when he incarnates as human being and the simplicity with which emperumān is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal, which is the prime cause for all his incarnations, says “For those who are involved with your divine feet, even when they are in samsāram, they will have the happiness of being in paramapadam”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ettiṟattum ottu ninṟu uyarndu uyarnda peṟṟiyōy** – you incarnate in the different species of *dēva* (celestial), *manuśya* (human), *tiryak* (animals) which roam or *stāvaram* (plants) which remain stationary, as one among them, harmoniously; you are one who does not give up his eminence, just as it has been said in yajur aśtakam 3-6-3-9 “*sa u śrēyān bhavati jāyamāna*:” (that supreme entity attains greatness only after being born); since you have the auspicious quality of an eminent person mingling with lowly people, oh one who has attained greatness as your basic nature, more than the others in all those species!

**peṟṟi** – natural greatness. The word *tiṟam* refers to the society of celestials, humans et al. Whether you incarnated amidst brahmā and Sivan as a celestial entity, viṣṇu, or as animals such as fish etc, since you were in harmony with the nature of those species, since you carried out activities which were beyond the capabilities of those species, you were splendorous in remaining greater than everyone else in that species. Even though you were that eminent, since you had carried out activities such as going as a messenger, being a charioteer etc, your qualities of simplicity had been revealed. Due to these reasons, āḻvār addresses him as *uyarndu uyarnda peṟṟiyōy*.

**muttiṟattu mūri nīr arāvaṇait tuyinṟa** – tiruppāṟkadal is the originating abode for the eminence and simplicity which had been mentioned in emperumān’s incarnations. It is expansive, filled with three types of water from rivers, rains and springs. In order to prevent even that water from being an irritant to his tender physical form, emperumān is reclining on the mattress of ādiśēśan. The term *muttiṟattu nīr* refers to the three types of water which join the ocean – water from river, water from rain and water which is generated by springs under the ocean bed. The term *mūri* refers to the expansive nature of the ocean.

**nin pattuṟutta sindaiyōdu ninṟu pāsam vittavarkku** – those who are standing with devotion wholeheartedly towards your divine feet and who have left attachment towards worldly pursuits. The term *pattu* refers to *bhakti*, devotion. The term *pattuṟutta* refers to an abundance of devotion. This refers to constantly meditating on the qualities of eminence and simplicity manifested in various incarnations and the quality of simplicity with which emperumān is reclining in tiruppāṟkadal, which is the starting point for all those incarnations. The implied meaning is that such people, who are constantly devoted to you, would also be detached from worldly pursuits.

**ettiṟattum inbam ingum angum engum āgumē** – these people \[who have constant devotion towards emperumān\] will be always happy, whether they are with their physical forms in this samsāram, or they have attained paramapadam, after leaving their physical form, or when they are going in the path of arcis (light), enroute paramapadam. Here, the term *tiṟam* refers to a state. Alternatively, *tiṟam* could refer to an attribute and through that, it could refer to the physical form. When one is in samsāram as a *mumukśu* (one who has desire for mōkśam), his form will be in the gross state. When he is ascending the path towards paramapadam, the form will be in the subtle state. When he is in paramapadam, it will be in *mukta* (liberated) form. The implied meaning is that despite the form that the ātmā has, the ātmā will always be happy. In all the states, the happiness of enjoying emperumān through his *svarūpam* (basic nature), *rūpam* (form), *guṇam* (auspicious qualities), *aiśvaryam* (wealth) will be always present, and there will nothing other than happiness. The considered opinion is that just as while engaging with worldly pursuits, only sadness is felt, while engaging with emperumān, only happiness will be felt.

Next, we will take up the 83<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 83. Mattulavu tan tulay

*mattulāvu taṇ tuḻāy alangalāy pulan kaḻalvittu vīḷvilāda bōgam viṇṇil naṇṇi ēṟinumettinōdu iraṇḍu enum kayiṟṟinān manandanaikkatti vīdilādu vaitta kādal inbamāgumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mattu ulāvu – honey being present continuously; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – full of tuḷasi; alangalāy – Oh one who has garland!; pulan – apt to be seen; kaḻal – your divine feet; vittu vīḷvu ilāda – not giving it up and going elsewhere; bōgam – the comfort of mōkśam; viṇṇil – in parama padam; naṇṇi ēṟinum – even if one were to reach it; ettinōdu iraṇḍenum – known as devotion; kayiṟṟināl – with a rope; manandanai – heart; katti – tying it (such that it does not engage with other pursuits); vīdilādu – without any break; vaitta – kept at your divine feet; kādal inbam – for the ecstasy of devotion; āgumē – will it equal?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has a garland of tuḷasi from which honey is dripping continuously! Your divine feet are apt to be seen and cannot be given up for anything else. For those who have tied their hearts with the rope of devotion towards those divine feet, without a break, will the comfort of reaching parama padam equal the affection towards your feet?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “For those with devotion, happiness will be felt without any distinction between this world and that world \[samsāram and mōkśam\]”. A query was raised as to whether one can get happiness in this world too, since jitantē ślōkam 1-4 says “samsāram sāgaram ghōram ananta klēśa bhājanam l tvāmēva śaraṇam prāpya nistaranti manīśiśaṇa: ll” (The ocean of samsāram is the repository for innumerable sorrows and is very cruel. Those who have won over their minds will attain you as the means and cross this ocean of samsāram). āḻvār responds in this pāsuram, saying “The happiness that one feels in parama padam will not match the happiness which one gets here, by carrying out devotion to your divine feet without any break”*

**mattulāvu taṇ tuḻāy alangalāy** – Oh one who has decorated with fresh tuḷasi garland from which honey is continuously dripping! From this it is clear that only that entity who is donning tuḷasi garland could be the supreme one and that this tuḷasi garland is symbolic of one who rules over both leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm) and nitya vibhūti (spiritual realm). Also, the beauty which results from decorating with the garland is also indicated.

**pulan kaḻal vittu vīdilāda bōgam** – the enjoyment in parama padam of your divine feet, which are apt to be seen, which are constantly seen by nityasūris and from which thoughts would not go to any other matter.

**pulan kaḻal** – the divine feet which are seen; the opinion is that they are divine feet which are apt to be seen.

**viṇṇil naṇṇi ēṟinum** – even if one were to reach parama padam.

**ettinōdu iraṇḍu enum kayiṟṟināl** – eight and two together will make ten, which is *pattu* in tamiḻ. The term *pattu* refers to bhakti. To highlight his devotion towards emperumān, āḻvār is indirectly referring through eight and two. Since devotion binds one’s mind, he symbolises devotion with *kayiṟu* (rope).

**manandanaik katti** – the implied meaning is that mind, which is the head of five jñānēndriyams (senses related to knowledge, namely eyes, ears, nose, tongue and skin) and the five karmēndriyams (senses related to activities such as hands, legs, speech and the two excretory organs), should be tied such that it does not go after other pursuits.

**vīdilāda vaitta kādal inbamāgumē** – the devotion kept at your divine feet, without any break, will be a source of comfort on its own, instead of being the means to attain mōkśam. The opinion here is that the comfort of mōkśam will not match the loving devotion carried out here which is the benefit by itself \[instead of bringing in a benefit\].

Next, we will take up the 84<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 84. Pinpirakka vaittanan

*pinpiṟakka vaittanan kol anṟi ninṟu tan kaḻaṟ(ku)anbuṟaikka vaitta nāḷ aṟīndanan kol āḻiyāntanṟiṟattor anbilā aṟivilāda nāyinēnenṟiṟattil enkol empirān kuṟīppil vaittadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

empirān – my svāmy (lord); pin – even after (this physical form comes to an end); piṟakka – to be born (further); kuṟippil – in divine mind; vaittanan kol – did he carry out a sankalpam (vow)?; anṟi – alternatively; than kaḻaṟku – in his divine feet; ninṟu – being firm; anbu uṟaikka vaitta nāḷ – the day when abundant devotion was kept; aṟindanan kol – has he taken a vow?; āḻiyān – one who is holding the divine disc; than tiṟattil – in matters related to him; oranbilā – without having any affection; aṟivilāda – being without any knowledge; nāyinēn – being lowly; en tiṟattu – towards me; empirān – my svāmy; kuṟippil vaittadu – having in his divine mind; en kolō – what is it?

**Перевод:**

**Has my lord taken a vow in his divine mind that I should be born repeatedly in this samsāram, after this form comes to an end or has he decided the day when I will have firm, abundant devotion towards his divine feet? I am a lowly person, without any affection or knowledge. What has my svāmy, who is holding the divine disc, thought of in his divine mind, about me?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār felt that he did not have the devotion which he had spoken about in the previous pāsuram “vīdilāda vaitta kādal inbamāgumē” (uninterrupted devotion at the divine feet of emperumān will give continuous happiness). He realised that he was still connected to his physical form. Since sarvēśvaran (supreme being) is capable of doing whatever he wishes and since the benefit of mōkśam is attained through his wish, āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Has emperumān decided that I should have parama bhakti towards his divine feet or has he decided that I should continue to suffer in this samsāram? What has he thought of me, in his divine mind?”*

**vyākhyānam** **(Commentary)**

**pin piṟakka vaittanan kol** – What has emperumān thought of in his divine mind as to how many more births should I take after getting rid of this physical form?  Alternatively, the verse could be split as “*pinbu iṟakka vaittana kol*” which will mean “Even after attaining knowledge about him, is he thinking of making me forget him and attain *ātmanāśam* (ruining of ātmā)?” Don’t *vēdavākyams* (passages from vēdas) too say that a person is said to exist only if he gets firm knowledge about emperumān and not to exist, otherwise, as brought out in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 6? “*asannēva sa bhavati* l *asad brahmēti vēda cēt* l *asti brahmēti cēd vēda* l *santamēnam tatō vidu*: ll”  
(Since the soul does not have knowledge about brahmam (supreme entity) he is considered as not existing; if it has (such knowledge), for that reason alone, he is considered to be existing)

**anṟi** – alternatively

**ninṟu than kaḻaṟku anbuṟaikka vaitta nāḷ aṟindanan kol** – has he taken a vow in his divine mind that he will create a day for me too when I will have firm devotion towards his divine feet and enjoy that experience? *anbu uṟaikka vaittal* refers to being with *parama bhakti* (a situation where one can sustain only in the presence of emperumān). *vaittu anubavikkum nāḷ* refers to reaching parama padam and enjoying service to emperumān there. Through these statements of queries, it is indicated that “For this jīvātmā, birth, forgetting emperumān and the resultant *ātmanāśam* are due to absence of devotion”. Taking birth in this samsāram is the reason for engaging with worldly pursuits. Engaging with worldly pursuits becomes the reason for forgetting emperumān. Thus, for the ātmā, taking birth is equivalent to forgetting emperumān.

**āḻiyān than tiṟattu** – in the matters related to emperumān, who is superior to everyone else since he is donning the divine disc.

**oranbilā aṟivilāda nāyinēn** – I, who am like a dog, without even the trace of devotion towards his *svarūpam* (basic nature), *rūpam* (divine physical form), *guṇam* (auspicious qualities), *vibhūti* (wealth) and not having the knowledge even a little bit which is the prerequisite for that devotion. The meaning for the term *anbilā aṟivilā* is “Not having even an iota of devotion and not having the knowledge to realise that even that is a deficiency”. Usage of the term *nāyinēn* (like a dog) signifies that just like a dog which will enter any place, I have also entered various physical forms through many births, being driven away by others just like that dog and being impure such that even if someone were to touch me, he / she will have to take a bath.

**entiṟattil** – I am a person who is unlike the samsāris, not to think ever of emperumān, as well as unlike muktas (those who have got liberated from samsāram and have attained parama padam) who enjoy him without any interruption.

**empirān kuṟīppil vaittadu enkol)** – what is my svāmy thinking about me in his divine mind? Not considering the knowledge which he had referred to in the 64<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*piṟandapin maṟandilēn*” or the rising affection which he had towards emperumān as referred to in the 61<sup>st</sup> pāsuram “*nadanda kāḷgaḷ nondavō*” as the means \[for attaining emperumān\], since emperumān’s thoughts \[what emperumān thinks\] would be the means, he feels that he is without devotion towards emperumān and without knowledge to realise that devotion.  He is going to say further in pāsuram 101 “*varam tarum kuṟippil vaittadāgil*” that emperumān’s thoughts alone would be the means. Specifically, he says here that the means for him is the thought of emperumān in his divine mind. The essence of this pāsuram is “Is he going to give me up, looking at my status since time immemorial or is he going to uplift me thinking that the relationship between us is such that my \[āḻvār’s\] destruction is emperumān’s loss”

Next, we will take up the 85<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 85. Naccaravanaik kidanda

*naccarāvaṇaik kidanda nāda pāda pōdinilvaitta sindai vānguvittu nīnguvikka nīyinammeyttan vallai ādalāl aṟīndanan nin māyamēuyttu nin mayakkinil mayakkal ennai māyanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

naccu – (towards enemies) spitting venom; arāvaṇai – on the mattress known as tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); kidanda – one who is reclining; nāda – Oh sarvēśvaran (lord of all!); pāda – your divine feet; pōdinil – in the lotus flower; vaitta – kept; sindai – my mind; vānguvittu – redeeming it from them \[your divine feet\]; nīnguvikka – to remove; nī – you, who are totally independent; inam – even now; meyttan vallai – truly, capable; ādalāl – hence; aṟindanan – I know that you are totally independent; māyanē – Oh, one who has amazing activities!; ennai – me, who has surrendered to you; nin māyamē uyttu – making me to be together with your divine nature; nin mayakkinil – in the worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sensory perceptions such as sound) etc, which are yours, and which make one to be bewildered; mayakkal – do not make be to be bewildered

**Перевод:**

**Simple Translation0**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār requests emperumān in this pāsuram “When I look at this physical form made of primordial matter, I do not know whether you wish, in your divine mind, to ruin this or to uplift me. You should ensure that my involvement with other \[worldly\] matters is removed such that my involvement with your divine feet is not changed”.*

Oh, sarvēśvaran who is reclining on the mattress, also called as ādiśēśan, who spits fire on enemies! You are capable of removing my mind, even now. which I have kept on your divine feet which are like lotus flowers. Hence, I know that you are totally independent. Oh, one who has amazing activities! Do not make me, who has surrendered to you, to be bewildered by making me, who is together with your divine nature, to be involved with worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc, which are also yours and which make one to be bewildered.

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**naccarāvaṇaik kidanda nāda** – Oh the lord of all who has reclined on tiruvanantāḻvān, who spits poison such that those who are not favourable to emperumān cannot get near him! āḻvār implies that emperumān is such an independent entity that he will get all services from those who are favourable to him, just as poygai āḻvār had said in mudal tiruvandādi 53 “*senṟāl kudaiyām* …” (when emperumān goes by walk, ādiśēśan will become an umbrella over his head; when emperumān sits, he will become his throne…).

**pāda pōdinil vaitta sindai** – \[my\] mind which is firmly attached to your divine feet which are like just-then blossomed flowers. This indicates that emperumān’s divine feet are ordained to be attained, and are like flower in terms of tenderness, fragrance, sweetness etc. and that āḻvār’s mind is engaged with those divine feet.

**vānguvittu** – taking the mind away from such divine feet. Just as it has been said by āḷavandār in the 27<sup>th</sup> ślōkam of stōtra ratnam “*tavāmrutasyandini pāda pankajē nivēśitātmā katam anyadiccti*? l *stitē’ravindē makarandanirbharē maduvratō nēkśurakam hi vīkśatē* ll” (How will the mind, kept on your divine feet (through your mercy) which secrete honey copiously, like anything else? Will a beetle leave aside a reddish lotus flower which secretes honey and go to a wild flower?) removing the mind which has been firmly kept on your divine feet and making it to loathe them.

**nīnguvikka nī inam meyttan vallai** – I have been away from you since time immemorial. You are truly capable of making my mind to be away from you further too.

**ādalāl aṟindanan** – I know that you are completely independent, since you are capable of transforming one entity into another, without the need to take anyone’s permission for doing so. emperumān asks him “What do you wish to do?” āḻvār responds saying

**nin māyamē uyttu nin mayakkinil mayakkal** – just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 7-14 “*daivī hyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā* l *māmēva yē prapadyantyē māyām ētām taranti tē* ll” (This māyai \[prakruti, the primordial matter\], which has been created by me, the lord and which has three guṇas \[of satva, rajas and tamas, viz. noble, passion and ignorance respectively) is difficult to cross over. Those who surrender to me will be able to cross over this), I request you not to let me go away from you by making me to engage constantly with worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc, which are part of this prakruti (primordial matter) and which are under your control. The term *māyamē* refers to the word *māyā* which is seen in the above-mentioned bhagavat gītā ślōkam, which in turn refers to prakruti (primordial matter) which has amazing power. The term *mayakku* refers to worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound), sparśam (touch) etc which are capable of ruining the knowledge of badda cētanas (souls still bonded to samsāram).

**ennai** – me, who has submitted ātmā as well as all the belongings of ātmā, and is looking up to you for being uplifted. emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “Will I ruin the knowledge of people like you who cannot sustain themselves without me, by engaging you with worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc? Don’t I do that only with svatantras (those samsāris who think that they are totally independent) who live life as they wish?” āḻvār responds saying

**māyanē** – Oh one who has amazing activities! Just as śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-15-157 says “*upamānam aśēśāṇām sādūnām yassadā’bhavat…*” (that prahlādāḻvān, who was taken as the analogy for all sādus (ascetics) all the time …) you are capable of making even prahlādāḻvān who is considered as the analogy for eminent people, to oppose you. In the *arumpadavurai* (explanation for difficult terms) in this commentary, it is stated that in the commentary for raghuvamśam 7<sup>th</sup> sargam 35<sup>th</sup> ślōkam, the brahmāṇḍa purāṇa vacanam quoted by mallinātan is as follows: “*virōcana virōdē’pi prahlāda*: *prāktanam smaran* l *viṣṇōstu kramamāṇasya pādāmbhōjam rurōda ha* ll” (Though prahlādāḻvān had enmity towards (his son) virōcanan, recalling his old enmity with indra, when trivikraman started measuring the worlds (to seize them from mahābali and hand over to indra), he stopped the lotus-like divine feet of trivikraman which measured the worlds). Similarly, you are capable of granting mōkśam even to śiśupālan who was constantly opposing you. īsn’t this your svātantryam (being totally independent)!

Next, we will take up the 86<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 86. Sadu sadu padane

*sādu sādu pādanē salangalanda poygai vāyādarāvin vanpidar nadam payinṟa nādanēkōdu nīdu kaiya seyya pādanāḷum uḷḷinālvīdanāga mey seyāda vaṇṇam enkol kaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sādu – the monster, śakata; sādu – one who destroyed; pādanē – Oh, one who has the divine feet!; salam kalanda – to make it appear as if water had been added to poison, it was that poisonous; poygai vāy – on the mound of river yamunā; ādarāvin – kāḷiyan, the snake, who was dancing in anger; van pidar – on his strong neck; nadam payinṟa – one who danced; nādanē – Oh my svāmy!; kōdu – with śrī pāncajanyam, the divine conch; nīdu – always together; kaiya – Oh, one who has divine hand!; kaṇṇanē – Oh, one who is easily approachable for those who take refuge under you!; seyya pādam – your reddish, divine feet; nāḷum uḷḷināl – if meditated upon every day; vīdanāga – (making me) to be in parama padam; mey seyāda vaṇṇam – not having done this, truly; enkol – why?

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has divine feet \[with which\] you destroyed the monster, śakata! Oh, my svāmy, who danced on the strong neck of the snake kāḷiyan, who was dancing in anger, on the mound of river yamunā, which was full of poison, with water having been added to it! Oh, one who has the divine hand which is always together with the divine conch, śrī pāncajanyam! Oh, one who is easily approachable to those who take refuge under you! Why have you not taken me truly to paramapadam, even though I am constantly meditating upon your reddish, divine feet?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān thinks in his divine mind “What is the reason for your doubt about me that I will remove you away from me and that I will bring you under the control of worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “You are capable of destroying enemies however strong they are; however, when I find that you are not fulfilling my desire that you should sever my connection with this physical form and take me under your feet, how could I not have that doubt?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sādu sādu pādanē**– oh, one who has divine feet with which you kicked the monster, śakata, even when you were an infant such that he died! The first term *sādu* refers to *śakata*, a wheel. The second term *sādu* refers to the act of kicking. The divine feet, which are apt to be attained by favourable ones, destroyed the enemy. This is the nature of his divine feet. Hasn’t nammāḻvār to mercifully said this in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-3-1 pāsuram “*vidamum amudamumāy*” (emperumān is both poison and nectar)? Through this, āḻvār hints that “I cannot say that I am losing the benefit because you are sad at removing hurdles”.

**salam kalanda poygai vāy** – on the mound of river yamunā, which is full of poison, with a little bit of water mingled with it. Was it not mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-7-3? “*tasyāścāti mahābhīma viśāgniśṟitavāriṇam* l *hratam kāḷiyanāgasya dadarśādivibhīśaṇam* ll”  
(kaṇṇan saw a mound of that river yamunā which had water boiling due to the cruellest fire of poison, and which was looking most dangerous)

**ādarāvin van pidar nadam payinṟa** – the opinion is that the gait of kaṇṇan, while walking atop the powerful head of kāḷiyan who was living in that river, appeared like a dance performed by experts in dance. *arā* – aravu, snake; the term *ādaravu* refers to the snake dancing in that mound. Alternatively, it can also be construed as snake, dancing in anger. The term *ādal* will refer both to gait and dance.

**nādanē** – Oh my lord, who is apt for my *svarūpam* (basic nature)! āḻvār is referring to his relationship with emperumān \[lord – possession\] in order to highlight that killing enemies is dear to him. Through this second exclamation, āḻvār hints that “There is no reason to doubt that I have lost out on my benefit because removing my hurdles is not apt for you and also because there is no relationship with you”.  āḻvār had mercifully mentioned the narrative about destroying śakatāsuran in order to indicate that any ego, which comes in the physical form which had been created for attaining emperumān and to enjoy him, is apt to be given up. He had mercifully mentioned the narrative about *kāḷiya nardanam* (dancing atop kāḷiyan) to indicate that connection with physical form, which is like mixing with poison for ātmā, an entity very dear to emperumān, is apt to be removed. The monster pervading in a favourable wheel is similar to ego pervading a favourable physical form \[favourable in order to carry out kainkaryam and to reach emperumān\]. Poison admixing with the sweet water of river yamunā is similar to the relationship of ātmā, which is dear to emperumān, with the physical form.

**kōdu nīdu kaiya** – Oh one who holds the divine conch of śrīpāncajanyam which is inseparable from the divine hand, as if to manifest that you have to prove your hand only through the conch! The opinion here is that āḻvār is hinting that it cannot be said that he is not attaining removal of hurdles because emperumān is not having the necessary implement in his hand or that he himself has the power in his hands for its removal.

**seyya pādam nāḷum uḷḷīnāl** – if one were to meditate constantly on the divine feet which have a reddish complexion, distinguishing them from his dark divine form and which attract all towards them. It is indicated through this that āḻvār is not losing out because he is holding on to something, leaving aside emperumān.

**vīdanāga mey seyāda vaṇṇam enkol** – while you (emperumān) are capable enough to remove my connection with samsāram, why are you not truly making me attain your divine feet? Due to the usage of the term that he has not truly attained emperumān, it is indicated here that he has attained the benefit to an extent and that he is not satisfied since he has not attained it completely. In other words, he has got benefit to the extent that emperumān has entered his heart just like nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 2-6-7 “*mudiyādaden enakkēlini*” (hereafter, everything is possible for me) and that he has been able to see emperumān through his mind, just as nammāḻvār had said in his tiruvāimoḻi 4-5-8 “*yāvar nigar agalvānattē*” (who, even in nityavibhūti, can match me?) It is this which āḻvār has mercifully said in places such as tiruccandaviruttam 64 “*piṟandapin maṟandilēn*” (I have not forgotten emperumān after getting knowledge about him). Though he could comfort himself, he says that since emperumān has not severed his connection with his physical form and given the experience similar to nityasūris, he says that he has not truly got the benefit.

**kaṇṇanē** – the purpose of your incarnating as kaṇṇan is only to make the jīvātmās to have desire in his matters, to start with, and to finally grant paramabhakti to them and to make them as muktātmās (liberated souls). You did not incarnate in order to get rid of karma, like us. Thus, when emperumān asked him the reason for his doubt about his granting āḻvār the final benefit, āḻvār says in this pāsuram “When you tell the reason for your not granting me mōkśam, the answer to your query will come on its own”

Next, we will take up the 87<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 87. Nerri perra

*neṟṟi peṟṟa kaṇṇan viṇṇin nādanōdu pōdin mēlnaṟṟavatta nādanōdu maṟṟumuḷḷa vānavarkaṟṟa peṟṟiyāl vaṇangu pāda nāda vēda ninpaṟṟalāror paṟṟu maṟṟadu uṟṟilēn uraikkilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

neṟṟi – on his forehead; peṟṟa – having; kaṇṇan – rudran, who has an eye; viṇṇin – for the world of celestial entities; nādanōdu – along with indra, who is the lord; podin mēl – residing on the lotus flower of divine navel \[of emperumān\]; nal tavatta nādanōdu – along with brahmā, who carries out penance; maṟṟumuḷḷa – others who are present; vānavar – celestial entities; kaṟṟa peṟṟiyāl – in the ways in which they have learnt; vaṇangu – vorśipping; pāda – Oh, one who has divine feet!; vēda nāda – Oh one is proclaimed as the lord of all, in vēdams \[sacred texts\]; uraikkil – if I were to state; nin paṟṟalāl – other than attaining you; maṟṟor paṟṟadu – any other entity which could be considered as refuge; uṟṟilēn – I do not have any connection

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has divine feet which are vorśipped, in ways which they have learnt, by rudra, who has an eye in his forehead, by indra, who is the lord of celestial entities, brahmā, who resides on the lotus-like flower of your divine navel, and who carries out good penance, and by all the other celestial entities! Oh, one who is proclaimed in the vēdas as the lord of all! If I were to state something it will be that I do not have any connection with any entity who could be considered as refuge, other than you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān thinks in his divine mind “You had said in the previous pāsuram ‘seyya pādam nāḷum uḷḷināl’ conveying the meaning that you have taken refuge under us. īsn’t there any refuge for you other than us?” āḻvār responds through three pāsurams, starting with this pāsuram, that he does not have any other refuge. brahmā and rudran, whom the people of the world look up to as their refuge, are themselves looking up to you for their positions. When you are superior to everyone else, who else, other than you, could be a refuge for me, says āḻvār, expressing his ananyagaditvam (no other place to go to), in this pāsuram.*

**neṟṟi peṟṟa kaṇṇan** – rudran who has an eye in his forehead. It appears from this verse that rudran considers the eye on his forehead, which has the power of destruction, as a great benefit for himself.

**viṇṇin nādanōdu pōdin mēl naṟṟavatta nādanōdu maṟṟumuḷḷa vānavar** – celestial entities such as varuṇan et al who partake of the *havis* (offering in ritual) offered by samsāris, along with indran, who is the lord of celestials, and along with brahmā, who was born in the lotus-like flower of divine navel of emperumān and who has taken that itself as his residential abode and who has carried out penance in order to create the fourteen worlds and to be the head of those worlds. By clubbing rudran and brahmā along with the other celestial entities, it is implied here that they are also *badda jīvas* (souls who are bonded to samsāram).

**kaṟṟa peṟṟiyāl vaṇangu pāda** – Oh one who has divine feet which are vorśipped by these celestial entities in the ways that they have learnt. Through saying that they worship the divine feet of emperumān and that they do so in different ways, it is implied that they are bound by the commands of vēdams for both what is to be done and what is to avoided and that there is difference in the levels of knowledge and power, among these celestial entities.

**nāda vēda** – this could be taken as two different exclamations, *nadā*! and *vēda*! ōr as a single term of *vēdanāda*! The meaning is that emperumān is mentioned in vēdas as the lord of all the worlds. In the previous verse the reason for saying that he is vorśipped by celestial entities is because he is the lord for them, as brought out in this verse. Has he not been said as the lord of all in vēdams such as taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11? “*patim viśvasyātmēśvaram śāśvatam śivam acyutam nārāyaṇam”* (nārāyaṇan alone is the lord of all the worlds, who is the lord for himself \[i.e. he does not have a lord\], who is always auspicious and who does not let go of his followers)

**nin parralāṟor paṟṟu maṟṟadu uṟṟilēn** – your divine feet alone will be my refuge in any state; I do not have connection with any other refuge. Since the term *uṟudal* refers to touching, when āḻvār says *uṟṟilēn* he confirms that he does not have contact with any other entity.

**uraikkilē** – he hints that even saying “I do not have contact with another deity” would be a blemish for him.

Next, we will take up the 88<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.
