## 88. Vellai velai

*veḷḷai vēlai veṟpu nātti veḷḷeyiṟṟu arāvaḷāyaḷḷalāyk kadainda anṟu aruvaraikkor āmaiyāyuḷḷa nōygaḷ tīr marundu vānavarkku aḷitta emvaḷḷalārai anṟi maṟṟor deyva nān madippanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veḷḷai vēlai – in the milky ocean, which is white in complexion; veṟpu – mandara, the mountain; nātti – keeping it \[as the staff for churning the ocean\]; eyiṟṟu – having teeth; arāvu – vāsuki, the snake; aḷāy – coiling it (around mandara); aḷḷalāy – such that the waves became dense; kadainda anṟu – on that day when it was churned; aru varaikku – to support the mountain which could not be supported \[otherwise\]; orāmaiyāy – as a unique tortoise; uḷḷa nōygaḷ – when the harm due to sage durvāsā’s curse; tīr – removing them; marundu – ambrosia, the medicine for it and the mercy of śrī mahālakśmi; vānavarkku – to the celestial entities; em vaḷḷalāraiyanṟi – other than emperumān who is the most magnanimous entity; maṟṟor deyvam – any other deity; nān – I (who do not know anyone other than that emperumān); madippanē – will I respect (as an entity)?

**Перевод:**

**When the mountain was used as a staff for churning the milky ocean, with vāsuki who has white teeth being used as the rope around the mountain, emperumān took the form of a unique tortoise in order the support the mountain which could not be held in place otherwise. Will I respect any deity other that magnanimous emperumān, who granted ambrosia, and the grace of śri mahālakśmi, as the medicine to get rid of the harm which came the way of celestials due to sage durvāsā’s curse?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that brahmā, rudran et al get their positions due to emperumān. In this pāsuram, he says “When they face a hurdle, they get rid of it with your mercy. When such is the case, will I ever consider anyone other than you as bhagavān?”*

**vyākhyānam Commentary)**

**veḷḷai vēlai veṟpu nātti** – keeping the mountain mandara as churning staff in the whitish milky ocean. The blackish complexion of emperumān would contrast with the white coloured milky ocean, making it enjoyable for wise people who do not desire any other benefit from him. However, for the sake of celestial entities who desire other benefits, he had to curtail that experience of those wise people and establish mandara mountain as the churning staff in the ocean.

**veḷḷeyiṟṟarāvaḷāy** – coiling the snake, vāsuki, who has white teeth, around that mountain as the rope, for churning. The reason for mentioning the white coloured teeth of vāsuki is: when the churning process started, vāsuki, unable to bear the pain, bared its teeth, manifesting its weakness; emperumān gave it sufficient strength to continue the churning action. Hasn’t this been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 1-9-91? “*tējasā nāgarājānam tatāpyāyitavān hari*: l *anyēna tējasā dēvān upabrumhitavān prabhu*: ll” (hari (emperumān) nurtured the snake vāsuki with a part of his radiance. With another part of his radiance, he gave strength to the celestial entities).

**aḷāy** – around

**aḷḷalāyk kadainda anṟu** – the depth of that ocean could not be estimated just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “*kānitas sagarēṇāyamaramēyō mahōdadi*: l *kartum arhati rāmasya jñātvā kāryam mahāmati*: ll” (this king of oceans, who is unfathomable, was severed by (śrī rāma’s ancestor) sagaran. This wise person \[referring to the king of oceans\] will know the importance of śrī rāma’s deed and would carry out his wish accordingly). On that day when the ocean was churned such that the waves were rising and hitting against one another, similar to tiruvāimoḻi 7-4-2 pāsuram “*kadal māṟu suḻanṟu aḻaikkinṟa oli*” (the sound of the ocean turning towards left and right alternately). *aḷḷal* – denseness; the waves were close, hitting one another. The term *anṟu* refers to that day when the celestial entities, who were proud that they were the lords, came looking up to emperumān in order to get rid of their danger that they faced about loss of their wealth.

**aruvaraikkor āmaiyāy** – incarnating as a unique tortoise to support that mandara mountain on his back so that the mountain will not get submerged in the ocean during churning. The term *or* would refer to uniqueness. Alternatively, *or āmai* could also mean that when he incarnated as a tortoise, the other tortoises in the ocean considered him as one among them. It is pertinent to recall parāśara maharishi’s ślōkam in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-99 “*kśīrōdamadyē bhagavān kūrmarūpī svayam hari*: l *mandarādrēr adiśtānam bhramatō’bhūn mahāmunē* ll” (bhagavāṇ hari (śrīman nārāyaṇan), in the form of divine tortoise, was the support for mandara mountain which was gyrating in the middle of milky ocean).

**uḷḷa nōygaḷ** – the problems which came in the way of celestial entities owing to the sage durvāsā’s curse. For the celestial entities who considered themselves to be lords of others, due to the curse of sage durvāsā, initially all their wealth disappeared. Due to this, they lost their mental strength; that led to loss of their wisdom and physical strength. Owing to loss of their physical strength, they lost to the demons. All these are clubbed under the term *nōygaḷ* (harm).

**tīrmarundu vānavarkkaḷitta** – one who gave them ambrosia as well as the distinguished mercy of śrī mahālakśmi, so that they could get rid of their harm. Through ambrosia, emperumān gave them back their physical strength and through pirātti’s distinguished mercy, he gave them back their wealth. This has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-106 and 1-9-111 ślōkams “*tatō’valōkitā dēvā*: *harivakśa*:*stalastayā* l *lakśmyā maitrēya śasā parām nirvrutimāgatā*: ll *pītē’mrutē ca balibhir dēvair daityasamūstadā* l *vadyamānā diśō bhējē pātāḷanca vivēśa ha* ll” (Oh maitrēya! Subsequently, owing to the grace of mahālakśmi who is mercifully residing on the divine chest of emperumān, the celestial entities attained superior comfort; with the ambrosia which the celestial entities took, the assembly of demons scattered in all four directions; they entered *pātālam* (nether world) too).

**em vaḷḷalāraiyanṟi maṟṟor deyvam nān madippanē** – emperumān is one who showed his magnanimity to me by calling those who seek alms from him as magnanimous people, just as he said in bhagavat gītā 7-18 “*udārās sarva ēvaitē jñānī tvātmaiva mē matam”* (All these people are magnanimous only; among them, the wise ones are my ātmā (they sustain me); this is my philosophy). Apart from such emperumān, would I, having no other means \[to attain emperumān\], consider any other deity as being apt to be vorśipped?

Next, we will take up the 89<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 89. Par migunda

*pār migutta bāramun oḻiccuvān aruccanantēr miguttu māyamākki ninṟū konṟu venṟi sērmāradarkku vān koduttu vaiyam aivar pāladāmsīr migutta ninnalālor deyvanān madippanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pār – for the earth; miguttu – causing abundantly; bāram – burden; mun – taking to the forefront, on his own; oḻiccuvān – in order to destroy; aruccunan – arjunan’s; tēr – chariot; miguttu – conducting it fully; māyam ākki – creating amazing acts (such as transforming day into night etc); ninṟu – remaining as charioteer; konṟu – killing the enemies; venṟi sēr – those who took pride \[in themselves\] as being victorious; māradarkku – great warriors such as duryōdanan et al; vān koduttu – granting them heaven (for those who get killed in wars); vaiyam – earth; aivar pāladām – making it attain the five pāṇḍavas; sīr – (their) fame; migutta – causing it to be huge; ninnalāl – other than you; or deyvam – a deity; nān – I; madippanē – will I think as existing?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān made himself to be in the forefront as the charioteer for arjunan in order to destroy the burden caused to the earth. He conducted arjuna’s chariot, carried out amazing acts (such as transforming day into night etc), killed enemies such as duryōdanan et al who considered themselves as great warriors. He gave them the heaven slated for those who get killed in wars and returned the kingdom to the five pāṇḍavas. Will I think as deity anyone other than you, who made the fame of pāṇḍavas to grow abundantly?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Apart from protecting brahmā, rudran et al, who are the heads of celestial entities, you \[emperumān\] lowered yourself status by being the messenger of pāṇḍavas and their charioteer, just as it has been brought out in mahābhāratam drōṇa parvam ślōkam 183-24“kriṣṇāśrayā: kriṣṇa balā: kriṣṇa nātāśca pāṇḍavā: l kriṣṇa: parāyaṇam caiśām jyōtiśāmiva candramā: ll” (pāṇḍavas have kriṣṇa as their refuge, their strength and their lord; just as moon is to other stars, kriṣṇa is their supreme refuge). Through your unhindered sankalpam (vow), you destroyed the army of their enemies and reclaimed their kingdom. āḻvār says “Will I consider anyone other than such you, as a deity?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**pār migutta bāram** – the burden which became excessive to earth who normally puts up with the harm created by people to a great extent. śrī bhūmādēvi herself appealed to emperumān, as brought by three ślōkams in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-1-23, 24 & 25 “*kālanēmir hatō yō’sau viṣṇunā prabhaviṣṇunā* l *ugrasēna suta*: *kamsa*: *sambhūta*: *mahāsura*: ll *ariśtō dēnuka*: *kēśī pralambō narakas tatā*: l *sundō’suras tatātyugrō bāṇāscāpi balē*: *suta*: ll *tatānyē ca mahāvīryā nrupāṇām bhavanēśu yē* l *samutpannā durātmānas tānna sankyātumutsahē* ll” (kālanēmi, the great monster who was destroyed by viṣṇu, has been born as kamsan, the son of ugrasēnan. ariśtan, dēnukan, kēśi, pralamban, narakan, sundan, bāṇan, the cruellest son of mahābali as well as other monsters who are great warriors but wicked, have been born in many royal families. I (bhūmādēvi) am unable to even count them).

**munnoḻiccuvān aruccunan tēr miguttu** – in order to eliminate this burden, emperumān took to the forefront and conducted arjunan’s chariot to places where enemies were present. Being in the forefront, ahead of arjunan, relates to making arjunan who was reluctant to engage in war, just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 1-47 “*ēvam uktvā’rjunas sankhyē ratōpasta upāviśat* l *visrujya saśaram cāpam śōkasamvignamānasa*: ll” (saying like this, arjunan dropped his bow together with arrows, with a despondent heart, and sat on the chariot) to listen to what bhagavān said, just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 18-73 “*naśtō mōha*: *smrutir labdā tvatprasādān mayācyuta* l *stitō’smi gatasandēha*: *kariśyē vacanam tava* ll” (Oh acyuta \[one who does not let go of his followers\]! My bewilderment (which was contrarian to knowledge) has been destroyed; with your mercy, true knowledge has been attained by me. I have been freed from doubts. I will engage (in war) as per your words). Also, among the four classes of army, namely foot soldiers, warriors on horses, warriors on elephants and warriors on chariots, the last mentioned is the most eminent. Thus, he took chariot to help the pāṇḍavas, as it is most respected by enemies. Since he had promised duryōdanan that he will not wield weapons during the war, he remained as the sāraty (driver) for arjunan and with the help of the chariot, he destroyed a major portion of the enemy’s army. Thus, he stood in the forefront and destroyed the burden of earth.

**māyamākki** – carrying out amazing activities – transforming daytime into night; taking to arms despite promising that he would not wield arms; for the sake of pāṇḍavas, his followers, he changed his nature of seeing the boundary between day and night in war; of speaking only the truth etc.

**ninṟu konṟu** – to make everyone say that “arjunan killed” he remained as a driver of chariot and not as warrior in the war, and destroyed the enemies.

**venṟi sēr mādarkku vān koduttu** – granting svargam, which is ordained for those who die in the war, to *mahāratars* (great warriors) such as kauravas like duryōdana et al who thought that they were great warriors and hence they will win the war, keeping the kingdom with them, people like drōṇa et al who came to assist the kauravas.

**vaiyamaivar pāladām sīr migutta ninnalāl** – duryōdana et al were forcefully occupying the land of pāṇḍavas saying “We will not yield even one foot of the land”; other than you who had redeemed that land and given it back to the pāṇḍavas, increasing your fame. The word *sīr* refers to fame. It could also be said that he increased the fame of pāṇḍavas by making people say that they won the war and made the land as theirs. Thus, it could be said that he was partial towards his followers and increased their fame.

**or deyvam nān madippanē** – I know that you are partial towards your followers. When such is the case, will I think that any other deity, who does not have this fame, is apt to be vorśipped?

Next, we will take up the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 90. Kulangalaya ir irandil

*kulangaḷāya īriraṇḍil onṟilum piṟandilēnnalangaḷāya naṟkalaigaḷ nālilum navinṟilēnpulangaḷaindum venṟilēn poṟiyilēn punida ninilangu pādam anṟī maṟṟor paṟṟīlēn em īsanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

poṟiyilēn – getting caught in sensory perceptions such as śabdam (sound) etc; pulangaḷ aindum – the five sensory organs; venṟilēn – I have not won over; nalangaḷāya – conducting in the correct path; nal – benevolent; kalaigaḷ – vēdams; nālilum – among the four; navinṟilēn – I have not learnt; īriraṇḍāya – in the four classes; kulangaḷ – among the four varṇams (classes) of brāhmaṇa, kśatriya, vaiśya and śūdra; onṟilum – even in one varṇam; piṟandilēn – I have not been born; punida – Oh, one who purifies; em īsanē – lord for us (who are without any means with us and who do not have any refuge)!; nin – your; ilangu – splendorous\|; pādamanṟi – other than the divine feet; maṟṟor paṟṟu – another means for being uplifted; ilēn – I do not have

**Перевод:**

**I have not won over the five sensory perceptions such as śabdam etc in which I have got caught. I have not learnt any of the four benevolent vēdams which conduct people in the correct path. I have not been born in any of the four varṇas. Oh, one who purifies all! Oh, one who is our svāmy! Other than your splendorous divine feet, I do not have another means to get uplifted.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Starting from the 87th pāsuram, āḻvār had told emperumān that other than him, there is no other deity apt to be surrendered to, emphasising his ananyagaditvam (having no other refuge). In this pāsuram, he says “Even though you are the one apt to be attained, I do not have any means, with me other than your divine feet” mercifully bringing out the absence of kaimmudal (any means) with him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kulangaḷāya ….. poṟīyilēn** – this pāsuram has been arranged in such a way that one could either start from *poṟiyilēn …* downwards and look at the four faults which āḻvār speaks about that he has, analysing the cause for each as a link to another, or one could start with the words as they lie in the pāsuram and analyse the effect of each as a link to another. \[This pāsuram has been written as a conversation between emperumān and āḻvār, by the *vyākyātā* (commentator) periyavāccān piḷḷai\]. emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine heart “Do you have anything \[any means\] with you in order to attain me?” āḻvār responds saying

**poṟiyilēn** – I have the five senses as my residence. I have got caught in the *prākruta viśayam* (matters related to primordial matter, in other words, worldly pursuits) such as *śabdam, sparśam, rūpam,* rasam and *gandam* which are responsible for samsāram. The implied meaning is “I have only this fault and no benevolent material to attain you”. Just like a trap laid out for rats, āḻvār calls these worldly pursuits as *poṟi* (trap). Similar to the trap for rat, these also will show what appears superficially as something appealing and encircle the person inescapably. emperumān next asks him “You know that you should not get caught in this trap. Did you not prevent your eyes, ears etc from going towards them?” āḻvār responds saying …

**pulangaḷ aindum venṟilēn** – while I was pulled by sensory organs such as eyes, ears etc, I went around, under their control, without winning over them. Hasn’t the power of sensory organs been mentioned in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 2-60 too? “*yatatō hyapi kauntēya puruśasya vipaścita*: l *indriyāṇi pramātīni haranti prasabham mana*: ll” (Oh, the son of kunti! The mind of even the wise people, who have engagement (with yōgam) is pulled strongly by the sensory organs). taittirīya nārāyaṇam says “*darmēṇa pāpam apanudati*” (By engaging in puṇya karma, one rids of one’s sins). emperumān further asks him “bruhadāraṇya upaniśat 6-4-23 says ‘*tasmād ēvamvit śāntō dānta uparatas titikśus* l *samāhitō bhūtvā ātmanyēvātmānam paśyēt* ll’ (Thus, one who knows like this, would be one who has controlled his internal and external sensory perceptions, who does not have desire (in deeds which are carried out with an end benefit in mind), with a patience, with a mind which is not muddled and who sees the supreme being (as indvelling soul) within his ātmā). In such instances, vēdas teach a person the ways to control sensory organs. Have you not learnt those vēdams?” āḻvār replies …

**nalangaḷāya naṟkalaigaḷ nālilum navinṟilēn** – *nalangaḷāya kalaigaḷ* are the former parts of vēdas which show the way to people depending on the predominance quality among *satva* (noble), *rajas* (passionate) and *tamas* (ignorance) possessed by them, just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 2-45 “*traiguṇya viśayā vēdā*: *nistraiguṇyō bhavārjuna*” (Oh arjuna! vēdas were created for people who have one of three qualities of satva, rajas and tamas, in excess; let you be one without satva guṇa which is admixed with rajas and tamas guṇas) \[in other words, let you have only satva guṇa, without any trace of rajas and tamas\]. These vēdas shower affection equivalent to that shown by a thousand parents, ordaining the means to achieve the end-goal. *naṟkalaigaḷ* are the later part of vēdam, relating to vēdāntam, the end portions of vēdas, which focus on mōkśam and the means for reaching mōkśam. āḻvār says that he has not learnt any of the four vēdas and their vēdantams. *naviludal* – to learn; *kalai* – vēdam. The implied meaning is that the benefit of carrying out deeds will result only for those who recite vēdams and that it is only with such deeds that sensory organs could be won over. āḻvār implies that he is too far from winning over sensory organs.

When emperumān asks him “Why have you not recited vēdams which are the first means for winning over sensory organs?” āḻvār replies …

**kulangaḷāya īriraṇḍil onṟilum piṟandilēn** – it is not because of an indifferent mind that I did not engage with vēdas. Since I was not born in a clan which is qualified to recite vēdas, I did not engage with them. Only those who are born in brāhmaṇa, kśatriya and vaiśya varṇams can learn vēdams. Even for those in the fourth varṇam of śūdras, by assisting the first three varṇas, they could get rid of their sins and may get to win over their sensory organs. But I was not born in any of these four varṇas, says āḻvār. He was born to bhārgava riśi, a highly qualified brāhmaṇa riśi. How could he then say that he was not born in any of the four varṇas? Just as kriṣṇa, who was born in the kśatriya clan of yadu, considered the covherd clan who brought him up, as his clan, since āḻvār was brought up by a person who was engaged in bamboo craft, he considers himself to be outside the four varṇas. Did not parāśara maharishi too say in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-20-49? “*ayam sa katyatē prājñai*: *purāṇārta viśāradai*: l  
*gōpālō yādavam vamśam magnamabhyuddariśyati* ll” (\[kriṣṇa\] is celebrated as gōpālan (one who tended to cows) by great wise people in the meanings of purāṇas; he is going to uplift the clan of yadu which had been languishing for long). pramāṇams (authentic texts) too say “*sa pitā yastu pōśaka*:” (only he who nurtures a child well is considered as the father), confirming that more than the one who gave birth, only he who nurtures and brings up the child is considered as the father of the child.

Instead of considering the meaning for the pāsuram starting with *poṟiyilēn*, going down from one reason to another just as it had been mentioned at the beginning, we could also derive the meaning in the way that the pāsuram has been composed, starting with *kulangaḷāya īriraṇḍil* …. viz. – Since I was not born in any of the four varṇas, I was not qualified to recite vēdam which is only for those born in the first three varṇas. Since I did not recite the vēdams, I did not understand the meanings of vēdams. Due to that, since I did not carry out activities mentioned in the vēdāntams, the second part of vēdams, I could not win over my sensory organs. Due to that reason, I got caught in worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc.

emperumān asks him further “If you do not have the means to prevent engaging with worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc, what have you thought of, as your means for getting uplifted?” āḻvār says

**punida** – Oh, one who is capable of purifying others! Don’t you have the sanctity in you with which you could purify those lowly people who do not have anything in them to purify themselves after engaging with worldly pursuits such as śabdam etc? The implied meaning is that emperumān’s grace will stand in the place of birth in a superior clan, the resultant connection with vēdam and the resultant carrying out of deeds in line with the meanings of vēdams.

**ninnilangu pādamanṟi maṟṟor paṟṟilēn** – your divine feet are such splendorous that they will remove darkness to any extent. Apart from those divine feet, which shine even more because of the mercy which they shower on the deficient people who do not have anything in themselves, I do not have any other means.

**em īsanē** – Oh one who fulfils the tasks through your relationship with people like us who do not have anything in them and who do not have anyone to protect them!

Next, we will take up the 91<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 91. Pannulavu menmoli

*paṇṇulāvu menmoḻip padaittadangaṇāḷ poruttueṇṇilā arakkarai neruppināl nerukkināykaṇṇalāror kaṇṇilēn kalanda suṟṟam maṟṟilēneṇṇilāda māya ninnai ennuḷ neekkal enṟumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

paṇ – musical tunes such as kuṟinji, gāndāram, kāmaram etc; ulāvu – being in abundance; men – being soft; moḻiyāḷ – one who speaks; padai – shining like the weapon sword; tadam – being wide; kaṇāḷ poruttu – for the sake of śrī janakarājan’s divine daughter who has eyes \[like the above\]; eṇṇilā – countless; arakkarai – demons; neruppināl – with the fire of arrows; nerukkināy – one who killed, you; kaṇ – my guide; alāl – other than you; or kaṇṇilēn – I do not have any other guide; kalanda – appropriate for the heart; maṟṟu suṟṟamilēn – I do not have other relative relatives; eṇṇilāda – countless; māya – Oh one who has amazing power; ninnai – you (possessing omnipotence); ennuḷ – from me; enṟum – at any point of time; neekkal – you should not separate

**Перевод:**

**sītāp pirātti, the divine daughter of King janakan, has wide eyes which shine like a sword, speaks softly and has abundant musical tunes. For her sake, you killed countless demons with the fire of arrows. I do not have anyone other than you to guide me. Oh, one who has amazing power! You, who are omnipotent, should not separate yourself, from me, at any point of time.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “Just as it has been said in pūrvamīmāmsā 3-7-19 ‘śāstraphalam prayōktari‘ (One who carries out activities will reap the benefits mentioned in śāstram), when you are the one who is going to benefit, should you not make attempts to clear the hurdles for attaining your benefit? Why do you say that ‘there is no means other than your divine feet’?” āḻvār responds saying “There is an old narrative for this, viz. when sītāp pirātti was in aśōka vanam, she did not kill the demons in order to attain you. Only if she had made any attempt like that on her own should I make an effort. Hence, you should not say ‘You should make efforts to protect yourself’ and discard me, who does not have any other refuge and who does not have anything with me”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**paṇṇulāvu menmoḻi** – one who has a soft way of speaking, with musical tunes. Does she not have the beauty of speaking, just as mentioned by śrī rāma, during his separation from sītāp pirātti, to hanuman, in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-15? “*madurā madurālāpā kimāha mama bhāmini* l *madvihīnā varārōhā hanuman katayasva mē* \|\|” (Oh hanuman! What message did sītai, who is sweet, who has sweet words, who is beautiful, who is distinguished for enjoyment and who is separated from me, convey for me? Please say that). In a further pāsuram of this prabandam (105), he is going to speak about periya pirāttiyār “*paṇṇai venṟa insol mangai*” (one who speaks words which will win over musical tunes).

**padait tadangaṇāḷ poruttu** – for the sake of śrī janakarājan’s divine daughter, who has eyes which are as expansive and are sharp like a sword. Did not śrī rāmapirān too say that he cannot live even for a moment, separated from the beauty of her eyes, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10? “*ciram jīviti vaidēhi yadi māsam dariśyati* l  
*na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi vinā tāmasitēkśaṇām* ll” (If sītāp pirātti can survive for a month \[without me\], she would be considered to be capable of living for a long time. I cannot survive, without her dark eyes, even for a moment). Thus, the opinion is that sītāp pirātti has beauty in her speech and her eyes, without which emperumān cannot sustain himself even for a moment. āḻvār was going to speak about the absence of any means with him, to uplift himself. But, the purpose of his speaking about the eminence of pirātti is to emphasise that ātmā, which is without any connection with *acit* (physical form), is as dear to emperumān as are periya pirātti and śrī kaustubam \[śrī kaustumam is the divine jewel worn by emperumān, representing all the ātmās\]. After gaining knowledge \[about emperumān\], isn’t ātmā, residing in the body, similar to sītāp pirātti staying in aśōka vanam, being unable to tolerate the imprisonment?

**eṇṇilā arakkarai** – should ātmā be protected \[by emperumān\] only if it has countless enemies like sītāp pirātti? If there are only enemies similar to the five sensory organs which I have, should I not be protected?

**neruppināl nerukkināy kaṇ** – this could be construed as: you, who rained arrows on the demons and destroyed them with that fire of arrows, are my guide. Else, this could be construed as: you, who had sent hanuman as your arrow, just as it had been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 1-39 “*yatā rāghava nirmukta*: *Sara*: *śvasanavikrama*: l *gaccēt tadvad gamiśyāmi lankām rāvaṇa pālitām* ll” (Just like the arrow, shot by śrī rāma, moves like one’s breath (without anyone being aware of it), I will enter lankā which is protected by rāvaṇa), burnt lankā and made the demons to shiver in fear, are my guide. Through this, it is hinted that “You should burn down my hurdles and destroy them”.

**alālor kaṇṇilēn** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-15 “*lōkānām tvam parō darmō viśvaksēnaś caturbhuja*: l *śārngadanvā hruśīkēśa*: *puruśa*: *puruśōttama*: ll” (You are the most righteous, among all the worlds; you have the army which spreads everywhere; you are together with four shoulders; you are the controller of the bow, śārngam; you are the one controlling all the senses; you are praised as puruśa and puruśōttama), there is no one to guide me other than you, who are the most eminent and readily available means. This āḻvār too mercifully mentions just like nammāḻvār did in tiruvāimoḻi 3-6-8 “*dayaradaṟku magan tannaiyanṟi maṟṟilēn tanjamāgavē*” (I do not have anyone as refuge other than the son of emperor daśaratan). While pirātti had the power to protect herself, she did not, in line with her nature of being totally dependent on emperumān, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 22-20 “*asandēśāttu rāmasya tapasaścānubālanāt* l *na tvā kurmi daśagrīva bhasma bhasmārha tējasā* ll” (Oh, one who is fit to be burnt down to ashes! Since rāmapirān has not given me an order, since I have to protect my *pātivratya darmam* (that I should look up to my husband to protect me), I have not burnt you down with the power of my *pativratāgni* (the fire of my vow of being totally dependent on my husband for my protection)). āḻvār says that when he does not even have any power, how could he protect himself?

**kalanda suṟṟam maṟṟilēn** – he says that he does not have any relative who is appropriate for his heart. My parents, who gave birth to me, left me in the forest saying ‘Let emperumān protect him’. I had given up my parents who nurtured me \[since then\], just as it has been mentioned in vihagēndra samhitai 22 as well as in śaraṇāgati gadyam “*pitaram mātaram dārān putrān bandūn sakīn gurūn* l *ratnāni danadānyāni kśētrāṇi ca gruhāṇi ca* ll *sarvadarmāmśca santyajya sarvakāmāmśca sākśarān* ll *lōkavikrānta caraṇau śaraṇam tē’vrajam vibhō* ll” (Oh bhagavān! Leaving aside father, mother, wife, children, relatives, friends, teachers, gems, wealth and food grains, fields, houses, all the means \[to attain you\] and all the benefits including *kaivalyam* \[where the ātmā enjoys itself\], along with any impression in these, I take refuge under your divine feet which had mercifully measured the worlds). Thus, just as lakśmaṇa said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 58-31 “*aham tāvan mahārājē pitrutvam nōpalakśayē* l *bhrātā bhartā ca banduśca pitā ca mama rāghava*: ll” (I have not considered daśarata maharāja as my father; for me, rāghava (śrī rāma) himself is my elder brother, nouriśer, relative and father), āḻvār says that emperumān himself is all relationships to him. When pirātti was alone in aśōka vanam, hanuman came and registered his presence. āḻvār says that he has none other than emperumān to even look at him with affection. emperumān asks āḻvār “sītāp pirātti had the distinction of having been born in a great clan. You said that you were not born in any of the four varṇas. \[How do I help you, then?\]” āḻvār says

**eṇṇilāda māya** – aren’t you one with inestimable amazing power? The meaning for this is: your power is such that you could grant mōkśam to a lowly creature such as jatāyu (a bird) which was not qualified to recite vēdams. When rāvaṇan was ruling lankā on the other side of the ocean, without any trouble, emperumān coronated his brother vibhīśaṇa on this side of the ocean. Did he see the clan of vibhīśaṇa’s birth when he did that? When emperumān persisted, saying “sītāp pirātti was very knowledgeable, just as it has been brought out in śrī rāmāyaṇa ayōdyā kāṇḍam 26-4 ślōkam “*dēvakāryam svayam krutvā krutajñā hruśta cētanā* l *abhijñā rāja darmāṇām rājaputram pratīkśatē* ll” (sītāp pirātti who knows returning a favour, who was happy in her divine mind and who knows the regal duties, carried out worship of emperumān and was awaiting the arrival of the son of emperor)” āḻvār says just as it has been said in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 10-10 “*anṟu carācarangaḷai vaigundattēṟṟi*” (On that day when śrī rāma ascended to parama padam, he took with him all the cētana (souls with knowledge) and acētana entities (souls without knowledge)) “did you examine the levels of knowledge of those in ayōdyā when you took everyone with you to paramapadam?”

**ninnai ennuḷ neekkal** – you should not separate yourself, who is capable of everything, from me, who is not capable of anything, by showing the absence of means etc in me.

**enṟumē** – the implied meaning is that even when I, out of bewilderment, go after any means to attain you, you should not separate yourself from me.

Next, we will take up the 92<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 92. Vidaik kulangal

*vidaikkulangaḷ ēḻadarttu venṟi vēṟkaṇmādarārkadikkalanda tōḷ puṇarnda kāliyāya vēlai nīrpadaittadaittu adiṟkidandu mun kadainda ninṟanakkuadaikkalam pugunda ennai anjal enna vēṇḍumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mun – at the start; vēlai – having waves; nīr – huge ocean; padaittu – created; adil kidandu – reclining on tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) which was a part of that huge ocean; kadaindu – (for the sake of celestial entities) churned it; adaittu – blocked (salty ocean, another portion of that ocean, during rāmāvatāram); vidai kulangaḷ ēḻu adarttu – being victorious over seven different types of bulls; venṟi – being victorious; vēl – looking like spear; kaṇ – having eyes; mādarār – nappinnaip pirātti’s; kadi kalanda – being fresh and fragrant; tōḷ – shoulders; puṇarnda – one who embraced; kāli āya – Oh cow herd, who tends to cows!; ninṟanakku – to you; adaikkalam pugunda – one who surrendered; ennai – (one without any other refuge) me; anjal enna vēṇḍumē – you should mercifully say a word “Do not fear”

**Перевод:**

**At the beginning of creation, you created the huge ocean and reclined on the milky ocean and churned it, which forms a part of that huge ocean. During the time of your incarnation as śrī rāma, you blocked a portion of that ocean which was salty. You won over seven bulls of different classes and embraced the fresh and fragrant shoulders of nappinnaip pirātti who had victorious, spear-like eyes. Oh covherd, who tends to cows, you should mercifully say a word “Do not fear” to me, who has surrendered to you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār, in his divine mind “You say that I should not separate from you. What have you done in our matter that we should not separate from you? What is it that we should do for you?” āḻvār replies “I, not having any other refuge, surrendered to your divine feet, which are the natural means \[to attain you\]. Just as you had said in bhagavat gītā 18-66 “sarvadarmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja l aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca: ll” (Giving up all righteous paths (which are considered as means), along with their impressions, attain me alone, as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins; do not grieve), you should mercifully say ‘Protecting you is my responsibility. There is no need for you to fear’”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vidaikkulangaḷ ēḻadarttu …..** – the pāsuram has to be transposed as *“mun vēlai nīr padaittu adil kidandu kadaindu adaittu vidaikkulangaḷ ēḻadarttu venṟi vēl kaṇ mādarār kadikkalanda tōḷ puṇarnda kāliyāya*! *ninṟanakku adaikkalam pugunda ennai anjal ennavēṇḍum*” and get the meaning.

**mun vēlai nīr padaittu** – At the beginning of creation, creating the ocean which is full of water and which has spread out everywhere, just as it has been mentioned in manu smruti 1-8 “*sō’bhidyāya śarīrāt svāt sisrukśur vividā*: *prajā*: *l* *apa Eva sasarjādau tāsu bhījam (vīryam) avāsrujat* ll” (Desiring to create many species of prajās (creations) from his divine form, that paramātmā (supreme entity) through his *sankalpam* (thought), created water first; in that water, he planted the seed (of his power)). The term *vēlai* will refer to boundary as well as waves; *nīr* refers to ocean; the term *vēlai nīr* will thus refer either to the boundary of ocean or to ocean with agitating waves.

**adil kidandu** – reclining on it. Reclining on the milky ocean with the thought “If these jīvas (various creations) who have been created, wish to attain us, we \[the plural form refers only to emperumān\], apt to be surrendered to, should not be in parama padam which cannot be approached by them but in a place which they can come to”.

**kadaindu** – As a result of his reclining in the milky ocean, when the celestial entities lost their wealth which disappeared in the milky ocean, and came to him for help, just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-65 “*praṇāma pravaṇā nāta daitya sainya parājitā*: l *śaraṇam tvāmanuprāptā*: *samastā dēvatēgaṇā*: ll” (Oh lord! We, the assembly of all the celestial entities, who were won over by the demonic entities, were engaged only with bowing down to you and have taken refuge under you), he churned the ocean for their sake and redeemed all their wealth.

**adaittu** – when the demon rāvaṇa et al crossed all the boundaries of acceptance \[of good behaviour\] and seized the worlds of celestial entities, emperumān incarnated as śrī rāma to destroy those demons. At that time, he \[built a dam across the ocean and\] blocked a part of that ocean.

**vidaikkulangaḷ …… kāliyāya** – the pāsuram further says that apart from helping celestial entities such as brahmā et al during their times of danger by being apt to be taken refuge under, in order to manifest himself to human beings who are much lower than the celestial entities \[in terms of knowledge and efforts\], he incarnated as kaṇṇan \[kriṣṇa\] and protected them too.

**vidaikkulangaḷ ēḻadarttu** – instead of saying *vidaiyēḻadarttu* (overcoming seven bulls) or *vidaikkulamadarttu* (overcoming the group of bulls), what is the reason for using the term *vidaikkulangaḷ ēḻadarttu* (controlling seven classes of bulls)? emperumān had said in bhagavat gītā 4-13 “*cāturvarṇyam mayā sruśtam guṇakarma vibhāgaśa*: l *tasya kartāramapi mām vidyakartāramavyayam* ll” (This world, starting with the four *varṇams* (classes) had been created by me, with the divisions based on (satva, rajas, tamas) guṇas and deeds (based on śamam and damam). Even though I have carried out this creation, know that I, who am without any karma (deeds) am non-doer). Just as there are differences among people, there are differences among animals too. Thus, there are bulls from different types among those seven bulls which emperumān controlled at the same time, in order to remove the hurdles which were in the way of his marrying nappinnaip pirātti. The term *kulam* refers to *varṇam* (class) or *jāti* (type). This āḻvār too had said in the earlier pāsuram “*kulangaḷāya īriraṇḍll onṟilum piṟandilēn*“, equating the word *kulam* with varṇam.

**venṟiveḷ kaṇ mādarar kadikkalanda tōḷ puṇarnda kāliyāya** – your \[emperumān’s\] eyes are such that they will win over anything or anybody, just as it has been brought out in jitantē ślōkam 1-1 “*jitantē puṇḍarīkākśa namastē viśvabhāvana* l *namastē’stu hruśīkēśa mahāpuruśa pūrvaja*: ll” (Oh, one who has eyes which are like reddish lotus! (This ātma entity) has been won over by you; (this) is not mine; Oh, one who created the worlds! Oh, one who controls all the sensory perceptions! Oh, one who is magnanimous! Let this (ātma entity) remain only for you and not for me). cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says of emperumān “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes) and in tiruvāimoḻi 9-9-9 “*tūdu sey kangaḷ*” (with eyes which are like messengers). While your eyes are capable of winning over everyone, nappinnaip pirātti is capable of winning over you through her eyes. Oh, one who embraced her fragrant shoulders and gave protection to the cows which were looking up to you for their protection, making her happy and considering that act too to be for your benefit!

**kadikkalanda tōḷ** – emperumān is considered as the repository of all fragrances as brought out in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of all the good fragrances and all the good tastes). The implied meaning in this verse “*kadikkalanda tōḷ”* is that nappinnaip pirātti’s fragrant shoulders will shower fragrance to emperumān’s divine form too. The term *kadi* will refer to beauty as well as fragrance. Since pirātti is at the boundary of adolescence, matching his youthful state, just as it has been mentioned in the saying – “*yuvatiśca kumāriṇī*” (being more in the stage of an adolescent, with a little bit of youthfulness), being appropriate for her stage of being a *mangai* (one who is around 12 to 13 years of age), he \[emperumān\] is in the stage, as said in rig vēda aśtakam 2-2-25 “*yuvā’kumāra*:” (one who is more in the stage of youth, with a little bit of adolescence), we can consider the meaning for *kadi* here as beautiful too.

**venṟivēl kaṇ** – eyes which are like a spear, causing victory.

**ninṟanakku adaikkalam pugunda ennai** – instead of surrendering to para stage (as in śrī vaikuṇṭam) or vyūha stage (as in tiruppāṟkadal) or other vibhava (incarnations) stages, I surrendered to you in your incarnation as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa).  It has been mentioned in śvētāsvatara upanishad 6-18 “*yō brahmāṇam vidadāti pūrvam yō vai vēdāmśca prahiṇōti tasmai* l *tam ha dēvam ātmabuddi prasādam mumukśurvai śaraṇam aham prapadyē* ll” (I, desiring to attain mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam), surrender to that supreme entity, who initially created brahmā and who instructed him all the vēdams) that parama puruśan who is the causative factor for the worlds is apt to be surrendered to. In the pramāṇam seen under the term *kadaindu* śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 1-9-65, it has been mentioned that kśīrābdinātan is apt to be surrendered to. In the śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 120-15 ślōkam “*lōkānām tvam parō darmō viśvaksēnaś caturbhuja*: l  
*śārngadanvā hruśīkēśa*: *puruśa*: *puruśōttama*: ll” (You are the most righteous, among all the worlds; you have the army which spreads everywhere; you are together with four shoulders; you are the controller of the bow, śārngam; you are one controlling all the senses; you are praised as puruśa and puruśōttama), it has been mentioned by sages that śrī rāma is apt to be surrendered to. Instead of surrendering to these forms, just as you \[emperumān\] had said in bhagavat gītā 18-66 “*sarvadarmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja* l *aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca*: ll” (Giving up all righteous paths (which are considered as means), along with their impressions, attain me alone as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins; do not grieve), I have surrendered to your divine feet.

**ennai** – me, who has no other refuge. a*daikkalam pugudal* refers to attaining emperumān with the thought that emperumān is responsible for everything. āḷavandār too, in his stōtra ratnam 60<sup>th</sup> ślōkam, mercifully says “*pitā tvam mātā tvam dayita tanayastvam priyasuhrut* *tvamēva tvam mitram gururasi gatiścāsijagatām* l *tvadīyas tvadbhrutyas tavaparijanas tvadgatiraham* *prapannaścaivam satyahamapi tavaivāsmi hi bhara*: ll” (you are the father to all the worlds; you are the mother too; you are the dear son too; you are the good-hearted friend too; you are the equal (to whom secrets too can be told); you are the teacher; you are the means (and the benefit) too. I, on the other hand, am yours; I am your servitor; I am your slave; I have you as my most cherished benefit; I have you as my means; when I am like this, am I not apt to be protected by you?)

**anjalenna vēndumē** – just as you had told arjunan not to grieve, in the carama ślōkam (bhagavat gītā 18-66), you should tell me too, not to grieve, without looking at your eminence and my lowliness as well as the abundance of hurdles for me, in attaining you, such as ignorance, karma (past deeds) etc. You had mercifully said in that carama ślōkam “Attain me alone as the means” For you who told arjuna “I will liberate from you from all sins, do not fear”, is it difficult to tell me the same?

Next, we will take up the 93<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 93. Surumbarangu

*surumbarangu taṇṭhuḻāy tudaindalarnda pādamēvirumbi ninṟu iṟainjuvēṟku irangu arangavāṇanēkarumbirunda kattiyē kadaṟkidanda kaṇṇanēirumbaranga venjaram turanda villi rāmanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

karumbu irunda – (on all sides) being naturally sweet like sugar cane; kattiyē – Oh one who is like a block of sugar!; kadal – in the milky ocean; kidanda – reclining; kaṇṇanē – Oh one who is easily approachable!; irumbu – on the bodies of enemies which are like firm iron; aranga – to sink deeply; vem saram – cruel arrows; vil – one who has the bow; irāmanē – Oh the divine son of emperor daśaratan!; arangavāṇanē – Oh the protector of śrīrangam!; surumbu – beetles; arangu – settled; taṇ – being comfortable; tuḻāy – the divine tuḷasi; tudanidu alarnda – blossoming on contacting; pādamē – only your divine feet; virumbi – being desirous of; ninṟu – always remaining; iṟainjuvēṟku – to me, who is praying; irangu – you should mercifully shower your grace

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is like a block of sugar, being naturally sweet on all sides! Oh, one who is easily approachable while reclining on the milky ocean! Oh, one who is the divine son of emperor daśaratan, having a bow from which you shot arrows which sank deeply on the firm, iron-like bodies of enemies! Oh, the protector of śrīrangam! The comfortable, divine tuḷasi, on which beetles have settled, blossoms on coming into contact with your divine feet. You should mercifully shower your grace on me, who is desirous of always praying to such divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had told emperumān ‘adaikkalam pugunda ennai anjal enna vēṇḍumē’ in line with the assurance given in bhagavat gītā 18-66 carama ślōkam “mā śuca:” (do not grieve), granting him refuge. When emperumān asks him “Is that enough?” āḻvār says “I sought refuge from you, who are the readily available means, so that you will not separate me from you, asking me to seek other means. That is not enough. You should show mercy on me such that I, desirous of enjoying your distinguished sweetness, give up all the other matters and enjoy only you”.*

**surumbu arangu** **taṇ tuḻāy tudaindu alarnda pādamē**– only the divine feet on which divine tuḷasi garland, which is invigorating and on which beetles have settled, shines after blossoming. Through this verse, the following three are indicated: (1) only that entity who has divine tuḷasi as a garland is the supreme entity. (2) Just as the divine tuḷasi becomes fresh and blossoms on being together with emperumān’s divine feet, even for those attain his divine feet, freshness and effect of blossoming will result. (3) Since they are decorated with the divine tuḷasi, emperumān’s divine feet have unmatched sweetness.

**virumbi ninṟu iṟainjuvēṟku irangu** – you should shower your grace on me who has started to enjoy you, after getting the desire to enjoy you. You should show your mercy and shower your grace on me saying“Let him come out of all the worldly pursuits which destroy his basic nature; let him enjoy me”. By doing this alone, my soul will get uplifted; hence, you should certainly do this.

**arangavāṇanē** – Oh one who is reclining in śrī rangam, being its lord! The purpose of your reclining in the temple is only to remove, through your beauty, the involvement with worldly pursuits and to create an involvement with you, for those who not have the means in them for protecting themselves, and who have started enjoying you. Just as toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār said in his tirumālai 16<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*pōdarē enṟu sollip pundiyil pugundu tanpāl ādaram perugavaitta aḻagan*” (emperumān’s beauty is such that it will make one to be amazed and to get involved with matters related to him), this āḻvār too says that it is emperumān’s beauty which is our means as well as our end goal.

**karumbirunda kattiyē** – āḻvār says that the teacher who created this interest in him towards emperumān was his \[emperumān’s\] sweetness only. Oh, one who is like a sugarcane, being naturally sweet on all sides! The term *katti* emphasises the fact that like sugarcane, there is no part which has to be discarded or which is unwanted, in emperumān. The usage of the term *“karubu pōl irunda*” indicates that unlike a block of sugar which needs to be heated in order to get the sweetness all around, emperumān is sweet by his nature. The meaning here is that he is like the naturally sweet block of sugar.

**kadal kidanda kaṇṇanē** – Oh, one who is reclining in the milky ocean! This indicates the root cause for the incarnation of periya perumāḷ (the presiding deity in śrī rangam). He says “Oh, one who has the simplicity to come down mercifully to recline in the milky ocean for removing the hurdles of your followers, even before they had prayed to you”. Since emperumān is reclining in the milky ocean in order to remove the hurdles of brahmā often, this indicates that the simplicity and beauty, seen in emperumān in tiruppāṟkadal, are seen in periya perumāḷ too.

**irumbaranga venjaram turanda vil irāmanē** – oh the divine son of emperor daśaratan, who holds the bow, with which you shot the fiery arrows which sank deeply on the bodies of enemies, which were firm, like iron! Through this verse, it is indicated that the valour and beauty of the divine son of emperor daśaratan are very much seen in periya perumāḷ, just as it has been mentioned by toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār in tirumālai 11 “*nam sēvaganār maruviya periya kōyil*” (the eminent temple where one, who is our lord and who is a great warrior, came to reside fittingly). Wasn’t the valour and beauty of the divine son of emperor daśaratan spoken of in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 83-8 too “*mēghaśyāmam mahābāhum stirasatvam drudavrutam* l *kadā drakśyāmahē rāmam jagata*: *śōkanāśanam* ll” (When will we see śrī rāma, who is dark like a cloud, who has long hands, who has firm strength, who has firm penance and who removes sorrows of the people?). The implied meaning for this verse is – when you are the ordained lord, you are in proximity, you are with unmatched sweetness and you are with the nature of destroying enemies and when I have the inclination towards you, there is no reason for me to lose out. Hence, you should mercifully shower your grace.

The pāsuram should be rearranged as *kadal kidanda kaṇṇanē* – *irumbaranga venjaram turanda vil irāmanē* – *arangavāṇanē* – *surumbu arangu taṇ tuḻāy tudaindu alarnda pādamē* – *virumbi ninṟu iṟainjuvēṟku irangu* to get the correct sequence of meaning.

Next, we will take up the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 94. Unin meya

*ūnin mēya āvi nī uṟakkamōdu uṇarcci nīānin mēya aindum nī avaṟṟuḷ ninṟa tūymai nīvāninōdu maṇṇu nī vaḷam kadaṟpayanu nīyānu nī adanṟi empirānum nī irāmanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uṟakkamōdu – along with sleep; uṇarcci – being awake; nī – under your control; vaḷam – being beautiful; kadal – the ocean’s; payanum – nectar and rubies \[wealth of the ocean\] which are beneficial; nī – under your control; ānil mēya – derived from cow; aindum – five materials; nī – under your control; avaṟṟuḷ – among those entities; ninṟa – remaining; tūymai – the sacredness too; nī – under your control; ūnil mēya – present inside the body; āvi – the five types of vital airs; nī – under your control; vāninōdu – along with the ethereal layer; maṇṇum – earth too; nī – under your control; yānum – I too (bound to this samsāram); nī – am your attribute; irāmanē – Oh, the divine son of emperor \[daśaratan\]!; adanṟi – apart from those; empirānum nī – you are the controller for us too, who do not have any means with us

**Перевод:**

**Sleep, along with wakefulness, is under your control. Nectar, rubies etc, which are present in the beautiful ocean, are under your control. The five materials which are derived from the cow are under your control. The sanctity within those five materials is also under your control. The five types of vital airs, present inside the body, are under your control. Ethereal layer, along with earth, is under your control. I too, bound to this samsāram, am your attribute. Oh, the divine son of emperor \[daśarata\], apart from that, you are the controller of us too, who do not have any means with us.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “While you should have asked me ‘Please remove all the hurdles which prevent me from enjoying you, grant all which facilitate that enjoyment and conduct it such that there is no break in that enjoyment’, you are merely asking me to have mercy on you. Is that enough?” āḻvār says “Since there is no entity which is not an attribute to you and since there is no entity which is fully independent of you, once you take mercy on me, whatever you had told me at the beginning will happen to me”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

The pāsuram has to be rearranged as *uṟakkamōdu uṇarcci nī – vaḷangadal payanum nī* – *ānil mēya aindum nī*, *avaṟṟuḷ ninṟa tūymai nī* – *ūnil mēya āvi nī* – *vāninōdu maṇṇum nī* – *yānum nī adanṟi empirānum nī irāmanē* to get the cogent meaning.

**uṟakkamōdu uṇarcci nī** – Due to excessive level of *tamō guṇam* (quality of ignorance, laziness, sleep etc) the minds of people, since time immemorial, have been in a state of sleep when the brain becomes blunt. When the *satva guṇam* (noble quality) is in excess, knowledge manifests itself. These two states \[of ignorance and wakefulness\] are under your \[emperumān’s\] control. Through this, it is implied that since you control ignorance, which is the reason for getting involved with worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc and knowledgeable state, which is the reason for engagement with you, your mercy alone is the means \[to attain you\]. This has been mentioned in māṇḍūkya kārikai too “*anādi māyayā suptō yadā jīva*: *prabudyatē*” (when jīvātmā, who is sleeping due to the timeless illusion of samsāram, awakens); since those who do not have knowledge resemble those who sleep, the act of sleeping is considered as being ignorant, in this context.

**vaḷam kadal payanum nī** – ocean, which attracts those who see it, has materials such as ambrosia, rubies etc; these materials were formed due to your *sankalpam* (resolution; oath). Symbolically, this example extends to all benefits. Thus, since it says that materials which are bound to samsāram and which are hurdles in reaching you are under your control, if you show mercy on me, these hurdles will disappear.

**ānil mēya aindum nī** – śāstrams (sacred texts\] say that there are five materials which are obtained from the cow, and which purify people: (1) cow’s dung, (2) cow’s urine, (3) cow’s milk, (4) curd from its milk and (5) ghee obtained from that curd \[through churning and clarifying the resultant butter\]. These five materials, called as *panca gavyam*, are under your control.

**avaṟṟuḷ ninṟa tūymai nī** – the sanctity, associated with those five materials \[obtained from cow\] resulted due to your *sankalpam*. Sacredness to any entity comes due to its association with you. Since brāhmaṇas and cow are instrumental for vorśipping emperumān, they are cited as cause for sanctity. It is pertinent to recall the pramāṇam (authentic text) here “*brāhmaṇānām gavāncaiva kulamēkam dvidākrutam* l *anyatra mantrās tiśtanti havir anyatra tiśtati* ll” (brāhmaṇas and cows are two subdivisions of the same class (of the clan of rituals). In one subdivision, of brāhmaṇas, mantrams (which are used in rituals) are present; in the other subdivision, of cows, havis (such as ghee etc which are used in the rituals) are present). Since karmas (rituals) are part of the worship of emperumān, they are considered to purify jīvātmās (souls). Just as it has been said in yājñavalkya smruti 3-34 “*kśētrajñasyēśvarajñānāt viśuddi*: *paramā matā*” (It has been thought that distinguished sanctity happens to samsāri cētana by knowing about emperumān), knowledge too, due to being associated with emperumān, purifies jīvas. Haven’t ślōkams in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 82-9 “*pāvanas sarvalōkānām tvamēva raghunandana*” (Oh raghunandana (śrī rāma)! You are the one who purifies all the worlds) and in mahābhāratam vanaparvam 88-27 “*pavitrāṇām hi gōvinda*: *pavitram paramucayatē* l *puṇyānāmapi puṇyō’sau mangaḷānānca mangaḷam* ll” (Among all the sacred entities, gōvindan (emperumān) is said to be the most sacred entity; among the virtuous entities, he is the most virtuous; among the auspicious entities, he is the most auspicious) too manifested emperumān’s quality of sanctity? Thus, it is implied that all the sacred materials, which remove the impurities which result from engagement with worldly pursuits, are under your control.

**ūnil mēya āvi nī** – the five types of vital airs, viz. prāṇa, apāna, vyāna, udāna and samāna, which sustain the body, are under your control. The implied meaning is that the physical form, sustaining itself or perishing, is under your control.

**vāninōdu maṇṇum nī** – visible ethereal layer does not hold on to anything for its support; wind etc roam around in that ethereal layer. Earth, which is in water, does not dissolve in it, and is supporting many materials. These two sustain themselves because of you. Here, the term *vān* refers to ethereal layer and *maṇ* refers to this *samsāra vibhūti* (materialistic realm). Alternatively, *vān* could be taken to mean parama padam which is attained by mukta jīvātmās (those who have got liberated and have attained mōkśam) and the meaning for this term would be – samsāram, which binds jivātmās and nitya vibhūti, which they attain when they get liberated, are under your control.

**yānum nī** – I, who am bound to samsāram, am your attribute, never separating from you. Thus, even during the time when I was not involved with you, just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 10-10 “*tēśām satatayuktānām bhajatām prīti pūrvakam* l  
*dadāmi buddi yōgam tam yēna māmupayānti tē* \|\|” (I affectionately shower intelligence to those who desire to be with me all the time and who worship me such that they can use it as a path to attain me) it was only you who created an involvement in me, towards you. Thus, even the involvement which you created in me towards you, is under your control.

**empirānum ni irāmanē** – the etymology for the word *rāma* is “*ramayati iti rāma*:” – you grant the wishes of those who desire them and make them happy. You are the one who is my benefactor by granting my wish, when I have nothing in me as my means. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 59-4 “*viśyē tē mahārāja rāmavyasanakarśitā*: l  
*api vrukśā*: *parimlānā*: *sapuśpānkurakōrakā*: ll” (Oh mahārāja! In your kingdom, even trees which are with flowers, buds etc, have withered away due to separation from śrī rāma) you are the lord for all the creations, including down to plants, which are stationary, who cannot sustain themselves in separation from you, due to your beauty and qualities. Just as it has been seen in the pramānām śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 17-11 “*ālamadya hi bhuktēna paramārtairalam ca na*: l *yatā paśyēma niryāntam rāmam rājyē pratiśtitam* ll  
*tatō hi na*: *priyataram nānyat kincit bhaviśyati* ll” (Today, we do not need food; we do not need any good material too; there is nothing dearer to us than seeing rāma who mercifully comes after his coronation) veren’t the people in ayodyā such that they could not sustain themselves in separation from śrī rāma? nammāḻvār too mercifully expressed this meaning in his tiruvāimoḻi 7-5-1 “*puṟpāmudalāp pul eṟumbādi onṟinṟiyē naṟpāl ayōttiyil vāḻum carācaram muṟṟavum naṟpālukku uyttanan nānmuganār peṟṟa nāttuḷē*” (He made all the entities starting with grass, ants etc including plant and animal lives, who live in the great kingdom of ayōdyā (where śrī rāma’s auspicious qualities flourish) to be with eminent nature, in this world created by brahmā).  Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 68-30 “*yā gatir yajñaśeelānām āhidāgnēśca yā gati*: l *apārāvartinām yā ca yā ca bhūmiprādāyinām* ll *mayā tvam samanujñātō gacca lōkān anuttamān* ll” ((Oh, the king of vultures!) You are allowed by me to go to those unique places which are the abodes for those who observe rituals, for brāhmaṇas who nurture three types of fire rituals and for those who attain mōkśam, from which none returns), only he who gave mōkśam to a bird, jatāyu, is the one who conducts our deeds too. Just as it has been said in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 10-10 “*anṟu carācarangaḷai vaigundattēṟṟi*” (On that day, when you made all the mobile and stationary creations to ascend to parama padam), only he who granted parama padam to all the jīvātmās in ayōdyā, is the one who will grant us too, our end goal \[of carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān and enjoying his resultant happiness\]. The implied meaning is that it is enough if emperumān showers his mercy on us.

Next, we will take up the 95<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 95. Adakkarum pulangal

*adakkarum pulangaḷ aindum adakki āsaiyām avaitodakkaṟuttu vandu nin toḻilkaṇ ninṟa ennai nīvidakkarudi mey seyādu mikkorāsai ākkilumkadaṟkidanda ninnalālor kaṇṇilēn em aṇṇalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

em aṇṇalē – Oh one who is my svāmy!; adakkarum – difficult to control; pulangaḷ aindum – five sensory organs; adakki – controlling them (such that they do not engage with other matters – worldly pursuits); āsaiyām avai – desire in other matters; todakku – connection, with their scent; aṟuttu – removing; vandu – subsequently; nin – your; toḻil kaṇ – in the work of service to you; ninṟa ennai – I, who stood firm; nī – you (who started with making me victorious over my sensory organs, until all these); vidakkarudi – thinking of giving up; mey seyādu – not completing till the end (the effort of uplifting me, which you had started); mikkorāsai ākkilum – even if you create an interest in other matters; kadal – in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean); kidanda – reclining (for my sake); ninnalāl – other than you; or kaṇṇilēn – I do not have another controller

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is my svāmy! You had made me to control my five sensory organs, which are difficult to control, removing any desire in matters other than those related to you, including the scent in that desire. Subsequently, I stood firm in service to you. Even if you think of giving up on me, not completing the task of taking me till the end and you think of creating desire in me towards other matters, I do not have a controller other than you, who are reclining in tiruppāṟkadal, for my sake.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, saying “empirānum nī irāmanē” āḻvār said that śrī rāmapirān, who is totally independent, and is capable of granting the wishes of those who seek, is the means. In this pāsuram, he mercifully affirms his faith in that means. He says “You have removed my attachments towards worldly pursuits, created desire towards you and brought me to the position where I had sought kainkaryam towards your divine feet. Even if you let go of me such that I could engage with other benefits, I do not have any refuge other than you”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**adakkarum pulangaḷ aindu adakki** – if a cētanan (one with knowledge) starts to control his five sensory organs, through an ācāryan (teacher)’s instructions and practising with śāstrams (sacred texts), emperumān controls his five senses from going towards other matters, manifests his divine form and attracts the sensory organs towards that form. He controls the cētanan’s sensory organs this way, making the cētanan to engage with him. If a query were to be asked – while the verse only says about controlling the senses, where is the mention about emperumān manifesting his divine form and making the cētanan to engage his senses with him – in the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*pulangaḷ aindum venṟilēn poṟiyilēn*” since āḻvār had expressed his inability to control his senses, it is clear that controlling of sensory organs mentioned here is only because of emperumān. The difficulty in controlling sensory organs is clear from the fact that even arjuna, who had bowed down to ūrvasi, considering her as his mother, mentioned to emperumān in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 6-34 “*cancalam hi mana*: *kriṣṇa pramāti balavad drudam* l *tasyā’ham nigraham manyē vāyōriva suduśkaram* ll” (Oh kriṣṇa! Mind is unstable, bewildering, strong (towards worldly matters) and wavering. I think that it is difficult to be controlled just as it is difficult to control the flow of wind).

**āsaiyāmavai todakkaṟuttu** – due to the sacred nature of emperumān’s divine form, he removed all my desires in worldly pursuits, completely. Since the reason for desire in worldly pursuits is only the previous deeds of a cētanan (virtues and vices), only emperumān can sever that connection.

**vandu nin toḻil kaṇ ninṟa ennai** – me who had reached the outer boundary of the benefit that a cētanan can attain, praying for kainkaryam (servitude towards your divine feet).

**nī vidak karudi** – starting with control of sensory organs and reaching to the stage of praying for *kainkaryam* you had made me achieve these. If you think about giving me up ….

**mey seyādu** – instead of taking the activity which was started, till the end. *mey seyyāmal iruttal* (not doing the proper task) refers to the poor conduct of starting an ātmā towards being uplifted and making him to engage with the exact opposite pursuit midway. Hasn’t emperumānār (bhagavat rāmānujar) mercifully said in gītā bhāśyam 3-6? “*anyatā sankalpya anyatācaratīti mityā cāra*: *sa ucyatē*” (after taking a resolve to act in a particular way, if a person acts in the exact opposite way, he is said to be acting with a false conduct)

**mikkor āsai ākkilum** – even if you create a great desire towards other matters; emperumān, who started to guide the cētana towards controlling his sensory organs, should not create desire in other worldly pursuits such as śabdam, sparśam, rūpam, rasam, gandam etc. Even if he creates a desire..

**kadal kidanda ninnalālor kaṇ ilēn** – even when I had not prayed for, you had left parama padam where you were ruling with your sceptre and came down to tiruppāṟkadal, which is in samsāram which is anatematic to you, for my benefit. Apart from such you, I have no else who can guide and conduct me.

**em aṇṇalē** – āḻvār says that the reason for emperumān to carry out benefit for him even when he had not prayed for it is because emperumān is his svāmy (lord). The implied meaning is that being his natural lord, even if emperumān were to give up on āḻvār, āḻvār will not let go of emperumān.

Next, we will take up the 94<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 96. Varambilada maya

*varambilāda māya māya vaiyamēḻum meymmaiyēvarambilūḻi ēttilum varambilāda kīrttiyāyvarambilāda palpiṟappu aṟuttu vandu nin kaḻalporundumā tirunda nī varam sey puṇḍarīganē*

**Пословный перевод:**

varambu ilāda – without a boundary; māya – Oh one who has mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) as your possession (under your control)!; māya – Oh one who has amazing qualities such as jñāna, śakti (knowledge, power) etc; puṇḍarīganē – Oh one who has lotus-like eyes!; vaiyam ēḻum – all the people who are in the seven worlds; meymmaiyē – truly; varambil ūḻi – countless kalpams (each kalpam is 4.32 million years); ēttilum – even if they praise; varambilāda – without boundary; kīrttiyāy – Oh one who has fame; varambilāda – each being countless; pal piṟappu – many births distinguished by differences among dēva etc species; aṟuttu – destroying; vandu – coming (to parama padam through the path of arcis, light); nin kaḻal – in your divine feet (which is the most enjoyable for nityasūris); porundumā – to attain without separating ever; tirunda – for me to get reformed; nī – you (who would look for jīvātmās to grant refuge); varam sey – should shower your mercy; puṇḍarīganē – Oh one who has lotus-like eyes!; vaiyam ēḻum – all the people who are in the seven worlds; meymmaiyē – truly; varambil ūḻi – countless kalpams (each kalpam is 4.32 million years); ēttilum – even if they praise; varambilāda – without boundary; kīrttiyāy – Oh one who has fame; varambilāda – each being countless; pal piṟappu – many births distinguished by differences among dēva etc species; aṟuttu – destroying; vandu – coming (to parama padam through the path of arcis, light); nin kaḻal – in your divine feet (which is the most enjoyable for nityasūris); porundumā – to attain without separating ever; tirunda – for me to get reformed; nī – you (who would look for jīvātmās to grant refuge); varam sey – should shower your mercy

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has mūlaprakruti, without a boundary, as your possession! Oh, one who has amazing qualities such as jñāna, śakti etc! Oh, one who has lotus-like eyes! Oh, one who has fame which cannot be exhausted even if all the people in the seven worlds keep praising you for many kalpams! You should shower your mercy on me such that I get reformed and attain your divine feet, without ever separating from them, after severing my connection with many births among various species such as celestial etc, with birth in each species being countless, and reach through the path of arcis.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had mercifully confirmed his firmness \[about attaining emperumān through him as the means\]. emperumān tells āḻvār “You had expressed your desire in several ways such as ‘anjalenna vēṇḍumē‘ (92nd pāsuram) and ‘irangu arangavāṇanē’ (93rd pāsuram). Determine what is it that you desire and tell me”. āḻvār prays for the ultimate benefit in this pāsuram saying “You should mercifully sever my connection with samsāram and take me under your divine feet”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**varambu ilāda māya** – Oh one who has under your control, the entity of primordial matter which has basic nature without a boundary and effectual entities such as mahat (great), ahankāram (ego) etc which are countless! The term *māyai* refers to primordial matter. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-7-25 “*mahāntam ca samāvrutya pradānām samupastitam* l *anantasya na tasyānta*: *sankhyānām vāpi vidyatē* ll”  
(the entity of primordial matter surrounds the entity of great; there is no destruction or measurement for that, which is without a boundary), since primordial matter is unbounded, āḻvār distinguishes it by saying “*varambu ilāda*” (without a boundary). Through this verse, it is implied that mūlaprakruti (primordial matter) which binds one to samsāram is under your \[emperumān’s\] control.

**māya** – Oh one who has amazing auspicious qualities such as *jñāna* (knowledge), *śakti* (power) etc. The term *māyā* would refer to any entity which is amazing. In the first instance, when that word was used, it referred to the amazing primordial matter. Here, this word refers to emperumān’s amazing auspicious qualities. The reason for combining the two is: for the jīvātmās, who are bound to that primordial matter and who surrender to you to get that bondage severed, only you have the auspicious qualities such as knowledge, power etc to sever that connection.

**vaiyam ēḻum meymmaiyē varambil ūḻi ēttilum varambilāda kīrttiyāy** – Oh one who has the fame of being the protector whose outer limit cannot be estimated even if all the people in the seven worlds become your vorśippers, knowing their respective servitude, and praise you, based on their respective nature, without engaging with any other entity, for countless kalpams! Through this, it is implied that it is not possible to estimate that fame of protection of yours, when you sever their connection with primordial matter and take them under your divine feet. Has it not been said in mahābhāratam karṇa parvam 83-65 “*varśāyutair yasya guṇā na śakyā vaktum samētairapi sarvalōkai*: l *mahātmana*: *śankhacakrāsipāṇēr viṣṇōr jiṣṇōr vasudēvātmajasya* ll” (one who is the supreme entity, who holds in his divine hands weapons such as the divine conch, disc, sword, who pervades everywhere, who is victorious and who is the son of vasudēvar, whose auspicious qualities cannot be spoken of, even if all the people in all the worlds get together for fourteen thousand years…) and in varāha purāṇam 73-94? “*catur mukhāyur yadi kōtivaktrō bhavēnnara*:*kvāpi viśudda cētā*: l *sa tē guṇānāmayutaikadēśam vadēnna vā dēvavara prasīda* ll” (We do not know if a person would exist with the longevity of many brahmās, has crores of faces, has a very pure heart and whether he would be able to speak one ten-thousandth of your auspicious qualities. Oh, one who is the most distinguished among lords! You should shower your mercy).

emperumān says in his divine mind “It is alright with praising us. Tell us your desire” āḻvār says

**varambilāda palpiṟappaṟuttu** – getting rid of countless births of many types. *pal piṟappu* (Many types of births) refers to births in the species of celestial, animal, human, plant. *varambilāda* (countless numbers in these births) refers to the countless births in each of these four species. Time is immemorial, without a beginning; ātmā is permanent; since the deeds of the ātmā are without any count, the result of those deeds, which is the number of births, is also without a count. āḻvār tells emperumān “Get rid of all those births which are countless”.

**vandu nin kaḻal porundumā** – reaching paramapadam through the path of arcis and uniting with the divine feet of emperumān, which are enjoyed by nityasūris (permanent dwellers of parama padam), without having an identity for the muktātmā (jīvātmā who has got liberated from samsāram), just as it has been mentioned in paramasamhitai ślōkam “*yēna yēna dātā gaccati tēna tēna saha gaccati* l *tadyatā taruṇa vatsā vatsam vatsō vā mātaram* *cāyā vā satvamanugaccēt tatāprakāram* ll” (Just like a cow which has a tender calf follows the calf, just as the calf follows the cow, just as a shadow follows a person, in the same way, (muktātmā) follows parama puruśan (supreme entity) wherever he goes).

**tirunda** – not thinking of anything other than your divine feet and considering only the ecstasy of carrying out service to you, just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 8-12-3 “*nōpajanam smarannidam śarīram*” (the muktātmā \[who has been liberated from samsāram\] does not even think of his \[just left behind\] physical form which is lying near his dear ones).

**nī varam sey** – just as this āḻvār had said in nāṇmugan tiruvandādi pāsuram 60 “*āḷ pārttu uḻi taruvāy*” ((emperumān is) going out in search of someone who would be willing to be his servitor), you, who had been looking for me since time immemorial, should shower your mercy on me such that I could get uplifted.

**puṇḍarīganē** – just as nammāḻvār had mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-1 “*sendāmarait tadam kaṇ senganivāy sengamalam sendāmarai adikkaḷ*” (your wide eyes are like lotus, your reddish fruit-like mouth is lotus and your divine feet are like lotus), this āḻvār says “Oh one who has several limbs which are like lotus!”. Alternatively, we could consider this as “*puṇḍarīkākśanē*” in which case it would refer to emperumān’s lotus-like eyes since the divine eyes fill out the entire divine form. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 5 says “*vijñānam yajñam tanutē karmāṇi tanutēpi ca*” (jīvan, who is referred to as jñānam (knowledge) since he is full of jñānam, carries out yajñams; he also carries out karmas). Since he is filled with jñānam, jīvan is referred to as jñānam. Since āḻvār had said “*varam sey*“, praying for emperumān’s mercy, and then calls him as *puṇḍarīganē*, the opinion is that glancing at āḻvār with his divine eyes is in itself, equivalent to showering his mercy on āḻvār.

Next, we will take up the 97<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
