## 97. Veyyavali

*veyya vāḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndu sīrkkaiya! seyya pōdil mādu sēru mārba! nādanē!aiyilāya ākkai nōy aṟuttu vandu ninnadainduuyvadōr ubāyam nī enakku nalga vēṇḍumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veyya – being cruel (towards enemies); āzi – the divine disc; śangu – śrī pāncajanyam, the divine conch; taṇḍu – śrī kaumōdakī, the divine mace; villum – śrī śārngam, the divine bow; vāḷum – śrī nandakam, the divine sword, all these; ēndu – holding; sīr kaiya – oh one who has divine hands!; seyya – being reddish; pōdil – being in the lotus flower; mādu – periya pirāttiyār; sērum – residing permanently; mārba – oh one who has divine chest!; nādanē – oh one who is the lord for all!; aiyil āya – having phlegm as its nature; ākkai – called as the body; nōy – disease; aṟuttu – destroying it; vandu – coming (to parama padam); nin adaindu – reaching you (who are to be attained); uyvadōr ubāyam – a means in order to carry out service and get uplifted; nī – you (the owner); nalga vēṇḍum – should grant, mercifully

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has the divine hands in which you are holding the divine weapons of disc, conch, bow, mace and sword, which are cruel (towards enemies)! Oh, one who has the divine chest in which periya pirāttiyār, who lives in a reddish lotus flower, resides permanently! Oh, one who is the lord to everyone! You, my owner, should grant me a means mercifully such that I am able to destroy this body which carries diseases like phlegm as its nature and reach you so that I could carry out service to you and get uplifted.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “You said ‘varam sey puṇḍarīganē’ in the previous pāsuram, asking me to shower mercy on you, without any reason. In order to shower my distinguished mercy on you so that you could get uplifted, should you not have a means with you?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram, saying “periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi) has mercifully taken residence in your divine abode. You are mercifully holding the five divine weapons in your divine hands. Should I have any means within me, in order to get rid of my enemies and to attain you? If you are going to make it compulsory \[that I should have a means within me to attain you\], you should mercifully grant me a means such that I could get rid of my hurdles, attain your divine feet and carry out service to you”. āḻvār says that when he has nothing in him and is looking up to emperumān for everything, just as emperumān grants him the ultimate benefit of carrying out kainkaryam, he himself should also grant him the means since āḻvār has nothing in him.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**veyya vāḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndu sīrk kaiya** – Oh one who permanently holds the divine weapons of disc, conch, bow, mace and sword, which are such cruel that enemies will fall down trembling on looking at them, and which you hold in your divine hands due to your affection towards your followers! The term *sīr* denotes motherly forbearance towards followers. Alternatively, it could be taken as an adjective for the divine hand, conveying the meaning “Even if they are mere hands without holding anything, they are such beautiful that one has to ward off evil spirits; in such hands, you are holding these divine weapons!” Do you have enemies who are to be destroyed, for which you are holding these weapons permanently? āḻvār says “āren’t you holding them only in order to destroy my enemies?” It is only to destroy sins which are the enemies of his followers that he is holding the divine weapons always, just as nammāḻvār too had mentioned in his periya tiruvandādi 87 “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān nammēl vinai kadivān*” (emperumān holds the divine disc permanently in his divine hand in order to destroy our sins).

**seyya pōdil mādu sēru mārba** – since periya pirāttiyār was born in the reddish lotus, she has a tender nature; since she is reddish and adds beautiful contrast to the dark bluish divine complexion of emperumān, she is like an ornament to his divine chest. Just as nammāḻvār has said in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum enṟu*” (Saying “I will not separate from the divine chest even for a moment”), oh one who holds periya pirāttiyār always on your divine chest! Through this series of verses, it is indicated that when periya pirāttiyār, who implores you to show patience towards sinners, when the divine weapons which destroy the sins which are hurdles in attaining emperumān, are present with emperumān, just like children will not suffer in the presence of their parents, will I (āḻvār) suffer due to connection with my physical form?

**nādanē** – Oh, one who is the svāmy for all! Through this term it is implied that “Even if periya pirāttiyār does not carry out *puruśakāram* (recommendatory role), even if you do not hold these weapons which destroy my enemies, in your hands, you should protect me merely by seeing me as your possession; when the possession is in distress, could the possessor remain quiet?”

āḻvār says further that beyond these, if you (emperumān) consider compulsively that I should have a means with me in order to attain you, you should grant me that means, mercifully.

**aiyilāya ākkai nōy aṟuttu** – destroying the disease of \[my\] physical form which has phlegm as its nature. ḍoesn’t the physical form, which those who think that “physical form alone is ātmā \[and that there is no entity which exists, called as ātmā\]”, become a disease for those who attain knowledge \[about emperumān\]. Hasn’t tirumaḻisaippirān \[this āḻvār\] mercifully mentioned in nānmugan tiruvandādi 79 “*ēynda tam mey kundamāgavē virumbuvar*” ((those who are desirous of attaining śrī vaikuṇṭam) will consider their physical forms, which are united with their souls, as diseases only)?

**vandu ninnadaindu** – reaching paramapadam and attaining you, who are with unmatched sweetness.

**uyvadōr ubāyam** – a means for me to carry out service and get uplifted

**nī enakku nalga vēṇḍumē** – you, who are the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, who are holding the divine weapons, who are having the capability to grant me whatever I do not have, should mercifully grant me who is looking up to you for everything. The term *nalgudal* refers to granting something without any reason, without expecting anything \[in the hands of the receiver\].

Next, we will take up the 98<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 98. Maram turandu

*maṟanduṟandu vanjamāṟṟi aimbulangaḷāsaiyumtuṟandu ninkaṇ āsaiyē todarndu ninṟu nāyinēnpiṟandiṟandu pēridarc cuḻikaṇinṟu nīngumāmaṟandidādu maṟṟenakku māya nalga vēṇḍumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māya – Oh, one who has amazing power!; maṟam – anger; tuṟandu – getting rid of; vanjam – deceit; māṟṟi – avoiding; aim pulangaḷ – five sensory organs’; āsaiyum – attachment (towards other matters); tuṟandu – getting rid of; ninkaṇ – in matters related to you; āsaiyē – only devotion; todarndu – desiring; ninṟa nāyinēn – this servitor, who is remaining; piṟandu iṟandu – the events of birth and death; pēridarc cuḻikkaṇinṟum – from the vicious cycle of; nīngumā – the way to get out of; maṟṟu – and kainkaryam (servitude); maṟandidādu – without forgetting (me); enakku – to me (who is incapable); nalga vēṇḍum – you should mercifully shower

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has amazing power! You should mercifully grant me, who is your servitor, kainkaryam (servitude) so that I could get rid of anger, avoid deception, get rid of attachments to other matters, have the desire only for devotion in matters related to you and get out of the vicious cycle of births and deaths.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān tells āḻvār “For me to shower my distinguished glance at you, you should have something in you. Could you compel me to give it myself?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “If I need to have something in me, you should mercifully ensure that I stay away from evil disposition and worldly pursuits due to desire in the parama puruśārtam (ultimate benefit) of kainkaryam and using that kainkaryam as the support, you should mercifully remove me from the great sorrow of samsāram”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṟam tuṟandu** – *maṟam* refers to anger. Its boundary is the inability to tolerate others’ eminence and thinking of harming others; getting rid of this anger.

**vanjam māṟṟi** – giving up deceit; *vanjam* – falsehood. Putting on a façade of being favourable, while behaving unfavourably (like an enemy)

**aim pulangaḷ āsaiyum tuṟandu** – getting rid of attachment of the five sensory organs towards worldly matters. āḻvār had said in the 90<sup>th</sup> pāsuram “*pulangaḷaindum venṟilēn poṟiyilēn*“, being caught up with matters such as śabdam (sound) etc. In this pāsuram, he says that he gave up attachment towards other matters, which is the reason for anger and deceit, with the mercy of emperumān. For those who have developed a taste towards kainkaryam, which is the ultimate benefit, vouldn’t attachment towards other matters happen on their own?

**ninkaṇ āsaiyē todarndu ninṟa** – having desire only in matters related to you. The word *todarnda* refers to relationship, viz. the desire to have love towards emperumān always.

**nāyinēn** – apart from getting out of the state of being unfavourable towards emperumān and having desire in the ultimate benefit \[of servitude\], I have no means within myself and am lowly, like a dog. I am also one who cannot take refuge under anyone else.

**piṟandu iṟandu pēridarc cuḻikkaṇ nīngumā** – getting out of the vicious cycle of repeated births and deaths. The term *piṟandu* is symbolic of the six different states of life namely (1) being born (2) being destroyed (3) changing (4) growing (5) reducing (6) remaining as it is. The term *iṟandu* is symbolic of going to *narakam* (hell) and other places.

**maṟandidādu maṟṟenakku māya nalga vēṇḍumē** – thinking of the hardship that I had undergone in this samsāram, my earlier bad conduct and your quality of having total independence, you should not forget me thinking “Let this person of poor conduct, who has been suffering in samsāram since time immemorial, suffer for some more time”. You should mercifully grant me the ultimate benefit of *kainkaryam* looking at my desire for it, looking at the relationship between you and me, removing the sins which are hurdles and which I cannot get rid of on my own. Through the term *maṟṟu*, āḻvār refers to the ultimate benefit of kainkaryam, which is attained after the hurdles of sins have been removed. The term *māyā* refers to emperumān’s amazing power of severing the vicious cycle of samsāram with alternating births and deaths, just as it has been mentioned in [bhagavad gītā 7-14](https://githa.koyil.org/index.php/7-14/) “*daivī hyēśā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā* l *māmēva yē prapadyantē māyām ētām taranti tē* ll” (This māyai \[prakruti, the primodial matter\], which has been created by me, the lord and which has three guṇas \[of satva, rajas and tamas, viz. noble, passion and ignorance respectively) is difficult to cross over. Those who surrender to me, will be able to cross over this).

Next, we will take up the 99<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 99. Katti nan sey

*kātti nānsey valvinaip payan tanāl manandanainātti vaittu nallavalla seyya eṇṇinār enakkēttadanṟi ennadāvi pinnai kēḷva ninnodumpūtti vaitta ennai ninnuḷ neekkal pūvai vaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pinnai kēḷva – Oh kaṇṇa, who is dear to nappinnaip pirātti!; pūvai vaṇṇanē – Oh one who has the complexion of bilberry flower (purple black)!; ennadu āvi – my ātmā; ninnodum – with you; pūtti vaitta ennai – me, who has tied it (such that it cannot be separated); nān sey – carried out by me; valvinai – strong, sinful deeds; kātti – showing (to me); payanṟanāl – with the result of those sinful deeds; manandanai – my heart; nātti vaittu – making me to accept saying “This has to be experienced”; nalla alla – cruel deeds which are not at all good; seyya eṇṇinār – (the messengers of yama, the lord of righteousness) are desirous of carrying out; enak kēttadu – which has been heard of (in śāstrams, the sacred texts); anṟi – removing them; ninnuḷ neekkal – you should not remove \[me\] from you

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is dear to nappinnaip irātti! Oh, one who has a blacking purple complexion! I have bound my soul to your divine feet such that it cannot be separated. I have heard \[in śāstrams\] that the messengers of yama, the lord of righteousness, are desirous of showing me the strong, sinful deeds which I had done, and the results of doing those deeds \[punishments to be meted out to me\], making me accept in my heart that these are to be experienced. You should remove these punitive measures and not remove me from you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān tells āḻvār “After you developed a taste in kainkaryam (servitude) which is the ultimate goal, you had severed your engagement with worldly pursuits. You say that this is the means available with you. Since time immemorial, you have carried out sinful deeds, which would lead to experiencing hardships in narakam (hell). What have you thought of as the way for getting rid of those?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “’Just as you had said in bhagavat gītā 18-66 ‘sarvadarmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja l aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca: ll’ (Giving up all righteous paths (which are considered as means), along with their impressions, attain me alone as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins; do not grieve), I have surrendered to your divine feet, with the puruśakāram (recommendatory role) of nappinnaip pirātti. I have submitted my protection to you. Other than this, is there any other means? Hence, you should remove my earlier karmas which are cause for attaining narakam and grant me unbroken enjoyment in your divine feet.”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kātti nān sey valvinai** – messengers of yama show my strong, sinful deeds \[to me\]; even if the deeds had been carried out through mind or mouth, they could be forgiven. The implied meaning is that messengers of yama showed me the deeds which I had done with my physical body, which are such strong that their results have to be experienced by going to hell. Before carrying out the punishment, they will ensure that I do not think “Oh, they are punishing me so horribly”

**payanṟanāl manandanai nātti vaittu** – making me understand in my mind properly, thinking “For the deeds done by me, experiencing the punishment is appropriate”

**nallavalla seyya eṇṇinār** – the messengers of yama would have thought of carrying out cruel punitive measures. They will inform the person who has reached hell, about the deeds carried out by the person, create the fortitude in the mind of the person “It is only correct that I have to experience the punishment for the cruel deeds” and only then will they mete out the punitive measures.

**enak kēttadanṟi** – removing such thoughts, which I have heard of from śāstram \[sacred texts\]. The implied meaning is – removing such punitive measures.

**ennadāvi pinnai kēḷva ninnodum pūtti vaitta ennai** – Oh kaṇṇa (kriṣṇa), who has affable qualities such as vātsalyam (motherly forbearance) since you are the dear consort of nappinnaip pirātti! I am full of sinful deeds, without any means in me; you made such a person to offer my soul to you. The implied meaning is that his submitting his soul to emperumān is also because of emperumān only. āḻvār recalls his earlier pāsuram (92) “*kāli āya – nin tanakku adaikkalam pugunda ennai*” (Oh the one who tends to cows, to whom I have submitted myself). *pūtti vaittal* refers to doing just as per what this āḻvār himself had said in pāsuram 95 – “*nī vidakkarudi mey seyādu mikkorāsaiyākkilum – ninnalālor kaṇṇilēn*” (even if you let go of me and make me engage with worldly pursuits again, I have none to protect me other than you).

**ninnuḷ neekkal pūvai vaṇṇanē** – Oh one who has a divine form which is having the tenderness and complexion of a bilberry flower! You should not remove me from you. You should mercifully allow me to enjoy your divine form which is tender like a blossoming flower.

The pāsuram has to be rearranged as *pinnai kēḷva – ennadāvi ninnodum pūtti vaitta ennai – kātti – nān sey valvinaip payanṟanāl manandanai nāttivaittu nalla valla seyya eṇṇinār enak kēttadanṟi – pūvai vaṇṇanē – ninnuḷ neekkal* in order to get the cogent meaning.

Next, we will take up the 100<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 100. Pirappinodu peridar

*piṟappinōdu pēridarc cuḻikkaṇinṟu(m) nīngumahduiṟappa vaitta jñānanīsaraik karaikkodēṟṟumāpeṟaṟkariya ninna pāda pattiyāna pāsanampeṟaṟkariya māyanē enakku nalga vēṇḍumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

peṟaṟkariya māyanē – Oh the amazing entity who cannot be attained (other than through your mercy alone)!; piṟappinōdu – along with birth; pēridar – creating huge sorrow through tāpatrayam (three types of diseases); suḻikkaṇ ninṟum – from the vicious cycle of samsāram; nīngum – the means for removing it; ahdu – (known through vēdams) tatvahitam (real entity, emperumān and the means to; iṟappa vaitta – kept hidden; jñāna nīsarai – those who follow vēdams for other benefits, thinking of themselves, with pride, as wise men; karaikkodu – taking them to the shore; ēṟṟumā – mentioned as means in vēdams, to uplift them; peṟaṟkariya – difficult to attain; ninna pāda – in matters related to your divine feet; pattiyāna – called as devotion; pāsanam – huge opulence; enakku – for me (who doesn’t know anything other than your mercy for everything); nalga vēṇḍum – you should mercifully shower

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the amazing entity, who cannot be attained other than through your mercy alone! tatvam (emperumān, the real entity) and hitam (means to attain that emperumān) are mentioned in vēdams as the means in order to remove the vicious cycle of samsāram through births and the resultant huge sorrow caused by tāpatrayam (diseases). But this is kept hidden by those who follow vēdams for other benefits, who think of themselves, with pride, as wise men. Devotion to your divine feet is the means, which is spoken of in vēdam, to uplift the people and take them to the other shore \[of paramapadam\]. You should mercifully shower that devotion to me, who does not know anything other than your mercy for everything.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār “In order to attain brahmānubhavam (experiencing supreme entity) without any break, should you not have parabhakti?” āḻvār tells him “You should mercifully shower that parabhakti too”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**piṟappinōdu pēridarc cuḻikkaṇ ninṟum nīngum ahdu iṟappa vaitta** – samsāram, through one’s birth, causes three diseases \[*tāpatrayam*\] namely *ādyātmikam* (disease caused by self), *ādidaivikam* (disease caused by emperumān) and *ādibhaudikam* (disease caused by other creations on earth). It creates huge sorrow, repeatedly. t*hatvam* (true entity, otherwise known as emperumān) and *hitam* (means to attain that emperumān) are the means mentioned in vēdams to sever this vicious cycle of births and *tāpatrayam*. But these are kept hidden. The term *ahdu* denotes *tatva hitam* which are famously known. How is the term *iṟappa vaitta* construed as ‘kept hidden’? The term *iṟakkai* refers to naśa (destruction). As per etymology, *naśa* means *adarśanē,* which in turn refers to that which is not shown; in other words, hidden. Thus, *iṟappa vaitta* would mean that which is kept hidden. bhagavat gītā 2-42 and 2-44 say “*yāmimām puśpitām vācam pravadanti avipaścita*: l *vēdavādaratā*: *pārta nānyadastīti vādina*: ll *bhōgaiśvarya prasaktānām tayā’pahruta cētasām* l *vyavasāyātmikā bhuddi*: *samādau na vidīyatē* ll” (Oh arjuna! There are some people with lowly knowledge who are involved with attaining *svargam* (heaven) etc which are mentioned in vēdams and who say that there is no benefit other than these which is mentioned in vēdams; The words of these people will be like flowers (enjoyments and opulence) which will never yield fruit. Those who listen to such lowly people will have their knowledge blunted, they will involve with enjoyments and opulence alone and they will never have firmness in their minds and intelligence). As mentioned in these two ślōkas, there are people who are conversant with vēdams, who will say that vēdams speak only about *svargam* etc and the means to attain such benefits. They will hide the entities of *tatvam* and *hitam*.

**jñāna nīsarai**– these are people who are truly in a low state but think, with pride, that they are omniscient. In his 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam, āḻvār had referred to *“yārum vaiyagattu onṟininṟu vāḻdalinmai kaṇḍum nīsar”* (even after seeing that no one lives forever in this world, these lowly people think that they will live forever), āḻvār had referred to those who do not know about the nature of this world as *nīsar*. In this pāsuram he says that even after knowing the temporary nature of this world, since they desire *svargam* etc which are lowly and transient benefits, āḻvār refers to them as *jñāna nīsar*.

**karaikkodēṟṟumā** – as the means to take them to the other shore and uplift them. Just as it has been said in kata upaniśat 1-3-9 “*vijñāna sāratir yastu manāpragrahavān nara*: l  
*sōdvana*: *pāramāpnōti tadviṣṇō*: *paramam padam* ll” (One who has knowledge as charioteer and mind as the controlling rein, will attain viṣṇu’s parama padam which is on the other side), the other side of samsāram is only *bhagavat viśayam* (matters related to emperumān). upaniśats have mentioned this as the means to attain that parama padam.

**peṟaṟkariya ninna pāda pattiyāna pāsanam** – parama bhakti, which is like opulence, which is holding on to your divine feet, and which is very difficult to attain. More than attaining emperumān, attaining the state of parama bhakti which is deeply attached to him, is more difficult. Hasn’t the greatness of devotion been mention in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-20-19 “*yā prītir avivēkānām viśayēśvanapāyinī* l *tvāmanusmarata*: *sā mē hrudayāt māmasarpatu* ll” (Let that indestructible affection which those, who do not have the knowledge of discrimination \[between the correct and incorrect\] have towards worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc, not leave my mind which is always meditating on matters related to you), and in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-20-27? “*darmārtakāmai*: *kim tasya muktistasya karē stitā* l *samasta jagatām mūlē yasya bhakti*: *stirā tvayi* ll” (To one who has unwavering devotion towards you, who are the causative factor for the worlds, what is the purpose of benefits such as *darmam* (righteousness), *artam* (opulence) and *kāmam* (pleasures)? He has the ultimate benefit of *mōkśam* (parama padam) itself with him)

**pāsanam** – ship. Here, symbolically it refers to the opulence present in a ship. Since opulence, as a means, is capable of achieving anything, it comes in the meaning of “means” here. Hasn’t poygai āḻvār too called devotion as wealth in his mudal tiruvandādi pāsuram 43 *danamāya tanē kaikūdum* (the wealth of kainkaryam to emperumān will happen on its own)? Did not upaniśats instruct that devotion is the means to attain your divine feet, as in kata upaniśat 1-2-23 “*nāyamātmā pravacanē na labhyō na mēdayā na bahūnā śrutēna* l *yamēvaiśa vruṇutē tēna labhyas tasyaiśa ātmā vivruṇutē tanūm svām* ll” (This supreme entity cannot be attained by hearing, by thinking or by meditation (devoid of devotion). To that person, who the supreme entity draws towards him, he manifests his auspicious divine form) and in tattirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 1-10? “*na sandruśē tiśtati rūpamasya na cakśuśā paśyati kaścanainam* l *hrudā manīśā manasā’bhikluptō ya ēnam viduramrutāstē bhavanti* ll” (The divine auspicious form of this supreme entity is not manifested for us too. None sees him through his eyes; only through devotion and unwavering mind is he thought of. Only those who know him, become muktas (get liberated from samsāram)). We have to add the words ‘mercifully grant’ that devotion to this.

**peṟaṟkariya māyanē** – Oh the amazing great entity who cannot be attained other than through your causeless mercy!

**enakku nalga vēṇḍumē** – you should grant me that devotion which is difficult to attain, through your causeless mercy. For those *jñānanīsar* (lowly people who have knowledge) who desire other benefits, you ordain them to carry out rituals such as *śyēna yāgam* (ritual to get rid of enemies), *jyōtistōma yāgam* (ritual to attain heaven) etc, gradually building up taste in them to ultimately desire *mōkśam* (parama padam). Even when they attain \[mōkśam\], it will be for their enjoyment and happiness. For that *svārta mōkśam* (liberation for one’s happiness \[and not for emperumān’s happiness\]), you have imposed devotion as the means. For me, who has no means other than you, who desires only your happiness while carrying out *kainkaryam* and not my happiness, you should mercifully grant that devotion which will help in enjoying you, since that will bring happiness to you.

Next, we will take up the 101<sup>st</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 101. Irandu uraippadu

*irandu uraippadu uṇḍu vāḻi ēmanīr niṟattamāvaram tarum tirukkuṟippil vaittadāgil mannusīrpparanda sindai onṟi ninṟu ninna pāda pangayamnirandaram ninaippadāga nī ninaikka vēṇḍumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ēma nīr – like a huge ocean; niṟattu – having a complexion; a(m)mā – oh one who is a relative to me!; irandu – praying to you; uraippadu uṇḍu – there is something which (I) have to appeal to you; vāḻi – may you live long!; varam tarum – bestowing benefits to this ātmā, as your svabhāvam (basic nature); tirukkuṟippil – in the divine mind; mannu sīr – firm benefit; vaittadāgil – if you determine (to give me); paranda – going after worldly pursuits; sindai – in my heart; onṟi ninṟu – staying firm (and not going after other pursuits); ninna pāda pangayam – your divine lotus-like feet; nirandaram – without any separation; ninaippadāga – to think; nī ninaikka vēṇḍum – you should think in your divine mind

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is having the complexion of a huge ocean and who is related to me! There is something which I have to tell you, as my prayer. If you determine in your divine mind to bestow benefits to this ātmā, then you should think in your divine mind that I should stop going after other pursuits and stay firm with your divine feet, without separating from them ever.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār “īsn’t the paramabhakti, which you are requesting us to bestow on you, attained by continuously meditating on us with sensory organs, which have been removed from worldly pursuits?” āḻvār says “In that case, you have to think in your divine mind that I should overcome my sensory organs and meditate on you without a break”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**irandu uraipopadu uṇḍu** – śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 124-19 says “*tam tu mē pradānam draśtum bharatam tvaratē mana*: l *mām nivartayitum yō’sau citrakūtamupāgata*: ll *śirasā yācatastasya vacanam na krutam mayā* ll” (My mind desires to look at my brother bharatan who had come to citrakūtam in order to take me back \[to ayōdyā\]. I could not carry out what he had prayed to me for, with his head). Just as bharatan had done at citrakūtam, I wish to get rid of all my pride, fall at your divine feet with my head and pray to you for a matter. emperumān is the ordained lord; while he is requesting for meditation without any break, on him, which is in line with his basic nature, the reason for āḻvār to feel shy is this – he is looking at the eminence of emperumān and his \[āḻvār’s\] poor conduct since time immemorial. If he thinks of his earlier poor conduct, he should feel shy to approach him. Even if he approaches, looking at the eminence of emperumān, he should feel shy thinking “Will such an eminent person reject my request?”

**vāḻi** – we could consider this as āḻvār praising emperumān in order for emperumān to glance at him mercifully or looking at the beauty of emperumān which is what he has to meditate upon, without a break.

**ēmanīr niṟattamā** – one who is having the complexion of the huge ocean, just as it had been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “*khānitas sagarēṇāyam apramēyō mahōdadi*: l *kartumarhati rāmasya jñātvā kāryam mahāmati*: ll” (This unfathomable huge ocean was severed by (śrī rāma’s ancestor) king sagara. The king of this ocean, who is a greatly wise person, knowing that this is śrī rāma’s wish, will fulfil it) and who is my natural relative! The term *amā* refers to relative. The word *ammān* has got shortened as *amā*. Has it not been mentioned that emperumān is naturally related to cētanan in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 1-15 “*sa nō bandur janitā sa vidātā dāmāni vēda bhuvanāni viśvā*” (paramātmā, who is the causative factor for the entire universe, is our relative. He alone bestows benefits; he alone grants us the enjoyments, the implements as well as the abode for enjoying those) and in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-12 “*nāham dēvō na gandarvō na yakśō na ca dānava*: l *aham vō bāndavō jātō naitaccintyamitō’nyatā* ll” (I am not a dēvan; not a gandarvan; not a yakśan; not a demon (these are all different types of celestial entities). I am born as your relative; my birth is not to be considered (by you people) in any other manner)?

Thus, until now āḻvār has said that he could not remain without praying to emperumān, that he has a divine form for which he could not remain without wishing him well and that he is a natural relative.

**varam tarum kuṟippil vaittadāgil mannu sīr** – the verse should be rearranged as *mannu sīr varam tarum kuṟippil vaittadāgil* to get cogent meaning. For those who seek benefits appropriate for the svarūpam (basic nature) of the ātmā, emperumān will always grant through his divine mind. Until he does this… The implied meaning is – if emperumān is thinking in his divine mind of granting that which is apt for my svarūpam. The term *sīr* refers to benefit. *mannu sīr* refers to the great kingdom of everlasting kainkaryam*.* Did not kulaśēkhara āḻvār too say in his perumāḷ tirumoḻi 10-7 “*adi sūdum arasu*” (the kingdom of kainkaryam where I don his divine feet on my head)?

Through the last two lines of the pāsuram, āḻvār is requesting for uninterrupted meditation, which is the reason for getting this kingdom of kainkaryam.

**paranda sindai onṟi ninṟu** – my mind which keeps going towards worldly pursuits such as *śabdam* (sound), *sparśam* (touch) etc should, instead, stay focussed only on you

**ninna pāda pangayam** – your divine feet which are attracting the mind since they have unbounded sweetness and since they are yours.

**nirandaram ninaippadāga nī ninaikka vēṇḍumē** – you should think in your divine mind that my mind should become capable of thinking only of you, without any break. My thoughts and deeds are without any purpose. The implied meaning is that only your thoughts will shower the benefit to me.

Next, we will take up the 102<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 102. Vilvilada kadalal

*viḷvilāda kādalāl viḷangu pādapōdil vaittuuḷḷuvēnadu ūna nōy oḻikkumā teḻikku nīrpaḷḷi māya panṟiyāya venṟivīra kunṟināltuḷḷu nīr varambu seydu tōnṟal onṟu sollidē*

**Пословный перевод:**

teḻikku – being uproarious; nīr – in the ocean; paḷḷi – reclining; māya – Oh one who is with amazing qualities such as simplicity etc!; panṟiyāya – incarnated as a wild boar; venṟi vīra – the victorious warrior!; kunṟināl – with mountains; tuḷḷu nīr – in the bouncing ocean; varambu seyda – one who built a dam; tōnṟal – Oh king!; viḷvu – getting separated; ilāda – not present; kādalāl – with affection; viḷangu – shining; pāda pōdil – in the divine feet which are like lotus; vaittu – keeping (my heart); uḷḷuvēnadu – I who am thinking (that those divine feet are the refuge); ūna nōy – the disease of connection with physical form which creates lowliness; oḻikkumā – the time when it will be severed; onṟu sollidē – you should mercifully say a word

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is reclining in the uproarious ocean with amazing qualities such as simplicity etc! Oh, the victorious warrior who incarnated as the wild boar! Oh, king who built a dam with mountains in the bouncing ocean! I have kept my heart on your divine feet which are like lotus and which are shining, with an inseparable affection. You should tell me a word about when you will sever my connection with my physical form which is like a disease, causing lowliness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār “In order to reach the stage of continuous meditation, without any break, connection with physical body has to be severed” āḻvār says “I have developed taste in kainkaryam; you are omnipotent; you should tell me a word that you will do this activity at this time in this way so that I could sustain myself until you sever the connection with this body which keeps following”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**viḷvilāda kādalāl** – carrying out affection to your divine feet, considering you alone as the benefit instead of leaving them and going after other benefits. ḍidn’t nammāḻvār too say in tiruvāimoḻi 1-6-5 “*viḷgai viḷḷāmai virumbi*” (desiring those who are always with him, considering him as the benefit instead of going after other benefits)?

**viḷangu pāda pōdil vaittu** – keeping the heart at the divine, lotus-like feet of emperumān, which are shining with that affection. emperumān’s divine feet blossom in the presence of followers who do not desire any other benefit. Through these two verses, āḻvār is asking “Is the connection with my physical body continuing because I consider other benefits or because you do not have affection towards your followers?”

**uḷḷuvēnadu ūna nōy oḻikkumā** – if you had thought of removing the disease of my connection with the physical form which is creating lowliness for me, when I think that those two divine feet alone are my refuge. āḻvār considers ignorance about the basic nature of jīvātmā (soul) and paramātmā (emperumān) and deficiency in enjoying emperumān as lowliness. These are caused by connection with physical form. In āḻvār’s thoughts, physical form and disease are synonyms. Through this verse, āḻvār is asking “Am I losing out because I am considering anything other than you as my refuge or because I have attachment towards my physical form?”

**teḻikku nīrp paḷḷi māya** – Oh one who is reclining in the ocean, which is uproarious because of connection with you, with amazing qualities such as simplicity, affableness etc! *teḻiikkai* – roaring.

**panṟiyāya venṟi vīra** – this abode of samsāram is not appropriate for your eminence. Yet, apart from reclining in the ocean for showering your mercy on your followers, you incarnated as a wild boar which is not shy of water and mud. Oh, the victorious warrior who dug out earth, during your incarnation as boar, defeating the monster hiraṇyākśan!

**kunṟināl tuḷḷu nīr varambu seyda tōnṟal** – ocean throws out things which it has within itself, due to its strong and bouncing waves. Oh, the divine son of emperor daśaratan, who built a dam across that ocean with mountains which will immerse in water! *tōnṟal* – king. The implied meaning in this is – while you have parama padam as your abode, for the sake of your followers, you recline in tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) manifesting your auspicious qualities of simplicity; you incarnate as a wild boar and lift earth which had been lost, defeating the enemies of your followers; you carry out activities which are beyond the capabilities of human race even when you incarnate as śrī rāma, in the human species; while you have no deficiency in your qualities of simplicity and omnipotence, do I need to have any means within me for you to sever my connection with this physical form?

**onṟu sollidē** – you are without any deficiency in your auspicious qualities. I am incapable of carrying out my activities. When this is the case, this continuity of my physical form is only because you have not thought of this in your divine mind. Until you decide in your divine mind about me, you should mercifully tell me a word “We will do this act, in this manner, at this time” so that I could sustain myself. This is the only request that I seek from you.

The pāsuram has to be read as *teḻikku nīrp paḷḷi māya, panṟiyāya venṟi vīra, kunṟināl tuḷḷu nīr varambu seyda tōnṟal, viḷvilāda kādalāl viḷangu pādapōdil vaittu, uḷḷuvēnadu ūnanōy oḻikkumā – onṟu sollidē* to get cogent meanings.

Next, we will take up the 103<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 103. Tirukkalandu serumarba

*tirukkalandu sēru mārba dēva dēva dēvanēirukkalanda vēda nīdiyāgi ninṟa ninmalākarukkalanda kāḷamēga mēniyāya nin peyarurukkalandu oḻivilādu uraikkumāṟu urai seyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiru – periya pirāttiyār (śrī mahālakśmi); kalandu – being together; sērum – residing permanently; mārba – Oh, one who has the divine chest!; dēva dēva dēvanē – Oh the lord of nityasūris, who are apt to be attained by celestial entities such as even brahmā et al!; irukkalanda vēda nīdiyāgi ninṟa – having as your nature, whatever has been mentioned in rig vēdam; ninmalā – Oh one who is without any impurity!; karu – along with gold; kalanda – mixed; kāḷamēga – like cloud; mēni – having divine form; āya – Oh kaṇṇa, the covherd; nin peyar – your divine names; urukkalandu – along with your divine form; oḻivilādu – without any break; uraikkumāṟu – the way to recite; uraisey – you mercifully say

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who has the divine chest where periya pirāttiyār is residing permanently! Oh, the lord of nityasūris, who are apt to be attained by celestial entities such as even brahmā et al! Oh, one who has as your nature, whatever has been mentioned in rig vēdam and who is without any impurity! Oh kaṇṇa, the covherd, who has a divine form like dark cloud laced with gold! You should mercifully tell me the way by which I could recite, without a break, your divine names, along with your divine form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār was anxious in the previous pāsuram, requesting emperumān to destroy the disease of connection with his physical form. emperumān says “We will sever the connection at the end of this birth. Why are you anxious?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “In that case, you should mercifully show me a way by which I would be able to meditate upon and recite your divine names which speak about your greatness, your simplicity and the beauty of your divine form”.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tirukkalandu sēru mārba** – oh one who has the divine chest in which periya pirāttiyār is residing and enjoying, doubting whether there will be any shortcoming in that experience, recalling continuously, just as nammāḻvār had said in his tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum*” (I will not separate from this divine chest even for a moment)! She is the head for all the people in all the worlds without any distinction among nityasūris (permanent dwellers of parama padam), muktātmās (those who had got liberated from samsāram and have attained parama padam) and badda jīvātmās (those who are still bound to samsāram), just as it has been said in śrī sūktam 9 “*īśvarīm sarvabhūtānām tvāmihōpahvayē śriyam*” (Oh śrīdēvi! I invite you, the controller of all creations). Your \[emperumān’s\] greatness is such that śrīdēvi, who is the head for all, desires you constantly.

**dēva dēva dēvanē** – just as it has been mentioned in the ślōkam in puruśa sūktam “*tēha nākam mahimānas sacantē yatra pūrvē sādyās santi dēvā*:” (in which paramapadam the sādyās, who have been present since time immemorial, are residing, they make that paramapadam great), nityasūris are such that they are apt to be attained by celestials who desire to attain mōkśam. Oh, one who is apt to be enjoyed by such nityasūris and who controls them! The three syllables \[of *dēva*\] refer to celestial entities such as brahma et al, nitya sūris and emperumān, respectively. The implied meaning is that he guides nityasūris who have as their pastime, carrying out kainkaryam constantly to emperumān and periya pirāttiyār.

**irukkalanda vēdanīdi āgi ninṟa ninmalā** – Oh, one who is mentioned by vēdams which have riks (hymns) in abundance and who is the exact opposite of all that is lowly! The term *nīdi* refers to good conduct and to one’s nature. You have the greatness of being the consort of śrī mahālakśmi just as it has been said in puruśa sūktam 2-5 *hrīśca tē lakśmīśca patnyau* (Oh supreme entity! śrī dēvi and bhūmi dēvi are your consorts); you are dear to nityasūris just as it has been mentioned in rig vēdam – viṣṇu sūktam “*tad viṣṇō*: *paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya*: l *divīva cakśurātatam* ll *tad viprāsō vipanyavō jāgruvāmsas samindatē viṣṇōr yat paramam padam* ll” (nityasūris constantly keep looking at that superior abode of viṣṇu. That supreme abode is effulgent like sūriyan (sun) who reveals everything, who is in the sky, who is like the eyes of the world. In that paramapadam, distinguished wise people, people who keep constantly vorśipping, ever alert nityasūris shine brightly) and you are the opposite of lowliness  just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 8-7-1 “*ya ātmā apahatapāpmā vijarō vimrutyur viśōkō vijighatśō‘pipāsas satyakāmas satyasankalpas sōnvēśtavya*:” (That ātmā who is without sins, without old age, without death, without sorrow, without hunger, without thirst, without desire unfulfilled and who has solemn vow, is apt to be searched for). vēdams show all these greatness of yours. That you are mentioned by the vēdams is clear from the three verses “*tirukkalandu sēr mārba*”*,* “*dēvadēva dēvanē*” and “*irukkalanda …. ninmalā*”

**karukkalanda kāḷamēga mēniyāya** – Oh kaṇṇa, who incarnated in the clan of covherds, who has a divine form which is like the dark cloud, mixed with gold! The word *karu* refers to gold. Just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*tasya madyē vahniśikā aṇīyōrdvā vyavastita*: l *nīlatōyadamadyastā vidyullēkēva bhāsvarā* ll” ((paramātmā’s divine auspicious form) agniśikhā is present amidst that lotus-like heart, with a minute form, looking upwards; it shines like lightning which has imbibed bluish cloud), it has been indicated here that his divine form has the splendour of lightning and is like a bluish cloud. The term *āya* (as in *mēniyāya*) refers to the simplicity of his incarnation, just as it has been said in puruśa sūktam uttarānuvākam “*ajāyamāno bahudā vijāyatē tasya dīrā*: *parijānanti yōnim*” (though (paramapuruśan, the supreme entity) is without a birth, he incarnates in many ways. Only the learned people will know his incarnations). The word *karu* will also refer to womb. In this case, *karukkalanda kāḷamēgam* will refer to a dark cloud which has water as its womb. It is pertinent to recall śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkintā kāṇḍam 28-3 ślōkam “*navamāsa drutam garbham bhāskarasya gabhastibhi*: l *pītvā rasam samudrāṇām dyau*: *prasūtē rasāyanam* ll” (sky, which is like a woman, drank water from the ocean through the rays of sun, held that water in her womb for nine months and let out that water, giving birth to a child which is the life-giving medicine called as water). Through this verse it is indicated that emperumān, in order to remove the distress of his followers, incarnated as kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) with a divine form, attracting their hearts.

**nin peyar urukkalandu oḻivilādu uraikkumāṟu uraiseyē** – you should mercifully show me a way such that I could recite, without a break, your divine names which have been mentioned in the faultless authentic text of vēdams as “the consort of periya pirāttiyār, as the lord of nityasūris who are indefatigable, the opposite of all lowliness, one who is with a divine form which is *aprākruta* (not made of primordial matter), one who is simple towards his followers etc”

Next, we will take up the 104<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 104. Kadum kavandan

*kadum kavandan – te cruel \[demon\] kabandanvakkaran – dantavakrankaran – karanmuran – muran, all tese demonssiram avai – teir headsidandu – breaking temkūṟu seyda – made \[teir bodies\] into piecespal – of many typespadai – veaponstadam – cool, like a pondkai – having \[tose veapons\] in your divine handsmāyanē – ōh one vho has amazing povers!kidandu – recliningirundu – sittingninṟu – standingiyangu – roaming aroundpōdu – during tose timesninna – yourspoṟkaḻal – divine feet vhic are desired like goldtodarndu – desiring temviḷvilādadu – vitout any intervalōr todarcci – te state of meditationnalgavēṇḍum – you śould mercifully do (for me)*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who has amazing powers and hold many types of divine weapons in your hands which are like cool ponds! You severed the heads of demons such as kabandan, dantavakran, karan, muran et al, breaking their bodies into pieces. You should mercifully grant me the benefit of meditating on your divine feet which are desired like gold, during the times whether we are reclining or sitting or standing or roaming around.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār requested emperumān to tell him a way by which he could continuously recite emperumān’s divine names. emperumān tells āḻvār in his divine mind “I have already shown in bhagavat gītā the way to follow karmas (rituals) with sātvika tyāgam (giving up results etc) just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 9-27 “yat karōśi yad aśnāsi yajjuhōśi dadāsi yat l yattapasyasi kauntēya tat kuruśva madarpaṇam ll” (Oh arjuna! Whichever deed you perform (to live in this world), whichever food you take (to survive), whichever rituals, donations or penance you carry out (which are based on vēdas), dedicate those to me) and in bhagavat gītā 4-28 “dravya yajñās tapōyajñā yōgayajñās tatā’parē l svādyāya yajñāna yajñāśca yata:samśitavratā ll” (There are people who earn money and carry out rituals with materials (such as vorśipping celestials, donation, yajñas (a type of ritual), hōmam (another type of ritual); there are people who carry out ritual based on penance (such as krucra, cāndrāyaṇa etc); there are people who carry out rituals of yōga such as going on pilgrimage, residing in divine abodes; there are people who perform svādyāya rituals such as learning vēdams; there are people who carry out jñāna yajñam such as discussing about vēdāntam, the end portions of vēdams which discuss about emperumān. Thus, those who are firmly engaged with karmayōgam (path of deeds), will keep doing various yajñams (rituals) with firm belief) so that sins, which are hurdles in enjoying me, will disappear. I have also said that after such sins are removed, one could purposefully spend time in reciting my divine names, vorśipping me, carrying out kainkaryam to me etc, just as in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 9-14 “satatam kīrtayantō mām yatantaśca drudavrutā: l namasyantaśca mām bhaktyā nitya yuktā upāsatē ll” (Those who constantly recite my divine names, who are firm in carrying out kainkaryam to me and who worship me, desire to be with me always and carry out devotion to me). You could follow those methods“. āḻvār says in this pāsuram “When you are capable of removing my hurdles, should I carry out karmayōgam and remove the hurdles? You should remove the hurdles and enable me to carry out reciting your divine names without any break and to enjoy you”. kadum kavandan vakkaran karan muran siram avaiidandu kūṟu seyda pal padait tadakkai māyanēkidandu irundu ninṟu iyangu pōdu ninna poṟkaḻaltodarndu viḷvilādadōr todarcci nalgavēṇḍumē ‘Word-by-Word Meaning*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kadum kavandan vakkaran karan muran siram avai** – the heads of demons such as cruel kabandan, dantavakran, karan, muran et al. The reason for qualifying kabandan alone as cruel is due to the fact that when śrī rāma was searching for sītāp pirātti, after being separated from her, in water bodies, forests, sky etc, this demon came to wage a war with perumāḷ. Feeling disgusted with that action, āḻvār calls him as cruel.

**idandu kūṟu seyda** – split their heads. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍm 94-22 “*cinnam bhinnam śarair dagdam prabhagnam śastra pīditam* l  
*balam rāmēṇa dadruśur na rāmam śīgrakāriṇam* ll” (Everyone saw the army of demons being pierced by arrows, split, burnt and tortured. None saw rāma who was carrying out the deed at such a fast pace) one who cut the bodies of enemies into pieces. He indicates that just as emperumān had destroyed the demons and monsters, he should destroy his hurdles of sins too.

**pal padait tadakkai māyanē** – Oh one who has amazing power, holding many types of weapons in your divine hands to destroy the demons, with which \[the divine hands\] you stroke your followers in order to remove their anguish! īsn’t it amazing that the same weapons which destroy the demons, also nourish the followers! The indication is ta t in order to remove my enemies, you do not have to hold weapons. It is enough if you take pity and show your mercy on me. The term *tadakkai* is a combination of *tadam* (cool) and *kai* (hand); *tadam* refers to water body, thus his divine hand is cool, like a water body. just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-17-28 “*apyēśa pruśtē mama hasta padmam kariśyati śrīmad anantamūrti*: l *yasyānguḷi sparśa hatākilāgai*: *avāpyatē siddirapāstatōśā* ll” (All the sins in people are removed through contact with the divine fingers of the divine hands of emperumān and they attain mōkśam (śrī vaikuṇṭam). Will kriṣṇa, whose form is without an end, keep his divine lotus-like hands on my back and shower his mercy on me?) and in line with nammāḻvār’s tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram 10-3-5 “*aṇimigu tāmaraik kaiyai andō adicciyōm talaimisai nī aṇiyāy*” (you should keep your divine, lotus-like hand on our heads, we are your servitors), isn’t his hand one which makes the followers comfortable by stroking them gently?

emperumān asks āḻvār “Yet, what is it that you want us to do?” āḻvār responds further …

**kidandu irundu ninṟu iyangu pōdum** – in whichever state we are in, whether reclining or sitting or standing or roaming, as part of living our lives in samsāram, just as it has been said in nāradīyam “*āsīnā vā śayānā vā tiśtantōyatra kutra vā* l *namō nārāyaṇāyēti mantraika śaraṇā vayam* ll” (We are those who hold on to the divine name of nārāyaṇa as the means, whether we are sitting or sleeping or standing somewhere) …

**ninna poṟkaḻal** – the desirable divine feet of yours, our lord, appropriate for my nature. The term *poṟkaḻal* refers to desirable divine feet, since his feet are desirable like gold. The divine feet are such that they cannot be let go of, even while living in samsāram.

**todarndu viḷvilādadōr todarcci nalga vēṇḍumē** – you should mercifully grant me the nature of coming forward on my own and continuously enjoying \[your divine feet\]. *todarcci* – the nature of enjoying abundantly. Through this pāsuram, āḻvār has mercifully said “You should remove my hurdles in enjoying you; you should also make me to continuously enjoy your divine feet, which are apt to be desired at all times, without any break”

Next, we will take up the 105<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 105. Mannai undu

*maṇṇai uṇḍu umiḻndu pin irandukoṇḍu aḷandu maṇkaṇṇuḷ alladu illai enṟu venṟa kālam āyināypaṇṇai venṟa insol mangai kongai tangu pangayakkaṇṇa! ninna vaṇṇam alladu illai eṇṇum vaṇṇamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇ kaṇṇuḷ alladu illai enṟu – thinking “This world will not sustain unless it comes under the glance of my eyes”; maṇṇai – earth; uṇḍu – (during deluge) swallowed; umiḻndu – (during creation) let it out; pin – later; irandu koṇḍu – got it back through alms (as vāmana, from mahābali); aḷandu – measured it (as trivikrama); venṟa – won over everyone; kālamāyināy – On one who has time as your wealth!; paṇṇai – tune; venṟa – winning over; insol – having sweet speech; mangai – youthful periya pirāttiyār’s; kongai – on the bosom; tangu – one who resides; pangayak kaṇṇa – Oh puṇḍarīkākśa (one with lotus-like eyes)!; eṇṇum vaṇṇam – the reason for my desire to enjoy your divine form; ninna vaṇṇam alladillai – nothing other than the divine beauty of your form

**Перевод:**

**During the time of deluge, thinking “This world will not sustain itself unless it comes under the glance of my eyes”, you swallowed it; you let it out (during creation); later, you retrieved it (as vāmana, from mahābali) and measured it (as trivikrama). Oh, one who won over the entity of time since it is your wealth! Oh, one who is with lotus-like eyes, who resides on the bosom of the youthful periya pirāttiyār who has sweet speech which is sweeter than tunes! The reason for my desire to enjoy your divine form is none other than the divine beauty of your form.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “What is the reason for not engaging with the means that I had mentioned and, instead of thinking with patience ‘Let emperumān give the benefit when he deems fit’ why do you urge me to give the complete enjoyment speedily?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “Since I wish to take refuge under you, who are having auspicious qualities, with your acceptance and periya pirāttiyār’s puruśakāram (recommendation), I do not desire to attempt on my own through any other means. Since the beauty of your divine form is engaging me deeply, I am unable to remain patiently and am showing urge in enjoying your divine form”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**maṇṇai uṇḍu umiḻndu** – when the deluge was about to swallow the worlds, without anyone praying to you, taking that danger alone as the reason, without seeing any distinction between the eminent and the lowly, you protected everyone by keeping the worlds \[with the people in them\] in your divine stomach; since they were like acit entities \[those without knowledge\], inside your stomach, you let out the worlds \[with the people\], during creation. The indication here is that you, who protect even those who are not engaged with you, should protect me who is totally engaged with you.

**pin irandu koṇḍu aḷandu** – after the world was created, mahābali thought that the worlds were his and seized them; you went to him, in the most inappropriate manner, as a mendicant, and took the worlds from him in alms, to return them to indra. Beyond the three worlds which were seized, you measured all the fourteen worlds, by keeping your divine feet on the heads of all he people. It is implied through this verse “You manifest your simplicity by engaging with people who do not show any involvement with you; should you not show those qualities to me, who is much lowlier than those people?” ḍidn’t emperumān manifest his qualities of affableness and simplicity in this incarnation, by keeping his divine feet on the heads of all the people!

**maṇ kaṇṇuḷ alladu illai enṟu** – What is the reason for emperumān’s engagement with these people even when they do not show any involvement with him? You had thought that unless you graced the worlds with your divine eyes, they could not sustain themselves.

**venṟa kālamāyināy** – you are the causative factor for the entity ‘time’ too, which is the causative factor for all the changes which take place with all the entities in the world. The indication here is that for people who are the target of birth and death, being under the control of time since they are servitors to others, there is no way other than through you to overcome the entity ‘time’. Has it not been said in mahābhāratam śanti parvam 196-9? “*kālam sa pacatē yatra na kālas tatra vai prabhu*:” (At paramapadam from where emperumān controls time, it cannot exercise its control). In this verse, the term *venṟa* comes as an attribute for time. Alternatively, this word could be added to the sequence *“maṇ kaṇṇuḷ alladu illai enṟu maṇṇai uṇḍu umiḻndu pin irandukoṇḍu aḷandu venṟa*” in which case the meaning will be – you had won the land seized by mahābali, praḷayam etc as your benefit.

**paṇṇai venṟa insol mangai kongai tangu pangayak kaṇṇa** – periya pirāttiyār has a sweet speech, as confirmed by śrī rāma, during his separation from sītāp pirātti, to hanuman, in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-15 “*madurā madurālāpā kimāha mama bhāmini* l  
*madvihīnā varārōhā hanuman katayasva mē* ll” (Oh hanuman! What message did sītai, who is sweet, who has sweet words, who is beautiful, who is distinguished for enjoyment and who is separated from me, convey for me? Please say that). Oh, kaṇṇa with lotus-like eyes, who resides on the bosom of such periya pirāttiyār who is always youthful! Through this verse, āḻvār indicates that emperumān, who is dear to periya pirāttiyār who is distinguished by her age, sweet speech and beauty, had showered distinguished mercy on him, egged on by his qualities such as helping in times of danger etc, forgetting his \[āḻvār’s\] bad conduct, which had been with him since time immemorial.

**ninna vaṇṇam alladu illai eṇṇum vaṇṇamē** – the reason for my urge in enjoying you, without any break, is nothing other than the beauty of your divine form. The implication is that more than your auspicious qualities of motherly forbearance, helping in times of danger, simplicity, affableness etc, it is only the beauty of your divine form which is making me not to remain patient, but to show my anxiety in being hasty.

Next, we will take up the 106<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 106. Karuttu edirnda

*kaṟuttedirnda kālanēmi kālanōdu kūda anṟuaṟutta āḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndināytoṟukkalanda ūnam ahdu oḻikka anṟu kunṟam munpoṟutta nin pugaḻkkalālor nēsamillai nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṟuttu – becoming angry; edirnda – opposed; kālanēmi – kālanēmi \[the monster\]; kālanōdu kūda – to be together with yama; anṟu – that day (when danger surrounded brahmā et al because of him); aṟutta āḻi – divine disc which severed his head; sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum – the other divine weapons of pāncajanyam \[conch\]; ēndināy – oh one who is donning (like donning a flower)!; toṟu – for the cows; kalanda – \[incident which\] happened; ūnam ahdu – that danger; oḻikka – to remove; anṟu – at an earlier time; kunṟam – the mountain gōvardanam; mun – before (anyone got hurt); poṟutta – tolerated by lifting the mountain; nin pugaḻkkalāl – other than your quality of helping in times of danger; nenjam – for my heart; or nēsamillai – there is no affection in any other matter

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is donning the divine weapons of disc, conch, mace, bow and sword, as if they were flowers, with which you severed the head of the monster kālanēmi so that he could be together with yama! At an earlier point of time, you lifted the mountain gōvardana, tolerating it, when danger threatened cows, before anyone got hurt. For my heart, there is no affection in any matter other than your quality of helping in times of danger.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about his engagement with the beauty of emperumān’s divine form by saying “ninna vaṇṇam alladu illai“. In this pāsuram, he says “Even if I engage with other divine qualities of yours, my heart does not engage with any quality other than the quality of your helping in times of danger”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kaṟuttedirnda kālanēmi kālanōdu kūda** – you made the monster kālanēmi, who came and opposed you due to his anger, to be together with yama, the lord of righteousness \[and death\]. This refers to the anger which kālanēmi had because of the wealth which celestial entities such as indran et al had and the mercy which you showed on them. He became angry with emperumān, who is friendly towards all, just as it has been said in bhagavat gītā 5-29 “*bhōktāram yajña tapasām sarvalōka mahēśvaram* l *suhrudam sarvabhūtānām jñātvā mām śāntim ruccati* ll” (Knowing me, who eats the \[fruits of\] rituals and penance, who is the lord of all the worlds and who is the friend of all creations, one attains peace). He became angry with emperumān who is the natural relative of all, just as it has been said in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 1-15 “*sa nō bandur janitā sa vidātā dāmāni vēda bhuvanāni viśvā*” (paramātmā, who is the causative factor for the entire universe, is our relative. He alone bestows benefits; he alone grants us the enjoyments, the implements as well as the abode for enjoying those) and in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-13-12 “*nāham dēvō na gandarvō na yakśō na ca dānava*: l *aham vō bāndavō jātō naitaccintyamitō’nyatā* ll” (I am not a dēvan; not a gandarvan; not a yakśan; not a demon (these are all different types of celestial entities). I am born as your relative; my birth is not to be considered (by you people) in any other manner). Just as it has been said from etymology of the word anjali (salutations offered to emperumān with folded hands, cupping the palms together), *“am jalayati iti anjali*” (since it makes emperumān to melt like a liquid, it is called as anjali), didn’t \[kālanēmi\] get destroyed in a matter in which he could have made emperumān to be like a liquid and mingle with him? īsn’t it your nature to take under your refuge those who come with affection and destroy those who come opposing!

**anṟu** – on that day when danger came the way of indran and other celestial entities.

**aṟutta āḻi sangu taṇḍu villum vāḷum ēndināy** – Oh one who donned the five divine weapons of disc, conch, mace, bow and sword, with which you severed the head of kālanēmi! Even after severing the head of kālanēmi with the divine disc, thinking “What harm will come to the followers from anyone?” you are holding the divine weapons, just as nammāḻvār had said in periya tiruvandādi 87 “*eppōdum kai kaḻalā nēmiyān*” (one who always holds the divine disc in his divine hand).

In the first part of the pāsuram, āḻvār mentioned about the danger that came the way of indran and others. In the second part he speaks about the danger which emperumān removed for the cows and covherds which came from indran.

**toṟukkalanda ūnamahdu oḻikka** – in order to remove the danger which came the way of cows. īndran was protected by emperumān when danger came his way from the monster kālanēmi. However, indran, who was favourable then, became angry when he was denied his food and ordered hailstorm. Unlike the way he protected indran, before danger came his way, emperumān protected the cows after they were caught in the hailstorm. The term *toṟu* refers to cows. The term k*alanda* refers to danger coming their way.

**anṟu** – during the time when cows, herdsmen and herdsvomen suffered due to the hailstorm, just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-11-13 “*tatas tad gōkulam sarvam gōgōpijanasankulam* l *atīvaṟtam harir druśtvā maitrēyācintayat tadā* ll” (Oh maitrēya! Subsequently, seeing gōkulam with abundance of cows, herdsmen and herdsvomen suffering terribly, bhagavān then started thinking).

**kunṟa mun poṟutta nin pugaḻkkalāl** – other than your auspicious quality of holding the gōvardana mountain and protecting. The indication is that since it is not natural for emperumān to fight with indran, he did not destroy indran even though he had the weapons for destroying him; he protected the cows etc which were to be protected, by holding up the mountain. The term *mun* indicates that he protected the cows, herdsmen and herdsvomen, before anyone was harmed. toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār, who described the same narrative, said in his tirumalai 36 “*maḻaikku anṟu varai mun ēndum maindanē*” (Oh one who has the strength with which you held the mountain on that day before harm came the way \[of cows and others\]). While toṇḍaradippodi āḻvār had used the term *ēndum* which means that he held the mountain casually, this āḻvār says *poṟutta* which indicates that he endured it. The reason for this is that while he did hold it casually, considering emperumān’s tenderness, āḻvār says that he endured it.

**ōr nēsamillai nenjamē** – While I engage with incarnations other than kriṣṇāvatāram and emperumān’s auspicious qualities other than that of helping in times of danger, my heart does not involve that way. Thus, the implied meaning of this pāsuram is – my heart does not involve with any auspicious quality of emperumān’s other than his quality of helping in times of danger, whether it was through weapons with which he destroyed the danger which befell indran et al or through holding up a mountain to protect cows and herds-people whom he considered as his own.

Next, we will take up the 107<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 107. Kay sinatta

*kāy sinatta kāsi mannan vakkaran pavuṇḍiranmāsinatta māli māsumāli kēsi dēnukannāsamuṟṟu vīḻa nāḷ kavarnda nin kaḻaṟkalālnēsa pāsam ettiṟattum vaittidēn emmīsanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

em īsanē – Oh one who is my svāmy (lord)!; kāy – to kill (noble people); sinatta – having anger; kāsi mannan – the king of kāsi; vakkaran – dantavakran; pavuṇḍiran – pauṇḍrakan; mā sinatta – having great anger; māli – māli; mā sumāli – sumāli, who had a huge form; kēsi – kēsi; dēnukan – dēnukan; nāsam – grief; uṟṟu – attained; vīḻa – to fall down, dying; nāḷ – their longevity; kavarnda – one who had taken; nin – your; kaḻaṟkalāl – other than the divine feet; ettiṟattum – in any matter; pāsam – rope; vaittidēn – I will not keep

**Перевод:**

**Oh, one who is my svāmy! Your divine feet had destroyed the longevity of monsters such as the king of kāsi, vo had anger to kill noble people, dantavakran, pauṇḍrakan, māli, who had great anger, sumāli, who had a huge form, kēsi and dēnukan, such that they fell down dying, in grief. I will not keep my affection on any matter other than your divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Through the previous two pāsurams, āḻvār spoke about emperumān’s divine beauty and the quality of helping in times of danger which were the root cause for the bhagavat bhakti (devotion towards emperumān) which was created in him and in his heart, respectively. In this pāsuram, he says that he will not keep his affection on anything other than emperumān’s divine feet which destroyed his strong karmas (past deeds), which were hurdles in attaining that devotion, just like they destroyed monsters.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kāy sinatta kāsi mannan** – the king of kāsi who had a destructive anger. The opinion is that since he had an abundance of tamōguṇam (ignorance, laziness etc), his anger could not be controlled until he destroyed noble people (people who had an abundance of sātvika guṇam).  *kāydal* – killing (root word for *kāy*\].

**vakkaran** – dantavakran, another monster, who had similar nature. The words *kāy sinatta* are to be added here too.

**pavuṇḍiran** – As if to compete with kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa), pauṇḍraka vāsudēvan donned the disc and conch and proclaimed, egoistically, that he is the real vāsudēvan.

**mā sinatta māli** – monster māli, who had huge anger.

**mā sumāli** – another monster, sumāli, who had a huge physical form. The word *māsinatta* could be added here too. The implied meaning is that this sumāli had a huge anger as well as a huge and beautiful form.

**kēsi** – yet another monster, who came in the form of a monstrous horse towards gōkulam (to kill kaṇṇan) causing fear amongst celestial entities when he came in the sky.

**dēnukan**– the monster dēnukan, who created fear among the covherd people and cows, who were following kaṇṇan. The reason for āḻvār to speak about the cruelties of these monsters was to indicate the cruelties caused by his karmas (past deeds) which were hurdles for him \[in attaining emperumān\].

**nāsamuṟṟu vīḻa nāḷ kavarnda** – took their lives such that they fell down, dead, experiencing grief. The term *nāsam* here refers to grief. *vīḻa* refers to falling down, dead. Alternatively, *vīḻdal* could be taken to refer to letting go, and the term here would refer to letting go of their lives. Until now, the pāsuram spoke about emperumān considering the enemies of his followers as his enemies and his destroying them without any mercy.

**nin kaḻaṟkalāl nēsa pāsam ettiṟattum vaittidēn** – Apart from your divine feet which have the nature of destroying the enemies, I will not keep the rope of devotion, with any other matter. He calls devotion s rope since devotion binds him with emperumān. ḍidn’t this āḻvār mercifully say in pāsuram 83 *ettinōdu iraṇḍenum kayiṟṟāl*, terming devotion as rope!

**em īsanē** – my svāmy! He says that the reason for emperumān to remove the sins which were responsible for his being involved with other matters \[worldly pursuits\] and to remove his sins which were hurdles in his developing devotion towards emperumān, correcting him, is because he is āḻvār’s svāmy only.

Next, we will take up the 108<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 108. Kedil sir

*kēdil sīr varattanāyk kedum varattayanarannādinōdu nattam āyirattanādu naṇṇinumvīdadāna bōgam eydi vīṟṟirunda pōdilumkūdum āsai alladonṟu koḷvanō kuṟippilē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kēdil sīr varattanāya ayan – brahmā who had got unlimited wealth through boon, his; kedum varattu – getting the act of destruction as boon; aran – sivan’s too; nādinōdu – along with their lands; nāttam āyirattan – indran, who has a thousand eyes, his; nādu – land; naṇṇinum – even if I attain; vīdu adāna – in the form of such mōkśam; bōgam – enjoyment; eydi – attaining; vīṟṟirunda pōdilum – even if it is there without any deficiency; kuṟippil – through the heart; kūdum āsai alladu – other than the desire of uniting with you; onṟu koḷvanō – will I desire?

**Перевод:**

**Even if I attain the lands of brahmā, who has unlimited wealth through boon, of sivan, who has the act of destruction as boon and of indran, who has a thousand eyes, along with such mōkśam and enjoy without any deficiency, will I desire, through my heart, anything other than the desire of uniting with you?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In pāsuram 106, āḻvār had mercifully said “nin pugaḻkkalālor nēsamillai nenjamē” affirming that emperumān’s quality of helping in times of danger was his refuge and in pāsuram 107, he had mercifully said “ninkaḻaṟkalāl nēsa pāsam ettiṟattum vaittidēn”, affirming that emperumān’s divine feet which remove his followers’ enemies are his refuge. Hearing this emperumān thinks in his divine mind “You could enjoy me in parama padam using these”. āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “Even if I get to enjoy all the opulence in this world along with the happiness of enjoying mōkśam, I will not desire any benefit other than paramabhakti, which is attaining you”. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāsiriyam 2 “ulagam mūnṟinodu nal vīdu peṟinum nēriya kādal anbil inbin tēṟal amudaveḷḷattānām siṟappu vittu – koḷvadu eṇṇumō teḷḷiyōr kuṟippē” (Will those with knowledge consider enjoying all the wealth of the worlds along with mōkśam, when compared with the act of submerging in the nectar-like ocean of affection for emperumān and the desire to attain him?) this āḻvār too says “I do not need mōkśam too. It is enough if I have paramabhakti towards you (sustaining in the presence of emperumān and perishing otherwise)”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**kēdil sīr varattanāyk kedum varattu ayan aran** – this should be rearranged as “*kēdil sīr varattanāya ayan kedum varattanāya aran*” to get cogent meaning. The term *varattanāya* has got transformed as *varattanāy* to fit with the metrics of tamiḻ poems. The meaning for this verse is – brahmā has obtained emperumān’s grace to have indestructible wealth till the end of *kalpa kālam* (the longevity of brahmā) and rudran, who has obtained emperumān’s grace to destroy all the entities which had been created by that brahmā. Here, the term *varattanāya* would refer to one who has obtained boon from emperumān. Alternatively, the term *varattan* could be taken to refer to *varadan*, one who bestows boons. Then the meaning will be: *kēdil sīr* – having indestructible wealth, *varattanāya ayan* – brahmā, who is capable of bestowing this boon on his followers; *kedum sīr* – one who has obtained destroying everyone, as his wealth, *varattanāya aran* – rudran, who is capable of bestowing this as a boon to his followers.

**nādinōdu nattamāyirattan nādu naṇṇinum** – even if I get the lands of brahmā and rudran, along with the land of indran who has thousand eyes. n*attam* – eye; *nattamāyirattan*– indran who has thousand eyes. The term *nādu* refers to the wealth in the land. In other words, if I alone were to get the wealth of the three celestial entities of brahmā, rudran and indran; it is not possible for one person to attain all their wealth. The opinion is – even if all the wealth were to come to me.

**vīdadāna bōgam eydi** – more than all the wealth mentioned above, attaining the enjoyment of mōkśam which is carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān, which is the ultimate benefit and when compared to which, all the wealth mentioned above will appear like a drop of water \[in an ocean\]. Usage of the term *vīdu adāna bōgam* indicates that it is the most eminent mōkśam of carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān, which is the ultimate benefit, and not the lowly mōkśam such as *kaivalya mōkśam* \[where an ātmā enjoys itself instead of carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān\] or the mōkśam attained by *sādanabhktas* \[those who carry out a means on their own to attain emperumān\] where, even after attaining emperumān, they focus on enjoying emperumān and becoming pleased \[instead of making him happy by carrying out kainkaryam to him\]

**vīṟṟirunda pōdilum** – *vīṟṟirukkai* refers to being with splendour. In other words, being without any shortcoming in the aids for carrying out kainkaryam to emperumān; or, being fulfilled with unmatched *brahmānandam* (supreme happiness). Wouldn’t aids for kainkaryam for an entity in mōkśam come through *sankalpam* (thought) alone? Alternatively, this could be construed as being with a splendour on having attained all the wealth of brahmā, rudran and indran, along with mōkśam, compared to nityasūris and muktātmās.

**kūdum āsai alladu onṟu koḷvanō kuṟippilē** – will I desire through my heart anything other than the stage of paramabhakti wherein I cannot sustain myself without you and with the thought of being with you all the time? It has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-20-27 “*darmārtakāmai*: *kim tasya muktistasya karē stitā* l *samasta jagatām mūlē yasya bhakti*: *stirā tvayi* ll” (To one who has unwavering devotion towards you, who are the causative factor for the worlds, what is the purpose of benefits such as *darmam* (righteousness), *artam* (opulence) and *kāmam* (pleasures)? He has the ultimate benefit of *mōkśam* (parama padam) itself with him) and in jitantē ślōkam 1-11 “*vijñānam yadidam prāptam yadidam stānam ārjitam* l *janmāntarē’pi mē dēva mābhūt tasya parikśaya*: ll” (Oh emperumān! Let there be no shortcoming in any birth to the knowledge that *adiyēn* (this servitor) has attained (that you are the means and the ultimate benefit) and the state that *adiyēn* has reached (about the firmness of this knowledge)). In line with these pramāṇams, if there is no slackening of faith in the firmness of means \[that it is only emperumān\] and that paramabhakti alone is the ultimate benefit, there is no need to even reach mōkśam; even if there is rebirth, it is not a hurdle. This is the belief of those who are deeply involved with emperumān through their knowledge and devotion.

Next, we will take up the 109<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.
