## 109. Surukkuvarai inriye

*surukkuvārai inṟiyē surungināy surungiyumperukkuvārai inṟiyē perukkam eydu peṟṟiyōyserukkuvārgaḷ tīkkuṇangaḷ tīrtta dēvadēvan enṟuirukkuvāy munikkaṇangaḷ ētta yānum ēttinēn*

**Пословный перевод:**

surukkuvāai inṟiyē – without a reason for shortening \[your divine form\]; surungināy – you became a vāmana (dwarf); surungiyum – even when you had shortened yourself like that; perukkuvārai inṟiyē – without a reason for enlarging; perukkam eydu – one who grew huge; peṟṟiyōy – Oh one who has the nature!; serukkuvārgaḷ – like mahābali et al, who harass (indran et al); tīkkuṇangaḷ – evil qualities (such as ego etc); tīrtta – one who removed; dēvadēvan – Oh sarvēśvara!; enṟu – like this; irukkuvāy – vēdams; munikkaṇangaḷ – multitude of sages; ētta – as they praised; yānum – I too; ēttinēn – praised

**Перевод:**

**Without any reason, you shortened your physical form and became a vāmana. Without any reason, having shortened yourself earlier, you grew huge. Oh, one who has this as your nature! Oh, sarvēśvara who removed evil qualities of entities such as mahābali et al! Just as multitude of sages praised you like this, I too praised.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān tells āḻvār in his divine mind “In the previous pāsuram, the devotion which you had towards me appeared similar to the devotion exhibited by those who think of me always, through their efforts, who desire to attain me through reciting my names always, just as I had mentioned in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 9-14 ‘satatam kīrtayantō mām yatantaśca drudavrutā: l namasyantaśca mām bhaktyā nityuyktā upāsatē ll’ (They constantly worship me, reciting my names constantly with devotion, taking efforts (in activities such as vorśipping me etc)! In the 101st pāsuram ‘nirandaram ninaippadāga nī ninaikka vēṇḍumē’ you had desired to think of me constantly! In the 103rd pāsuram ‘nin peyar urukkalandu oḻivilādu uraikkumāṟu urai sey‘, you had desired to recite the divine names continuously. In the previous pāsuram kūdum āsaiyalladu onṟu koḷvanō you had desired devotion. Do you desire devotion which is the means to attain mōkśam?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “It is not like that. Your amazing qualities and activities impel me to speak that way, treating that itself as the benefit \[and not expecting any benefit out of speaking like that\]. I spoke that way only after seeing vēdams and those who follow vēdams, speak like that. I did not speak like an upāsakan \[one who worships emperumān and is devoted to him in order to attain emperumān through that means\] using it as a means”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**surukkuvārai inṟiyē surungināy** – unlike shortening due to karmas (past deeds) or due to time or due to the urging of another entity, you shortened yourself as vāmana impelled by your mercy towards your followers. samsāri jīvas (those who live in samsāram) are born as worms or insects due to their past deeds and due to the jīvas who gave birth to them. purāṇams say that those who were with huge form in earlier epochs, become shorter in kaliyugam (present epoch) and towards the end of kaliyugam, they will be just the size of a thumb. In order to remain invisible to demons when he entered lankā, hanuman, for carrying out śrī rāma’s work, took a short form, as we have read in śrī rāmāyaṇam. The indication is that your dwarfing is unlike these.

**surungiyum** – even as you shortened yourself

**perukkuvārai inṟiyē perukkam eydu peṟṟiyōy** – Oh one who has the nature of enlarging yourself, merely due to your mercy, unlike samsāri jīvas who enlarge themselves due to their karma or due to time or impelled by another entity! *peṟṟi* – nature; natural quality. Through these two lines, it is described that it is natural for emperumān to take an appropriate form for taking alms, to seek alms and, once the water, symbolic of giving alms fell on his palm, due to the ecstasy of achieving what he had desired, he took a huge form, keeping his divine feet on the heads of everyone in the fourteen worlds without looking at differences among them.

Now, through the third line, āḻvār speaks about emperumān destroying the ego of entities such as mahābali, carrying out beneficial act for them too and carrying out the task for indran.

**serukkuvārgaḷ** – śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-13-14 says “*na yaśtavyam na dātavyam na hōtavyam katancana* l *bhōktā yajñasya kastvanyō hyaham yajñapati*: *prabhu*: ll”  
(*yāgam* (ritual) should never be carried out; charity should never be given; *hōmam* (another type of ritual) should not be done. More than me (vēna maharāja) who can be the enjoyer of *yajñam*? Am I not the lord for *yajñam* and one who gives the benefit for the *yajñam*?) Entities such as mahābali, like vēna maharājā seen just now in the above mentioned ślōkam, were living with their ego, transgressing the command of emperumān and not regretting that they had transgressed.

**tīkkuṇangaḷ tīrtta dēva dēvanē** – Oh the lord of all celestial entities who removed the faults such as ego and possessiveness, which remove the basic nature of entities, by transforming yourself as a mendicant and who returned the lands of celestial entities such as indran et al which they had lost.

**enṟu irukkuvāy munikkaṇangaḷ ētta** – through this pāsuram, vēdams and, multitude of sages treating vēdams as pramāṇams, praised the action of trivikrama who measured the three worlds, just as it has been mentioned in viṣṇu sūktam “*vicakramē prutvīmēśa ētām* – *trir dēva: prutvīmēśa ētām*” (this emperumān measured the earth – the lord measured the three worlds through three steps \[of his divine foot\]) and in viṣṇu darmam 43-33 “*sanghais surāṇām divi bhūtalastais tatā manuśyair gaganē ca khēsarai*: l  
*stuta*: *kramān ya*:*pracakāra sarvadā mamāstu māngalya vivruddayē hari*: ll” (Through the multitude of celestial entities in the ethereal layer, through human beings likewise from the earth, and through those who roam in the ethereal layer, bhagavān was vorśipped. Let that bhagavān hari (who incarnated as trivikraman) remain for me, for bringing auspiciousness, in abundance). In these instances, after seeing sages such as saunaka praising you…

**yānum ēttinēn** – since I cannot sustain myself without speaking about such activities \[of yours\] for passing time purposefully, I too praised your affableness and simplicity of keeping your divine feet on the heads of all the people, without seeing any distinction, and your quality of destroying enemies. The indication is that I did not praise you like an *upāsakan* with the thought of utilising this as a means to attain mōkśam.

Next, we will take up the 110<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 110. Tuyanayum anriyum

*tūyanāyum anṟiyum surumbulavu taṇ tuḻāymāya ninnai nāyinēn vaṇangi vāḻttum īdellāmnīyu nin kuṟippinil poṟuttu nalgu vēlai nīrppāyalōdu pattar sittam mēya vēlai vaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

surumbu – beetles; ulāvu – roaming around; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – having divine tuḷasi; māya – Oh, the amazing entity!; vēlai nīr – water of the oceans; pāyalōdu – along with the mattress; pattar – devotees’; sittam mēya – as one who had entered their hearts; vēlai vaṇṇanē – Oh one who has the complexion of ocean!; tūyanāyum – thinking of sacredness (due to the basic nature of knowledge); anṟiyum – not thinking of impurity (which results from ego and possessiveness which came to that basic nature); nāyinēn – I, who am lowly, like a dog; ninnai – you (who are most sacred and most enjoyable); vaṇangi vāḻttum īdellām – all these by which I salute you and praise you; nīyum – you too (who are an ocean of patience); nin kuṟippinil – in your divine mind; poṟuttu – forgiving me; nalgu – you should shower your mercy

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the amazing entity who has tuḷasi, around which beetles are roaming! Oh, one who has the complexion of ocean, remaining on the ocean as a mattress, as well in the hearts of your devotees! I, a lowly dog, am thinking of sacredness (arising out of knowledge which is basic nature for me) and am not thinking of impurity which results from ego and possessiveness of that basic nature. You should forgive me in your divine mind and shower your mercy on me who saluted you and praised you, who are like an ocean of patience.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram, āḻvār is apologising to emperumān for praising him, since he is not apt for praising the most distinguished entity, emperumān. Just as it has been mentioned in rig vēdam – viṣṇu sūktam “tad viṣṇō: paramam padam sadā paśyanti sūraya: ldivīva cakśurātatam ll tad viprāsō vipanyavō jāgruvāmsas samindatē viṣṇōr yat paramam padam ll” (nityasūris constantly keep looking at that superior abode of viṣṇu. That supreme abode is effulgent like sūriyan (sun) who reveals everything, who is in the sky, who is like the eyes of the world. In that paramapadam, greatly wise people, people who keep constantly vorśipping, ever alert nityasūris shine brightly), emperumān is praised by nityasūris, who have no connection with samsāram, and by sages and celestial entities, while he\[āḻvār\] has been in samsāram since time immemorial and is not apt for praising him. However, you should mercifully think in your divine mind “Since he cannot sustain himself without praising me, he has praised me” and bear with me, conveying his apology for saying in the previous pāsuram that he had praised emperumān (nānum ēttinēn)*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**tūyanāyum anṟiyum** – Just as it has been said in yājñavalkya smruti 3-34 “*kśētrajñasyēśvarajñānāt viśuddi*: *paramā matā*” (It has been thought that distinguished sanctity happens to samsāri cētana by knowing about emperumān), when I think of your quality of purifying \[others\], I feel reassured that I am qualified to praise you; when I look at the beauty of your divine form which is attractive, I attain you, not thinking of my being immersed in samsāram since time immemorial. Alternatively, just as it has been mentioned in bhagavat gītā 4-38 “*na hi jñānēna sadruśam pavitram iha vidyatē* l *tat svayam yōgasamsidda*: *kālēnātmani vindati* ll” (In this world, there is no entity similar to knowledge for purifying. One who has attained *karmayōgasiddi* (the readily available path of deeds), over a period of time, attains that knowledge in his ātmā automatically), thinking of the purity that resulted from *ātmasvarūpa jñānam* (knowledge which came from basic nature of ātmā), one can be firm that one is apt to praise \[emperumān\] and one can attain emperumān by not thinking of the impurity which resulted from ego and possessiveness to that ātmā. This verse could be added to *vaṇangi vāḻttum īdellām* to get cogent meaning.

**surumbulāvu taṇ tuḻāy māya** – āḻvār mentions the reason for not thinking of his lowliness and emperumān’s eminence and being bewildered that he was qualified to praise emperumān. Oh, one who has amazing activities and auspicious qualities, donning the cool, divine tuḷasi garland around which beetles keep roaming to imbibe the honey flowing within that tuḷasi! īsn’t your sweetness such that even beetles, which do not have discerning knowledge, come with desire! The indication here is that your beauty of being decorated with tuḷasi and your divine and auspicious qualities and activities alone have made me fall head over heels.

**ninnai** – you, who are superior to everyone else, purifying others and apt to be enjoyed by nityasūris.

**nāyinēn** – I, who am like a dog. The implication is that just as there is no boundary to your eminence, there is no boundary to my lowliness too.

**nāyinēn** – dog is one which will enter any space which is open, which will get hit and driven away from all those spaces and which, if one were to touch it even by mistake, will create impurity which can be removed only after having a bath. There is no limit to the number of physical forms that I have entered. There is no limit to the insults that I have suffered after taking those forms. My basic nature is such that even if you \[addressing emperumān\] were to touch me after being pleased, it will create an impurity in you.

**vaṇangi vāḻttum īdellām** – Am I asking for being apologised, after engaging with one fault? Haven’t I committed many faults? All my wrongful activities of bowing down with my head on your divine feet and praising you to my heart’s content.

**nīyum** – I cannot stay without bowing to you and praising you, because of my affection towards you. You, having patience as your basic nature, should tolerate those activities.

**nin kuṟippinil poṟuttu nalgu** – just as you had tolerated the faults of kākāsuran and śiśupālan in your divine mind, you should tolerate my faults too in your divine mind and shower your mercy on me thinking “this person \[referring to āḻvār\] will not be steadfast in opposing us”.

āḻvār now gives the reason for emperumān tolerating him.

**vēlai nīrp pāyalōdu pattar sittam mēya vēlai vaṇṇanē** – Oh one who has the nature of removing all the distress from the hearts of your followers by standing tall with the mattress of tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) and having a form similar to that ocean, removing the ennui of those who behold you!

āḻvār says “Since you remain with the thought of entering the hearts of your followers at an appropriate time, reclining on the milky ocean, and enter the hearts of those who have a little affection for you, removing their ennui, you should tolerate me and shower your mercy on me”.

Next, we will take up the 111<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 111. Vaidu ninnai

*vaidu ninnai vallavā paḻittavarkku(m) māṟil pōrseydu ninna seṟṟattīyil vendavarkkum vandunaieydalāgum enbar ādalāl em māya nāyinēnseyda kuṟṟam naṟṟamāgavē koḷ jñāla nādanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ninnai – you, who are apt to be praised; vallavā – to the extent possible; vaidu – abused; paḻittavarkkum – even those who insulted you, such as śiśupālan et al; māṟil – unmatched; pōr – war; seydu – carried out; ninna – your; seṟṟam – called as anger; tīyil – in the fire; vendavarkkum – those who got burnt such as vāli et al; unai – you (who are the supreme entity); vandu eydalāgum enbar – sages said that \[you\] could be attained; ādalāl – hence; em māya – my lord, who has amazing qualities!; jñāla nādanē – one who is the owner for the entire universe!; nāyinēn – I, who am very lowly; seyda kuṟṟam – the faults I carried out; naṟṟamāgavē – as good qualities; koḷ – mercifully consider

**Перевод:**

**You, who are apt to be praised, were abused to the extent possible, by śiśupālan et al, and they got burnt in the fire of your anger in the unmatched war. Sages have said that even such people could attain you, who are the supreme entity. Oh, my lord, who has amazing qualities! Oh, the owner of the entire universe! You should hence mercifully consider the faults which I, a lowly person, carried out, as good qualities.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, considering his lowliness, āḻvār requested emperumān to bear with him. In this pāsuram, considering emperumān’s quality of vātsalyam in which emperumān considers even someone’s fault as a good quality, āḻvār requests emperumān to consider the faults, which resulted from his desirable activities such as praising etc which he carried out in order to spend his time purposefully, and the faults which he carried out due to his carelessness, as good qualities.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vaidu ninnai** – You are apt to be praised, just as it has been said in sahasranāmam 684 and 685 “*stavya*: *stavapriya*:” (one who is appropriate to be praised; one who desires being praised). You were made as the target of abuse by śiśupālan; in which way did he do it? He reviled you saying that you were born in the clan of *yadu* who had lost the right to rule; that you had given your women to other *kśatriyas* \[rulers\], and called yourself as a *kśatriya* \[ruler}; you were born in the clan of covherds who did not know the difference between left hand and right hand.

**vallavā paḻittavarkkum** – to people such as śiśupālan et al who ridiculed, not only targeting your clan of birth but also your auspicious qualities as faults and insulted you to the extent that they could. In other words, ridiculing your admirable qualities such as going as a messenger which he considered as being lowly even for the clan of *yadus*, being a charioteer, stealing butter, taking away girls etc. The term *vallavā* refers to the extent to which such people \[śiśupālan et al} went in order to insult you through faulty words. It could also be considered that they utilised all the strength that they had in reviling emperumān.

**māṟil pōr seydu ninna seṟṟattīyil vendavarkkum** – those who were burnt in the unmatched war that they engaged with you and got burnt. āḻvār terms the battle between sugrīvan and vāli as unmatched. Through these two verses, āḻvār is referring to śiśupāḷan, vāli et al.

**vandunai eydalāgum enbar** – sages say that by insulting you and engaging in war with you, through these ways, people could attain you. śrī parāśara maharishi says in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 4-15-15 that śiśupālan attained mōkśam through this way “*tāvacca bhagavaccakrēṇāsu vyāpādita*: *tatsmaraṇa dagdākilāgasancaya*: l *bhagavatā antamupanīta*:*, tasminnēva layamupayayau* ll” (śiśupālan was immediately killed by the divine disc of emperumān; since he thought of emperumān, his bundles of sins got burnt out; having been killed by emperumān, he attained emperumān). śrī vālmīki maharishi too mentioned about the way vāli attained mōkśam in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 17-8  
“*tadastram tasya vīrasya svarga mārga prabhāvanam* l *rāmabāṇā sanakśiptam āvahat paramām gatim* ll” (That arrow which was shot from śrī rāma’s bow, and which showed the path to parama padam, gave mōkśam to the valorous vāli).

**ādalāl** – Since maharishis have said that those who abused you and those who fought with you too could attain you due to the eminence of your \[emperumān’s\] qualities

**emmāya** – oh, one who manifested to me the amazing qualities such as mercy, patience, vātsalyam \[motherly forbearance\] etc through which you tolerate the faults of your followers!

**nāyinēn seyda kuṟṟam naṟṟamāgavē koḷ** – you should consider even the faults which I, having no other refuge and looking up only to you for everything, carried out through carelessness, as good qualities.

**jñāla nādanē**– oh one who is the unconditional lord of the universe! This term gives the reason for his being the owner of the universe, without any distinction between those who have knowledge and those who do not have.

Next, we will take up the 112<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 112. Valgalagi nalgal

*vāḷgaḷāgi nāḷgaḷ sella nōymai kunṟi mūppeydimāḷu nāḷadu ādalāl vaṇangi vāḻttu en nenjamēāḷadāgu nanmai enṟu nanguṇarndadu anṟiyummeeḷvilāda bōgam nalga vēṇḍum māla pādamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjamē – Oh my heart, which is under my control!; vāḷgaḷāgi – like a saw which cuts longevity; nāḷgaḷ – days; sella – as they pass; nōymai – due to disease; kunṟi – becoming weak; mūppu – old age; eydi – attaining; māḷum nāḷ adu – the time to die has come; ādalāl – hence; vaṇangi vāḻttu – bow down to the divine feet of sarvēśvaran (lord of all) and praise; adanṟiyum – not only that; āḷadāgum – that servitude; nanmai enṟu – that it is puruśārttam(ultimate benefit); nangu uṇarndu – knowing well; vaṇangi vāḻttu – bow down and praise; meeḷvilāda – not having to return; bōgam – the enjoyment of kainkaryam (carrying out service); mālapādam – only the divine feet of sarvēśvaran who is the supreme entity; nalga vēṇḍum – should grant us

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my heart which is under my control! Like a saw which cuts our longevity, days keep passing. As one attains old age, becoming weak due to disease, the time to die has come. Hence, bow down to the divine feet of sarvēśvaran and praise. Not only that, know well that this servitude is the ultimate benefit. Only the divine feet of sarvēśvaran, the supreme entity, should grant us the enjoyment of carrying out service to them, and which is never having to return \[to samsāram\]**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Through the two previous pāsurams, āḻvār requested emperumān to tolerate the faults in him and to mercifully consider his faults as good qualities, respectively. In this pāsuram, he tells his divine heart “We do not know as to when our life will end. Until then, spend your time by bowing to his feet and praise them”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**vāḷgalāgi nāḷgaḷ sella** – just like a sword (like a saw) which cuts the tree of our longevity, as days pass by. The indication is that since longevity is being slowly cut off, it will create fear in everyone. samsāris, who consider their bodies as souls, will be fearful that the body is slowly decaying. Just as it has been said in śrī viṣṇu tatvam 6 “*vicitrā dēha sampatti: īśvarāya nivēditum* l *pūrvamēva krutā brahman hasta pādādi samyutā* ll” (Oh brāhmaṇa! This strange body, which is together with hands and legs, and which is like a wealth, has been created to be proffered to sarvēśvaran, in the earliest time itself), those who are desirous of attaining mōkśam will detest the wasting away of time since the time to reach emperumān is getting wasted. Those who attain him for self-ingratiation will be fearful of the time getting wasted in enjoying emperumān.

**nōymai kunṟi mūppeydi** – even while being in youthful days, due to strong bodily diseases and mental diseases, people would lose strength and due to that, will attain old age. In adolescent age, knowledge will not blossom. In old age, strength will be less. In youthful stage, which is in between the two, due to reasons of diseases etc, both knowledge and strength will start getting reduced. The hurdles in the person’s life are of this type.

**māḷum nāḷadu** – following this old age, death will also reach one day. Alternatively, *māḷum* – life will end one day. *nāḷadu* – that could be considered as the nature of longevity.

**ādalāl vaṇangi vāḻttu** – since longevity is uncertain, since there are many hurdles even to the life remaining, don’t be under the false impression “We will do it tomorrow”. Even when life is still there, bow down at the divine feet of emperumān, as the advantage of having been provided with a head. Just as viṣṇu darmam 1-3 says “*sā jihvā yā harim stauti taccittam yat tadarpitam* l *tāvēva ca karau ślājñau yau tat pūjā karau karau* ll” (Only that which praises hari \[emperumān\] is called as tongue; only that which has been proffered to him is heart; only those which carry out worship to him are the distinguished hands), try to praise him as the purpose of having been provided with a mouth. We could also construe this to mean that since we do not have any way other than to experience emperumān, try to bow down to him and to praise him.

**en nenjamē** – Oh heart, which is under my control! The indication here is that – it is your duty to think of emperumān; I do not have to control you for that purpose. īsn’t he thinking in his divine mind too, just as he had said in pāsuram 105 “*nin pugaḻkkalāl nēsamillai nenjamē*” indicating that his heart is involved with emperumān more than he!

**āḷadāgum nanmai enṟu nanguṇarndadu anṟiyum** – the verse should be rearranged as “*adanṟiyum āḷadāgum nanmai enṟu nanguṇarndu vaṇangi vāḻttu*” adding the last two words to get cogent meaning. The indication is that apart from the meaning of passing time purposefully, as mentioned earlier, “Carrying out servitude to him is our superior benefit; understand our basic nature and try to bow down to him and to praise him”. Just as sahasranāmādyāyam 5 says “*tamēva cārcayan nityam bhaktyā puruśam avyayam* l  
*dyāyan stuvan namasyamśca yajamānas tamēva ca* ll” (One who keeps vorśipping only that supreme entity who has indestructible qualities, every day, by reciting his divine names with devotion, by meditating on him, by praising him and by bowing down to him (will get relieved from all hurdles)),

śāstras say that bowing down to emperumān and praising him are the means to attain him. Are you implying that this should be done as a means to attain emperumān? āḻvār responds through the last verse..

**meeḷvilāda bōgam nalga vēṇḍum māla pādamē** – vorśipping and praising are carried out not as means to attain emperumān. Only the divine feet of emperumān, who is more eminent than all the rest, are the means to grant us the permanent enjoyment of kainkaryam, which will remain until the ātmā remains \[for ever\], just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 8-15-1 “*sa kalvēvam vartayan yāvadāyuśam brahmalōkamabhi sampatyatē* l *na ca punarāvartatē* l *na ca punarāvartatē* ll (that *mumukśu* (one who has desire in attaining mōkśam) lives like this until his life ends, and (once the life ends) attains mōkśam. He does not return here). Your vorśipping and praising are the benefits in themselves. The activities of vorśipping and praising are part of the benefits which one gets even after attaining parama padam, just as it has been mentioned in mahābhāratam śanti parvam 344-45 “*muktānām lakśaṇam hyētat yaccvēta dvīpivāsinām* l  
*nityānjaliputā hruśtā nama ityēva vādina*: ll” (the symbol of *śvētadvīpavāsis* (those who live in śvētadvīpam) is also the symbol of muktas. They will be constantly performing anjali (cupping the palms in salutation); (hence) they will be happy; they will have the characteristic of saying *nama*: (I belong to emperumān) through their mouths constantly) and as said in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 4-3-6 “*tasya haitasya puruśasya rūpam yatā māhārajanam vāsa*:” (that divine form of supreme entity is like a golden dress). The indication is that āḻvār tells his heart to do that here itself.

Next, we will take up the 113<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 113. Salam kalanda

*salangalanda senjadaik kaṟutta kaṇḍan veṇḍalaippulan kalanga uṇḍa pādagattan van tuyar kedaalangan mārbil vāsa nīr koduttavan adutta sīrnalam koḷ mālai naṇṇum vaṇṇam eṇṇu vāḻi nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nenjamē – Oh, my mind!; salam – with waters of \[river\] gangā; kalanda – being together; senjadai – having reddish coloured matted hair; kaṟutta kaṇḍan – having bluish throat; veṇ talai – in the white coloured skull of brahmā’s head; pulan kalanga – such that all his sensory organs were bewildered; uṇḍa – took food; pādagattan – rudran, who had the sin of brahmahatti (harming a brāhmaṇa), his; van – severe; tuyar – grief; keda – to be annihilated; alangal mārbil – in the divine chest decorated with divine garland; vāsa nīr – fragrant water; koduttavan – one who gave and protected; adutta sīr – derived from the auspicious qualities (which are apt for his basic nature); nalangoḷ – having eminence; mālai – sarvēśvaran (lord of all); naṇṇum vaṇṇam – his mercy which is the means to attain him; eṇṇu – keep thinking of; vāḻi – may you live long

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my mind! rudran, who has reddish matted hair along with the waters of gangā as well as a bluish throat, took food from the white coloured skull which had stuck to his hand and which he contracted due to brahmahatti pāpam of plucking it from his father, brahmā’s head. His senses got bewildered due to this; to annihilate his severe grief, emperumān sprinkled fragrant water \[sweat\] from his divine chest, decorated with divine garland. Keep thinking of the mercy of that emperumān, who has great eminence, derived from his auspicious qualities, which are apt for his basic nature and who is he lord of all. May you live long.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “meeḷvilāda bōgam nalga vēṇḍum” asking his heart to bow down to the divine feet of emperumān and to praise him until attaining kainkarya bhōgam (the enjoyment of servitude). Hearing this, his heart told him “This enjoyment is attained only by those who are at the threshold of pāratantryam (total dependence on emperumān). We were at the exact opposite end, having been spoiled by ahankāram and mamakāram (ego and possessiveness, respectively). How will we get that benefit?” āḻvār tells his heart, in this pāsuram, “emperumān removed brahmahatti pāpam (the sin of harming a brāhmaṇa) of rudran who had severed the head of his father, brahmā, because of his ego. The same emperumān will remove our ahankāram and mamakāram which are the opposite of pāratantryam and grant us this benefit. You be firm that he is the means \[to attain him\]”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**salam kalanda sem sadaik kaṟutta kaṇḍan** – rudran who has reddish matted hair, within which he had contained the entire waters of river gangā, and who has a throat with bluish colour derived from consuming the hālakāla poison which came out during the churning of *tiruppāṟkadal* (milky ocean). His holding waters of gangā and having bluish throat indicate his strength. It is also indicated, due to his reddish hair, that he derived the strength by performing penance. The reason for explaining about his strength is due to the fact that it was due to his ego which he had as a result of his strength that he severed his father’s head. Just as the *satva guṇas* (noble qualities) of eminent people would be the refuge for other eminent people, strength derived from ego of those with *tamō guṇas* (qualities of ignorance) would be the reason for harming others.

**veṇ talai** – just as tirumangai āḻvār had said in periya tirumoḻi 6-9-1 “*mudaiyadartta siram ēndi*” (holding brahmā’s skull which was having foul smell), since rudran severed one of the five heads of brahmā, it got stuck to his hand due to the curse. Since he was holding it for a long time, all the blood etc in that skull had got dried off fully, leaving a white coloured skull in rudran’s hand.

**pulan kalanga uṇḍa** – rudra recalled the time when he had the strength and wealth due to which many took refuge under him and his present condition of lowliness due to which he had to beg for food which he had to take in that skull due to his act of offence.

**pādagattan van tuyar keda** – to remove the severe grief which came about due to the sin of severing the head of brahmā who was eminent, who had created the worlds and who was also his father. The term *van tuyar* (severe grief) indicates that there was no expiation for this sin and that this could not be got rid of by experiencing it fully too. Only through the supreme entity’s omnipotence could this be removed.

**alangal mārvil vāsa nīr koduttavan** – one who filled up that skull with fragrant water from his divine chest which had the divine garland as the symbol for his supremacy of being the protector of all. The reason for adding the term *alangal mārbu* as an adjective for emperumān is – he is the protector for all, irrespective of any distinction between those who come to him for other benefits or who come, seeking himself. *vāsa nīr* denotes that the water came from his divine form which has all the fragrances, just as cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 says of emperumān “*sarva ganda*: *sarva rasa*:” (he is the repository of the good fragrances and all the good tastes). In the 42<sup>nd</sup> pāsuram this water was shown as blood through the term “*ūṟu sengurudi*” (secreted with reddish blood) to indicate the trueness of the incarnation, that even when he incarnates with aprākruta (divine) form/qualities, he appears to have a prākruta (worldly) form.

**adutta sīr nalangoḷ mālai** – sarvēśvaran, who has the eminence of having auspicious qualities which are apt for his basic nature.

**naṇṇum vaṇṇam eṇṇu** – keep thinking of his mercy which is the means to attain him. There is no doubt that emperumān, who had removed the sin of rudran, who had ego, will remove ego and possessiveness of ours too, we being those who do not consider other benefits. All that you have to do is to keep thinking of his quality of compassion alone which is the means to attain him. In other words, there is no way by which we can attain him through our efforts. We have to remain firm with the thought that he will grant himself through his quality of compassion.

**nenjamē vāḻi** – Oh my heart, let this state of firmness happen to you!

Next, we will take up the 114<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 114. Inamaya ettu

*īnamāya ettu nīkki ēdaminṟi mīdu pōyvānam āḷa vallaiyēl vaṇangi vāḻttu en nenjamējñānamāgi nā(jñā)yiṟāgi jñālamuṟṟum ōr eyiṟṟuēnamāyidanda mūrti endai pādam eṇṇiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

en nenjamē – Oh, my mind!; īnamāya – causing deficiency (of contraction in knowledge); ettu – the five entities of avidyā (ignorance), karma (deeds of vice and virtue), vāsanā (impression of worldly matters), ruci (repeatedly engaging with worldly pursuits) and prakruti sambandam (connection with primordial matter, our physical forms) and the three tāpams (ādyātmikam, ādi bhaudikam and ādi daivikam); nīkki – removing; ēdaminṟi – being relieved of all the sorrows; mīdu pōy – going up (over leelā vibhūti, through the path of arcis (light)); vānam āḷa vallaiyēl – if you desire to enjoy parama padam; jñānamāgi – as one who grants ātmajñānam (knowledge about ātmā); jñāyiṟāgi – being the cause for getting knowledge through senses, like sūriyan (sun); jñālam muṟṟum – entire world; ōr eyiṟṟu – through the unique tusks; ēnamāy – as wild boar; īdanda – one who dug out; mūrti – one who has divine auspicious form; endai – my svāmy’s (lord’s); pādam – the divine feet; eṇṇi – thinking \[of them\] as the means; vaṇangi vāḻttu – try to bow down and praise

**Перевод:**

**Oh, my mind! If you desire to reach parama padam through the path of light, after getting rid of eight entities which are hurdles causing deficiency, try to bow down to the divine feet of my svāmy who incarnated as the wild boar to dig out earth from deluge through his unique tusks, who grants us knowledge about ātmā and who is the cause for getting knowledge through senses, like sun, who has a divine auspicious form; try to praise him.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction āḻvār’s heart asked him “If he is the means, what is that we have to do?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram, saying “Keep thinking that it is the divine feet of śrī varāhap perumān (incarnation as wild boar), who lifted earth which had got immersed in the deluge, which is our means, helping us with granting knowledge at the beginning and ending with taking us to parama padam. Until this śarīram (physical body) lasts, keep praising him, in order to pass your time purposefully”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**īnamāya ettum nīkki** – removing eight characteristics which cause lowliness in samsāris. These eight are: (1) *avidyā* (ignorance), (2) *karma* (deeds of virtue and vice), (3) *vāsanā* (impression, in worldly matters, which has been present since time immemorial), (4) *ruci* (repeated engagement with worldly pursuits), (5) *prakruti sambandam* (connection with the physical form), and the three diseases of (6) *ādyātmikam* (caused by self through physical and mental faculties), (7) *ādi bhaudikam* (caused by other creations) and (8) *ādi daivikam* (caused by dēvatās).  Since all these eight entities cause contraction in knowledge of the jīvātmā, they are called as *īnam*. *īnam* refers to anything which causes lowliness.

**ēdaminṟi** – getting rid of all the grief in this samsāram; Since the 8 causes \[listed previously\] are removed, all the grief caused by these 8 will also get removed, *ēdam* – grief.

**mīdu pōy vānam āḷa vallaiyēl** – reaching parama padam which is above this leelā vibhūti through the path of arcis (light). Just as cāndōjña upaniśat 7-25-2 says “*sa svarāt bhavati*” (that muktātmā becomes *svatantran* (independent) not under the control of karma (deeds)), if you had prayed for life without any deficiency.

**vaṇangi vāḻttu en nenjamē** – Oh my heart, which has been helpful to me in enjoying emperumān instead of leading me in your way! For spending time purposefully, try and bow down to his divine feet and to praise him.

If bowing down to the divine feet and praising is the way to spend time purposefully, what is the means to achieve this? āḻvār says through the last two lines that it is only his divine feet.

**jñānamāgi nāyiṟāgi** – being the knowledge which removes the inner darkness of ignorance, and being sūryan (sun) which removes the outer darkness, manifesting the outer entities. Through this, it has been mentioned that emperumān alone is the cause for knowledge about internal ātmā, and for knowledge about external objects which are known through sensory organs. Only if external objects are known as they are, would it be possible to let go of them. Only if ātmā is known as it is, would it be possible to have desire in the ultimate benefit, which is appropriate to the *ātmasvarūpam* (basic nature of ātmā). Thus, it is only sarvēśvaran who grants all types of knowledge.

**jñālamuṟṟum ōreyiṟṟu ēnamāy idanda mūrti** – one who incarnated as the wild boar, dug out earth with his unmatched tusks and who is with a distinguished divine form. Through the term *murti* it refers to emperumān who has a divine form which is apt to be enjoyed by nityasūris \[permanent dwellers of parama padam\], having the six distinguished qualities of *jñānam* (knowledge), *śakti* (strength), *balam* (strength), *aiśvaryam* (wealth), *vīryam* (valour) and *tējas* (resplendence). The indication here is that emperumān, with such a divine form, incarnated as boar, which is not apt for his eminence, and lifted up earth which had got immersed in deluge.

**endai** – this could be construed as either one who manifested that quality of affability and made me as his servitor or that the reason for taking such an inapt form is because he is the lord of earth.

**pādam eṇṇiyē** – be firm in considering his divine feet alone as the means. This should be added to *vaṇangi vāḻttu* to get cogent meaning. āḻvār says that just as he lifted earth from deluge as the great boar, he will lift us also, from the ocean of samsāram and that we should have firm faith, worship and praise him. The pāsuram has to be rearranged as “*īnamāya ettu nīkki ēdaminṟi mīdu pōy vāṇmāḷa vallaiyēl – jñālamāgi nāyiṟāgi, jñālam muṟṟum ōr ēnamāy idanda mūrti endai pādam eṇṇiyē vaṇangi vāḻttu en nenjamē”,* to get cogent meaning.

Next, we will take up the 115<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 115. Attanagi annaiyagi

*attanāgi annaiyāgi āḷum empirānumāyottovvāda palpiṟappu oḻittu nammai ātkoḷvānmuttanār mugundanār pugundu nammuḷ mēvinārettināl idarkkadal kidatti ēḻai nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

muttanār – as one who is in mōkśam; mugundanār – being called as mukundan, since he grants mōkśam; ottu – appearing to be identical due to knowledge being the basic nature; ovvāda – appearing to be different, due to differences in the species of birth such as dēva etc; palpiṟappu – various births of ours; oḻittu – removing them; nammai ātkoḷvān – in order to take us as his servitors; attanāgi – as father; annaiyāgi – as mother too; āḷum empirānumāy – being the lord, having us as servitors; pugundu – pervading us; nammuḷ – with us; mēvinār – mingled; ēḻai nenjamē – Oh ignorant heart!; ettināl – for what reason; idarkkadal – in the ocean of grief; kidatti – are you remaining?

**Перевод:**

**emperumān, who is in mōkśam, is called as mukundan since he grants us mōkśam. He removes various births of ours in which we remain as identical because of having knowledge as basic nature, as well as remain different due to the species in which we are born, such as dēva etc. He is our father, he is our mother too and he is the lord, having us as his servitors. Oh, ignorant heart! For what reason are you remaining in the ocean of grief?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had instructed his heart to think that only emperumān’s divine feet are the means. In response, his heart told him “Just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 3-14-2 ‘avākyanādara:’ (paramātmā (supreme entity) is one who does not speak to anyone, due to his majestic nature and who is without any regard (towards any entity)), emperumān does not expect anyone \[to help him\]. We have accumulated many karmas (deeds) which will make us to take many births. Once we think that his divine feet are our means, will he come to protect us?” feeling sad. Addressing that sadness, āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “emperumān has all relationships with us; with that as the reason, he will remove all our hurdles; he considers granting himself to us as his benefit”. just as emperumān had given us refuge, as in bhagavat gītā 18-66 “sarvadarmān parityajya māmēkam śaraṇam vraja l aham tvā sarva pāpēbhyō mōkśayiśyāmi mā śuca: ll” (Giving up all righteous paths (which are considered as means), along with their impressions, attain me alone as the means; I will liberate you from all your sins; do not grieve), āḻvār gives refuge to his heart.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**attanāgi** – as father, who does only good for his children

**annaiyāgi** – as mother, who does only what they like.  A mother will not do the help which a father does. The father will not do the help which the mother does. The indication is that emperumān alone does both types of help. ḍoesn’t mahābhāratam āraṇya parvam 192-56 also say “*sarvēśāmēva lōkānām pitā mātā ca mādava*: l *gaccadvamēnam śaraṇam śaraṇyam puruśarśabhā*: ll” (Oh, the best among men! For all the worlds, it is only *lakśmīnātan* (emperumān, the divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi) who is the lord. Take refuge under him who is apt to be taken refuge under)? There is another way to consider the meanings of these two verses:

**attanāgi annaiyāgi** – the indication is that he creates knowledge and nurtures the created knowledge. īsn’t the ācāryan who creates knowledge considered as father, as mentioned in the verse “*utpādaka brahmapitrōr garīyān brahmada*: *pitā*“\\ (Between the biological father and the father who gave instructions about brahmam, it is only he who gave instructions about brahmam, is considered as eminent)? When the meaning is considered to be in this manner, it explains the verse *jñānamāgi* seen in the previous pāsuram.

**āḷum empirānumāy** – as my lord, who makes me as his servitor. When desire is created from the knowledge referred to earlier, he will be the lord who makes me as his servitor. Through these three relationships, it is indicated that emperumān remains as our relative in all the ways. Has it not been mentioned in subālōpaniśat 16 “*mātā pitā bhrātā nivāsa*: *śaraṇam suhrut gatir nārāyaṇa*:” (nārāyaṇan remains as the mother, father, brother, residence, means to attain mōkśam, friend and one who is attained in mōkśam) as well as in periya tirumoḻi pāsuram 1-1-6 “*empirān endai ennudaic cuṟṟam enakkarasu ennudai vāḻnāḷ*” (He is my benefactor, my father, relative in all ways, my lord and the sustainer of my life)?

**ottovvāda palpiṟappu oḻittu nammai ātkōḷvān** –What is the reason for remaining as my relative in all these ways? Through the next two lines, āḻvār says that it is to remove our hurdles and to accept kainkaryam from us, in line with our basic nature.

**ottovvāda palpiṟappu oḻittu** – being discernible as celestial, human, animal and plant species, removing countless births in each of these species. The term *ottu ovvāda* refers to the identical nature of *ātmasvarūpam* (basic nature of ātmā) on the one hand; on the other hand, to a jīvātmā, the birth of a child etc is desirable while the birth of enemy is undesirable. Alternatively, this could be construed as being identical through one form and being different through another form. The indication is that while all the jīvātmās are identical since they have knowledge as their basic nature, when looked at in terms of their forms, they differ as celestial, human, animal and plant species. Has it not been mentioned in mudal tiruvandādi 49 “*magiḻalagonṟē pōl māṟum pal yākkai*” (just as the seed of *magiḻam* tree \[a flower bearing tree, which has a sweet fragrance\] can be kept in a restricted area or an expansive place, the ātmā, based on its karma, could enter either the body of an ant or of a brahmā), taking births repeatedly?

**nammai ātkoḷvān** – in order to accept kainkaryam \[servitude\] from us, who have been samsāris since time immemorial, just as he accepts kainkaryam from nityasūris; since his \[āḻvār’s\] divine mind has been with him in all the births, starting with the first birth during creation, he is including his mind too along with him and uses the term *nammai* \[us\].

**muttanār** – one who does not have even the impression of samsāram in him. When referring to those who had got liberated, the term *muktar* is used. This will mean that the person had been in samsāram and has got rid of it at one point of time. In emperumān’s matter, it is not the case of *pradvamsābhāvam* (present at one point of time and disappearing at another point of time); instead, it is the case of *prāgabhāvam* (never present at all) when it comes to connection with samsāram. īsn’t emperumān always the opposite of anything which is lowly? Through this verse, the distinction with muktas has been mentioned.

**mugundanār** – Since the syllable *mu* refers to mukti (liberation) and the syllable *ku* refers to earth, from etymology, mukum – “*muktibhūmim dadāti iti mukunda*:” this term would refer to one who grants the land of liberation. Since nityasūris, who have had no connection with samsāram since time immemorial, have not been differentiated through the term *muttanār*, this term \[mugundanār\] by bringing in the ability for emperumān to grant mōkśam, which nityasūris do not have, differentiates nityasūris from emperumān. Thus, through the two terms \[muttanār and mugundanār\] emperumān’s being complete with qualities such as *jñāna, śakti* etc which are needed to remove our hurdles and his magnanimity in granting mōkśam have been mentioned.

**pugundu nammuḷ mēvinār** – just as bhagavat gītā ślōkam 16-19 mentions “*tānaham dviśata*:*krūrān samsārēśu narādamān* l *kśipāmyajas, aśubhānāsurīśvēva yōniśu* ll”  
(I push those who hate me, who are cruel, who are the lowliest among humans and who are inauspicious, into samsāram, that too in monstrous clans), in our hearts, where we have been acting in such a way that emperumān had given up on us for a very long time, emperumān entered and mingled with us harmoniously. āḻvār says that without looking at our lowliness and his eminence, affirming that he will take responsibility for all our deeds, emperumān pervaded his mind.

**ettināl idarkkadal kidatti** – why are you immersed in the ocean of sorrow? Is it because we know everything which will be beneficial for us and he does not know? Is it because we have the strength to carry out beneficial acts for us and he does not have? Is it because we have the qualification to carry out our activities ourselves and he does not have that qualification? When we are ignorant, incapable and without any qualification and he is omniscient, omnipotent and fully qualified, and when he is ready to help us without looking at his eminence and comes down to our low levels to help us, why are you languishing in the ocean of sorrow?

**ēḻai nenjamē** – Oh ignorant heart! Before engaging with matters related to emperumān, you did not feel even sorry that “We are without any connection with him” and were in an ignorant state, thinking as if you were sitting atop an elephant. Now, after engaging with him, after he has taken responsibility for our actions, feeling sorrowful is only an act of ignorance, says the āḻvār.

*ēḻaiyar* – without knowledge.

The pāsuram has to be rearranged as “*muttanār mugundanār – ottovvāda palpiṟappu oḻittu nammai ātkoḷvān – attanāgi annaiyāgi Alum empirānumāy – pugundu nammuḷ mēvinār; ēḻai nenjamē – ettināl idarkkadal kidatti”*.

Next, we will take up the 116<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 116. Maru seyda

*māṟu seyda vāḷarakkan nāḷulappa anṟu ilangainīṟu seydu senṟu konṟu venṟi koṇḍa vīranārvēṟu seydu tammuḷ ennai vaittidāmaiyāl namankūṟu seydu koṇḍu iṟanda kuṟṟam eṇṇa vallanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māṟu seyda – one who came opposing; vāḷ – one who had the weapon; arakkan – rāvaṇan’s; nāḷ – life; ulappa – to be decimated; anṟu – on that day (when he separated sītāp pirātti); nīṟu seydu – burnt it to ashes; senṟu – went with an army of monkeys; konṟu – killed (rāvaṇa, along with his relatives); venṟi koṇḍa – one who became victorious; vīranār – the great warrior, son of emperor daśaratan; tammuḷ – in his divine mind; ennai vēṟu seydu – making me different; vaittidāmaiyāl – since he did not keep; naman – yaman; kūṟu seydu koṇḍu – separating me from perumāḷ; iṟanda kuṟṟam – past faults; eṇṇa vallanē – is he capable of thinking?

**Перевод:**

**The great warrior, śrī rāma, son of emperor daśaratan, on that day, went to lankā with an army of monkeys, burning it down to ashes. He killed rāvaṇa, along with his relatives, decimating the life of rāvaṇa, who came opposing him, with his weapon. When that śrī rāma did not keep me differently in his mind \[accepting me as his servitor\], is yaman \[the lord of righteousness\] capable of thinking of my previous faults and separating me from perumāḷ?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said “emperumān had destroyed our karmas which were responsible for our taking countless births and has accepted us as his servitor”. His divine heart told him ”Are we not in the position of being punished by yama, having committed sins which are responsible for taking us to hell?” āḻvār responds in this pāsuram “After the divine son of emperor daśaratan, who is our refuge, has accepted us, could yama analyse our faults?”*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**māṟu seyda** – \[rāvaṇan\] opposed śrī rāma who was loving towards his enemies too, just as it has been brought out in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 50-56 “*sakhē*! *rāghava*! *darmajña*! *ripuṇāmapi vatsala*! l *abhyanujñātum iccāmi gamiśyāmi yatāgatam* ll”  
(\[garuda tells śrī rāma\] Oh friend! Oh, one who appeared in the clan of raghu! Oh, one who knows righteousness! Oh, one who has motherly forbearance even towards enemies! I would like to take leave of you. Let me go back the way I came) and in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 18-34 “*ānayainam hariśrēśta dattamasyābhayam mayā* l *vibhīśaṇō vā sugrīva*! *yadi vā rāvaṇa*: *svayam* ll” (Oh, sugrīva, the distinguished among monkeys! Bring him, in here. Whether he is vibhīśaṇa or he is rāvaṇa himself, he has been granted refuge by me). Unlike his younger brother vibhīśaṇa who was favourable towards śrī rāma, instead of escaping from the wrath of emperumān, since he was wicked, he ended up opposing śrī rāma.

**vāḷ arakkan nāḷ ulappa** – to destroy the life of rāvaṇa, who was bestowed with a sword by śankaran. Since he felt that he had the strength with the sword given by Sivan and the boon of long life granted by brahmā, he thought “When I have this sword with me, no one can defeat me; with the life that I have obtained through the boon, no one can kill me”. Despite knowing that śrī rāma had killed vāli who was stronger than himself \[rāvaṇa\] and that he had coronated sugrīva, trusting his sword and his longevity, rāvaṇa came opposing śrī rāma.

**vāḷ aṟakkan nāḷ ulappa** – to destroy that sword and that life of rāvaṇa. emperumān destroyed those which rāvaṇa had thought were his strengths. u*lappu* – end; In tiruvāimoḻi pāsurams 5-8-4 “*ulappilānē*”and 6-10-1 “*ulappil kīrttiyammānē*” the world *ulappu* has been used in the same meaning.

**anṟu** – on that day when rāvaṇa had nurtured enmity with śrī rāmā, just as it has been mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 5-7-7 “*suri kuḻaṟkanivāyt tiruvinaip piritta kodumaiyil kaduvisai arakkan*” (rāvaṇan who showed cruel haste in separating sītāp pirātti, who had distinguished hair and reddish lips). Apart from harassing sages et al, who were noble, apart from destroying the abodes of celestial entities, apart from crossing emperumān’s order just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-13-14 “*na yaśtavyam na dātavyam na hōtavyam katancana* l *bhōktā yajñasya kastvanyō hyaham yajñapati*: *prabhu*: ll”  
(*yāgam* (ritual) should never be carried out; charity should never be given; *hōmam* (another type of ritual) should not be done. More than me (vēna maharāja) who can be the enjoyer of *yajñam*? Am I not the lord for *yajñam* and the one who gives the benefit for the *yajñam*?) did he not carry out the most intolerable act of separating the divine couple of sītā and śrī rāma, who were the lifeline of entire world, keeping them on either side of the ocean and nurture enmity?

**ilangai nīṟu seydu** – even though rāvaṇa had nurtured enmity like this, emperumān had thought in his divine mind “Let us attempt to make him as a favourable person and prevent him from getting destroyed”. He sent hanuman as a messenger and made him to burn down lankā.

**senṟu** – since rāvaṇa did not change into a favourable person, śrī rāma went from riśyamūkha parvam to the shore of ocean, built a dam across the ocean which was like a protective fortress for lankā, reached lankā and lay siege on it.

**konṟu** – even after all these happened, just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*dvidā bhajyēyamapyēvam na namēyam tu kasyacit* l *ēśa mē sahajō dōśa*: *svabhāvō duratikrama*: ll” (Even if split into two, I will not bow down to anyone like this. This is my fault, from birth. One’s nature cannot be crossed), the intransigent behaviour of rāvaṇa did not change. Hence, emperumān killed rāvaṇa along with his relatives.

**venṟi koṇḍa vīranār** – he became victorious over rāvaṇa and stood with the wealth of valour.

**vēṟu seydu tammuḷ ennai vaittidāmaiyāl** – since he did not think \[of me\] in his divine mind “He is different from us”. *tammūḷ vēṟu seydu* refers to emperumān thinking in his divine mind “We are the *kartā* (doer) for this person’s deeds and we are the enjoyer of the results”. Normally, this is how emperumān’s thoughts will be towards matters related to samsāris. The term *ennai* refers to me who has surrendered to his divine feet, submitting all my responsibilities \[to sustain myself\]. The implication is that since he has kept me, who has surrendered to him, in his divine mind in such a way that he will consider my enemies as his enemies, he will not make me to experience the results of all the sinful deed which I had carried out since time immemorial.

**naman kūṟu seydu koṇḍu** – yaman, who analyses the faults carried out by everyone and metes out punishment accordingly, could estimate my faults and separate me from emperumān. *kūṟu seygai* – separating. The indication is that while the masculine divine son of emperor daśaratan has thought of me as belonging to him, could it be possible for yaman to separate me from emperumān? In the first two lines of this pāsuram, āḻvār is mercifully speaking about the divine son of emperor daśaratan. Just as it has been said in perumāḷ tirumoḻi 10-10 “*anṟu carācarangaḷai vaigundattēṟṟi*” (On that day, making all the movable and immovable jīvātmās to ascend to parama padam), discounting the sinful deeds of animals and plants which would make them to attain yaman, emperumān showered mercy on them and took them with him to parama padam. āḻvār is thinking of this distinction and says this way.

**iṟanda kuṟṟam eṇṇa vallanē** – is he capable of estimating the sinful deeds carried out since time immemorial? Through the term *iṟanda kuṟṟam*, āḻvār is referring to the *sancita karma* which is the collection of all the sinful deeds carried out in previous births and the sinful deeds carried out already in this birth, for which one has not felt remorse and for which one has not carried out any atonement. For jīvātmās in samsāram, these faults have to be got rid of only by experiencing the results \[punishments\] for these acts. For those who have surrendered to emperumān, he removes these by his mercy. The term *eṇṇa vallanē* refers to yaman not in a position to even think that this jīvātmā has carried out sinful deed, sitting in his abode.

Next, we will take up the 117<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 117. Accanoyodu allal

*accanōyodu allal piṟappu āya mūppu ivaivaitta sindai vaitta ākkai māṟṟi vānil ēṟṟuvānaccudan anandakīrtti ādi andam illavannaccu nāgaṇaik kidanda nādan vēda gīdanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

accam – fear; nōy odu – disease, along with these; allal – mental agonies; mūppu – old age; pal piṟappu – many births; āya ivai – all these; vaitta sindai – mind, which experiences these; vaitta ākkai – physical form which is the abode for all these; māṟṟi – avoiding; vānil – to parama padam; ēṟṟuvān – one who has the basic nature of making the jīvātmā to ascend to parama padam; accudan – one who will never let slip his followers from his hands; ananda kīrtti – (as a result of that) having boundless eminence; ādi andam illavan – having no beginning or end (to the activities which he carries out for his; naccu – spitting poison towards those who ae unfavourable; nāgaṇai – on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); kidanda – one who is reclining; nādan – one who is the lord of all; vēda gīdan – it is only emperumān who is praised like this by vēdams

**Перевод:**

**Avoiding physical forms and, mind, which resides in these forms and which leads to fear, diseases, mental agonies, old age and countless births, emperumān has the nature of making a jīvātmā to ascend to parama padam. He will never let slip his followers from his hands, and has boundless fame as a result of this. There is no beginning or end for the activities which he carries out for the benefit of his followers. He reclines on the mattress of ādiśēśan, who spits poison towards those who are unfavourable to emperumān. It is only emperumān, who is the lord of all, who is praised like this, by vēdams.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that karmas which beget punishment from yama will not affect emperumān’s followers. Even if these are removed, prārabda karmam (karmas which have started yielding end results) and which are responsible for begetting many births are still there. Hence, many sorrows will arise due to that. When this query was raised, āḻvār responds in this pāsuram saying that when emperumān is there to remove those karmas along with the physical forms which are required to experience the results of those karmas and to take us towards parama padam, there is no deficiency in the benefits which we will attain. Past karmas, called as pūrvāgham are composed of three parts: (1) sancita karmas, which give countless physical forms \[to experience and get rid of those karmas\], (2) pāpa karmas (sinful deeds) which are responsible for going to narakam (hell) and (3) prārabda karmas which have started yielding the results and which could be got rid of, through experiencing the results, in about 8 to 10 births. Among these three, for an upāsakan, who attempts to attain mōkśam through the means of bhakti (devotion to emperumān), all the karmas other than prārabda karmas will get destroyed \[through emperumān’s divine mind\]. To get rid of the prārabda karmas, he has to keep taking births and experience the results. For a prapannan (one who surrenders to emperumān), through the mercy of emperumān, among the prārabda karmas, whatever has to be experienced in this birth alone will remain; all the rest will get destroyed.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**accam nōyōdu allal pal piṟappāya mūppivai** – fear, disease which leads to grief for the physical form; along with these, mental agony, called as *ādi*, old age, many births, all these. While fear is part of the mental agony which has been termed as *ādi*, the reason for referring to it at the beginning exclusively is because both *ādi* and *vyādi* (diseases) are common to everything. In other words, looking at the symptoms which are the indications of ensuing disease, the agony which one feels in the mind is fear. It is common to both agonies of *ādi* and *vyādi*. *vyādi* is that which creates agonies in the physical form, such as head ache etc; *ādi* is that which creates mental agonies such as *kāmam* (desire), *kōpam (anger), lōbham* (greed), *mōham* (bewilderment), *madam* (pride), *mātsaryam* (jealousy) etc. Combining the words “*nōyōdallal*” is due to the fact that among the three types of *tāpam* (pain), in *ādyātmikam* (that which is caused by self) which affects both the body and mind, both are present. This is representative of the other two *tāpams* which are caused by *ādibaudikam* (pain caused by other creations) and *ādi daivikam* (pain, caused by dēvatās, such as thunder, lightning etc). These three constitute *tāpatrayam* (three types of pain). Reference to *mūppu* (old age) is again representative of the other two stages of childhood and youth. Since it causes many births, āḻvār calls *prārabda karmas* as *pal piṟappu*. The term *āya ivai* refers to all the comforts and the sorrows which we experience with our physical forms.

**vaitta sindai** – the mind which thinks of all these

**vaitta ākkai** – the physical form which is the abode for that mind

**māṟṟi** – getting rid of the physical and mental pains which we suffer, which are the results of *prārabda karmas*, the sensory organs which experience these pains, the physical body which is the repository of all these.

**vānil ēṟṟuvān** – one who has the nature of lifting us to parama padam, where there is no space even to think of these, just as it has been said in cāndōjña upaniśat 8-12-3 “*nōpajanam smarannidam śarīram*” (the muktātmā \[who has been liberated from samsāram\] does not even think of his \[just left behind\] physical form which is lying near his dear ones).

Who is the one having this as his nature?

**accudan** – one who does not let slip of those who have attained his divine feet.

**anandakīrtti** – one who has unbounded fame as a result of protecting his followers like this.

**ādiyandam illavan** – one who is without any beginning and end. In other words, this speaks about the benefits which emperumān is intending to carry out for his followers, which are without a beginning and an end.

**naccu nāgaṇaik kidanda nādan** – one who manifests his quality of never separating from his followers through his act of reclining on ādiśēśan. ādiśēśan spits poison on the enemies of emperumān and has the qualities of softness, coolness and fragrance, typical of snakes. One who reclines on such ādiśēśan.

**nādan** – the indication is that the reason for his not letting slip his followers and taking incarnations to help his followers, even lowering himself, is because he is the lord of all.

**vēda gīdanē** – one who has been praised in nārāyaṇa anuvākam as *acyutan*, who does not let go of his followers and as the lord of all jīvātmās, appropriate for their basic nature. Hasn’t this been mentioned resoundingly in taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 11 “*patim viśvasyātmēśvaram śāśvatam śivam acyutam nārāyaṇam”* (nārāyaṇan alone is the lord of all the worlds, who is the lord for himself \[i.e. he does not have a lord\], who is always auspicious and who does not let go of his followers)?

This pāsuram is in the manner of āḻvār mercifully telling his heart, .

Next, we will take up the 118<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 118. Sollinum tolirkanum

*sollinum toḻiṟkaṇum todakkaṟāda anbinumalli nanpagalinōdu āna mālai kālaiyumvalli nāṇmalarkkiḻatti nāda pāda pōdinaippulliyuḷḷam viḷvilādu pūṇḍu mīṇḍadillaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

valli nāḷ malar kiḻatti nāda – Oh, the lord of periya pirāttiyār who is your consort, who has a well spread out, just blossomed lotus as her residence!; sollinum – in words; toḻil kaṇum – in engagements with physical body; todakku – continuity; aṟāda – without change; anbinum – in devotion; nal – being good; allinōdu Ana – being together with night; mālai – at dusk; nal pagalinōdu (Ana) – being together with the pleasant morning; kālaiyum – at dawn; pāda pōdinai – your divine feet which are like lotus; uḷḷam – mind; pulli – embracing; viḷvilādu – without separating; pūṇḍu – engaging very well; mīṇḍadillaiyē – has not taken a step back (from that experience of divine feet)

**Перевод:**

**Oh, the lord of periya pirāttiyār who is residing on a well spread-out lotus which has just blossomed, and who is your divine consort! My mind is embracing your divine feet which are like lotus, without separating ever, both during dusk when day is together with the pleasant night and at dawn when night is together with the pleasant morning, through words, physical body and continuous devotion, without any change. It is engaging very well, without taking a step back from the experience of your divine feet.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār had, in the previous pāsuram “vaitta sindai vaitta ākkai māṟṟi”, said that emperumān would remove our hurdles up to the prārabda karmas (deeds which have started yielding results); also, in line with his pāsuram 112 “meeḷvilāda bōgam nalgavēṇḍum“, pāsuram 115 “nammai ātkoḷvān” and pāsuram 117 “vānil ēṟṟuvān“, he had told his divine heart that emperumān has taken as his deed, āḻvār carrying out brahmānubhava kainkaryams (servitude, with the rich experience of emperumān). Even though he does not have any means with himself, should he not have the involvement in attaining him, who is the ultimate benefit? In this pāsuram, he makes an appeal to emperumān regarding the involvement which his divine heart has towards attaining emperumān.*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**sollinum toḻiṟkaṇum todakkaṟāda anbinum** – reciting prayers and the divine names \[of emperumān\] through the faculty of speech, carrying out activities such as circumambulation, performing *anjali* (in salutation of emperumān) which are done through the faculty of physical body and having devotion which is filled in the faculty of mind, thus through the three faculties of *manōvākkāyam* (mind, speech and body) having engagement only with emperumān. The term *todakkaṟāda* mentions that this devotion is without any discontinuity. For others, *bhagavat bhakti* (devotion towards emperumān) will coexist with engagement with worldly matters. For āḻvār, *bhagavat anubhavam* (experiencing emperumān) will be the way of life.

**allum nanpagalinōdu Ana mālai kālaiyum** – the verse should be separated as “*allōdu Ana mālaiyum pagalinōdu Ana kālaiyum*” to get the correct meaning. The implied meaning is that (1) during night time, when it is pitch dark and one’s desire goes out towards engaging with women, (2) during day time, when one sets out to engage with work for earning livelihood, (3) along with the evening time of dusk when it is together with night and (4) the morning time of dawn when it is together with daytime, during all the time, only emperumān was engaged with, just as he was engaged with through all the three faculties. The adjective *nan* (as in *nanpagalinōdu*), refers to good \[or pleasant\]; thus, āḻvār mercifully says good morning and good night as well as to good dawn and good dusk, since all these times are spent only in *bhagavat anubhavam*.

**valli nāḷ malark kiḻatti** – since she has the well spread out, just then blossomed lotus flower as her residence, she excels that flower in fragrance, tenderness etc. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum*” (I will not separate \[from emperumān\] even for a moment), since she does not separate from him at any point of time, oh the supreme entity who falls head over heels and enjoys her! The term *valli* (creeper) refers to periya pirāttiyār who is slender like a creeper, having just blossomed lotus as her dwelling. Through this, it is indicated that it is only the togetherness of emperumān and periya pirāttiyār which is apt to be attained for his divine mind.

**pādapōdinaip pulli** – holding on to your divine feet which are like lotus flower. Thus, until now, it has been indicated that āḻvār was holding on to the divine feet of emperumān, who is the divine consort of periya pirāttiyār, through all three faculties, at all the times. *pulgudal* – embracing.

**uḷḷam vilvilādu pūṇḍu** – with my mind engaging \[with you\] very much, without any discontinuity.

**mīṇḍadillaiyē** – starting to enjoy the togetherness of perumāḷ and pirātti, and not feeling satiated, not coming out of that even to enjoy their auspicious qualities.

Next, we will take up the 119<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam.

## 119. Ponni sul

*ponni – by te divine river kāvirisūḻ – being surroundedarangam mēya – one vho has mercifully come to te templepūvai vaṇṇa – one vho has te complexion of a purple bilberry flovermāya – ōh periya perumāḷ, vho is an amazing entity!kēḷ – you śould mercifully listenennadu āvi ennum – knovn as my ātmāvalvinaiyuḷ – inside te heap of strong sinskoḻundu eḻundu – (called as diligence tovards you) sprout coming outunna pādam enna ninṟa – mentioned in vēdāntams (te second part of vēdams dealing vit emperumān) as your divine auspicious formoṇ sudar – as a unique effulgencekoḻu malar – in te tender divine form vhic is like a huge flovermanna vandu – coming firmlypūṇḍu – engagingvāttaminṟi – vitout deficiencyengum – in your svarūpam (basic nature), rūpam (divine form), guṇam (auspicious qualities) and vibhūti (vealt), everyvhereninṟadē – pervaded*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**Oh, periya perumāḷ, who is an amazing entity, who has the complexion of purple bilberry flower, who has mercifully come to the temple surrounded by the divine river kāviri! Inside my ātmā, which is like a heap of sins, my diligence has sprouted to engage with your divine form which has a unique splendour and is like a huge flower. It is engaging without any deficiency with your svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam and vibhūti, pervading everywhere.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān asks āḻvār in his divine mind “Your mind, which is an acētanam (without knowledge) and which is an aid for ātmā, has affection for us, just like a kartā (doer). This huge love which you have towards us is something which we have not seen in samsāram. Why did this huge love develop towards us?” āḻvār says in this pāsuram “īsn’t this the benefit for your attempt to make a substance out of me, showing your divine beauty and quality of simplicity?” ponnisūḻ arangamēya pūvai vaṇṇa māya kēḷennadāvi ennum valvinaiyuḷ koḻundu eḻunduunnapādam enna ninṟa oṇsudark koḻumalarmanna vandu pūṇḍu vāttam inṟi engum ninṟadē Word-0by-Word Meanings*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**ponnisūḻ arangamēya pūvai vaṇṇa** – Oh, one with the divine complexion of a purple-coloured bilberry flower, who resides for me constantly in śrī rangam, which is surrounded by the river kāviri. īsn’t the divine river kāviri like a garland on the shoulder, flowing on both sides of the temple, as a decorative ornament! Just as virajā river has surrounded parama padam, kāviri has surrounded śrī rangam. emperumān resides constantly in parama padam in order to be enjoyed by nityasūris who do not have any connection with samsāram. He resides in tiruppāṟkadal in order to make the dwellers of śvētadvīpam, which is in its proximity, to enjoy him occasionally. You reside in śrī rangam constantly in order to create an involvement in me, who is a heap of sins, towards you and to make me enjoy you. Through the term *pūvai vaṇṇa*, it has been shown that he has a divine form which attracts others towards him and which is like a purple-coloured bilberry flower in terms of tenderness and complexion.

**māya** – Oh, one who is an amazing entity! The implied meaning is that he has an amazing affability and simplicity which cannot be seen in anyone else.

**kēḷ** – you should mercifully listen to how I had developed an involvement through your divine beauty and simplicity. Even if you, who had helped me in developing this, had forgotten, I, who am thankful towards you, will not forget.

**ennadāvi ennum valvinaiyuḷ koḻundeḻundu** – being called as *ātmā*, which is a heap of sins, an involvement towards you sprouted. ḍidn’t this involvement, which normally comes after getting rid of sins which are hurdles in carrying out *karmayōgam* (attaining emperumān through the path of deeds) come in me even when I had a heap of sins, due to the eminence of your divine beauty? āḻvār is thinking of his previous state of being the repository of sins instead of thinking that his *svarūpam* (basic nature) is one of *jñānam* and *ānandam* (knowledge and happiness, respectively).

**unna pādam enna ninṟa oṇsudark koḻumalar** – Here, the word *pādam* represents emperumān’s tirumēni (divine form).  The divine form which has been mentioned in vēdāntams as being effulgent and as being soft, like a flower. The implied meaning is that just as it has been said in rig vēda aśtakam 2-2-25 “*yuvā’kumāra*:” (one who is more in the stage of youth, with a little bit of adolescence), the reason for the appearance of involvement in him is the divine form which is at the start of the youthful state, and which is very tender. Since āḻvār is a *śēśabhūtar* (servitor), he calls the divine form as *pādam* (divine feet).

**manna vandu pūṇḍu** – engaging \[with emperumān’s divine feet\], without any separation. *pūṇḍu* indicates that emperumān’s divine feet were solely engaged with him, instead of going to anyone else.

**vāttaminṟi engum ninṟadē** – without being disturbed by the lowly entities of worldly pursuits such as śabdam (sound) etc, the involvement stood fixed on your *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam, vibhūti¸*in all these. This involvement initially held on to his divine form. This divine form, since it manifests the six important qualities of *jñāna, bala, aiśvarya, vīrya, śakti* and *tējas* (knowledge, strength, wealth, valour, power and splendour respectively), impelled that involvement in these six qualities. Those qualities urged that involvement to engage with *divyātma svarūpam* (the divine basic nature of emperumān’s ātmā) which is their abode. That *divyātma svarūpam* impelled āḻvār’s involvement in the *ubhayavibhūti* (the spiritual realm of parama padam and the materialistic realm of samsāram) which are controlled by it. Thus, the indication is that in my (āḻvār’s) involvement and devotion, your (emperumān’s) *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam* and *vibhūti* became immersed.

Next, we will take up the 120<sup>th</sup> which is the final pāsuram of this prabandam.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
