## 120. Iyakkarada

*iyakkaṟāda palpiṟappil ennai māṟṟi inṟu vanduuyakkoṇ mēgavaṇṇan naṇṇi ennilāya tannuḷēmayakkinān tan mannu sōdi ādalāl ennāvidāniyakkelām aṟuttaṟāda inba vīdu pēṟṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

iyakku aṟāda – without change in continuity; pal piṟappil – from the multitude of births; ennai – me (who is ignorant); māṟṟi – in order to take; inṟu vandu – (without any reason) coming (when I do not remember) today; uyakkoḷ – one who uplifted me; mēgavaṇṇan – one who has his divine form like a cloud; naṇṇi – coming near (to the place where I was); tannil āya ennuḷē – in my heart which is united with him; than – his; mannu – permanent; sōdi – splendorous divine auspicious form; mayakkinān – mingled (inseparably); ādalāl – hence; iyakku elām – avidyai (ignorance), karmam (past deeds) etc which follow one another; aṟuttu – destroyed; aṟāda inba vīdu – mōkśa vīdu (the abode of parama padam) which is without an end, and which is full of happiness; peṟṟadu – attained

**Перевод:**

**In order to uplift me, who has been having multitude of births, without any change in that continuity, emperumān, who has the divine form like a cloud, came to where I was and united his splendorous, auspicious divine form with me. As result, he destroyed my avidyai, karmas, vāsanā, ruci, dēha sambandam etc completely, enabling my ātmā to attain the abode of parama padam, which is without an end and which is full of happiness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that for the affection which was born out of emperumān’s divine beauty, that divine beauty itself became the target, through the verse “unna pādam enna ninṟa oṇsudark koḻumalar manna vandu pūṇḍu“. In this pāsuram, he says that just like when an elephant is lying down, someone getting atop it with his efforts, he had not held on to emperumān’s divine form. Without any reason, since periya perumāḷ kept his divine form in āḻvār’s mind, without separating it from him, āḻvār mercifully says in this pāsuram “I received permanent kainkaryam with unique brahmānandam (happiness of experiencing emperumān), getting rid of all hurdles”. Just as nammāḻvār had said in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-10 “en avāvaṟac cūḻndāyē” (you united with me such that all my desires got fulfilled), this āḻvār too expresses his gratitude for the help granted mercifully by emperumān, and brings the prabandam to its end. It is clear from this that svagata svīkāram, which is holding on to emperumān through one’s means, and is similar to getting on top of the elephant which is lying down, is full of pitfalls and that āḻvārs, who saw emperumān when he manifested himself to them, are paragata svīkāra niśtars (those who are firmly rooted in the principle of taking refuge under emperumān when he decides to take them under his refuge)*

**vyākhyānam (Commentary)**

**iyakkaṟāda pal piṟappil ennai māṟṟi** – Just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 2-6-8 “*māṟi māṟip pala piṟappum piṟandu*” (I have been born in multitude of births, continuously), thinking of changing me who had been without knowledge \[about emperumān\] since time immemorial, having been born in the four species of celestial, human, animal and plant, continuously in each of these species. Since it has been said that birth has been continuous, when karma is being experienced with one physical form, it leads to engaging in deeds which would lead to more physical forms, thus not leaving any gap between the physical forms. The word *iyangal* refers to abundance. The syllable *ng* has been transformed to *kk* and the word becomes *iyakkal*. Could *māṟṟi* \[changing\] be construed as *māṟṟa ninaittu* \[thinking of changing\]? In tiruvāimoḻi 1-4-10 “*uyir mudalā muṟṟumāy kadalāḻi nīr tōṟṟi*” (being the controller of all the entities such as ātmā etc, and incarnating in the ocean), the term *āy* (as in *muṟṟumāy*) comes in the meaning of *āvadaṟkāga*. In the same manner, here, the term *māṟṟi* will have the meaning of *māṟṟugaikkāga* which means to think of changing.

**inṟu vandu uyakkoḷ mēga vaṇṇan naṇṇi** – *kārmēgavaṇṇan* (one with the complexion of dark coloured louds), who removed my involvement with other pursuits and gave me knowledge \[about him\] which grew into devotion \[towards him\], approached me where I was even when I had not expected it, without any reason. *naṇṇudal* – to embrace. Since āḻvār had spoken about tiruvarangam in his pāsurams 117 “*naccu nāgaṇaikkidanda nādan*” and 119 “*ponni sūḻ arangamēya pūvai vaṇṇa*”, even in this pāsuram, we should consider that he is speaking only about the divine form of periya perumāḷ \[śrī ranganātan\] when he says *uyakkoḷ mēgavaṇṇan*, considering that divine form of emperumān alone as his means to get uplifted.

**ennilāya tannuḷē** – inside him, when he is within me. The word “en (I)” will not stop merely with me, but will extend up to him \[emperumān\] since he is my a*ntaryāmi* (indvelling soul). Thus, this will refer to “inside my heart”, which is his attribute, never separating from him. īsn’t āḻvār mercifully saying here, what has been said in *vēdavākyams* (sentences in vēdams) such as cāndōjña upaniśat 6-8-7 “*tat tvamasi*” ((Oh śvētakētu!) That brahmam (brahmātmā \[the ātmā of supreme entity\] resides within you)), instructing through the words of an ācāryan (teacher), and kauśīdakī upaniśat vēdavākyam “*aham brahmāsmi*” (I am brahmam (brahmātmā resides within me)) when the muktātmā goes to paramapadam and meditates on the supreme entity! The reason for mentioning the connection here that he is the *śarīram* (physical form) and emperumān is the ātmā (the soul residing inside his *śarīram*) is to show that emperumān considered the deed of āḻvār, who is emperumān’s *śarīram*, as his \[emperumān’s\] deed and helped in carrying it out.

**mayakkinān than mannu sōdi** – he united his divine form, which is dear to me, which is splendorous and apt for his basic nature, with me, inseparably. The word *mayakkam* will also come in the meaning of uniting. In other words, he did it in such a way that I could never let go of him ever.

**ādalāl** – since he came on his own and gave me all these benefits

**en āvidān** – my soul, which is fully under his control and which is dear to him, similar to his *kaustubam* (a divine chain)

**iyakkelām aṟuttu** – severing everything, which come continuously as cause-effect chain, starting with *avidyā* (lack of knowledge), *puṇya pāpa karmas* (deeds which result in virtue or sin), *viśaya vāsanai* (involvement with worldly matters, continuing with past impressions), *viśaya ruci* (continuous involvement with worldly pursuits) and *dēha sambandam* (connection with a physical form which is the transformation of primordial matter). In the first line of this pāsuram, āḻvār had mentioned *iyakkaṟāda pal piṟappil*, severing the connection with physical form which is the reason for everything. Here, He adds the ignorant deeds which are effected by that connection with physical form, the impression and repeated involvement which happen as a result of being connected to that physical form, and says that emperumān severs everything.

**aṟāda inba vīdu peṟṟadē** – \[the ātmā\] attained mōkśam which is in the form of kainkaryam \[servitude to emperumān\] which is with unmatched *brahmānandam*, which is without any break and which lasts until ātmā lasts \[in other words, for ever\].

This brings us to the end of this prabandam.

periya pirāttiyār samēda periya perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
tirumaḻisaippirān tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam  
periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruadigaḷē śaraṇam

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
